# Application audit — 2026-08-15

**Frontend, backend and Android, audited for correctness. AI surfaces deliberately excluded.**

**126 distinct defects**, each anchored to a line of real code and each put through an
adversarial verification pass whose instruction was to destroy it. Two findings were destroyed
and are listed, with the reason, at the end — a register that only records its successes is not
evidence of anything.

---

## What this is, and what it is not

This answers one request: *"Go through the entire frontend, backend, and android, and audit what
all remains to be fixed, what all requires refinement, and has not been correctly or completely
implemented … we are not going through the ai surfaces for now, but the rest of them should be
extremely sound as well, ai is an additional layer, not the entire thing."*

**IT IS A REGISTER OF DEFECTS, NOT A PLAN.** Nothing here has been fixed. It is deliberately
separate from `docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md`, which holds only defects that have been through the
fix-and-pin cycle; items are promoted from here into that file as they are taken on. Keeping them
apart is what stops this document's volume from diluting a register that is meant to be short and
wholly actionable.

### Scope, stated precisely

| In scope | Out of scope |
|---|---|
| Auth, roles, permissions, object-level authorization | AI layers: register / proofread / expand / translate / caption / subtitles |
| The design-workshop record, stage save/merge/delete semantics | Dictation tiers, the ASR runtime, sherpa-onnx, Tier 2 models |
| Artisans, crafts, products, processes, search, reference data | The device-tier probe and the AI-verb daily cap |
| Media, uploads, entitlement, export | `backend/app/services/ai.py` |
| Reports, templates, annexures (the non-AI ones) | |
| Settings, secrets, preferences, analytics, tasks, review | |
| The offline draft/sync state machine on **both** clients | |
| Forms, field rendering, validation, derived fields, rich text | |
| Designer-defined **custom sections** — authored questions, not AI | |
| Contract parity between web, handset and server | |

**The working tree was audited, not `HEAD`.** There are ~196 modified and ~105 untracked files of
work in flight; the files on disk are the application as it stands.

### How it was produced

15 domain audits ran in parallel, each given an explicit brief, the repository's CodeGraph index,
and a standing instruction that a finding it could not anchor to a line it had actually read was
not a finding. Each domain's output then went to a **separate adversarial verifier** whose default
posture was that every finding was wrong, and whose job was to open the cited file, re-read the
control flow, hunt for the guard or caller-side check that would make the failure unreachable, and
kill it. Verifiers rewrote the mechanism, severity and remedy where the finder had them wrong, and
were invited to report defects the finder had missed.

30 agents, 0 errors, 1,878 tool calls, 6.58 M tokens, 65 minutes.

| Verdict | Count | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| CONFIRMED | 95 | The verifier tried to refute it and failed |
| PARTIAL | 31 | Real, but the finder got the mechanism, severity or remedy wrong — corrected here |
| REFUTED | 2 | Does not hold. Listed at the end with what kills it |

**31 of 126 findings were corrected by their verifier**, 18 more were
found *by* a verifier and had been missed by the audit proper, and 2 were
destroyed outright. That is 51 findings — **40% of this
document** — that a single-pass audit would have got wrong, missed, or reported falsely. It is the
whole argument for the second pass, and the reason to distrust any audit that did not run one.

### Severity

| | BLOCKER | CRITICAL | MAJOR | MINOR | LOW | Total |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **backend** | 1 | 1 | 12 | 17 | 13 | **44** |
| **frontend** | 0 | 1 | 18 | 10 | 4 | **33** |
| **android** | 1 | 2 | 11 | 7 | 10 | **31** |
| **cross-surface** | 0 | 3 | 8 | 5 | 2 | **18** |
| **Total** | 2 | 7 | 49 | 39 | 29 | **126** |

* **BLOCKER** — data loss, corruption or an authorization bypass reachable in ordinary use
* **CRITICAL** — a feature dead on the wire, or wrong data reaching a delivered report
* **MAJOR** — wrong behaviour a user will hit
* **MINOR** — real, but narrow
* **LOW** — latent; needs unusual conditions

### Read these first

1. **[CRITICAL]** (backend) Any designer can attach a questionnaire — and every sitting recorded against it — to any other designer's workshop by id, and it prints in that workshop's report annexure  
   `backend/app/api/routes/questionnaire_forms.py:313`
2. **[BLOCKER]** (backend) PATCH /processes/{id} rewrites and deletes the whole step list with no authorization check and no audit entry, for any signed-in account  
   `backend/app/api/routes/processes.py:224`
3. **[CRITICAL]** (cross-surface) Product, tool and process media carry no fetchable URL for any caller, including the uploader and a master admin, because those routes call public_encode with no viewer  
   `backend/app/api/routes/products.py:135`
4. **[CRITICAL]** (frontend) Adding a collection row and then deleting it records a phantom deletion, which later earns the sweep and deletes every server row of that collection the browser has never seen  
   `frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/[id]/stages/[stageKey]/page.tsx:801`
5. **[CRITICAL]** (cross-surface) GET /media/jobs validates statusFilter against the RECORD status enum, so the media processing queue panel 422s on every load and is permanently stuck on "Loading processing jobs…"  
   `backend/app/api/routes/media.py:906`
6. **[CRITICAL]** (android) An authoritative stage the designer emptied offline is skipped by every later push and scored "Backed up to the server"  
   `android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/WorkshopSync.kt:1383`
7. **[CRITICAL]** (android) Opening an emptied stage online re-seeds the deleted rows through `fromRemote`, which ignores `emptiedEntities`, and the next save erases the deletion record  
   `android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/StageScreen.kt:519`
8. **[CRITICAL]** (cross-surface) The handset never sends `scope` to the REF picker endpoint, so every WORKSHOP-scoped reference field on the phone is served the un-narrowed list  
   `android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/WorkshopRepositoryApi.kt:847`
9. **[BLOCKER]** (android) The handset's rich-text editor judges a document empty by text alone, so a caption-less photograph is never written and clearing a caption nulls the field  
   `android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/RichTextOps.kt:305`

## BLOCKER — 2

### [BLOCKER] (backend) PATCH /processes/{id} rewrites and deletes the whole step list with no authorization check and no audit entry, for any signed-in account

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** backend/app/api/routes/processes.py:224-253 (route signature 228, `exclude={"steps"}` at 231, guard at 239, `_sync_steps` call at 249-250) and processes.py:124-126 (`_sync_steps`'s delete); backend/app/core/deps.py:577-593 (`assert_can_contribute_fields`); backend/app/services/access.py:96-118 (`record_revision`) and 121-146 (`guard_record_edit`). Contrast backend/app/api/routes/workshops.py:627-636 and backend/app/api/routes/tools.py:207-232.

**What it does.** The route is gated by `Depends(get_current_user)` alone — every signed-in account, down to a role that `require_record_creator` would refuse. Line 231 builds `data = clean_data(payload.model_dump(exclude_unset=True, exclude={"steps"}))`, so the step list is deliberately kept out of `data` and therefore out of every guard that reads `data`. `guard_record_edit` (access.py:121) is called with that step-less `data`; for a non-admin, non-owner, non-professor, non-EDIT-grantee it falls through to `assert_can_contribute_fields(record, user, data)`, whose `locked_fields` comprehension (deps.py:582-588) iterates `data.items()` — with `data == {}` the list is empty and the guard returns silently. `record_revision` (access.py:96) iterates the same `data`, finds `changes == {}` and returns before creating a `RecordRevision`. Control then reaches `if payload.steps is not None: await _sync_steps(process_id, payload.steps)`, which computes `removed = [step.id for step in existing if step.id not in keep]` and issues `db.processstep.delete_many(where={"id": {"in": removed}})`. I looked specifically for the guard that would stop this and it does not exist: no `assert_can_contribute_relation`, no workshop-access check, no owner test on the steps path. The repository does this correctly twice elsewhere — `workshops.py:627-636` counts the existing links and calls `assert_can_contribute_relation` before `replace_workshop_artisans`, and `tools.py:207-232` resolves `_may_manage_tool_links` before touching `ToolArtisan`.

**How it fails.** Two reachable routes to it. (a) Raw request: any signed-in account sends `PATCH /api/processes/{id}` with body `{"steps": []}`. `ProcessUpdate` (schemas/records.py:387-397) declares `steps: list[ProcessStepInput] | None = None`, so the body validates; `data` is `{}`; `guard_record_edit` passes; `if data:` skips the row update; `_sync_steps(process_id, [])` computes `removed` = every existing step id and deletes them. HTTP 200, empty `steps` in the response. (b) Through the shipped UI, no crafting required: `frontend/components/forms/ProcessForm.tsx:482-497` sends the whole payload on save. A researcher who did not create the process opens it, deletes one step and saves without touching anything else — every scalar field matches the stored row, so `values_match` is true for all of them and `locked_fields` is still empty, the guard passes, and `_sync_steps` deletes the step. The contributor guard that is supposed to protect another person's populated data never sees the only thing the request changed.

**What it costs.** Every documented step of any process in the repository can be deleted or replaced by any account that can sign in, in one request, answered 200, with no `RecordRevision` written — so an admin cannot reconstruct what was there. The `.docx` process annexure and the process detail then print an empty or a stranger's step list. The photographs attached to the deleted steps are orphaned in the same request (see the media-orphan finding), and there is no audit row tying the two together.

**Remedy.** Guard the relation before it is written, using the privilege `guard_record_edit` already returns, exactly as `update_workshop` does: `privileged = await guard_record_edit(process, current_user, data, "process")`; then `if payload.steps is not None:` → `if not privileged: assert_can_contribute_relation(process, current_user, await db.processstep.count(where={"processId": process_id}) > 0, "steps")` → `await _sync_steps(...)`. Separately, feed the audit: `record_revision` must be given a synthetic `steps` entry carrying the before/after id+name lists, since `REVISION_SKIP_FIELDS` (access.py:80-89) does not skip it and it is currently never handed one.

**What the verifier checked.** Tried to kill it four ways. (1) Route dependency: `Depends(get_current_user)` at processes.py:228, and `get_current_user` (deps.py:414-418) applies no role test — only `create_process` uses `require_record_creator`. (2) Router registration: `app/api/router.py:47` includes the processes router with no extra dependency, and `app/main.py:625` includes `api_router` bare — no middleware-level gate. (3) `require_record` (records.py:646-650) is a plain `find_unique` + 404; it applies no ownership or visibility filter, and `viewable_where` (records.py:516-533) returns `{}` anyway. (4) The guard itself: I read `assert_can_contribute_fields` in full — the early return is only for admin/owner; for everyone else the comprehension over an empty `data` yields `[]` and the function returns None. `ProcessUpdate` is not `extra=forbid`-blocked for a steps-only body. Nothing kills it. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md — the seven open entries are the LIKE wildcards, the stale Prisma client, the web stage fold, the handset non-last-row deletion, `PUT /custom-sections` LWW, `StageSaveResultDto.removed`, and `patchDraftHeader`; none touches processes. Severity held at BLOCKER: silent data loss plus an authorization hole, reachable through the shipped UI by an ordinary non-owner researcher, not only by a crafted request.

### [BLOCKER] (android) The handset's rich-text editor judges a document empty by text alone, so a caption-less photograph is never written and clearing a caption nulls the field

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/RichTextOps.kt:305; android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/RichTextEditor.kt:308-309, 379-384, 402-404, 456; android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/report/RichText.kt:244-279; backend/app/services/rich_text.py (RichBlock.is_empty, IMAGE arm); frontend/lib/richText.ts:414-417

**What it does.** Verified as quoted. `fun isEmptyDoc(doc: RichDoc): Boolean = doc.blocks.all { isJsBlank(it.text) }` (RichTextOps.kt:305) has no IMAGE arm, while the same file's model at report/RichText.kt:278-279 has one — `val isEmpty: Boolean get() = if (kind == BlockKind.IMAGE) media.isEmpty() else text.isBlank()` — as do the server and the browser. `RichBlock.text` (report/RichText.kt:265-274) is the concatenation of spans, so a caption-less IMAGE block yields `""` and `isJsBlank("")` is vacuously true. `signatureOf` (RichTextEditor.kt:308-309) returns `"EMPTY"` for such a document and `emit` (379-384) both short-circuits on it and passes `onChange(null)` on it. `commit` (392-404) ends in `emit(next)`, so the image-insert path at :456 goes through it.

**How it fails.** Path A (verified end to end): the field's stored value is null → `fromJson(null)` → `forEditing` → one empty paragraph → `lastEmitted = "EMPTY"` (RichTextEditor.kt:363). The designer taps Photograph; `bridge.attach` copies the bytes and registers a descriptor (FieldRenderer.kt:131-152 `DwMediaBridge.attach`), `commit(insertImage(doc, anchor, id), moveFocus = true)` runs; `insertImage` (RichTextOps.kt:736-769) splices an IMAGE block with `spans = emptyList()` when the caption is empty. `signatureOf(next)` is `"EMPTY"`, equal to `lastEmitted`, so `emit` returns at line 381 and `onChange` is never called — the toast at :457-460 still says "Photograph placed." Path B: a document whose only block is an IMAGE with a caption; deleting the caption produces `"EMPTY" != lastEmitted`, so line 383 fires `onChange(null)`; `StageScreen.kt:1285-1287` routes that to `Map.put(key, null)` which is `this - key` (StageScreen.kt:1955-1956), so the key is removed from the singleton payload and the stored value goes with it on the next save.

**What it costs.** Inline photographs cannot be added from the handset unless the designer also types a caption — silently, and behind a success message — and an existing image-only narrative is destroyed by an ordinary caption edit. The orphaned bytes really are referenced by nothing that prints: `renderMediaAnnexure` (ReportScreen.kt:1338-1377) gathers plates from `imagesOf(entity, values, …)`, i.e. from FIELD VALUES, so a descriptor no field value references appears in no annexure and on no page.

**Remedy.** Delegate to the definition that already exists rather than writing a second one: make `isEmptyDoc` `doc.blocks.all { if (it.kind == BlockKind.IMAGE) it.media.isEmpty() else isJsBlank(it.text) }`, or call `doc.isEmpty` (report/RichText.kt:289-291, which is `blocks.all { it.isEmpty }`). Keep `isJsBlank` for the text arm. Two regression tests: placing an image into a blank field must emit a document, and clearing the caption of an image-only field must not emit null.

**What the verifier checked.** I tried to kill this five ways. (1) Is the editor's `RichBlock` a different type from the report model's, so that `it.text` means something else? No — RichTextOps and RichTextEditor operate on the same `com.designprototype.workshop.report.RichDoc`/`RichBlock`, whose `isEmpty` already carries the IMAGE arm; that is precisely what makes the two predicates disagree on the same object. (2) Does `insertImage` also insert a paragraph that would carry text? No — it splices exactly one IMAGE block (RichTextOps.kt:757-766) and only adds an empty paragraph when the document had no blocks. (3) Is the Photograph button reachable in the real product, or only in previews? `RichTextEditor(` has exactly one call site (FieldRenderer.kt:294) and it passes `media = media, mediaField = field`, i.e. the same bridge every media field on the stage uses — so yes. (4) Does `onChange(null)` get ignored somewhere downstream, which would refute Path B? No — `put` explicitly removes the key (StageScreen.kt:1955-1956) and `save_stage` writes the entry's `data` from the payload, so the field's previous document is not merged back. (5) Does the photograph survive somewhere else, making the loss recoverable? The descriptor survives in the draft's media list and would sync, but nothing in the report reads it (see consequence), so the finder's 'referenced by nothing' holds for everything a reader sees. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md (checked all headings). BLOCKER stands: silent, unannounced loss of record content on a path a designer walks normally.

---

## CRITICAL — 7

### [CRITICAL] (backend) Any designer can attach a questionnaire — and every sitting recorded against it — to any other designer's workshop by id, and it prints in that workshop's report annexure

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** backend/app/api/routes/questionnaire_forms.py:313-314 (upload), :527-528 (create), :580-583 (patch); backend/app/services/records.py:646 (require_record); consumed at backend/app/services/questionnaire_forms.py:291-294 via backend/app/services/design_workshops.py:2161-2163 and backend/app/api/routes/design_workshops.py:3215

**What it does.** All three routes that set `Questionnaire.designWorkshopId` gate the caller only as a designer and then check that a workshop row EXISTS. `require_record` (records.py:646) is a bare `find_unique` — `record = await delegate.find_unique(where={"id": record_id})` — with no creator test, no `DesignWorkshopViewer` test, no admin test and no `deletedAt` filter. `_require_designer` (questionnaire_forms.py:98-103) is `can_run_design_workshops`, which any DESIGNER passes. `PATCH` additionally calls `_require_owner`, but that is ownership of the QUESTIONNAIRE, not of the workshop being pointed at. `report_items` (services/questionnaire_forms.py:291-294) then reads `where={"designWorkshopId": design_workshop_id, "isActive": True}` with no permission filter, on the stated ground that "the attachment is what puts the questionnaire inside the workshop's own access boundary" — an assumption these three routes do not establish.

**How it fails.** Designer B (any DESIGNER account, no viewer row on workshop W, 404'd by `GET /api/design-workshops/W`) issues `POST /api/questionnaires` with `{"title":"…","designWorkshopId":"W"}`, or `PATCH /api/questionnaires/{B's own form}` with the same field. Both return 200/201. On W's next report generation, `_report_inputs` sees `SpecialSection.ANNEXURE_QUESTIONNAIRES` in the template (routes/design_workshops.py:3197) and calls `attach_report_questionnaires` → `report_items`, which loads B's questionnaire and all of its sittings — respondent names, interviewer notes, every answer — into the .docx as an annexure of A's fieldwork. `questionnaire_warnings` fires only for an attached questionnaire with no answers, so an answered one is printed silently. Symmetrically, everyone on W now passes `_works_on_this_questionnaires_workshop` (questionnaire_forms.py:145-155) and can read B's sittings through `GET /questionnaires/{id}` and download them via `/xlsx`.

**What it costs.** Survey data of arbitrary provenance, under arbitrary respondents' names, is injected into a colleague's report submitted to a government body under that colleague's name, with no warning to either party; and the injector's own respondents' names and answers are pushed into a stranger's access boundary. A mistyped or stale workshop id produces the same result by accident.

**Remedy.** At all three sites replace `require_record(db.designworkshop, …)` with `load_workshop_or_404(workshop_id, current_user, for_edit=True)` (backend/app/services/design_workshops.py:100), the same helper `save_stage_data` and `save_custom_sections` use — it admits creator, admin and viewer-grant holder and refuses a soft-deleted workshop. Detaching (`designWorkshopId: null`) needs no check. Note that this closes only the ATTACHMENT door; see the `missed` entry below for the sitting-injection door it leaves open.

**What the verifier checked.** Read all three routes verbatim (313-314, 527-528, 580-583) — the quoted code exists. Read `require_record` at records.py:646: it is exactly the two-line find_unique the finder quotes, no deletedAt filter. Checked `_require_designer` (98-103) and `_require_owner` (178-180) — neither touches the workshop. Grepped the whole module for `load_workshop_or_404`: zero hits. Checked whether the schema constrains it — `QuestionnaireCreate.designWorkshopId` is a plain optional string. Checked the consumer chain end to end: report_items (291-294) has no filter; attach_report_questionnaires (design_workshops.py:2141-2174) only catches exceptions; the route gates on `draws_questionnaires` (routes/design_workshops.py:3197). Counted the templates: `QUESTIONNAIRE_ANNEXURE` is used at report_templates.py:354, 367, 400, 431, 448 and 479 — that is ALL SIX templates, not five as the finder wrote, so the exposure is broader than claimed. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md (searched the Open section and the closed registers). Severity kept at CRITICAL: wrong data reaches a delivered report and a cross-account read boundary is opened, reachable by one ordinary API call.

### [CRITICAL] (frontend) Adding a collection row and then deleting it records a phantom deletion, which later earns the sweep and deletes every server row of that collection the browser has never seen

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/[id]/stages/[stageKey]/page.tsx:801-808 (patchCollection); frontend/components/designworkshop/EntityForm.tsx:565-576 (addRow/removeRow); frontend/lib/designWorkshopStore.ts:1100 (removedFrom union in putDraftStage), :1799-1815 (foldStageInto's emptied branch), :2832-2836 (stageSweep); backend/app/services/design_workshops.py:1184 (sweep_entities)

**What it does.** Verified verbatim. `patchCollection` at page.tsx:801 is a plain (non-memoised) function that reads `collections` off the render closure, so after `addRow` has pushed N+1 rows and React has re-rendered, `removeRow`'s N-row array satisfies `rows.length < (collections[entityKey] ?? []).length` and the entity is pushed into `removedFrom`. `CollectionTable.removeRow` (EntityForm.tsx:573) holds the removed row and discards it. `putDraftStage` UNIONs `removedFrom` into the draft (designWorkshopStore.ts:1100: `Array.from(new Set([...previous.removedFrom, ...(data.removedFrom ?? [])]))`), so the phantom is sticky and cannot be withdrawn.

THE FINDER GOT THE MECHANISM RIGHT BUT PICKED THE WEAKER OF THE TWO CONSEQUENCES, AND MISSED THE ONE THAT NEEDS NO SECOND USER. Their read-stage scenario needs a concurrent writer, because an authoritative browser resends every row it holds. The severe path runs through `foldStageInto`, which exists in the working tree (designWorkshopStore.ts:1730) and closes the open finding "the web has no fold": at :1799 it does `if (emptied.has(entityKey)) { const unseen = serverRows.filter(isUnseen).length; if (unseen > 0) sweptRows[entityKey] = unseen; continue; }` — a collection named by `removedFrom` is deliberately NOT folded back in — and the fold then stamps `serverLoadedAt`. So a phantom entry causes the fold to withhold the office's rows AND to grant the authority that lets the very next save delete them.

Their "for ever" claim about the never-read arm is now wrong for the same reason: `foldStageInto` earns the authority on the next online open, which converts the false alarm into a real sweep rather than leaving it stranded.

**How it fails.** Stage 13 of a workshop written up in the office holds six `prototype` rows. A designer opens stage 13 in the field with no signal — `adoptServerStage` is never reached, `serverLoadedAt` stays null, the form shows no prototype rows. They press "Add prototype", see the empty panel open, realise it belongs on stage 15, and press the bin on the row they just created. Net change: nothing. `patchCollection` sees 0 < 1 and writes `removedFrom: ['prototype']`; the autosave banks it. They need not press Save at all. Days later they open stage 13 with a connection: `adoptServerStage` takes the dirty branch, `foldStageInto` hits `emptied.has('prototype')`, refuses to fold the six server rows in, records `sweptRows.prototype = 6`, and stamps `serverLoadedAt`. The form still shows no prototypes. They correct one word elsewhere and press Save. `stageSweep` now returns `{replaceCollections: true, emptiedEntities: ['prototype']}`; the payload names no prototype rows; `sweep_entities = (touched_entities | {'prototype'}) & collection_keys` includes it, and all six rows are soft-deleted under an HTTP 200 reported as "Stage saved — 0 added, 1 updated, 6 removed".

**What it costs.** Pressing Add and then Delete on one blank row — the most ordinary correction a form offers, and one that changes nothing anywhere — arms an authoritative "these are now exactly the rows" claim that destroys every row of that collection the browser has never downloaded. No second user and no save of the phantom itself are required. The one sentence written to warn about it (`foldNotice`'s swept-rows paragraph) is stored on the draft and rendered nowhere on the web (see `missed` below), so the designer's only signal is a `removed: 6` buried in the save notice.

**Remedy.** Make the page record WHICH row went instead of inferring it from a count. Give `CollectionTable` an `onRowRemoved(row: DwRow)` (or a second argument to `onRowsChange`), and in `patchCollection` add the entity to `removedFrom` only when the removed row carried an `_entryId`, or when its `_clientKey` is present in `banked.current.collections[entityKey]` — i.e. the row has been written to the draft and could therefore exist on the server. A row minted by `blankRow()` in this render has never been anywhere and its removal is not a deletion. The same test must gate the `sweep.withheld` sentence and `foldStageInto`'s `emptied` set, so neither can name a collection the designer never emptied. The finder's remedy is correct as far as it goes; it must also reach `foldStageInto`, which is the arm that turns the phantom into row loss.

**What the verifier checked.** Read page.tsx:801-808, EntityForm.tsx:565-576, designWorkshopStore.ts:1100, 1730-1830, 2832-2836 and design_workshops.py:1150-1195 in full. Tried to kill it four ways. (1) Is `patchCollection` memoised so it reads a stale `collections`? No — its own comment says it is deliberately not a `useCallback`, and it is wired directly at page.tsx:1394 as `onRowsChange={(rows) => patchCollection(entity.key, rows)}`, so the post-add render's closure sees N+1. (2) Does `stageSweep`'s never-read gate make it harmless? Only until the next online open — `foldStageInto` at :1730 now exists and stamps `serverLoadedAt` on a dirty stage, which is what makes the phantom destructive rather than merely noisy. (3) Is it already in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md? The count comparison is mentioned only in passing inside the CLOSED blocker "one row deleted on a never-read browser deleted five rows", whose fix was the authority gate; the phantom itself, and the `foldStageInto` amplification, are recorded nowhere. The open item "a withheld deletion has no way back" is the OPPOSITE defect and is itself now fixed in the tree by `foldStageInto`. (4) Does the server scope the sweep narrowly enough to save it? No — design_workshops.py:1184 unions `touched_entities` with `payload.emptiedEntities`. Severity raised from MAJOR to CRITICAL because the no-concurrency path deletes committed repository rows.

---

### [CRITICAL] (android) An authoritative stage the designer emptied offline is skipped by every later push and scored "Backed up to the server"

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/WorkshopSync.kt:1383-1389 (pushStages), :829-831 + :885-887 (statusOf), :1911-1912 (isAuthoritative), :1108 (syncOneWorkshop early exit), :319-321 (isFullySynced)

**What it does.** Both passes ask the same two questions and neither consults the deletion records. `pushStages`: `val empty = stored == null || (stored.values.isEmpty() && stored.rows.isEmpty() && stored.custom.isEmpty())` then `if (empty && record?.signature.isNullOrBlank()) continue` (:1383-1389). `statusOf` has the byte-identical pair at :885-887 (`return@forEach`). The only reader of `emptiedEntities`/`deletedRowKeys` in `statusOf` sits at :829-878 behind `if (remoteId != null && !isAuthoritative(stored, record))`, and `isAuthoritative` is `stored?.stageSeen == true` (:1911-1912). So for a stage that IS authoritative (read online once), holds nothing but a recorded deletion, and has no acknowledged signature, `unsentDeletions` is not incremented and the stage is then swallowed by the empty-return — `pendingStages` stays 0, `isFullySynced` is true (:319-321), `syncOneWorkshop` returns at `if (statusOf(...).isFullySynced) return true` (:1108) and `pushStages` would `continue` anyway. The payload the phone declines to build is legal and complete: `buildStageBody` with `authoritative = true` emits `replaceCollections = true` and `emptiedEntities = [...]` (:1833-1842). `retryWorkshop` is no escape hatch — it clears failures and re-enters the same `pushStages` gate.

**How it fails.** A collection-only stage (the load's own comment at StageScreen.kt:515-517 notes eight of the twenty-two declare no singleton). Morning, in signal: the designer opens the stage; StageScreen.kt:604-610 takes the `fromRemote` branch, `seen = true`, six costing rows on screen; nothing typed, so no signature is ever recorded. Afternoon, no signal: they delete all six rows. `onRowsChange` (StageScreen.kt:1330-1367) sets `emptied` and `deletedRows`; `persistLocally` writes `rows = []`, `stageSeen = true`, `emptiedEntities = ["costLine"]`, `deletedRowKeys = [...]`; `saveAndSync` calls `pushStage`, whose PUT throws offline and returns `StagePush.NotSent` recording nothing (WorkshopSync.kt:1543-1553). That evening, back in signal: `statusOf` skips the deletion block (authoritative) and returns early on `empty && signature blank`; the workshop scores fully synced and the list row reads "Backed up to the server" (:329). No later pass ever builds a payload for that stage.

**What it costs.** A deliberate deletion of a whole collection never reaches the repository, while the phone actively asserts the opposite. The six superseded costing lines stay in the server record and print in the .docx the ministry receives; no counter, no problem line and no banner mentions them again. Distinct from the recorded open finding (which is about a NON-last-row deletion having no record at all): here the record exists, the authority exists, and the payload is simply never built.

**Remedy.** Compute the owed deletions BEFORE the empty test in both places and fold them into "has something to say": `val owed = stored?.emptiedEntities.orEmpty().filter { key -> spec.collections.any { it.key == key } }` plus the `deletedRowKeys` equivalent, then `if (empty && owed.isEmpty() && owedRows.isEmpty() && record?.signature.isNullOrBlank()) continue/return@forEach`. In `statusOf` the authoritative case must count as PENDING, not as `unsentDeletions` (it is sendable): let the stage fall through to the existing `signatureOf(built.body) != record?.signature` comparison, which already answers true. Pin with a test: draft with `stageSeen = true`, `rows = []`, `emptiedEntities = ["costLine"]`, no sync record — assert `isFullySynced == false` and that the pass issues a PUT carrying `emptiedEntities`.

**What the verifier checked.** Opened every cited site. Line numbers had drifted (~4 lines in pushStages, and statusOf now also carries an `owedRows` block at :846-878) but the quoted code is present verbatim. Tried to kill it four ways: (1) does the screen's own push cover it? `pushStage` (:1517-1562) has NO empty gate, so it WOULD send — but only online; offline it returns `NotSent` and records nothing, which is exactly the scenario. (2) Does the failed push leave a record that keeps the workshop unsynced? `noteStage` is not called on that path; only `record.failure != null` from a background 4xx adds a `problems` line, and `problems` is not part of `isFullySynced` (:319-321). (3) Does `retryWorkshop`/manual sync bypass the gate? No — it funnels into the same `pushStages`. (4) Is it the recorded open finding? No: OPEN_FINDINGS.md:163-189 covers non-last-row deletions with NO record; `deletedRowKeys` now exists in the tree and this defect is about the authoritative branch being unreachable in both counters. Severity kept at CRITICAL: wrong data reaches the delivered report and the status line claims the opposite.

### [CRITICAL] (android) Opening an emptied stage online re-seeds the deleted rows through `fromRemote`, which ignores `emptiedEntities`, and the next save erases the deletion record

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/StageScreen.kt:519-520 (holdsWork), :604-610 (the seed branch), :2042-2064 (fromRemote), :696 (emptied re-read), :2135-2137 (the filter that drops the record); android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/DwStageFold.kt:284-305 (the branch that would have honoured it)

**What it does.** `holdsWork = local != null && (local.values.isNotEmpty() || local.rows.isNotEmpty() || local.custom.isNotEmpty())` (:519-520) has no term for `emptiedEntities`, so a stage whose entire content was one collection is "holds no work" the moment that collection is deleted. The load therefore falls to `else if (remoteId != null)` (:604-610), which adopts the server's bucket verbatim via `fromRemote` (:2042-2064) — a function with no notion of `emptiedEntities` — and returns `StageLoad(..., seen = true, downloadFailed = false)` with `foldNotice` unset, so `noteTitle`/`downloadNote` are both null (:665-693) and nothing is said. `dwFoldServerStage`, the ONE reader that declines to re-add rows for an emptied entity and counts them in `sweptRows` (DwStageFold.kt:284-305), is unreachable on this branch. `emptied` is then re-read from disk at :696 and still holds the key, but `persistLocally` writes `emptiedEntities = (emptied + existing…).filter { key -> state.collections[key].orEmpty().isEmpty() }` (:2135-2137) — the collection is no longer empty, so the first debounced save deletes the last record that the deletion happened, and `stageSeen = true` then lets the next payload re-affirm all six rows with `replaceCollections`.

**How it fails.** Two entry points, both ordinary. (a) The unsent-deletion case of finding 1: the designer reopens the stage next morning with signal to check it went up; the six rows they deleted are back on screen with no notice, they touch one cell, and the record is gone. (b) Worse, the case the app itself instructs: an UNREAD stage that was emptied is counted by `unsentDeletions` and `statusOf` prints "Open the stage once with a connection and it goes up on the save straight after" (WorkshopSync.kt:873-877). Following that instruction on a collection-only stage takes this branch — because the draft now holds nothing — so instead of the fold earning authority and carrying the deletion, `fromRemote` re-seeds the rows, the next save clears `emptiedEntities`, `unsentDeletions` falls to 0 and the workshop reports itself fully synced over a deletion that was reversed.

**What it costs.** An explicit deletion is silently reversed and then forgotten, including along the remediation path the status screen prescribes. The rows were never deleted server-side, so there is no `deletedAt` and no client key to recover from — DwStageFold.kt:58-63 names this as the loss that is "not recoverable by anybody, because nobody knows to look". Withdrawn rates and retired tools print in the delivered report with nothing on the phone contradicting them.

**Remedy.** Route the emptied case through the fold: make the branch test `holdsWork || local?.emptiedEntities?.isNotEmpty() == true` (and the same for `deletedRowKeys`), so `dwFoldServerStage` — which already honours the deletion, keeps `emptiedEntities` and announces `sweptRows` — is the reader of the server's copy. Equivalently, teach `fromRemote` to take the local `emptiedEntities` and skip those entity keys; the fold reuse is smaller and already tested. Independently, `persistLocally`'s filter must not drop a key merely because the collection is non-empty in the CURRENT state when those rows arrived from the server rather than from the designer — `recordStageSent` (WorkshopSync.kt:2282-2326) is the correct place for the clear, scoped to what an acknowledged payload carried.

**What the verifier checked.** Checked every line. Cited numbers drifted (holdsWork is 519-520 not 506-507; the seed branch 604-610 not 591-597; the filter 2135-2137 not 2091-2093) but the code is verbatim. Attempts to refute: (1) Is there any other guard that filters re-seeded rows by `emptied`? Grepped every use of `emptied` in StageScreen (328, 696, 774, 987, 1339-1342) — none filters `state`. (2) Does the branch write the re-seeded rows to disk at load, or only in composition? Only the fold branch persists (:593-595), so with no edit the disk record survives — but the screen still shows the rows silently and the first keystroke destroys the record. (3) Does `canReach`/offline save it? `canReach` only gates the fold branch; this branch attempts the read unconditionally, so it fires exactly when the designer has signal, which is when they check. (4) Is it the same defect as finding 1? No — finding 1 is the pass never sending; this is the read reversing it, and (b) above is reachable without finding 1's preconditions. Severity CRITICAL confirmed, and raised in confidence by the app's own advice leading into it.

### [CRITICAL] (cross-surface) The handset never sends `scope` to the REF picker endpoint, so every WORKSHOP-scoped reference field on the phone is served the un-narrowed list

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/WorkshopRepositoryApi.kt:847-853 (declaration at :848); android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/WorkshopRepository.kt:509-555 (key at :518, call at :546-551); backend/app/api/routes/design_workshops.py:1277; backend/app/services/design_workshops.py:457-462; frontend/components/designworkshop/StageReferenceField.tsx:105-107; frontend/lib/designWorkshops.ts:991-1001

**What it does.** Verified as quoted. The Retrofit method `designWorkshopReferences` declares exactly four parameters — `@Path("id")`, `@Query("model")`, `@Query("filterBy")`, `@Query("search")` — and no `scope`. `WorkshopRepository.designWorkshopReferences` (line 509) does take a `scope: String`, spends it at line 518 on `DwReferenceStore.cacheKey(model, scope, workshopId.orEmpty(), filterValue)` and at line 525 on `anyForModel`, and then calls the API at 546-551 without it. The server's route signature is `scope: str = Query(REF_SCOPE_ALL, max_length=16)` (routes/design_workshops.py:1277) and `reference_options` adds the narrowing clause only under `if scope == REF_SCOPE_WORKSHOP and spec.workshop_where and record.workshopId:` (services/design_workshops.py:459-461). So an omitted parameter is served as ALL. The browser does send it (`scope: field.refScope` at StageReferenceField.tsx:107, forwarded verbatim by `listStageReferences`, designWorkshops.ts:995).

**How it fails.** I confirmed the four WORKSHOP-scoped REF fields exist and are all served by `REFERENCE_MODELS` (not by the in-record `_dw_entity` branch, which would have made scope moot): `stage_definitions.py:455-456` `processStep.processRef` → model `Process`; `:605` `existingProduct.artisanRef` → `Artisan`; `:609-610` `existingProduct.productRef` → `ProductDocumentation`; `:1054-1057` `prototype.productRef` → `ProductDocumentation`; all with `ref_scope=W_SCOPE`. The same four appear as `"refScope":"WORKSHOP"` in the handset's bundled `design-workshop-schema.json` (4 occurrences), and `Process`, `Artisan`, `ProductDocumentation` are all keys of `REFERENCE_MODELS` (design_workshops.py:305, 333, 373), each with a `workshop_where` the server would have applied. A designer opens stage 5 on the phone and taps the artisan picker: the request carries no scope, the server returns `scope: "ALL"`, `scopedToWorkshop: false`, and the first 50 rows by `{"name": "asc"}` (`REFERENCE_LIMIT_DEFAULT = 50`, design_workshops.py:202) of the whole artisan table.

**What it costs.** The workshop narrowing that the browser gets is dead on the wire for the handset — the surface the fieldwork is actually done on. The picker offers strangers, `hydrateFromReference` writes their name/village/phone/craft onto the row, and those values print in the report. The cache-key half is real too and I verified it: `DwReferenceStore.cacheKey` (DwReferenceStore.kt:274-278) sets `owner = workshopId` whenever the scope string is WORKSHOP, so the ALL-scoped answer is filed under the workshop-owned key and keeps being served offline after a fix.

**Remedy.** Add `@Query("scope") scope: String? = null` to `WorkshopRepositoryApi.designWorkshopReferences` and pass `scope.takeIf { it.isNotBlank() }` from `WorkshopRepository` line 546. Because the wrong list is already on disk under the WORKSHOP-owned key, the fix must also invalidate cached entries whose owner is a workshop id (or bump the file-name scheme) or phones keep serving the whole-table list. Pin it with a test that asserts on the serialised request URL, not on the Kotlin argument — the argument has always been correct.

**What the verifier checked.** I tried four ways to kill this. (1) Is `scope` added elsewhere on the wire — an interceptor or an overload? `grep -rn refScope` over `android/app/src/main/java` returns only StageSchema.kt:191 (the DTO field), DwReferenceField.kt:273/279 and :506/512 (the two call sites), and the DwReferenceStore KDoc; there is no second API method and no query-param interceptor. (2) Does the server derive the scope itself when it is absent? No — `list_references` defaults it to `REF_SCOPE_ALL` and its own docstring says deriving it server-side was deliberately rejected. (3) Would the `_dw_entity(model)` branch (services/design_workshops.py:436-440) short-circuit and give an inherently workshop-scoped answer anyway? Only for entity-named models (e.g. `DwParticipant`); the four affected fields name `Process`/`Artisan`/`ProductDocumentation`, which are `REFERENCE_MODELS` keys, so they fall through to the scope-sensitive path. (4) Is it in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md? I read every `###` heading — the seven open items are the LIKE wildcards, the stale Prisma client, the web's missing fold, the handset's non-last-row deletion, last-write-wins `PUT /custom-sections`, `StageSaveResultDto.removed`, and `patchDraftHeader`. Not this. Severity kept at CRITICAL: a narrowing feature that is dead on one client and wrong data reaching a report both match the CRITICAL band.

### [CRITICAL] (cross-surface) GET /media/jobs validates statusFilter against the RECORD status enum, so the media processing queue panel 422s on every load and is permanently stuck on "Loading processing jobs…"

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED · independently found by 2 agents

**Where.** backend/app/api/routes/media.py:906-907; backend/app/services/records.py:454 and :459-479; frontend/components/media/MediaJobsPanel.tsx:119-132, :154, :258-259; frontend/lib/media.ts:1758-1768; frontend/app/(protected)/media/page.tsx:416

**What it does.** `GET /media/jobs` (media.py:897, correctly declared before `GET /{media_id}` at :966, so routing is not the issue) narrows on the MediaProcessingJob table but validates the caller's filter against the wrong enum:

```python
    if statusFilter:
        where["status"] = enum_filter_or_422(statusFilter, RECORD_STATUSES)   # media.py:907
```

`RECORD_STATUSES = frozenset({"DRAFT", "PENDING", "APPROVED", "REJECTED", "NEEDS_REVISION"})` (records.py:454), while `enum MediaProcessingJobStatus` in `backend/prisma/schema.prisma:126-132` is `QUEUED | PROCESSING | COMPLETED | FAILED | CANCELLED`. The intersection is empty, so `enum_filter_or_422` (records.py:474-478) raises 422 `"status must be one of APPROVED, DRAFT, NEEDS_REVISION, PENDING, REJECTED"` for every value the panel can send. Two lines up, the same file does it correctly for a different column (`enum_filter_or_422(mediaType, MEDIA_TYPES, field="mediaType")`, media.py:607), so the shape of the fix already exists in the same function.

The finder's control-flow reading is exact and I could not break it. `load()` (MediaJobsPanel.tsx:119-132) issues the FAILED-count probe unconditionally inside a `Promise.all`, and `listMediaProcessingJobs` (media.ts:1758-1768) only drops a falsy filter — `statusFilter: "FAILED"` always travels. So the "All" pill 422s too. The `.catch` at :143-148 deliberately sets only `error` and leaves `data` untouched, and `items = data?.items ?? null` (:154) with `items === null` rendering "Loading processing jobs..." (:258-259) — so the first (and every) load leaves the panel in its loading state under a red error bar.

**How it fails.** Any signed-in account opens /media (the panel is rendered unconditionally at media/page.tsx:416, and the route is `get_current_user`, not admin-gated). Both requests in the `Promise.all` return 422; `apiFetch` throws; `data` stays `null`. The panel shows the heading, the six filter pills, a red bar reading "status must be one of APPROVED, DRAFT, NEEDS_REVISION, PENDING, REJECTED", and "Loading processing jobs..." indefinitely. Every pill re-issues the same pair and fails identically.

**What it costs.** The whole media-processing queue surface is dead on the wire on the web: no job row, no `MediaProcessingJob.error` reason, no Retry button and no "Run queue now" outcome ever renders. A transcription that exhausted its attempts is again indistinguishable from one never requested — the exact state the panel's own header comment (MediaJobsPanel.tsx:20-39) says it was written to end. The headline count is also inert: `failedTotal` is only assigned on the success path, so the sentence at :215-221 always falls through to the literal "Nothing has failed.", a false statement about data the page never received. Nothing pins it: `frontend/e2e/media-jobs.spec.ts:31` is `test.skip(!EMAIL || !PASSWORD, …)`, and no backend test drives this route.

**Remedy.** Add a job-status constant beside `RECORD_STATUSES` in `backend/app/services/records.py` — `MEDIA_PROCESSING_JOB_STATUSES = frozenset({"QUEUED", "PROCESSING", "COMPLETED", "FAILED", "CANCELLED"})` — and at media.py:907 call `enum_filter_or_422(statusFilter, MEDIA_PROCESSING_JOB_STATUSES, field="statusFilter")`, matching the `field=` usage already at :607 so the 422 names the parameter the client actually sent. Leave :613 (MediaFile.status) on `RECORD_STATUSES` — that one is right. Pin with a backend test driving `GET /media/jobs?statusFilter=FAILED` against Postgres and asserting 200; the Playwright spec cannot substitute because it is credential-skipped.

**What the verifier checked.** I tried four ways to kill it. (1) Route shadowing — enumerated every decorator in media.py: `/jobs` is at :897 and `/{media_id}` at :966, so `/media/jobs` reaches this handler; not a 404. (2) A client-side guard — read `listMediaProcessingJobs` in lib/media.ts:1758-1768; it forwards `statusFilter` verbatim and only drops falsy values, so the hard-coded `"FAILED"` probe always travels. (3) A separate job-status constant already in use — grepped `enum_filter_or_422` and `RECORD_STATUSES` across `backend/app`; media.py imports only `RECORD_STATUSES` (lines 51-56) and no job-status frozenset exists anywhere in the tree. (4) The panel surviving the error — read the `.catch` and the render: `data` is never set, `items === null`, so it renders the loading string, not an empty state. Confirmed the enum values against `backend/prisma/schema.prisma:126-132`. Not present in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md (read the Open section; grepped the file for `media/jobs`, `MediaProcessingJob`, `statusFilter`).

### [CRITICAL] (cross-surface) Product, tool and process media carry no fetchable URL for any caller, including the uploader and a master admin, because those routes call public_encode with no viewer

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** backend/app/api/routes/products.py:135,154,161,187; backend/app/api/routes/tools.py:128,146,153,179; backend/app/api/routes/processes.py:49 (`_encode_light`) and :67 (`_hydrate`); the mechanism at backend/app/services/records.py:50 (`_MEDIA_URL_KEYS`), :56 (`_MEDIA_MARKER = "objectKey"`), :121-127 (the drop) and :174-177 (the viewer-less branch); consumed at frontend/app/(protected)/products/page.tsx:182-188, frontend/app/(protected)/tools/page.tsx:198 and frontend/components/forms/ProcessForm.tsx:124-145 (fed from `initial.media` / `step.media` at :254,:258).

**What it does.** `public_encode(obj)` with no `viewer` takes the branch at records.py:174-177 and sets `allowed = set()` — an EMPTY set, not `None`. `_redact_sensitive` then tests `media_urls is not None and _MEDIA_MARKER in value and value.get("uploadedById") not in media_urls`; `_MEDIA_MARKER` is `"objectKey"`, which is `String @unique` on `MediaFile` (prisma/schema.prisma:791) and therefore present on every encoded media node, and no uploader id is in an empty set — so `url`, `publicUrl` and `objectKey` are popped from every media node. `products.RELATIONS` (products.py:42-49) and `tools.RELATIONS` (tools.py:42-50) both declare `Relation("media", "mediafile", …, many=True)`, and `processes._hydrate` encodes process and per-step media at line 67. None of those call sites passes `current_user`. The URL is real data that is being thrown away: `media.py:525` sets `data["url"] = public_url_for_key(data["objectKey"])` on every upload. The sibling routes prove the intent — artisans.py:269,303,313,375 pass `current_user`, media.py:144 passes `viewer` plus a resolved `media_urls`, and search.py:153 pays for `media_url_owners(current_user)` with a comment saying why.

**How it fails.** A designer uploads three photographs against a product, then opens /products. `GET /api/products` returns each product with `media: [{id, originalFilename, mediaType, …}]` and no `url` key; products/page.tsx:182 builds `url: media.url` as `undefined`, so `MediaPreviewTile` renders a placeholder and the lightbox offers no open/save. Identical on /tools. Worse for processes: the process detail is the ONLY source of step media on the web — ProcessForm seeds `existingPreMedia` from `initial.media` (line 258) and each step's `existingMedia` from `step.media` (line 254), and unlike every other edit page it does not mount `components/media/ExistingMedia.tsx`, which re-fetches through `GET /media` (media.py:144, viewer passed) and would have worked. So process and process-step photographs are unviewable on every web surface that shows them, for the person who uploaded them seconds earlier and for a MASTER_ADMIN alike.

**What it costs.** Product, tool and process media is visible-but-unopenable through the product list, the tool list and the process form. The rows travel, so the UI proves a file exists and then refuses to render or download it — which reads as broken storage rather than as a policy, and gives the designer nothing to act on. The entitlement policy is not being enforced here; it is being applied to a caller nobody named, which is the failure mode records.py:174-177's own comment says the empty set exists to make safe.

**Remedy.** Pass the viewer at all eight sites. For the two list routes and the process detail, pass the resolved grant set the way search.py:153 does: `public_encode(items, current_user, media_urls=await media_url_owners(current_user))` — one query, and only below professor. For the create/patch responses, `public_encode(created/updated, current_user)` is enough. For processes specifically, `_encode_light` and `_hydrate` must take the viewer as a parameter and pass it through, since both are called from three routes.

**What the verifier checked.** Checked whether the URLs arrive some other way. `media.py:525` always writes `url`, so the column is populated. Checked whether the web fetches media separately for these surfaces: `ExistingMedia.tsx:58` does re-fetch `/media` (which passes a viewer, media.py:144) — but grepping `MediaPreviewTile` call sites shows products/page.tsx, tools/page.tsx and ProcessForm.tsx feed it from the record payload, NOT from ExistingMedia, so no re-fetch rescues them. Checked whether `objectKey` might be absent from the encoded node (which would stop the marker firing): it is a non-nullable `@unique` column on MediaFile, and these are full-model `find_many`/`include` reads with no `select`. Checked whether an admin escapes: the viewer-less branch is taken before any rank test, so rank is irrelevant. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md. Severity CRITICAL held — the feature is dead on the wire for three record types on every role.
