## MAJOR — 49

### [MAJOR] (backend) Unlinking an artisan or craft from a workshop clears the column and leaves the mirrored join row, so the record stays in that workshop for every query that reads the join

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** backend/app/api/routes/artisans.py:374 (`await link_workshop_artisan(updated.workshopId, updated.id)`) and backend/app/api/routes/crafts.py:143 (`await link_workshop_craft(updated.workshopId, updated.id)`); the add-only mirror at backend/app/services/workshop_access.py:479-497 and 499-513; the reads at backend/app/services/record_filters.py:136-141 (`artisan_workshop_clause`) and :175-179 (`craft_workshop_clause`), plus artisans.py:243-245 and crafts.py:70-76; the rule it defeats at backend/app/services/records.py:244-251 (`CLEARABLE_KEYS`).

**What it does.** `workshopId` is in `CLEARABLE_KEYS` (records.py:252-268), so `clean_data` keeps an explicit `null` and `db.artisan.update` clears the column — that half works, and the comment above it records that it was added precisely because unlinking used to be a silent no-op. The mirror is then called with the NEW value: `link_workshop_artisan(updated.workshopId, …)` returns immediately on `if not workshop_id` (workshop_access.py:486-487), and its docstring states it "never removes a link". No other code path removes a `WorkshopArtisan`/`WorkshopCraft` row on an artisan or craft edit — `replace_workshop_artisans`/`replace_workshop_crafts` are reached only from `PATCH /workshops` (workshops.py:627-636), i.e. from the workshop side. The join row written by the mirror is indistinguishable from an admin-curated roster row, so there is nothing to remove selectively even if the code wanted to. Every read that honours the join keeps returning the record: `artisan_workshop_clause` branch 2, `craft_workshop_clause` branch 2, the singular `?workshopId=` filters on both list routes, and the datasets/data-browser paths that import those clauses (datasets.py:299,301).

**How it fails.** A designer files an artisan against the wrong workshop, re-opens the artisan, picks the blank/'no workshop' option and saves. `ArtisanForm` sends `workshopId: null`; HTTP 200; `Artisan.workshopId` is NULL and the form shows the record unlinked. The `WorkshopArtisan` row survives, so `GET /artisans?workshopId=<that workshop>` (artisans.py:243-245, which ORs the column with `{"workshops": {"some": …}}`) still returns the artisan, and so does `?workshopIds=` through `artisan_workshop_clause`. Re-filing the artisan under a second workshop adds a second join row without removing the first, so the artisan is now counted in both.

**What it costs.** A workshop's artisan and craft rosters silently retain records that were deliberately removed from them, and the two screens disagree with no way to tell which is right: the record's own page says unlinked while every workshop-scoped list and export says otherwise. Workshop-scoped counts are over-stated, and re-filing a record double-counts it. This is the same silent-no-op the `CLEARABLE_KEYS` comment says was already paid for once, surviving one layer down.

**Remedy.** Give the mirror the previous value and make it bidirectional: capture `artisan.workshopId` (resp. `craft.workshopId`) BEFORE the update, then `sync_workshop_artisan(previous_id, next_id, artisan_id)` — delete the `(previous, record)` row when `previous != next`, then upsert `(next, record)`. If the add-only rule must hold for admin-curated rosters, the join needs a `source` column so only the COLUMN-sourced row is removed; today the two are indistinguishable, which is what makes the current behaviour unfixable in place. Do not solve it by ceasing to read the join — `craft_workshop_clause`'s docstring (record_filters.py:170-176) records that on the live repository the join is the only link the crafts have.

**What the verifier checked.** Looked specifically for a remover. Grepped every write to `workshopartisan`/`workshopcraft`: the only deletes are inside `replace_workshop_artisans`/`replace_workshop_crafts`, called from `PATCH /workshops` only — never from the artisan or craft PATCH. Confirmed the mirror's early return is on the NEW value, so a null cannot even reach a hypothetical delete branch. Confirmed the join is genuinely read by the record-side filters (artisans.py:243-245 ORs it in; `artisan_workshop_clause` branch 2). Confirmed `workshopId` really is in `CLEARABLE_KEYS`, so the column half does clear (otherwise the finding would collapse into 'nothing happens at all'). The only thing that weakens it is that the add-only behaviour is DOCUMENTED intent in workshop_access.py:481-484 — but the documented intent is about not clobbering an admin roster, and it produces an unlink that the user is told succeeded and that did not. Kept MAJOR: wrong data reaches workshop-scoped lists and exports, but nothing is destroyed and an admin can fix the roster from the workshop side. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

---

### [MAJOR] (backend) `/export/dataset` never emits an artisan that no workshop reaches, so their details.txt and their own photographs are fetched from the database and silently discarded — while the module's comment claims an `_Unlinked` area means 'nothing is dropped'

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED · found by the verifier, not the original audit

**Where.** backend/app/api/routes/export.py:39-45 (the '_Unlinked area so nothing is dropped' claim), :313-358 (the only place an artisan is emitted), :360-370 (the _Unlinked fallbacks), :192-205 (the media fetch), backend/app/api/routes/data_browser.py:867-904 (workshop_reaches_artisan), backend/app/schemas/records.py:75-80

**What it does.** Artisans are emitted in exactly one place: inside `for ws in workshops:` (export.py:313), narrowed to `ws_artisans = [a for a in artisans if workshop_reaches_artisan(ws, a)]` (:325), then written out at :350-352 (`add_text(abase, "details.txt", ...)` and `add_media(abase, "artisan", artisan.id)`). The `_Unlinked` fallback at :360-370 covers products, tools and interviews — and NOT artisans. `workshop_reaches_artisan` (data_browser.py:885-904) evaluates three OR terms: `Artisan.workshopId == ws.id`, membership in that workshop's `WorkshopArtisan` links, and `Artisan.craftId` in the workshop's declared crafts. An artisan matching none of the three is reached by no workshop and therefore never emitted at all — no folder, no details.txt, and none of their own media.

Both `craftId` and `workshopId` are optional on create (`ArtisanCreate`, backend/app/schemas/records.py:75-80: 'The workshop this artisan was documented at. Optional everywhere') and both columns are nullable (schema.prisma:539, :545), so such an artisan is ordinary rather than exotic. And their media is not merely absent — it is FETCHED: `media_or` includes `{"artisanId": {"in": ids}}` over ALL artisans (export.py:192-205), `_media_slot` keys it at `("artisan", aid)`, and `add_media` is never called with that id, so the rows are loaded from Postgres and thrown away, uncounted by `totalMedia` and unflagged by `truncated`.

**How it fails.** A researcher records an artisan without picking a workshop and without a craft — both fields the form permits to be blank — and attaches their portrait and three technique photographs. Every screen shows the artisan and their photographs (`GET /artisans`, `GET /media`, the data browser's `by-type` and `by-uploader` folders). The researcher then downloads the repository zip from `/export/dataset`. The artisan has no folder anywhere in the tree, no `details.txt`, and none of their four photographs. `totalMedia` counts only the emitted files, `truncated` is `false`, and the client presents the download as complete. The same silent drop takes any artisan whose craft is simply not on their workshop's declared craft list and who has no direct `workshopId` or `WorkshopArtisan` row.

**What it costs.** An archival export that the module's own header describes as filing everything, 'plus an _Unlinked area so nothing is dropped', drops whole artisan records and their field photography with no count, no flag and no log line. This is the same class of defect the file has already been burned by once — export.py:317-324 records that the export previously used route 1 of `workshop_reaches_artisan` alone and 'every artisan the browser lists under this workshop was silently absent from the zip'. That fix corrected which artisans a workshop reaches; it did not add a home for artisans no workshop reaches.

**Remedy.** Give artisans the `_Unlinked` treatment the other three record types already have. After the workshop loop, track `placed_artisans` the way `placed_products`/`placed_tools`/`placed_interviews` are tracked at :257-259, and emit any artisan not in that set under `_Unlinked/<artisan name>` with their `details.txt`, `add_media(abase, "artisan", artisan.id)`, and their products/tools/interviews nested beneath — reusing `emit_product`/`emit_tool`/`emit_interview` so an unlinked artisan's subtree is shaped identically to a linked one's, and so those records stop landing in the flat `_Unlinked/<artisanName>` bucket separately from the artisan they belong to. Pin it with a test that creates an artisan with `workshopId=None` and `craftId=None`, attaches one media row, and asserts the manifest contains both their `details.txt` and that file — a test that fails on today's code.

---

### [MAJOR] (backend) `GET /api/media/{id}` and `GET /api/media` serve `transcriptText` in full to every signed-in account, so the per-media-file gate on transcript text is decoration

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED · independently found by 2 agents

**Where.** backend/app/api/routes/media.py:966-970 (`get_media`) and :582-626 (`list_media`); backend/app/services/records.py:516-533 (`viewable_where`), :17-49 (`_SENSITIVE_KEYS`, `_IDENTITY_KEYS`, `_MEDIA_URL_KEYS`) and :88-133 (`_redact_sensitive`); MediaFile columns at backend/prisma/schema.prisma:795-796

**What it does.** Verified as claimed. `get_media` is `Depends(get_current_user)` and applies no row filter at all: `media = await db.mediafile.find_unique(where={"id": media_id}, include=INCLUDE)` then `_public(media, current_user)`. `list_media` composes `viewable_where(current_user, owner_field="uploadedById")`, which returns `{}` — its docstring at records.py:517 says "everything, for every signed-in account". `_redact_sensitive` drops only `passwordHash`, masks only `aadhaarNumber`/`pehchanCardNumber`, and drops only `url`/`publicUrl`/`objectKey` — `transcriptText` and `transcriptSummary` are in none of the three lists and both are real columns on MediaFile. The policy is explicit, not accidental: records.py:40-42 states "the ROW still travels for everybody: the filename, the type, the caption, THE TRANSCRIPT … Only the URL is withheld". That is directly opposite to workshop_transcripts.py:313-322, which calls the same read "a leak", and to the entitlement docs/PERMISSIONS.md:449 records as held.

**How it fails.** A CROWDSOURCE_VOLUNTEER (rank 10) calls `GET /api/media?mediaType=AUDIO&pageSize=100`; every page carries the verbatim transcript of every artisan interview in the repository — names, incomes, family circumstances — with no grant, no workshop and no role above the authentication floor. In the other direction, the co-designer refused those transcripts by the finding above reads them anyway by lifting the media ids out of the stage data he can already read and calling `GET /api/media/{id}` once per id.

**What it costs.** The narrowest control in this domain buys nothing: the effort spent gating transcript text on the design-workshop surface is undone by the media surface serving the same column unconditionally, and docs/PERMISSIONS.md:449 tells a reviewer a boundary exists that one request disproves. Either open reads are wrong for transcripts (a recorded testimony is not a filename) or the design-workshop gate is wrong; both rules are live and the permissive one wins on every path a client has.

**Remedy.** Settle it in one place. If a transcript is repository-open like a caption, delete the media predicate from `load_transcript_items` and the refusal warning from `attach_report_transcripts`, and correct docs/PERMISSIONS.md:449 — that also resolves the finding above. If a transcript is takeable data like a URL, add `transcriptText`/`transcriptSummary` to a per-node entitlement in `_redact_sensitive` beside `_MEDIA_URL_KEYS` (the node already carries `uploadedById`, which is exactly what the URL decision reads), so `GET /media`, `GET /media/{id}` and `GET /search` withhold them from callers who may not take that uploader's data. Do not leave both rules standing.

**What the verifier checked.** Read media.py:966-970 verbatim — no filter, confirmed. Read list_media at :582-626 and confirmed `vis = await viewable_where(...)` then `if vis: where["AND"] = [vis]`, with viewable_where returning `{}` at records.py:533, so nothing is composed. Read `_redact_sensitive` at records.py:88-133 line by line: the only removals are `_SENSITIVE_KEYS` (passwordHash), `_IDENTITY_KEYS` (masked) and `_MEDIA_URL_KEYS` (url/publicUrl/objectKey) — transcript columns are untouched. Confirmed `transcriptText`/`transcriptSummary` exist on MediaFile (schema.prisma:795-796) and are therefore encoded by `public_encode`. Looked for a route-level guard on `get_media` and for an alternate redactor — there is none; `_public` (media.py:120-152) only adds `displayFilename`. Read records.py:30-42 and confirmed the openness is a stated policy, which is what makes this a live contradiction rather than an oversight. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md. Kept at MAJOR: it is a real read-boundary hole reachable by the lowest role, but it is a deliberate documented policy on one of the two surfaces, so it is a policy collision to settle rather than an unnoticed auth bypass.

---

### [MAJOR] (backend) Media attached to a process or a process step is orphaned invisibly when its parent dies — `/media/orphans` cannot list it and `/media/{id}/relink` refuses to re-attach it

**Verdict:** PARTIAL

**Where.** backend/app/api/routes/media.py:628-640 (ORPHAN_FK_FIELDS), :743-762 (list_orphan_media), :726-740 (_relink_delegate), :765-795 (relink_media), backend/app/api/routes/processes.py:76-125 (_sync_steps) and :256-260 (delete_process), backend/prisma/schema.prisma:1210 and :1232, backend/app/api/routes/export.py:98-109 and :243-247

**What it does.** Every quoted line is exact and the mechanism holds. `ORPHAN_FK_FIELDS` has seven entries, all FK-backed, and every condition in `list_orphan_media` is `{linkedRecordType: t, linkedRecordId: {not: None}, fk: None}` — a tag-only row has no `fk` to be null, so no condition can ever match it. `_relink_delegate` has the same seven plus media/misc, so `POST /{id}/relink` answers 400 'Unsupported record type for re-linking' for a process or step id. The clients really do send these tags: `frontend/components/forms/ProcessForm.tsx:520,536,589,619` write `linkedRecordType: "process"` and `"processstep"`, and `processes.py:57-59` confirms 'Media is linked purely through linkedRecordType/linkedRecordId … so no MediaFile foreign keys are needed'. schema.prisma:1210 cascades Process from ProductDocumentation and :1232 cascades ProcessStep from Process.

TWO CORRECTIONS, ONE OF WHICH MAKES IT WORSE THAN REPORTED. (a) The far more ordinary trigger is not a product delete: `_sync_steps` (processes.py:76-125) hard-deletes every ProcessStep the form did not re-send — `db.processstep.delete_many(where={"id": {"in": removed}})` — so simply editing a process to drop a step orphans that step's photographs, with no delete of anything else. (b) The export drop is sharper than stated: the row IS fetched (export.py:196 puts `{"workshopId": {"in": ids}}` in `media_or`, and the media keeps the `workshopId` it inherited at upload, since MediaFile.workshopId is only SET NULL when the WORKSHOP is deleted), `_media_slot` then returns `("process", dead_id)` and `add_media` is never called with that key — so it is loaded from the database and silently discarded. The finder's parenthetical 'a key media_by is never read at' is wrong in general: export.py:269 and :272 DO read `("process", proc.id)` and `("processstep", step.id)` for live processes.

ALSO OVERSTATED: 'appear nowhere'. The rows remain in `GET /api/media`, in `/search`, and in the data browser's `by-type` taxonomy (data_browser.py:56-63, 'every file in the repository grouped purely by what it is'), and they keep their inherited `workshopId` so they still count under that workshop's `by-uploader` folders.

**How it fails.** A researcher opens a process form, deletes one step that was entered twice, and saves. `_sync_steps` hard-deletes that ProcessStep. Its photographs survive in MediaFile and in S3 with `linkedRecordType="processstep"` and a `linkedRecordId` pointing at nothing. They disappear from the process detail screen (processes.py `_hydrate` looks them up by the live step ids), they are absent from `/media/orphans` because no `fk: None` condition can match a tag-only row, `/export/dataset` fetches them and drops them without counting, and an admin who finds their ids by hand cannot re-attach them: `POST /media/{id}/relink` with `linkedRecordType: "processstep"` answers 400. The identical failure follows a Process delete (processes.py:260) or a cascade from a ProductDocumentation delete, and the same shape applies to the `designWorkshop` tag the browser writes at frontend/lib/designWorkshopStore.ts:2814.

**What it costs.** Ordinary editing of a process silently removes its step photography from every screen a field worker uses and from the repository zip, with no recovery path through the UI — while the recovery feature built for exactly this failure reports the repository as clean. The bytes keep accruing storage cost. The files are not gone from the database and are still reachable through the flat `by-type` browser and `GET /media`, which is why this is MAJOR rather than a blocker.

**Remedy.** Give the orphan query the tag-only question. For `process`, `processstep`, `designWorkshop` and `media`/`misc` a `fk: None` predicate cannot express it, so resolve them the way `_tagged_parent` (media.py:643-682) already does: read the distinct `(linkedRecordType, linkedRecordId)` pairs for those tags, one query per delegate, and emit the media whose parent id is not in the live set. Add `process` -> `db.process` and `processstep` -> `db.processstep` to `_relink_delegate` — they contribute nothing through `media_relation_data`, so only the tag pair is rewritten, which is exactly how they were attached in the first place, and `inherit_parent_workshop(..., replace=True)` already handles the step -> process hop via `_tagged_parent`. Separately, and cheaper: have `_sync_steps` refuse to delete a step that still has media tagged to it, or re-tag that media onto the parent process before deleting, so the ordinary edit path stops creating orphans at all.

**What the verifier checked.** Read media.py:628-795 in full and processes.py:1-135 and :250-262. Verified `ORPHAN_FK_FIELDS` and `_relink_delegate` contents character by character. Tried to kill it four ways. (1) Is there a cleanup of tag-only media on process/step delete? Grepped `linkedRecordType` across processes.py and products.py — the only hit is a docstring; nothing deletes or re-tags. (2) Is `process` really a tag clients send? Confirmed in ProcessForm.tsx at four sites. (3) Does the export really drop it? Traced `media_or` (export.py:192-219) — the row is fetched via workshopId, then `_media_slot` (:98-109) keys it at `("process", dead_id)` and `add_media` is only ever called with live ids, so it is fetched and discarded. This also killed the finder's claim that the slot key is 'never read at'. (4) Is it really invisible everywhere? No — `by-type` (data_browser.py:56-63), `GET /media` and `/search` all still list it, which is the strongest correction and why I did not raise the severity further. Checked docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md — not recorded. Marked PARTIAL: mechanism confirmed, but the most reachable trigger (`_sync_steps`) was missed and the 'appear nowhere' claim is false.

---

### [MAJOR] (backend) Any designer holding a questionnaire id can add sittings and answers to another team's questionnaire, and they print in that workshop's report — the write path has no membership test the read path spent a whole helper on

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED · found by the verifier, not the original audit

**Where.** backend/app/api/routes/questionnaire_forms.py:821-848 (`create_entry`) and :879-908 (`record_answers`), both gated only by `_require_questionnaire` at :106-108; contrast the read gates at :504-510 and :255-263

**What it does.** `_require_questionnaire` is `_require_designer(user)` plus `require_record(db.questionnaire, id)` — role and existence, nothing else. `create_entry` uses only that (its docstring says "Not owner-gated") and `record_answers` deliberately passes `rewriting=False` to `_entry_in`, so neither asks whether the caller owns the form, is an admin, or works on the workshop the form is attached to. The READ side of the same module asks all three: `read_questionnaire` (:504-510) and `download_questionnaire` (:255-263) compute `record.ownerId == user.id or is_admin(user) or await _works_on_this_questionnaires_workshop(record, user)`, and the read docstring at :495-501 names the exact vectors by which a stranger obtains the id — "a shared link, a browser history entry, a report annexure". The write path is open to every one of those vectors. `report_items` (services/questionnaire_forms.py:304-308) then loads `questionnaireformentry.find_many(where={"questionnaireId": {"in": ids}})` with no filter, so every injected sitting prints.

**How it fails.** Designer B was a co-designer on workshop W last season and still has the questionnaire id in their browser history (or obtained it from a report annexure, or from the attach-anywhere hole in finding 1). Their `DesignWorkshopViewer` row has since been removed, so `GET /questionnaires/{id}` now 403s them at :504-510. `POST /api/questionnaires/{id}/entries` with `{"respondentName":"…"}` still returns 201, and `PUT /api/questionnaires/{id}/entries/{entry}/answers` still writes every answer — `save_answers`' authorship rule only protects answers that ALREADY have text. On W's next report, `attach_report_questionnaires` prints the fabricated sitting, respondent name and all, in the annexure.

**What it costs.** A revoked co-designer, or any designer who has ever seen the id, can write named respondents and answers into another team's research instrument, and those answers reach a document submitted to a ministry under someone else's name. Revoking a viewer grant closes the read but not the write, which is the wrong way round. This survives the remedy proposed for finding 1: gating the ATTACHMENT does nothing about writes to a form that is already correctly attached.

**Remedy.** Gate the two write routes with the same predicate the reads already use. In `create_entry` and `record_answers`, after `_require_questionnaire`, require `record.ownerId == current_user.id or is_admin(current_user) or await _works_on_this_questionnaires_workshop(record, current_user)` — the identical expression at :505-510, which should be lifted into one `_may_use_questionnaire(record, user)` helper so the read and write halves cannot drift. That keeps the documented intent ("a form only its author may answer is a form nobody uses") for the workshop's own team while closing it to designers with no relationship to the workshop, and it makes revoking a viewer row take effect on writes as it already does on reads.

---

### [MAJOR] (backend) GET /tasks/options caps the assignee, workshop and artisan pickers with no search parameter and no truncation flag, and the assignee cap is applied before the rank filter

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** backend/app/api/routes/tasks.py:982-1007 (route signature at 982-986; users take at 990; rank filter at 991-996; workshops take at 998; artisans take at 1007), consumed by frontend/app/(protected)/settings/tasks/page.tsx:72 and frontend/components/tasks/AssignmentBuilder.tsx:87-98,312-329

**What it does.** The route signature is `async def task_options(current_user = Depends(require_admin), workshopId: str | None = Query(None))` — there is no `search` parameter of any kind. Its three reads are `db.user.find_many(order={"name": "asc"}, take=500)`, `db.workshop.find_many(order={"date": "desc"}, take=200)` and `db.artisan.find_many(where=artisan_where, order={"name": "asc"}, take=500)`, and the returned payload (lines 1012-1031) carries `assignees`, `workshops`, `artisans`, `sections` and `recordTypes` — no truncation flag on any of them. The assignee list is then thinned in PYTHON after the read (`for user in users if user.id != current_user.id and (is_master_admin(...) or role_rank(user) < role_rank(current_user))`), so the 500-row window is drawn from every account in the table and only then reduced to the assignable ones. Neither `name` order has a tiebreaker, so which rows fall inside the cut among equal names is arbitrary — the repository has already been bitten by exactly that (`docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md`, closed 2026-08-13: 204 accounts share the name "Sync Test", and that name is where the viewer picker's cut landed). The client cannot compensate: `AssignmentBuilder` builds the artisan multi-select purely from `options.artisans` (line 323) and the assignee picker purely from `options.assignees`, filtering in the browser over whatever arrived.

**How it fails.** An admin opens Assign work to give a documentation batch to a designer whose name sorts late in the alphabet. `GET /tasks/options` returns the first 500 accounts by name, thinned to those ranking below the admin; the designer is not among them and there is no search box that can reach past the cut, because the parameter does not exist. The picker shows the same nothing it would show for a colleague who had never been given an account. Separately, with the workshop filter left empty, only the first 500 artisans by name are offered, so a late-sorting artisan cannot be named in an artisan subset at all; and only the 200 most recent workshops can be chosen as the batch's workshop, so a batch cannot be attached to an older workshop.

**What it costs.** On the numbers this repository measured on 2026-08-13 and recorded in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md (3632 accounts, 731 artisans), both 500-row cuts are live today. The assignee cut is the serious half: a colleague who cannot be found in the picker is indistinguishable from a colleague who does not exist, and because the rank filter runs after the take, the assignable list is shorter than 500 by however many higher-ranked accounts happen to sort early. This is the same class the eligible-viewers pass closed on 2026-08-13 ("hidden from you" vs "nobody matched"), reopened in a different endpoint. The finder's own failure scenario overstates one part: leaving the artisan subset empty means "every artisan in scope" (AssignmentBuilder.tsx:316), so an unnamed artisan is still covered by the task and `_count_records` is not distorted — the real loss is that a specific late-sorting artisan cannot be named, and a late-sorting colleague cannot be assigned at all.

**Remedy.** Give the route what `eligible_viewers` was given: `search: str | None = Query(None, max_length=120)`, folded into the same `where` as the take (name OR email for users, name OR place for artisans, title OR place for workshops) — searching after the take searches only the first 500 names of the alphabet. Move the rank filter into the user query (`role: {"in": [roles ranking below the caller]}`, unfiltered for a master admin) so the cap applies to rows that are already assignable, and exclude `current_user.id` there too. Make both name orders total (`[{"name": "asc"}, {"id": "asc"}]`). Read `take=CAP + 1`, trim, and return `assigneesTruncated` / `artisansTruncated` / `workshopsTruncated` so the dialog can say the list was cut — both clients already decode a `truncated` flag on the viewer picker.

**What the verifier checked.** Read tasks.py:982-1031 verbatim: the quoted lines exist exactly as cited and the route really has only two parameters. I looked specifically for a caller-side rescue and found the opposite — frontend/app/(protected)/settings/tasks/page.tsx:72 re-fetches `/tasks/options?workshopId=…` on every workshop change and passes the result straight into AssignmentBuilder, whose `allArtisans = options?.artisans ?? []` (line 87) and artisan `options={allArtisans.map(...)}` (line 323) are the only source the picker has; there is no second endpoint it can search. I checked whether the rank filter could make the cut harmless — it cannot, it runs in Python at 991-996 over the already-truncated `users`. I checked OPEN_FINDINGS.md: the eligible-viewers truncation is CLOSED (2026-08-13) and is a different service/route, so this is not a duplicate of an open item. The population numbers are second-hand — I did not query the database — but they are this repository's own measurements recorded in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md (3632 accounts; 731 artisans), which is the same evidence the finder relied on. Severity kept at MAJOR: an admin cannot assign work to a large fraction of the roster and gets no signal that anything was hidden.

---

### [MAJOR] (backend) A re-pointed reference leaves the previous record's values standing in every field the new record leaves blank, so the row names artisan B under artisan A's phone, village and photograph

**Verdict:** PARTIAL

**Where.** backend/app/services/design_workshops.py:721-724 (the `if value in (None, ""): continue` guard, which runs before the `replaced` test at :728-730), against the contract in the function's own docstring at :666-669; the mapping at services/stage_schema.py:570-579; the source lambda at design_workshops.py:314-331

**What it does.** Quoted code exists as quoted. `replaced` is computed once per REF field (`was = str(item.previous.get(spec.key) or ""); replaced = bool(was) and was != str(ref_id)`, :719-720) and the docstring promises that a row now naming a different record has EVERY mapped field rewritten. It does not: `if value in (None, ""): continue` is evaluated first and unconditionally, so the rewrite is applied only field-by-field where the NEW record has something to say. `REFERENCE_MODELS["Artisan"].data` returns raw columns and metadata lookups (`phone`, `localName`, `gender`, `village`, `experienceYears`, `photo`), all of which are `None` on a thinly documented artisan, and `_reference_photos` returns nothing for an artisan with no MediaFile. Verified `participant.photo` is `IMG` = `FieldType.IMAGE` (stage_definitions.py:340), not IMAGE_LIST, so the `target.type.is_multi` arm at :729 does not protect it either. The finder's claim that the designer gets NO signal is wrong, and that is the part I could kill: the web applies the identical rule client-side (`hydrateFromReference`, frontend/lib/designWorkshops.ts:1113-1125 — same `raw === ""` early continue), so the stale values stay in the boxes on screen and the server merely stores what the form is already showing. There is no client/server divergence here, and the row is visibly wrong at the keyboard.

**How it fails.** Reachable on the web, today. A designer picks artisan A on a stage-3 participant row; name, localName, gender, specialisation, experienceYears, village, phone and photo hydrate. She notices the phone number belongs to a different Sita Devi, re-opens the picker and chooses artisan B, whose record holds a name and a craft and nothing else. Client-side, `hydrateFromReference` rewrites `name` and `specialisation` and writes nothing for the rest; server-side `hydrate_entries` runs with `replaced = True` and takes the same decision, so the stored row is `artisanRef = B` with B's name beside A's phone, A's village, A's gender and A's photograph. It is also reachable on Android across sessions: `lastHydration` in DwReferenceField.kt:231 is `remember`-scoped, so on a re-pick made after the screen was reopened it is empty, `mayWrite` (DwReferenceField.kt:761) is false for every filled box, and the phone rewrites nothing at all while the server rewrites `name` — the two surfaces then disagree about the row.

**What it costs.** A participant table in the .docx submitted to the ministry can print one artisan's name over another artisan's photograph, village and telephone number, with `artisanRef` pointing at the first and the denormalised copy half the second's. The copy IS the historical record by design (stage_schema.py:535-550), so nothing can re-resolve it later. It is MAJOR rather than CRITICAL because both clients render exactly what will be stored at the moment of the re-pick — the wrong photograph is on screen — so this is a stale value a designer can see and clear, not a silent divergence behind their back.

**Remedy.** The finder's remedy is right and is already implemented on the handset, which is the strongest evidence it is the intended rule: `hydrationPatch` (android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/DwReferenceField.kt:766-773) clears what the previous record wrote and this one has nothing to say about, with the comment "Leaving it would attach the old artisan's phone number to the new artisan's name". Mirror it server-side: inside the `for spec in item.entity.fields` loop, before the mapping loop, `if replaced: for target_key in mapping.values(): t = item.entity.field(target_key); if t is not None and not t.type.is_multi: item.data.pop(target_key, None)`. The multi arm stays untouched (a gallery is never replaced). The same three lines are owed to `hydrateFromReference` in frontend/lib/designWorkshops.ts, or the browser will keep sending back what the server just cleared on the next merge save. Pin with a test that hydrates from a fully populated artisan, re-points at a name-only artisan and asserts `phone`, `village` and `photo` are ABSENT.

**What the verifier checked.** Read hydrate_entries in full (:659-742) and the mapping it uses (stage_schema.py:565-643). Checked for an upstream guard: none — `previous` is the stored row's data, `was`/`replaced` are computed exactly as quoted. Checked the target field's type by running the registry (`participant.photo` is IMAGE). Checked whether the client clears the stale keys so the server never sees them: the WEB does not (same early continue), the ANDROID client does (`hydrationPatch` emits nulls) but only within one composition and only where the value still equals what it wrote — and a cleared key is dropped by `validate_entry` (stage_schema.py:1131-1148) and then restored by the `merge` branch (design_workshops.py:1076) on an unread stage, so the clearing is not universal either. Checked the tests: frontend/e2e/reference-hydration-unit.spec.ts:169-179 pins the replace case only with a fully populated second record, and :245-250 pins the blank-source case only on an EMPTY row — neither covers replace-with-a-blank, so the behaviour is not deliberately pinned. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md. Severity downgraded from CRITICAL to MAJOR because the finder's supporting claim about invisibility is false.

---

### [MAJOR] (backend) A transcription job that dies before the provider answers leaves the clip's `transcriptStatus` at QUEUED, and QUEUED is on the settled list, so no later stage save can re-queue it

**Verdict:** PARTIAL

**Where.** backend/app/services/media_queue.py:219-220 (enqueue writes QUEUED), :332-334 (batch catch-all), :505-557 (_process_job raise sites), :730-751 (_handle_job_failure), backend/app/services/workshop_transcripts.py:60 and :187-193

**What it does.** Confirmed as described. `MediaFile.transcriptStatus` is written back only by `_apply_transcription_result` (media_queue.py:570-588), reached only after the provider answered. Any exception earlier in `_process_job` — the `dictation_consent.transcription_verdict` read (:530), `get_object_bytes` (:534), `transcribe_audio_bytes` (:536), `load_app_settings`/`transcription_mode` (:546) or `refine_transcript_text` (:548) — propagates to the batch's `except Exception` (:332-334) into `_handle_job_failure`, which updates `mediaprocessingjob` and nothing else (:741-751). At `job.attempts >= job.maxAttempts` (default 3, config.py:226) the job is FAILED and the clip still reads `transcriptStatus="QUEUED"`, `transcriptError=None`. `enqueue_stage_transcriptions` then skips it: `_SETTLED_TRANSCRIPT_STATUSES` at workshop_transcripts.py:60 contains QUEUED, and the filter at :187-193 excludes settled rows. `/media/complete` cannot rescue it either — `_finish_pending_media` (media.py:399-404) re-enqueues only `if processing_requests and not (existing.processingJobs or [])`, and a FAILED job is still a job. The `refine_transcript_text` case is the sharpest: the provider has already been paid and the raw text is sitting in `result` at :548, discarded unwritten.

WHERE THE FINDER IS WRONG: 'Nothing can rescue it … `retry_media_processing_job` resets the job but never the clip' is false. Resetting the JOB is sufficient — media.py:927-945 puts it back to QUEUED with `runAfter=now`, the worker re-runs `_process_job`, and a successful (or provider-answered-failed) run writes the clip's status through `_apply_transcription_result`. So there are two escapes, not one: the admin 'Transcribe now' button AND `POST /media/jobs/{id}/retry`. Both are admin-only and neither is offered to the designer.

**How it fails.** A designer syncs an eleven-minute interview at the end of a field day. Overnight S3 is briefly unreachable, or the object's multipart upload was aborted and the key does not resolve; `get_object_bytes` (media_queue.py:534) raises on all three attempts over the retry ladder. The job ends FAILED with a botocore error in `MediaProcessingJob.error`. The clip reads `transcriptStatus="QUEUED"` for ever. Saving the stage again does nothing (`enqueue_stage_transcriptions` treats QUEUED as settled). `report_annexures.annexure_warnings` (the function actually named `missing_transcript_note` in two comments does not exist — report_annexures.py:519-536) reports it as "N recording(s) have no transcript yet … (1 still being transcribed)", so the report warning actively tells the designer the transcript is on its way. The only recovery is an admin finding the job in the queue panel and pressing Retry.

**What it costs.** A recording silently and permanently loses its transcript, and every designer-facing surface says it is still coming. The report's transcript annexure is short by that interview under a warning that names the wrong reason. Recovery exists but only through the admin queue panel — which, per the separate finding below, currently 422s on every load on both clients.

**Remedy.** Write the clip's terminal state where the job's terminal state is written. In `_handle_job_failure` (media_queue.py:730-751), after the job update, when `exhausted and job.jobType == TRANSCRIPTION`, set `MediaFile.transcriptStatus = FAILED` and `transcriptError = error` — but only when `transcriptText` is empty, mirroring `_record_transcription_refused:262-263` so a clip transcribed months ago is never relabelled and `_transcript_write`'s hard-won rule (:440-449) is not violated. FAILED is deliberately absent from `_SETTLED_TRANSCRIPT_STATUSES` (workshop_transcripts.py:57-59 says so out loud), so the very next stage save picks the clip up — the same behaviour the consent-refusal path already relies on. Separately, wrap the `refine_transcript_text` call at :548 in a `try` that falls back to the raw `result`, so a paid-for transcript is never thrown away by an optional second hop.

**What the verifier checked.** Read media_queue.py:190-225, :286-353, :440-502, :505-588 and :673-751 in full, plus workshop_transcripts.py end to end. Tried four refutations. (1) Does the provider-failure path already set the clip FAILED? Yes — `_apply_transcription_result` calls `_transcript_write(result, status)` BEFORE raising at :588, so a provider that answered FAILED does mark the clip and the clip IS re-queued next save; only pre-answer exceptions strand it. That narrows the finding but does not kill it. (2) Does `/media/complete` retry re-enqueue? No — `_finish_pending_media` requires an empty `processingJobs` list. (3) Does anything else write `transcriptStatus`? Grepped: only `_apply_transcription_result`, `_transcript_write`, `_record_transcription_refused`, the enqueue at :220, and `POST /media/{id}/transcript`. (4) Is the annexure really silent? Read report_annexures.py:519-536 — it warns, but classifies QUEUED as 'still being transcribed', so it misinforms rather than stays silent. Checked docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md — not recorded. Marked PARTIAL because the 'nothing can rescue it' claim is refuted and two comment references (`missing_transcript_note`) name a function that does not exist. MAJOR kept: it needs a transient outage but leaves no self-healing path a designer can reach.

---

### [MAJOR] (backend) GET /search and GET /map/points answer "which artisans belong to this workshop" with a column-only clause, while /artisans, /datasets and /questionnaire use the three-way shared helper declared twenty lines above it

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** backend/app/services/record_filters.py:331-349 (the workshop loop in `build_record_wheres`) applying `workshop_clause` (:84-112, whose only artisan branch is `{"workshopId": {"in": ids}}` at :103-104) to the `artisans` bucket, against `artisan_workshop_clause` (:115-148) and its three callers: backend/app/api/routes/artisans.py:247, datasets.py:299, questionnaire.py:793. `build_record_wheres` is called from search.py:66 and map_points.py:782 and :969.

**What it does.** `build_record_wheres` is the module's single source of the shared filter vocabulary — its own comment at record_filters.py:320-328 says the map, the search box, the completion matrix and the consolidated questionnaire all read it from there "so they cannot disagree about what 'this workshop' contains". The loop then iterates all five buckets and calls the generic `workshop_clause`, which for a non-workshop table tests only the `workshopId` column. `artisan_workshop_clause`, declared in the same file with a docstring in capitals saying an artisan belongs to a workshop THREE ways (column, `WorkshopArtisan` roster, having sat in an interview taken there) and that "two screens disagreeing about who was at a workshop is two screens disagreeing about what the workshop's data IS", is imported by artisans.py, datasets.py and questionnaire.py and by nothing inside `build_record_wheres`. So `?workshopIds=X` narrows the artisans bucket by one reading on /search and /map/points and by three readings on /artisans, /datasets and /questionnaire.

**How it fails.** An artisan reaches a workshop only through the roster (their record predates the `Artisan.workshopId` column, or the roster was filled in from the workshop form) or only through an interview taken at that workshop. A designer opens Search, ticks that workshop in the scope control and searches the artisan's name: the artisans bucket returns 0 rows and `totals.artisans` is 0. The same tick on the artisan list page returns the artisan, because artisans.py:247 uses the three-way clause. The map draws no artisan pins for that workshop for the same reason. Nothing on either screen says which is right.

**What it costs.** The search box and the map under-report a workshop's artisans against the artisan list, the ZIP export and the questionnaire completion matrix. The scope reads as "this workshop has no artisans" over a workshop whose roster is sitting in the database — the exact failure `record_filters`'s own docstrings are written to prevent, occurring inside that module.

**Remedy.** Fork on the bucket inside the loop, the way datasets.py:296-301 already does: `clause = artisan_workshop_clause(ids, include_unassigned) if bucket == "artisans" else workshop_clause(ids, include_unassigned, is_workshop_table=(bucket == "workshops"))`. `artisan_workshop_clause` is always non-empty, so the `else` impossible-predicate branch never applies to it. Note the crafts bucket does not exist in search/map, so `craft_workshop_clause` needs no equivalent here.

**What the verifier checked.** Checked whether some caller re-applies the three-way clause after `build_record_wheres` returns: search.py:77-81 takes `wheres["artisans"]` verbatim into `db.artisan.count`/`find_many` (lines 103-107) with no further clause; map_points.py calls it at two sites and I found no post-hoc artisan clause. Checked whether `workshop_clause` might special-case artisans — it does not; its only fork is `is_workshop_table`. Checked that the three named callers really do use the helper (grep for `artisan_workshop_clause` returns exactly artisans.py:247, datasets.py:299, questionnaire.py:793 plus the definition). The finder's incidental note about `data_browser.workshop_artisan_reach` being a fourth spelling I did not verify and have dropped from the write-up rather than pass through unchecked. Kept MAJOR rather than higher: it under-reports on two read surfaces, destroys nothing, and its bite depends on the repository actually holding roster-only or interview-only artisans — which `artisan_workshop_clause`'s docstring says is why the helper exists. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

---

### [MAJOR] (backend) The workshop scope resolves "which artisans" one way on /search and /map/points and a different way on /artisans, /datasets and the questionnaire index — from the same module that owns both readings

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** backend/app/services/record_filters.py:335-349 (the scope loop; the plain clause is applied to all five buckets) against backend/app/services/record_filters.py:115-148 (`artisan_workshop_clause`); reached from backend/app/api/routes/search.py:66 and backend/app/api/routes/map_points.py:782 and :969. The three-armed reading is used at backend/app/api/routes/artisans.py:247, backend/app/api/routes/datasets.py:299 and backend/app/api/routes/questionnaire.py:793

**What it does.** The loop at 338-343 calls `workshop_clause(ids, include_unassigned, is_workshop_table=(bucket == "workshops"))` for every bucket, so the artisans bucket gets the bare column predicate `{"workshopId": {"in": ids}}` (line 105). `artisan_workshop_clause`, defined 200 lines above in the same file, returns a three-armed OR — the column, `{"workshops": {"some": {"workshopId": {"in": ids}}}}` (the WorkshopArtisan roster) and `{"questionnaireInterviews": {"some": {"interview": {"is": {"workshopId": {"in": ids}}}}}}` (having sat in an interview taken there) — and its docstring states all three count "which is why this is a shared helper rather than an inline clause on whichever screen needed it first. Two screens disagreeing about who was at a workshop is two screens disagreeing about what the workshop's data IS." So search and the map miss any artisan linked only by roster row or only by interview, while Browse artisans, the dataset export and the consolidated questionnaire index all count them.

**How it fails.** A researcher ticks one workshop in the shared workshop scope and asks for artisans. On /search (and on the map's artisan layer) only artisans carrying `Artisan.workshopId` are returned; an artisan whose only link is a `WorkshopArtisan` row — which `replace_workshop_artisans` (backend/app/api/routes/workshops.py:145-158) writes for every artisan picked in the workshop form's "linked artisans" multi-select, WITHOUT ever setting `Artisan.workshopId` — or whose only link is having sat in an interview taken at that workshop is absent. The same researcher opens Browse artisans with the same workshop ticked and sees them. Two screens, one scope control, two answers to "who was at this workshop".

**What it costs.** An empty or short artisan result under a workshop scope reads as "this workshop documented no artisans", which is the exact failure the module docstring at 324-334 claims this shared vocabulary prevents ("The map, the search box, the completion matrix and the consolidated questionnaire all read it from here, so they cannot disagree about what 'this workshop' contains") — and that claim is false today for the artisans bucket, because the questionnaire index does not read it from here at all (it calls `artisan_workshop_clause` directly at questionnaire.py:793). No data is destroyed; the harm is a wrong, under-counted answer on two screens with no signal that anything was excluded.

**Remedy.** Branch on the bucket inside the loop at 338: `artisan_workshop_clause(ids, include_unassigned)` for `"artisans"`, `workshop_clause(..., is_workshop_table=True)` for `"workshops"`, the plain clause for the rest. The artisan helper never returns None (it returns `{"id": {"in": []}}` when nothing can match), so the `else` arm at 344-349 is unaffected. Two existing tests pin the current shape and must be updated in the same change, not deleted: `backend/tests/test_workshop_scope.py:99` asserts `wheres[bucket]["AND"] == [{"workshopId": {"in": ["w1"]}}]` for the artisans bucket, and `:109` asserts the unassigned form; the replacement assertion is the three-armed OR.

**What the verifier checked.** Read record_filters.py:84-148 and 316-351 in full — the quoted loop is exact and no bucket-name branch exists anywhere in it. I hunted for a rescue at the call sites: search.py:66 and map_points.py:782/:969 pass the wheres straight through with no artisan-specific re-filtering (grep for `artisan_workshop_clause` returns only artisans.py:247, datasets.py:299, questionnaire.py:793 — none of them the search or map routes). I checked whether the divergence could be unreachable because every artisan carries the column: it cannot — POST/PATCH /artisans mirrors the column into the join (`link_workshop_artisan`, artisans.py:300 and :374), which is one direction only, while `replace_workshop_artisans` (workshops.py:145-158) writes join rows with no column write at all, so the roster-only shape is produced by a live, current UI path and not merely by legacy rows. I deliberately checked the finder's cited evidence and it is partly wrong: they quoted `craft_workshop_clause`'s "every craft on the live database had a NULL column" as proof for artisans, and backend/app/api/routes/data_browser.py:851-854 records something different for artisans on the live repository (`WorkshopArtisan` empty for the workshop examined, artisans reached through the craft). That weakens their specific data claim but not the defect: the workshop form's roster writer and the interview arm both produce link-only artisans, and the two readings provably differ. Severity kept MAJOR — a scope control giving two different answers on two screens is behaviour a user hits, and the direction (silent under-count) is the dangerous one. Not present in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

### [MAJOR] (backend) A below-Professor `canDownloadDataset` holder is handed the object URL and `objectKey` of every file in the repository by `GET /media` and `/search`, while `/data` and `/export/dataset` refuse those same files to the same account

**Verdict:** PARTIAL

**Where.** backend/app/services/records.py:180-186 (public_encode default) and :195-224 (media_url_owners), backend/app/core/deps.py:187-189 (can_download_dataset), backend/app/api/routes/data_browser.py:341-382 (_scope_for), backend/app/api/routes/export.py:143-148 (media_vis), backend/app/api/routes/media.py:142-144, backend/app/api/routes/search.py:171

**What it does.** Every quoted line is exact. `can_download_dataset` (deps.py:187-189) is `has_rank(user, "PROFESSOR") or bool(get_value(user, "canDownloadDataset"))` — a per-user grantable boolean independent of the ladder. Both `public_encode`'s default (records.py:181) and `media_url_owners` (records.py:216) return `ALL_MEDIA_URLS` (= `None`) for such a holder, and `None` makes the per-node test at records.py:121-124 unreachable, so `url`, `publicUrl` AND `objectKey` survive on every media node. `_public` passes `await media_url_owners(viewer)` (media.py:143), so `GET /media` takes that path; so does `/search` (search.py:171) and `questionnaire_consolidation` (:181).

The same repository decides the opposite for the same account on the download surfaces, and says why: data_browser.py:344-349 — "``canDownloadDataset`` is a GRANTABLE boolean, so a researcher can hold it without ranking Professor+ … The permission means 'download the data you can SEE'" — and `_scope_for` (:378-382) plus `dataset_manifest`'s `media_vis` (export.py:148) both use `owned_or_granted_where(..., owner_field="uploadedById")`, which for a below-Professor caller is a real OR over own uploads plus granted owners (records.py:556-573). `media_url_owners`' own docstring premise — "both may already download the whole repository" — is therefore false for exactly the account it is widening.

**How it fails.** An admin grants `canDownloadDataset` to a RESEARCHER. That researcher calls `GET /api/media?pageSize=100` (or `/api/search`) and receives `url` and `objectKey` for every media row in the repository, including uploads by accounts that have granted them nothing. On the same session `/api/data/media/{that_id}/download` answers 404 (data_browser.py:2949-2957, `find_first` under `scope.media`) and `/api/export/dataset` omits the row from the manifest (export.py:222-225, `media_where` AND-ed with `media_vis`). Whether the URL actually yields bytes depends on the deployment's bucket/CDN being anonymously readable — which records.py:28-49 asserts as the premise of the whole `_MEDIA_URL_KEYS` mechanism ("it is a fetchable object URL, stored on the row"), and `objectKey` is in that list precisely because `public_url_for_key` is deterministic (s3.py:160-172).

**What it costs.** One account-level boolean bypasses the per-uploader grant system for the URL of every file in the repository, on two of the widest read endpoints, while the two download endpoints written for that exact concern refuse the same account. The URLs handed out carry no expiry and no auth, so they survive revocation of the permission, of the grant, and of the account.

**Remedy.** Delete `can_download_dataset(viewer)` from both branches (records.py:181 and records.py:216) so the media-URL entitlement is rank-plus-grants only — the identical predicate `_scope_for` and `dataset_manifest` already use — and correct the `media_url_owners` docstring premise at :198-199, which is the sentence that made this look safe. `media_url_owners` then returns `{viewer_id, *granted_owner_ids}` for such a caller. I verified the remedy breaks nothing: `datasets.py:356` passes `media_urls=ALL_MEDIA_URLS` explicitly behind `require_dataset_admin`, and `list_orphan_media` (media.py:744) binds an admin, so both keep full URLs by their own gate rather than by this default. Pin it with a test asserting that a below-Professor `canDownloadDataset` holder gets `url` on exactly the media ids `/export/dataset` puts in its manifest.

**What the verifier checked.** Read records.py:136-224 and deps.py:187-189 line by line; the quoted code is exact. Tried to kill it by looking for a narrower gate upstream of `_public` on `GET /media` — there is none beyond `get_current_user`. Tried to kill it by checking whether `can_download_dataset` can actually be true below Professor: it reads a `canDownloadDataset` column on User, so yes. Tried to kill it by checking whether the download siblings really disagree — read `_scope_for` (data_browser.py:378-382), `download_media`'s scoped `find_first` (:2949-2957) and `dataset_manifest`'s `media_vis` (export.py:143-148, 222-225); they do. Checked `docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md` — not recorded. Severity lowered from CRITICAL to MAJOR because it requires a deliberate admin grant of a permission whose OWN docstring in deps.py:186-188 says 'May download the entire dataset' — i.e. deps.py and data_browser.py disagree about what the boolean means, so which side is the bug is a decision an owner has to make, not an unambiguous hole; and because no unprivileged account can reach it.

### [MAJOR] (backend) The transcript preview tells a granted co-designer the workshop has zero recordings, because a DesignWorkshopViewer grant does not satisfy `owned_or_granted_where`

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** backend/app/services/workshop_transcripts.py:325-336; backend/app/services/records.py:536-573; surfaced at backend/app/api/routes/design_workshops.py:1307-1336 (`list_workshop_transcripts`)

**What it does.** `load_transcript_items` AND-composes `owned_or_granted_where(viewer, owner_field="uploadedById")` under the media-id list. That predicate (records.py:558-573) short-circuits to `{}` only at PROFESSOR and above; DESIGNER is rank 35 (deps.py:43) and PROFESSOR is 40, so a designer falls to the two-clause OR: their own uploads, or an uploader who has granted them a `DataAccessGrant`. A `DesignWorkshopViewer` row — the mechanism by which two designers run one workshop — is neither. `list_workshop_transcripts` (routes:1323-1336) then reports `"total": len(payloads)` and `"withTranscript"` off the filtered list, under a docstring promising "Every recording this workshop collected", and computes no withheld count. The report path does compute one: `attach_report_transcripts` (services/design_workshops.py:1807-1814) does `refused = len(audio_references(entries)) - len(items)` and warns, precisely because "an annexure two recordings short looks exactly like two recordings that were never made".

**How it fails.** Designers Priya and Ravi run one workshop; an admin gives Ravi a `DesignWorkshopViewer` row, so `load_workshop_or_404` admits him (design_workshops.py:108-113) and the route's own guard passes. Priya uploads six interview recordings. Ravi opens the transcripts screen — the preview whose stated purpose is that "a designer about to append transcripts to a report submitted to a ministry needs to see what they are appending" — and gets `{"items": [], "total": 0, "withTranscript": 0}` with no explanation. If he then generates the report, the report path tells him "6 recording(s) … could not be included", so the preview and the generator contradict each other and the preview is the one that lies.

**What it costs.** An empty list that reads as "nothing exists" when it means "withheld from you", landing on the exact feature `DesignWorkshopViewer` was built for. A designer can conclude recordings were lost in sync and re-record interviews that are sitting in the database.

**Remedy.** (1) Required either way: make the refusal visible. `list_workshop_transcripts` can compute `len(audio_references(entries)) - len(items)` itself with no signature change and carry it as `withheld`, with the report path's own sentence. (2) Decide whether a viewer grant should confer reads on the workshop's OWN audio — it should; docs/PERMISSIONS.md §4.4.4 already argues that withholding the questionnaire's copy of an interview while showing the stage's copy protects nothing, and stage 8's `surveyResponse` carries the same testimony in text the grantee can already edit. Add a third clause to the media predicate scoped to ids referenced by a stage of a workshop the caller may load. NOTE: whether (2) is worth doing at all depends on the media-surface finding below, which serves the same `transcriptText` to every signed-in account with no filter — settle that first.

**What the verifier checked.** Read workshop_transcripts.py:305-336 verbatim; the predicate is applied exactly as quoted. Read `owned_or_granted_where` at records.py:536-573 in full — confirmed `has_rank(user, "PROFESSOR")` is the only short-circuit and the OR has exactly two clauses (own uploads, DataAccessGrant), so a viewer row cannot satisfy it. Confirmed DESIGNER=35 < PROFESSOR=40 at deps.py:43-45. Read the route at 1307-1336 and confirmed there is no withheld/refused term anywhere in the response, unlike services/design_workshops.py:1809-1814 which computes one for the same call. Looked for a guard that would make it unreachable: the route calls `load_workshop_or_404` first, which explicitly admits viewer-grant holders (design_workshops.py:108-113), so the exact population that reaches the route is the population the media predicate then refuses. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md. Checked the out-of-scope list: this is media-file entitlement, not `asr_*`/`dictation_*`/`ai_layers`, so it is in scope. MAJOR upheld.

---

### [MAJOR] (frontend) View Data's "Browse by type" renders the first 100 rows of a record type with no pager, no total and no notice, and its "Narrow to one record" box can only search inside those 100

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** frontend/app/(protected)/data/page.tsx:547-552 (`BrowseTypeDef.load` signature), :561, :573, :585, :602, :614, :626-627, :643, :658 (the eight loaders), :805 (`recordOptions`), :826-833 (the ComboBox), :852-882 (the table); contrast :792 and :1606-1608

**What it does.** Verified. `BrowseTypeDef.load` is typed `() => Promise<BrowseRow[]>` (data/page.tsx:552) — it has no page or search parameter to give it one — and all eight implementations share one shape:

```tsx
      sortRecent((await listResource<Artisan>("/artisans", { pageSize: 100 })).items).map((x) => ({   // :561
```

`PageResult.total` is discarded in all eight. The panel renders `(rows ?? []).map(…)` inside a `max-h-96 overflow-auto` box (:851-882) with no `<Pagination>` and no row count anywhere, and `recordOptions = (rows ?? []).map(…)` (:805) feeds a `ComboBox`, which filters client-side over exactly that array.

The finder's contrast is accurate and is what makes this a defect rather than a limitation: the same file surfaces truncation twice — `currentTree?.truncated` prints "This listing was truncated at the server cap — open subfolders to see everything it holds." (:1606-1608), and the dataset download surfaces `manifest.truncated` verbatim (:792, again at :1401). This panel knows the vocabulary and does not use it.

**How it fails.** A researcher opens View Data and picks "Media" to find a file from last season. `/api/media` total is 854 (measured, docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md:968). The table lists the newest 100 and the scroll box simply ends — no count, no "more". They type the filename into "Narrow to one record", which filters only the 100 already in memory, and get "No records". The file exists and they have its name; this screen tells them it does not.

**What it costs.** The screen whose purpose is "browse everything we hold" withholds ~88% of the media table and ~77% of the artisan table (854 and 431 measured, against a 100-row page) with no indication, and its record picker turns a present record into an absent one. "Not in the repository" and "past the first hundred rows" render identically, on the page a researcher goes to precisely when they cannot find something.

**Remedy.** Minimum, and cheap: keep the whole `PageResult` in `load()` and, when `total > rows.length`, print the count and the cut in the sentence the tree panel at :1606-1608 already uses. Proper: widen `BrowseTypeDef.load` to `(page: number, search: string)`, drive it from a `<Pagination>` and a server-side `search=` term — all eight endpoints accept `search`/`page`/`pageSize` — and feed the ComboBox from the server rather than from the loaded array.

**What the verifier checked.** Checked for a compensating control before accepting it: read the whole `BrowseByTypePanel` render (:805-900) — no `<Pagination>`, no total, no "load more"; the only other controls are the record-type `Dropdown` and the "Download dataset" button. Checked whether the ComboBox searches server-side — `ComboBox` in components/ui/Dropdown.tsx:142-179 delegates to `SearchableSelect` over the `options` array it is passed, so it cannot reach an unloaded row. Checked whether `load` could be handed a page by some caller — its type at :552 takes no arguments and the only call sites are `def.load()`. Confirmed the sibling truncation notices exist at :792 and :1606-1608. Reachability is measured (OPEN_FINDINGS.md:968), not assumed. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

### [MAJOR] (frontend) A design-workshop id the account may not open renders as a real, editable 22-stage workshop, because `ensureDraft` fabricates a local record before the server is asked

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/[id]/page.tsx:204, :247-259, :299, :377-379, :424-442; frontend/lib/designWorkshopStore.ts:973-1006 (`ensureDraft`); frontend/lib/permissions.ts:277-284 (the route guard) and :325-337 (`routeMatches`/`routeGuardFor`); backend/app/services/design_workshops.py:100-136 (`load_workshop_or_404`)

**What it does.** `ROUTE_GUARDS`'s `/design-workshops` row (permissions.ts:277) gates on `canRunDesignWorkshops`, a role SET, not a per-record check — every designer passes it for every workshop id. The page then calls `ensureDraft(id)` unconditionally for any non-local id (:204) and immediately `setDraft(local)` (:206). `ensureDraft` (store :973) writes a fresh draft to IndexedDB with `remoteId = id`, an empty header and `headerDirtyAt: null`. `getDesignWorkshop(local?.remoteId ?? id)` then 404s, because `load_workshop_or_404` raises 404 with detail "Record not found" for a non-creator, non-admin, non-viewer (services/design_workshops.py:128-136). A 404 is neither `isUnreachable` nor >= 500, so it falls to the generic branch at :259 and only calls `setError`. `detail` is derived from the fabricated draft at :299 (`draft ? { ...draftHeader(draft), id: ... } : null`), so it is non-null; and the stage list renders on `registry !== null && draft !== null` (:433), which is also satisfied. The page paints one red line above a complete workshop panel and all 22 clickable stage rows.

**How it fails.** A designer is forwarded a colleague's workshop link — she is neither creator nor a named viewer, so the API 404s. She sees a red "Record not found" line above a workshop panel ("Untitled design workshop", 0%, status badge) and twenty-two numbered stage rows, plus the Cards & tags / Import photographs / Report history / Report buttons. Reading the 0% as "not started yet" she opens stage 4. There the same fabricated draft makes `canReconcile` true, `getDesignWorkshopStage` 404s, the catch at stages/[stageKey]/page.tsx:583-621 seeds an empty form, clears `loading`, and the full editable stage form renders with the amber "This device has never downloaded this stage" notice. She types the day's interview into it and it autosaves to the local draft.

**What it costs.** A record the account is forbidden to read is presented as an empty record it may fill in. Worse than the finder stated: the fabricated draft never appears in the workshops list either — /design-workshops/page.tsx:427-443 prepends a local draft only when `draft.remoteId === null || offline`, and this one has a `remoteId` and the browser is online — so once she has typed into it, the day's capture is reachable only by re-entering the original URL, and the sync pass will PUT it into a workshop the server permanently refuses. The same shape also turns a mistyped cuid into a convincing blank workshop rather than "no such workshop".

**Remedy.** Do not fabricate a record before the server has been asked. Either defer `ensureDraft(id)` until `getDesignWorkshop` has succeeded, falling back to `loadDraft(id)` for the offline path (the only case that legitimately needs a local copy for a server id), or keep the seeding and gate the render: when the detail read failed with a 404 — not `isUnreachable`, not 5xx — and the local draft has never been reconciled (`lastSyncedAt === null` and no stage carries `serverLoadedAt`), render a dead-end panel in the `RouteLocked` anatomy saying "no such design workshop, or not one this account may open" instead of `detail` and the stage list. Keep the wording ambiguous between the two, matching `load_workshop_or_404`'s own deliberate refusal to distinguish them (services/design_workshops.py:133-136).

**What the verifier checked.** Verified every link in the chain rather than the summary. (1) permissions.ts:277-284 — the guard's `can` is `canRunDesignWorkshops`, a set membership over `user.role` (:400-403), with no record argument; `routeGuardFor` matches by longest path prefix only. (2) store :973-1006 — `ensureDraft` returns any existing draft, otherwise writes one with `remoteId` set to the passed id and returns it; no server call, no ownership check beyond stamping `ownerUserId: sessionUserId`. (3) backend :122-136 — three distinct 404 raises, all with detail "Record not found", the third specifically for the non-creator/non-admin/non-viewer case with a comment explaining the choice. (4) The render gates: `detail` at :299 keys on `draft`, not on `error`; the stage `<ol>` at :433 keys on `registry`/`draft`, not on `error`; and `{error ? ... : null}` at :377 is an additive banner, not a branch. (5) I tried to find a rescue in the stage page and found the opposite — stages/[stageKey]/page.tsx:583-621 explicitly re-seeds and clears `loading` on the failure path, so the form is editable. (6) Corrected two of the finder's citations: `detail` is at :299, not :395, and `ensureDraft` starts at :973, not :937. (7) I downgraded the finder's `category: auth` in substance: no server data is disclosed and no write succeeds, so this is not an authorization bypass — it is a client-side state defect with an authorization-shaped trigger, which is why MAJOR and not BLOCKER. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md (the nearest entry, :229 `patchDraftHeader`, mentions `ensureDraft` only to explain why that latent PATCH is harmless).

### [MAJOR] (frontend) The readiness screen throws away loadCustomDefinition's 'unknown' source, so a failed definition read produces a green 'every required field is filled in' over a workshop with unanswered required custom questions

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/[id]/readiness/page.tsx:161-168, :226-231 and :264-277; frontend/lib/designWorkshopStore.ts:2242-2257 (`CustomDefinitionSource`), :2296-2297 (the `unknown` return) and :2313-2315 (`customFieldsFor`); frontend/lib/customSections.ts:342-347; frontend/components/designworkshop/CustomSections.tsx:156

**What it does.** Exactly as described, with corrected line numbers (the finder cited store:1986-1992; the real `catch { if (!held) return { definition: null, source: "unknown" }; … }` is at :2296-2297). The readiness effect tests `if (cancelled || !held.definition) return;` and drops `held.source` on the floor. With source `unknown` the draft's `customDefinition` stays null, `customFieldsFor` → `customFieldsForStage(null, …)` → `customStageBlocks(null,…).flatMap(...)` → `[]`, so no required custom field is counted, `blocking` is empty, `isSubmittable` is true and the page renders the success block: 'Every required field across all {n} stages is filled in… Nothing on this workshop will be refused for a missing Basic field.' The three-state type exists precisely for this (`CustomDefinitionSource` doc at :2242-2257: a per-workshop definition 'has no floor by construction'), and the stage form is the one screen that honours it — `page.tsx:282` holds `customSource` and `CustomSections.tsx:156` renders a dedicated `source === "unknown"` state. The page's own comment at :148-159 states the rule it breaks. I also found a SECOND, wider door in the same screen: when `getDesignWorkshop` itself fails the effect returns at :175-179 and `loadCustomDefinition` is never called at all, so a draft that holds a fortnight of answers but has never cached a definition scores the same false green under an amber banner that positively asserts 'That is the same arithmetic the repository does — nothing on this page needs a server to be right' (:226-231).

**How it fails.** A cluster office adds three required questions to stage 11 through the custom-sections editor. The designer's laptop created the workshop online and then worked the fortnight offline, so no definition was ever cached. On the last afternoon, in flaky signal, `GET /{id}` lands (no offline banner) and `GET /{id}/custom-sections` does not; `loadCustomDefinition` returns `{definition: null, source: "unknown"}` and the `.then` returns immediately. The screen prints the green tick and 'Nothing on this workshop will be refused for a missing Basic field.' The generated .docx then prints stage 11 three short in the completeness annexure and the download banner carries 'Stage 11 (…): 3 required field(s) not recorded'. The fully-offline variant of the same laptop produces the identical false green with only the reassuring amber banner.

**What it costs.** The one screen whose entire purpose is answering 'can I pack up?' answers yes on evidence it knows it does not have, with no visual difference from a genuinely complete workshop. Answers that only exist while the designer is in the village are lost.

**Remedy.** Keep the source: hold `const [definitionSource, setDefinitionSource] = useState<CustomDefinitionSource | null>(null)`, set it for all three values in the `.then`, and (a) render an amber line above the list when it is `unknown` — 'this browser could not read the questions this workshop adds of its own, so anything they ask is not counted below' — and (b) degrade the success wording to 'every required field in the standard form is filled in' instead of 'nothing will be refused'. Do the same for the branch at :175-179, where the definition is never even attempted: the offline banner at :226-231 must stop claiming arithmetic parity when `draft.customDefinition` is null. The stage index (page.tsx:240-244) discards the source too, but there it only mis-draws a progress bar.

**What the verifier checked.** I hunted for a guard. `customFieldsForStage` has no bundled floor — `customSections.ts:342-347` flattens `customStageBlocks(definition, …)` and a null definition yields nothing, so the score silently loses the custom requirements rather than failing. I checked whether the `unknown` state is unreachable for a permissions reason that would make it constant rather than intermittent: `GET /{workshop_id}/custom-sections` (routes/design_workshops.py:1140-1156) is guarded by the same `load_workshop_or_404` as the workshop read, so anyone who can read the workshop can read the definition — meaning `unknown` really is the transient/offline case the finder describes, not a permanently-403 class of user. I checked whether an earlier fetch would normally have cached it (the stage page and the stage index both call `loadCustomDefinition`), which is what narrows the window — but a browser whose every prior fetch failed holds `held === null` and lands in the `unknown` branch, and I found the wider offline path where the fetch is not attempted at all. Not in OPEN_FINDINGS. Severity kept at MAJOR because of that second path, which needs no partial-connectivity coincidence.
