### [MAJOR] (frontend) The report screen and report history pass the raw route param to five server calls, so both 404 on any workshop reached by its local draft id

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/[id]/report/page.tsx:291, :335, :499, :747, :864; frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/[id]/report/history/page.tsx:142; frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/page.tsx:386 and the createLocalOffline that feeds it (~:733-753); backend/app/services/design_workshops.py:122

**What it does.** Every quoted line exists as quoted. The mount effect calls `getDesignWorkshop(id)` (:291), `previewDesignWorkshopReport(id, template || undefined)` (:335), `downloadDesignWorkshopReport(id, {...})` (:499) and `saveDesignWorkshopStage(id, REPORT_STAGE_KEY, ...)` (:747) with the raw route param, and passes the same param as `workshopId` to `ReportSettingsPanel` (:864), whose own `save()` posts to it (ReportSettingsPanel.tsx:147). `report/history/page.tsx:142` does the same with `fetchDesignWorkshopReportHistory(id)`. Every sibling route resolves the server id first — stage index `getDesignWorkshop(local?.remoteId ?? id)` at [id]/page.tsx:215, readiness at readiness/page.tsx:144, codes at codes/page.tsx:160, and `CustomSectionsEditor.tsx:200` writes the rule out (`const target = draft?.remoteId ?? (isLocalWorkshopId(workshopId) ? null : workshopId);` — the finder cited :188/:451, the actual line is :200). The report page already loads the draft at :272 (`loadDraft(id)`, which `matchesId` resolves by either id) and reads only `stages` off it, never `remoteId`. On the server, `load_workshop_or_404` is `find_unique(where={"id": workshop_id})` (design_workshops.py:122), so a `dwlocal-` id is a hard 404 'Record not found'.

**How it fails.** Offline (or on one transient 5xx — the `isTransient` catch at page.tsx:~365) the create falls back to `createLocalOffline`, which returns `draft.localId`, and :386 does `router.push(`/design-workshops/${created.id}`)`, landing the browser on `/design-workshops/dwlocal-…`. The stage index, all 22 stage forms, Cards & tags, readiness and custom sections keep working from that URL because each resolves `remoteId`; the Report and Report-history buttons in that same header ([id]/page.tsx:365, :369) carry the route id straight through. Once signal returns and the draft syncs, the record exists on the server and the report is genuinely generatable — but pressing Report from that tab issues `GET /api/design-workshops/dwlocal-…`, gets 404, and paints 'Record not found' in the red banner at :594; preview, both downloads, the accent save and the Report-settings save fail the same way, and Report history is dead identically.

**What it costs.** From the URL the application itself navigated the designer to, the deliverable of the whole product is unreachable and the message says the record does not exist — the one sentence that sends a designer hunting for lost fieldwork. The rest of the app disagrees with those two screens about whether the workshop exists.

**Remedy.** Resolve the server id before any request, exactly as `CustomSectionsEditor.tsx:200` does: hoist the existing `loadDraft(id)` result out of the effect at :270-283, derive `const remoteId = draft?.remoteId ?? (isLocalWorkshopId(id) ? null : id)`, and use it for `getDesignWorkshop`, `previewDesignWorkshopReport`, `downloadDesignWorkshopReport`, `saveDesignWorkshopStage` and `ReportSettingsPanel`'s `workshopId`. When it is null, render the honest 'this workshop has not reached the repository yet' state instead of a 404. Same in `report/history/page.tsx` for `fetchDesignWorkshopReportHistory`. Internal `<Link>` hrefs must keep the route id — they are routes, not API calls.

**What the verifier checked.** I tried four ways to kill it. (1) Does the server resolve local ids? No — `load_workshop_or_404` does a `find_unique` on the primary key (design_workshops.py:122-124) and 404s. (2) Are the Report / Report-history links hidden for a local draft? No — [id]/page.tsx:365 and :369 render them unconditionally in the header, interpolating the route `id`. (3) Does the report page hold a draft it could resolve from? Yes, `loadDraft(id)` at :272 (matched by `matchesId`, store line 745/757), and it ignores `remoteId`. (4) Is it in OPEN_FINDINGS? No — nothing there touches id resolution on the report routes. What I did knock down is the finder's 'permanently dead for the whole life of the workshop': once the draft syncs, the list page renders the SERVER row (page.tsx:430-443 merges by `remoteId`) and its Report link at :695 uses `workshop.id`, i.e. the server id — so a fresh navigation from the list works and the URL is corrected. The failure is scoped to the tab/URL the create pushed (and to any bookmark of it), which is why I downgraded CRITICAL to MAJOR rather than confirming the finder's severity.

### [MAJOR] (frontend) 'Save as the workshop's colour' writes stage 20 with no unsent-draft guard and never updates the local draft, so the saved colour is silently replaced by the next sync

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/[id]/report/page.tsx:277-278, :742-768, :381-389, :868; frontend/components/designworkshop/report/ReportSettingsPanel.tsx:210-216 and :239; frontend/components/designworkshop/report/ReportAccentPicker.tsx (the save button, `disabled={saving}`, and `commit()` calling `onSave` from every dismissal path); frontend/lib/designWorkshopStore.ts:2660-2664 and :2139-2143; backend/app/services/design_workshops.py:1057

**What it does.** All the load-bearing code is as quoted, with two line corrections. The page computes `setStage20Pending(Boolean(stage && (stage.dirtyAt !== null || stage.removedFrom.length > 0)))` at :277-278 and hands it to `ReportSettingsPanel` as `draftPending` (:868), where it both disables Save (`disabled={saving || !dirty || !online || draftPending}`, :239) and prints 'Saving from here would be undone the moment those changes sync' (:210-216). The accent picker's `onSave` (:742-758) performs the same write to the same entity of the same stage and consults neither `stage20Pending` nor `online`; its button is `disabled={saving}` alone, and `commit()` routes Escape / click-away / blur / `AnchoredPopover.onClose` into the same `onSave`. The push that overwrites it is real: `buildStageEntries` sends a READ stage's singleton with no `merge` flag (designWorkshopStore.ts:2660-2664 — the finder cited :2324-2328, which is `adoptServerStage`), and `save_stage` replaces a singleton's `data` wholesale unless `if entry.merge and previous:` (design_workshops.py:1057). The finder's claim that `adoptServerDetail` refuses to fold a dirty stage is CORRECT in this tree: :2139-2143 keeps only `completeness` and explicitly does not stamp `serverLoadedAt` (the new `foldStageInto` at :1729 is wired into `adoptServerStage` only). Where the finder is wrong is the emphasis: the missing guard is not the only door. `refreshWorkshop` (:381-389) calls `getDesignWorkshop` and `setDetail`/`setSettings` only — it never adopts into IndexedDB — so even when stage 20 is CLEAN the local draft keeps a copy with no `themeAccent`, and the next thing that dirties stage 20 on this device (opening the stage form offline and saving anything) pushes that stale singleton without `merge` and erases the colour.

**How it fails.** Path A (the finder's): stage 20 is edited offline and banked dirty; signal returns; the designer drags a brand colour and presses 'Save as the workshop's colour'. The button is enabled, the write succeeds, the sheets repaint, and minutes later the sync pass pushes the still-dirty stage-20 singleton — which has no `themeAccent` — with no `merge`, and the server replaces the row. Path B (no pending draft needed): the colour is saved while stage 20 is clean; the draft is never told; the designer later opens stage 20 offline, ticks one box and saves; that push replaces the singleton from a local copy that never learned the colour.

**What it costs.** A stage-20 answer the designer deliberately saved is discarded in silence, and the one control on screen that understands the hazard (the Save button three inches below, disabled with an explanation) is not consulted by the write that causes it. The next report prints in the old colour — the exact complaint this Save button was added to fix.

**Remedy.** Two changes, not one. (a) Gate the write: because `commit()` calls `onSave` from every dismissal path, the guard must live inside the `onSave` handler at :742 (return early and set an explanatory error when `stage20Pending` or `!online`), with the panel's own sentence beside the button; disabling the button alone is not enough. (b) Make the local draft learn the value after a successful write — but note that `adoptServerDetail` alone (the finder's remedy) does NOT fix path A, because its dirty branch at :2139-2143 refuses to fold; either route the accent write through the store so the draft's stage-20 singleton is updated in place, or make that branch use the existing pure `foldStageInto` (:1729).

**What the verifier checked.** I tried to kill this on the wire semantics first: if the push carried `merge: true` the colour would survive, so I read `buildStageEntries` — merge is attached only when `neverRead` (`(stage?.serverLoadedAt ?? null) === null`, :2612, applied at :2660-2664 and :2714), and a stage the browser has read pushes a bare replace, which `save_stage` honours wholesale (design_workshops.py:1057 is the only preserving branch). I then checked whether anything else re-reads the draft after the accent save — `refreshWorkshop` does not, and no `adoptServerDetail` call exists anywhere on the report page. I checked whether the working tree's new web fold rescues it: `foldStageInto` exists (:1729) and `adoptServerStage` uses it, but `adoptServerDetail`'s dirty branch still returns early without `serverLoadedAt`, so the finder's mechanism stands. Not a duplicate of the OPEN_FINDINGS 'missing web fold' entry, which is about withheld deletions. Severity kept at MAJOR rather than reduced to MINOR only because path B needs no pending stage-20 draft at all.

### [MAJOR] (frontend) The stage form banks a FAILED local write as saved: `mutate` swallows every transaction error into `null` and the autosave's `write()` ignores the result

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/[id]/stages/[stageKey]/page.tsx:691-703 (quoted lines are exact), :652-672 (the `sameSnapshot` short-circuit that makes it permanent), :844-908 (`save`, which reads the DRAFT back off disk); frontend/lib/designWorkshopStore.ts:885-921 (`mutate`, the blanket `.catch(() => null)` at :918)

```ts
// stages/[stageKey]/page.tsx:691-703
let written = false;
const write = async () => {
  if (written) return;
  written = true;
  const saved = await putDraftStage(target, stageKey, payload);
  banked.current = snapshot;
  flushRef.current = null;
  setLocalPending(false);
  const next = saved?.stages[stageKey];

// designWorkshopStore.ts:916-920
    await req(store.put(updated));
    return updated;
  }).catch(() => null);
  if (next) await refreshDrafts();
```

**What it does.** Exact as quoted. `mutate` collapses three different outcomes into `null`: the draft was not found, the draft belongs to another session (the owner filter at :902), or the transaction failed (`req` rejects on `onerror`, and `transact` rejects on `onabort`/`onerror` at :511-515 — a `QuotaExceededError` on a readwrite put arrives by exactly that route). `putDraftStage` returns that `null` unchanged. `write()` then advances `banked.current`, nulls `flushRef` and clears `localPending` unconditionally, and because the autosave effect's only guard is `sameSnapshot(snapshot, baseline)` at :661, the identical values are never written again. The amber "Saved on this device only" chip is turned off by the same lines. WORSE THAN THE FINDER SAID: `save(submit)` does not send React state — it calls `flushLocal()` and then re-reads the draft from disk (`const draft = target ? await loadDraft(target) : null` at :852, `buildStageEntries(stage, draftStage)` at :896), so pressing "Save stage" after a failed local write uploads the STALE draft and reports success, or reports "There is nothing in this stage on this device to send" — the typed text has no path to the server at all.

**How it fails.** A designer is twelve days into a workshop; IndexedDB for this origin holds a fortnight of photograph blobs, persistence was never granted (`requestPersistence` swallows its own failure at :1321) and the disk is nearly full. They type four paragraphs into stage 8. The debounce fires, the readwrite transaction is rejected with `QuotaExceededError`, `mutate` returns null, and the page banks the paragraphs and clears the pending chip. Navigating away runs `flushLocal`, which finds `flushRef.current === null` and does nothing. They return later and the stage is exactly as it was. Pressing "Save stage" instead does not save them either: it reads the draft off disk and sends what is stored there. (The second route the finder gives — designer B signs in and every `mutate` on A's still-open form fails the owner check — is real but narrow: signing in normally remounts the app.)

**What it costs.** Total, silent loss of everything typed since the last successful write, on exactly the laptop and the week this store exists to protect, with the UI positively asserting the opposite and the explicit Save button unable to recover it. The repository already handles this correctly one screen away — the photo intake wraps the same class of write in a try/catch and prints "Nothing could be written to this device's storage. If the browser is in private mode or its storage is full…" (photos/page.tsx:397-399).

**Remedy.** Make the failure legible rather than swallowing it. In `mutate`, separate "no such draft / not this session's" (a legitimate `null`, returned from inside the transaction at :903) from "the transaction failed" (let it reject, or return a discriminated result) — a bare `.catch(() => null)` cannot express both. In `write()`, advance `banked.current`, clear `flushRef` and clear `localPending` only when `saved` is non-null; on null keep the baseline and the armed flush so the next render retries, reset the `written` latch (otherwise the retry is a no-op), and put the photo page's sentence in the page's `error` slot.

**What the verifier checked.** Verified the quoted block character-for-character at :691-703 and the `.catch(() => null)` at :918. Traced `transact` (:501-517) and `req` (:483-488) to confirm a quota/abort failure really does surface as a rejection rather than a resolved null — it does, via `transaction.onabort`/`onerror`. Looked for a caller-side check that would rescue it: the autosave effect (:652-707) has none, `flushLocal` (:710-715) has none, and `save` (:844+) makes it worse by re-reading from disk. Checked whether `refreshDrafts` re-publishing would re-seed the form — it does not; `seed` runs only from the load effect keyed on `[id, stageKey]`. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

---

### [MAJOR] (frontend) The strict "Save and check required fields" pass discards the per-field `errors` the 422 carries, so a refused answer is marked on no box and named nowhere, while the previous save's marks stay on screen

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/[id]/stages/[stageKey]/page.tsx:1073-1075 (the 422 branch of save()'s catch), :843-846 (what save() resets), :948-951 and :965 (the success path that does it correctly); frontend/lib/api.ts:143-147 (describeApiDetail's object branch), :195 (`throw new ApiError(response.status, message, body)`), :41-49 (ApiError.payload); backend/app/api/routes/design_workshops.py:1035-1039

**What it does.** Every link verified. The comment at page.tsx:1071-1072 claims "`describeApiDetail` has already turned it into a sentence" naming the fields; it has not. `describeApiDetail`'s object branch (api.ts:143-147) reads ONLY `detail.message` and returns it, so the `{scope: {field: message}}` map the route puts under `detail.errors` (routes:1038) is never read. The map is not lost to the client — api.ts:195 passes the whole parsed body as `ApiError.payload` — and `placeStageErrors(errors, rowKeys)` already exists and is called on the success path at page.tsx:948. It is simply never called on this branch.

The scope is wider than "required fields": `result["errors"]` under `submit=true` contains coercion refusals as well, because `validate_entry(entity, entry.data, enforce_required=payload.submit)` returns both kinds into one map (services:1004-1013). So the half the non-strict save marks on boxes disappears entirely under the strict save.

The compounding half is confirmed too: page.tsx:843-846 is `setSaving(true); setError(null); setNotice(null);` — `setErrors`, `setUnplaced`, `setDropped` and `setDroppedCustom` are not reset, and neither the offline early return at :883 nor the empty-payload early return at :896 clears them.

One detail of the finder's scenario needed checking and holds: the write IS committed before the refusal, because routes:1034 awaits `save_stage(...)` and only then raises at :1035, and the browser's `markStagePushed` at page.tsx:990 is skipped by the throw — so the stage keeps its unsent state and will be re-pushed by the sync pass. I also confirmed the local "still needed" list cannot cover the gap: `localScore` is used only on the OFFLINE branch (page.tsx:884-890) and never on the online strict path.

**How it fails.** A designer finishes stage 17 online and presses "Save and check required fields". Two required boxes are empty and one cost figure was typed as "65OO" with letter O's. `save(true)` reaches the server; `save_stage` writes everything it could read (keeping the previously-stored good value under the refused key, services:1053-1055) and returns `errors: {"costMaterialLine[2]": {"amount": "...not a valid number"}, "costSheet": {"totalCost": "Total cost is required"}}`; the route raises 422 with that map under `detail.errors`. `apiFetch` builds `message` from `detail.message` alone, so the browser prints one red banner reading "Some required fields are missing." No box is marked. `costMaterialLine[2].amount` — a real refusal of a real answer on row 3 of a 28-row table — appears on no screen at all, and no local list can surface it because locally that box IS filled, with "65OO". Meanwhile any red marks left by the previous save are still drawn on boxes the designer has since corrected.

**What it costs.** The second Save button exists to answer "is this stage actually finished", and on the one answer that matters it reports a refusal it will not locate. A coercion refusal reached under `submit=true` is invisible on every surface of the web client. Stale marks from an earlier save sit on boxes nobody objected to, sending the designer to fix an answer that is fine — the exact failure `placeStageErrors`/`strandedRefusals` were written to prevent.

**Remedy.** In `save()`'s catch, before the generic message: when `err instanceof ApiError && err.status === 422`, read `(err.payload as {detail?: {errors?: Record<string, Record<string,string>>}})?.detail?.errors`, run it through the same `placeStageErrors(errors, rowKeys)` the success path uses — hoisting `rowKeys` out of the `try` so the catch can see it — and call `setErrors`/`setUnplaced` with the result, so the existing banner and box marks work unchanged. Separately, reset `errors`, `unplaced`, `dropped` and `droppedCustom` alongside `setError(null)` at the top of `save()` AND on the two early returns at :883 and :896, so no failed or short-circuited save can leave the previous save's marks standing. Also correct the false comment at page.tsx:1071-1072.

**What the verifier checked.** Read page.tsx:843-1080 in full, api.ts:115-200, routes/design_workshops.py:1020-1041, services/design_workshops.py:975-1060. Looked for the guard that would make this unreachable: (a) does the page pre-check completeness locally and refuse to send when required fields are missing, so the 422 is never reached online? No — `localScore` at page.tsx:878 is consumed only inside the offline branch at :883-893; the online strict save always goes to the server. (b) Does `describeApiDetail` fall through to an array branch that would enumerate the errors? No — the array branch at api.ts:117 only handles pydantic's `[{msg, loc}]` shape; a dict `detail` takes the object branch and returns `detail.message`. (c) Does some other reader on the page pick `errors` off `ApiError.payload`? Grepped `placeStageErrors`/`setErrors`/`setUnplaced` across the page: the only call sites are :948-950, on the success path. (d) Is it a duplicate? docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md's part-closed entry about refusal COUNTS concerns `refusedAnswers` vs `Object.keys(errors)` in the sync pass, which is a different function and a different defect. Severity MAJOR held: it is not silent data loss (the good data lands and the refused key keeps its previous value), but a refusal the user cannot locate on a 28-row table is wrong behaviour they will hit.

### [MAJOR] (frontend) The strict pass names no field: on a submit 422 the web throws away the per-field errors the route puts in the body, so "Save and check required fields" answers with one generic sentence and marks nothing

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED · found by the verifier, not the original audit

**Where.** frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/[id]/stages/[stageKey]/page.tsx:1044-1076 (the catch, which has no 422 branch) and :948-950 (the only call of `placeStageErrors`, on the 200 path), frontend/lib/api.ts:144-148 and :187-195, against backend/app/api/routes/design_workshops.py:1036-1039

**What it does.** The route puts the full per-field map in the 422 body: `detail={"message": "Some required fields are missing", "errors": result["errors"]}`. `apiFetch` turns that into `new ApiError(422, describeApiDetail(detail, …), body)`, and `describeApiDetail`'s object branch returns `detail.message` alone — the `errors` map is reachable only through `err.body`, which nothing reads. In `save()`, the throw skips `placeStageErrors`/`setErrors`/`setUnplaced` entirely, so the catch falls through every branch to `setError(err.message)`: the screen shows "Some required fields are missing" with no box marked and no field named. The page HAS the machinery — `placeStageErrors`, `collectionErrors`, `strandedRefusals` — and the local mirror `localStageCompleteness`, which names the missing fields, is used only on the offline branch (:883-893).

**How it fails.** A designer presses "Save stage" (200, errors cleared), then presses "Save and check required fields" on a stage where a required field is blank. The server answers 422 naming that field. The page prints "Some required fields are missing" and marks nothing, so the designer must walk a 40-field form by eye. The same happens when the fault is a coercion refusal rather than a missing required field, and then the sentence is also wrong about what to look for.

**What it costs.** The one control whose entire purpose is to say WHICH questions are outstanding says nothing, on the surface where a designer is most likely to be checking a stage before submission — while the answer is sitting unused in the response body. This is the failure `DwStageRefusal`'s docstring names on the handset ("Not 'some answers were not saved' — a designer who has driven home from a cluster cannot act on that"), still standing on the web.

**Remedy.** Two lines either side. Client: in the catch, `if (err instanceof ApiError && err.status === 422)` read `err.body?.detail?.errors`, run it through the same `placeStageErrors(errors, rowKeys)` the 200 path uses (the row keys are still in scope) and set the same banner. Server: adopt the remedy already proposed for the 422 — return the whole result under `detail` — so the client also gets `completeness.missing`, and choose the message from the errors rather than asserting "required". Pin with a Playwright test that stubs a 422 with a two-field error map and asserts both boxes are marked.

### [MAJOR] (frontend) The /media upload form's "linked record" picker is page one of its type, so newly uploaded media cannot be attached to any record outside the newest 100 of that type

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED · found by the verifier, not the original audit

**Where.** frontend/app/(protected)/media/page.tsx:73-119 (`loadEntryOptions`), :74 (the shared `params`), :220-239 (the effect that fills it), :388-396 (the ComboBox); frontend/components/ui/Dropdown.tsx:142-179

**What it does.** `loadEntryOptions(type)` builds the second dropdown of the upload form — the record a file is being attached to — from `const params = { pageSize: 100 }` (media/page.tsx:74) shared by all eight branches, e.g.

```tsx
    case "artisan": {
      const page = await listResource<Artisan>("/artisans", params);
      return sortRecent(page.items).map((x) => ({ value: x.id, label: `${x.name} · ${x.place}` }));
    }                                                                        // :76-79
```

`PageResult.total` is discarded in every branch, 100 is the endpoints' ceiling (`pageSize: int = Query(20, ge=1, le=100)`), and the options feed a `ComboBox` (:388-396) which — per components/ui/Dropdown.tsx:142-179 — filters client-side over exactly the array it is handed. So the "type to search" affordance searches only the newest 100 rows and there is no page 2, no total and no notice. This is a third, distinct instance of the page-one-picker class, on a surface neither reported finding covers: FunnelFilters narrows a list, the View Data panel browses one, and this one decides what a newly uploaded file is attached to.

**How it fails.** A designer uploads photographs on /media and chooses "Artisan" as the linked record type. The entry dropdown offers the newest 100 artisans of the measured 431 (docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md:967; 731 at :51). The artisan the photographs are of was entered months ago, so typing their name into the ComboBox returns nothing. The placeholder reads "Select an entry" — never "showing 100 of 431" — so the screen presents the absence as a fact about the repository.

**What it costs.** Media is uploaded against the wrong record or against none at all: the upload button only requires `linkedType` (`disabled={… || !linkedType}`), not an entry. Misfiled or unlinked media does not appear in that record's "Previously uploaded media" panel and does not travel with the record, and has to be repaired later through the relink route — for every artisan, product, tool, workshop, craft, process or interview outside the newest hundred of its type. Same shape as the [HIGH] viewer-picker entry already closed in the register: an unreachable row and a never-created row look identical, except that here the consequence lands on a write rather than a read.

**Remedy.** Give `loadEntryOptions` a `search` argument and drive it from the ComboBox's typed term (all eight endpoints accept `search=`), debounced like the media list's own search at :212-216 — the fix the viewer picker took. Until that lands, keep `page.total` from each `listResource` call and render "showing 100 of N — type to narrow" beside the dropdown, so the cut is legible rather than silent.

### [MAJOR] (frontend) Switching the Sitting dropdown on the questionnaire answer screen re-seeds the boxes and clears the dirty set without saving, destroying every answer typed into the section being left

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** frontend/app/(protected)/questionnaires/[id]/answer/page.tsx:306-312 (the switch), :146-166 (the seeding effect), :209-219 (`saveSection`'s dirty filter and early return), :251-260 (`goToSection`, the contrasting safe path), :396 (the promise on screen)

**What it does.** Unsaved answers are recorded in `dirty.current`, a `Set<string>` added to on every textarea `onChange` (:440) and every note `onChange` (:452). The SECTION transition saves first — `goToSection` awaits `saveSection(section, { silent: true })` and aborts on false (:254-255). The SITTING transition does not: the dropdown's `onChange` at :308-312 only calls `setEntryId(next)`, `setSectionIndex(0)` and `router.replace(...)`. `entry` is `useMemo`-derived from `entryId` (:130-133), so it becomes a different object, which fires the effect at :146-166. That effect sees `seededFor.current !== entry.id`, overwrites `draft` and `notes` from the newly chosen sitting's stored answers (:163-164) and resets `dirty.current = new Set()` (:165). Choosing the blank "Choose a sitting" option takes the `!entry` branch at :147-153, which does the same thing with empty objects. Nothing is written to the server first, and the record of what was unsaved is destroyed in the same commit.

**How it fails.** A designer on section 3 has typed eleven answers and two notes. She opens the Sitting dropdown to check something a respondent said in an earlier interview. `setEntryId` fires, the seeding effect replaces `draft`/`notes` and empties `dirty.current`. She switches back: section 3 shows only what the server holds. Pressing "Save this section" is now a no-op — `saveSection` filters `answerableQuestions(target)` on `dirty.current` (:213) and returns `true` at :219 with an empty list, before the toast at :230, so there is not even a confirmation to contradict her.

**What it costs.** Eleven interview answers taken in front of a respondent are destroyed by looking at another sitting, with no prompt, no toast and no trace, on a screen that says in writing at :396 "Saved automatically when you move to another section."

**Remedy.** Route the sitting switch through the same gate as the section switch: make the dropdown's `onChange` async, `const ok = await saveSection(section, { silent: true }); if (!ok) return;` before `setEntryId(next)`. If a designer must be able to switch while a save is being refused, raise `UnsavedChangesDialog` when `dirty.current.size > 0` and apply the switch only on Save or Discard. The `entry`-keyed seeding effect at :146-166 must not be the thing that decides typed work is expendable.

**What the verifier checked.** Read the whole 507-line file rather than the cited ranges. I looked specifically for anything that would save before the switch: there is no `onBeforeChange`, no flush in the `Dropdown`, and `saveSection` is called from exactly three places — `goToSection` (:254), the "Save this section" button (:473) and the "Finish this sitting" button (:488). None of them is on the sitting-switch path. I also checked whether the seeding effect might be skipped: `seededFor.current === entry.id` is the only early return and the new sitting's id is by construction different. I confirmed the compounding detail the finder asserted — that a subsequent "Save this section" sends nothing — by reading :212-219: the `.filter((question) => dirty.current.has(question.id))` and `if (!answers.length) return true;` are exactly as claimed, and the early return sits above the success toast so the press is entirely silent. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

### [MAJOR] (frontend) The questionnaire answer screen has no leave guard and no beforeunload, so the back control, the header's own link and closing the tab all discard the current section's answers

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** frontend/app/(protected)/questionnaires/[id]/answer/page.tsx:95, :282-291, :396, :439-455 — and the whole file, which contains no `useLeaveGuard`, no `UnsavedChangesDialog` and no `beforeunload`

**What it does.** `dirty.current` (:95) names exactly which answers are unsaved and is maintained on every keystroke (:440, :452), and nothing consults it on any exit path. A repo-wide grep for `useLeaveGuard` does not list this file, so `interceptor.current` is null and `BackButton`'s `if (interceptLeave()) return;` (BackButton.tsx:29) falls straight through to `router.back()`. A repo-wide grep for `beforeunload` returns eleven hits across crafts/page.tsx, workshops/page.tsx, ProcessForm.tsx, lib/forms.ts and lib/media.ts — none in this file. The header at :282-291 renders `PageHeader` with the default `back = true` plus a second unguarded exit, a plain `<Link href={`/questionnaires/${id}`}>Edit the questionnaire</Link>`, which no interceptor covers even in principle. The only two things that save are the "Save this section" button (:473) and `goToSection` (:254).

**How it fails.** A designer finishes the last section of a sitting — twelve answers — and instead of "Finish this sitting" presses the round back arrow to return to the questionnaire, or taps "Edit the questionnaire" in the header because she has spotted a typo in a prompt, or the laptop battery dies and the tab closes. All three discard the twelve answers with no prompt: `interceptLeave()` returns false because nothing registered, the header Link is a plain client navigation, and there is no `beforeunload` installed.

**What it costs.** A whole section of a recorded interview is lost silently, on a screen whose description line (:284) says "Each section is saved as you move through it" — true only of the two section-navigation buttons. The back arrow prompts on every record form in the app, so the designer has no reason to distrust it here.

**Remedy.** Track the dirty count in state alongside the ref (the guard reads through a ref that is refreshed on every commit, but the boolean handed to `useLeaveGuard` must itself change) and call `useLeaveGuard(dirtyCount > 0, () => setLeavePromptOpen(true))`, rendering `UnsavedChangesDialog` with `onSave={() => void saveSection(section)}` and `onDiscard={() => { dirty.current = new Set(); setLeavePromptOpen(false); router.back(); }}`. Add the tab-close half with `useUnsavedChanges` from lib/forms.ts:75-90, or an inline `beforeunload` in the ProcessForm:399-406 shape. The header's "Edit the questionnaire" Link needs the same treatment as a button that calls `useLeaveInterceptor()` first, since a `<Link>` is outside `BackButton` entirely.

**What the verifier checked.** Read the file end to end. Confirmed by two repo-wide greps (`useLeaveGuard|useLeaveInterceptor|UnsavedChangesDialog` and `beforeunload`) that this path appears in neither. Checked `UnsavedChangesGuard.tsx` for any provider-level default guard: `intercept()` is `interceptor.current?.() ?? false` with no fallback, and `useLeaveGuard`'s cleanup nulls the slot on unmount, so an earlier form's interceptor cannot be lingering. Checked `PageHeader.tsx` — `back = true` is the default and this call site does not opt out. Corrected one thing in the finder's write-up: `lib/forms.ts:75-90` is not something "every record form gets" — it is imported by ArtisanForm, ProductForm, ToolForm and DesignerProfileForm only, while ProcessForm rolls its own — but that does not weaken the finding, since this page has neither. This is a distinct defect from the sitting-switch one above (different trigger, different remedy) and neither is in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

### [MAJOR] (frontend) Every dropdown in the shared funnel is page one of its endpoint and discards `total`, so with 431+ artisans the artisan filter cannot offer most of them and says nothing about it

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** frontend/components/FunnelFilters.tsx:68-71, :88-101, :110, :112-113; backend/app/api/routes/artisans.py (pageSize `le=100`), workshops.py:199, crafts.py:55

**What it does.** All three loads are page 1 at the endpoints' hard ceiling and keep only `items`:

```tsx
        const [workshopResult, craftResult] = await Promise.all([
          listResource<FunnelWorkshop>("/workshops", { pageSize: 100 }),
          listResource<Craft>("/crafts", { pageSize: 100 })
        ]);                                         // :68-71
    listResource<Artisan>("/artisans", { craftId: value.craftId || undefined, pageSize: 100 })   // :91
```

I verified 100 is the maximum askable on all three routes (`pageSize: int = Query(20, ge=1, le=100)` in artisans.py, workshops.py:199 and crafts.py:55), so this is not a tunable left low — it is the ceiling. `/artisans` orders `createdAt desc`, so `artisans` is the newest 100 rows of the table, full stop. `result.total` is discarded in both loaders and nothing on screen distinguishes a short list from a cut one.

The compounding step is real: `artisanOptions = workshopArtisanIds.size ? artisans.filter(a => workshopArtisanIds.has(a.id)) : artisans` (:113). The workshop's own artisan ids are NOT used to fetch — they only filter the newest-100 array — so a workshop whose people predate the newest hundred contributes nothing to the dropdown, and the control renders as "All artisans" plus a few recent names.

**How it fails.** The corpus already exceeds the cap: docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md:967 records `/api/artisans` total=431 measured live against this database, and :51-52 records 731 in the same file. A designer on /products (or /tools, /processes, /questionnaire — the four pages passing `showArtisan`) picks last month's workshop from the funnel and opens "Filter by artisan" to reach a person entered six weeks ago. That artisan is not in the newest 100, so they are not an option; there is no search box, no "showing 100 of 431", and no page 2. The workshop dropdown has the same shape and bites once the repository passes 100 workshops.

**What it costs.** The same class the register closed at [HIGH] for the design-workshop viewer picker ("353 eligible accounts were invisible in the picker, and looked exactly like colleagues who had never been empanelled", OPEN_FINDINGS.md:511) — an absence that reads as a fact about the data. Here it sits on the funnel at the top of four list pages, so an artisan who cannot be selected is an artisan whose products, tools, processes and interviews cannot be filtered for anywhere. The workshop DEFAULT is unaffected, because /workshops is ordered by occurrence date server-side and the most recent really is on page 1 — which is what has kept this quiet.

**Remedy.** (1) Make the artisan picker ask the server: swap the `Dropdown` at FunnelFilters.tsx:152-161 for the existing `ComboBox` (components/ui/Dropdown.tsx:142) driven by a debounced `search=` on `/artisans`, the same fix the viewer picker took. (2) Keep `result.total` on all three loads and, when it exceeds `items.length`, render the register's existing truncation sentence so the cut is never silent. (2) alone converts an invisible-record bug into a legible one and is a few lines.

**What the verifier checked.** Tried to refute on three fronts. Ceiling: read the `pageSize` Query declarations on /artisans, /workshops and /crafts — all `le=100`, so 100 is genuinely the maximum and no larger page can be asked for. Reachability: the >100 corpus is not my inference, it is measured live in OPEN_FINDINGS.md:51-52 and :967. Compensating control: the `Dropdown` at :152-161 renders `artisanOptions` only, `ComboBox` (Dropdown.tsx:142-179) filters client-side over the array it is handed, and there is no search input on the funnel — nothing reaches a row past the cut. Also checked the workshop arm: `workshops` is re-sorted client-side by occurrence date AFTER the server's page-1 cut, so the sort cannot recover a workshop that fell off. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md — the closed viewer-picker entry is a different module (`design_workshop_viewers.py`) and was fixed there.

### [MAJOR] (frontend) The custom-sections editor tracks `dirty` but guards no exit, so the round back control throws away an entire authored question set

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** frontend/components/designworkshop/CustomSectionsEditor.tsx:153 (`const [sections, setSections] = useState<DwCustomSection[]>([])`), :307-310 (`dirty`), :1239 / :1247 / :1259 (its only three readers); frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/[id]/custom-sections/page.tsx:43-52

**What it does.** The whole authored definition lives in the `sections` React state at :153 with no localStorage or IndexedDB backing — the only store import in the file is `isLocalWorkshopId, loadDraft` (:105), both reads. `dirty` at :307-310 compares `JSON.stringify(definitionBody(sections))` against `JSON.stringify(definitionBody(baseline))`, i.e. the exact bytes the PUT would carry, so the component knows precisely when there is unsaved work. Grepping `dirty` in the file returns four hits after the definition: the Save button's `disabled` (:1239), the Discard button's `disabled` (:1247), the "Nothing has changed yet." caption (:1259) and the comment at :1237. Nothing else. There is no `useLeaveGuard` call, no `beforeunload`, and no `pagehide`/`visibilitychange` flush (all confirmed by repo-wide grep). The page at custom-sections/page.tsx:43-52 renders `PageHeader` with the default `back = true` plus an `actions` `<Link href={`/design-workshops/${id}`}>All 22 stages</Link>` — two exits side by side, neither of which asks.

**How it fails.** A designer authors three new sections across stages 6, 11 and 17 with fifteen questions, help text, units and required flags. Before saving she taps "All 22 stages" (or the back arrow) to check what stage 11 already asks. The component unmounts, `sections` goes with it, and on return `load()` re-reads the server and shows the definition as it was before she started.

**What it costs.** The designer-defined instrument the whole fortnight's fieldwork will be recorded against is lost on a single tap, with no prompt and nothing on disk to recover from. This is distinct from the recorded last-write-wins `PUT /custom-sections` (OPEN_FINDINGS.md:191): that one loses a colleague's SAVED work to a stale tab, this one loses your own UNSAVED work to the back button, and neither remedy touches the other.

**Remedy.** Call `useLeaveGuard(dirty, () => setLeavePromptOpen(true))` in `CustomSectionsEditor` and render `UnsavedChangesDialog` wired to the existing `save()` for Save and to the existing baseline reset (`setSections(baseline)`) for Discard. The "All 22 stages" Link is not covered by `BackButton`, so it must become a button that calls `useLeaveInterceptor()` before navigating. Install a `beforeunload` while `dirty` for the tab-close path.

**What the verifier checked.** Read the component's state declarations, the `dirty` memo and both its button sites, then grepped the file for `localStorage`, `designWorkshopStore`, `pagehide` and `visibilitychange` — the only hit is a read-only `loadDraft` import at :105. Checked repo-wide `useLeaveGuard` and `beforeunload` greps: this file appears in neither. Checked whether the page might opt out of the back control — it does not, and it adds a second exit of its own. I specifically tried to refute this as a duplicate of OPEN_FINDINGS.md:191 (`PUT /custom-sections` last-write-wins) and it is not: that entry is entirely about a stale `customSchemaVersion` on the wire, its remedy is an optional digest field plus a 409, and it says nothing about the leave path. Corrected the finder's citation: `sections` is declared at :153, not :141 (:140 is `stored`), and the button sites are :1239/:1247, not :1223/:1231.

### [MAJOR] (frontend) A valid date typed outside a sibling-derived bound is discarded with no message and the box reverts on blur, so the earlier end of a range cannot be corrected without clearing the later one first

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** frontend/components/forms/DateTimeField.tsx:342-354 (handleText), :356-359 (revert), :240 (`onBlur={onRevert}`); frontend/components/designworkshop/FieldInput.tsx:555-568 (the DATE branch and its min/max); frontend/components/designworkshop/stageFieldRoles.ts:170-182 (dateRangePartner)

**What it does.** Quoted code exists verbatim. `handleText` treats a well-formed but out-of-range date exactly as it treats unparseable text — `if (lowerBound && parsed < lowerBound) return; if (upperBound && parsed > upperBound) return;` — after `setText(next)` has already put the characters in the box, so nothing is committed. `revert()` is wired unconditionally to the input's `onBlur` at :240 and rewrites the box from the stored `iso`. There is no error state on this path: `invalid` on the DATE branch is `Boolean(error)`, and `error` is only ever the message a SAVE came back with. The calendar path is visibly blocked (`disabled={[{before}, {after}]}`), the typed path is not.

I checked the one thing that would have made this worse or unreachable: `parseTypedDate` requires a FOUR-digit year (`/^(\d{1,2})[/\-.](\d{1,2})[/\-.](\d{4})$/`), so no half-typed prefix such as "16/03/20" parses into a bogus in-range year and commits. The refusal is exactly the one the finder describes and nothing else.

One correction to their framing: the refusal is not entirely invisible — the box visibly snaps back to the old value on blur. What is missing is any statement of WHY, and any way to enter the value at all without first clearing the sibling.

**How it fails.** Stage 1's `workshopIdentity` entity declares `startDate`/`endDate`, which `dateRangePartner` pairs (both DATE, same entity, same stem, opposite markers, unique partner), so `FieldInput` passes `max={partnerIso}` to Start date. The record holds 12/03/2026–14/03/2026. The sanction order is revised and the workshop runs 16–18 March. The designer types 16/03/2026 into Start date: `parseTypedDate` succeeds, `parsed > upperBound` (2026-03-14), `handleText` returns without committing, and the box shows "16/03/2026" while focused. They Tab to End date; blur fires `revert()`, which rewrites the box as "12/03/2026" with no message. They type 18/03/2026 into End date, which is above `min` (12/03) and commits. Save. The record reads 12/03/2026–18/03/2026. Nothing on either side validates the pair at save time (the pairing exists precisely because no server validator looks at it, per stageFieldRoles.ts:147-158), so the wrong start date is stored and prints on the report cover.

**What it costs.** A date the designer typed is refused with no explanation, and the only way to move the earlier end of a range forward is to clear the later end first — a rule stated on no screen. The stage's progress bar still reads complete, because the old value is still filled. The guard against a negative-duration workshop is bought by making the more common act — correcting the first date — silently impossible.

**Remedy.** Do not swallow a parsed-but-out-of-range date. Add `onOutOfRange?: (iso: string, bound: "min" | "max") => void` to `DateFieldProps`, call it from the two bound checks in `handleText`, and have `FieldInput`'s DATE branch render a message under the box naming the sibling by its label ("16/03/2026 is after End date, 14/03/2026 — change the end date first"). At minimum, `revert()` must not silently overwrite text that parsed correctly: keep the typed text, set `aria-invalid`, and leave the refusal visible and addressable. The finder's remedy is correct; only their "no message anywhere" wording overstates it, since the revert itself is visible.

**What the verifier checked.** Read DateTimeField.tsx:200-400 and FieldInput.tsx:517-570 and stageFieldRoles.ts:120-182. Looked specifically for a guard that would make this unreachable: (a) whether a partial parse commits an intermediate value that would make the revert benign — it cannot, `parseTypedDate` demands \d{4} for the year; (b) whether `FieldHint` or any sibling draws a message for a rejected-but-parsed date — it draws only `field.help`/`field.unit` and the save-time error; (c) whether the effect at :321-327 would restore the typed text — no, it only runs on `iso` change and is skipped while focused; (d) whether `dateRangePartner` is narrow enough that stage 1 does not actually pair — it does pair (`startDate`/`endDate`, both DATE, both live, stem "date", unique); (e) whether a server-side pair validator catches the resulting reversed/wrong range — none exists, which is the stated reason this UI bound was added. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md. MAJOR held: not narrow (it is every range correction where the new start is after the current end), reachable by an ordinary edit, and the stored result is wrong data on a report cover.

### [MAJOR] (frontend) ProcessForm never registers a leave interceptor, so the page header's back control silently discards a fully documented process

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** frontend/components/forms/ProcessForm.tsx:15 (dialog imported, guard not), :397, :399-406, :420-423, :919-928; frontend/app/(protected)/processes/page.tsx:182-186 and :205-209; frontend/components/PageHeader.tsx:8 (`back = true`); frontend/components/BackButton.tsx:29

**What it does.** `UnsavedChangesProvider` (protected layout :47) holds a single interceptor slot; a form claims it with `useLeaveGuard(dirty, onBlocked)` and `BackButton` consults it via `if (interceptLeave()) return;` before either navigation branch. A repo-wide grep for `useLeaveGuard` returns exactly six registrants — crafts/page.tsx:126, workshops/page.tsx:247, ArtisanForm:370, ProductForm:123, ToolForm:125, DesignerProfileForm:129 — and ProcessForm is not among them. ProcessForm imports `UnsavedChangesDialog` (:15) but not `useLeaveGuard`, computes `const dirty = !saving && signature !== initialSignature;` at :397, and feeds it to exactly one consumer for leaving: `requestCancel()` at :420-423, wired to the form's own Cancel button at :911. So `interceptor.current` is null while the process form is on screen and `interceptLeave()` returns false. `PageHeader` defaults `back = true` and both /processes render modes (create at :182, edit at :205) use the default, so a `BackButton` is on screen in both.

**How it fails.** A researcher opens /processes?new=1 from the Record menu, names the process, picks the artisan and product, adds six steps with names and notes, and attaches media to each step. Before saving she presses the round back arrow at the top-left — the only back control the page offers. `interceptLeave()` returns false, `router.back()` fires, ProcessForm unmounts, and every step, note and picked file is gone with no prompt. The identical action on /artisans/new, /products/new, /tools/new, /crafts, /workshops or the designer profile raises the "Unsaved changes" dialog.

**What it costs.** A whole documented process — steps, notes, media selections and the artisan/product links — is destroyed by one tap on the app's standard back control, on a surface where five sibling forms have taught the user that control is safe. Reachable in the edit mode too, where the same tap discards an in-progress correction to an existing record.

**Remedy.** Add `import { useLeaveGuard } from "@/components/UnsavedChangesGuard";` and call `useLeaveGuard(dirty, () => setGuardOpen(true));` beside the `dirty` computation at :397. The dialog and its `onKeepEditing` / `onDiscard` (which already calls `onCancel()`) / `onSave` wiring exist at :919-928, and the /processes page's `onCancel` (`router.replace("/processes")` for create, `setEditing(null)` for edit) is already the correct destination — so this is the one line the other five forms carry.

**What the verifier checked.** I tried to kill this three ways. (1) Grepped the whole frontend for `useLeaveGuard`/`useLeaveInterceptor`/`UnsavedChangesDialog`: ProcessForm.tsx appears only at :15 (dialog import) and :919 (render) — no guard registration anywhere in the file. (2) Read `UnsavedChangesGuard.tsx` in full: `intercept()` is `interceptor.current?.() ?? false`, and `useLeaveGuard`'s effect cleanup does `ctx.register(null)` on unmount, so no other form's stale interceptor could cover ProcessForm either. (3) Checked whether the back control is actually rendered: `PageHeader` defaults `back = true` and neither /processes header passes `back={false}`. The one thing the finder did not say, which I checked and which does NOT rescue it: ProcessForm DOES install a `beforeunload` handler at :399-406, so the tab-close and reload paths are warned. Only the in-app client-side navigation is unguarded — which is precisely the path the finder described. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md (grepped for leave guard / BackButton / UnsavedChanges: no hits).

### [MAJOR] (frontend) Two tabs on one browser each run their own sync pass: `syncing` is module state, so an offline-created workshop is created twice and every staged photograph is uploaded twice

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** frontend/lib/designWorkshopStore.ts:3019-3035 (the per-realm `syncing` guard), :3081-3110 (the create's check-then-act), :3162-3210 (the media loop's identical shape); frontend/components/designworkshop/DraftSyncBanner.tsx:137-155 (drain on mount) and :157-164 (drain on `online`); frontend/app/(protected)/layout.tsx:50

```ts
// designWorkshopStore.ts:3019-3030
let syncing: Promise<DwSyncResult> | null = null;
export function syncDesignWorkshopDrafts(): Promise<DwSyncResult> {
  if (syncing) return syncing;

// designWorkshopStore.ts:3081 and :3163
if (!draft.remoteId) { const created = await createDesignWorkshop({...});
...
if (media.remoteMediaId || !media.blob) continue;
```

**What it does.** Verified exactly as described. `syncing` deduplicates only within one JavaScript realm. `DesignWorkshopDraftBanner` is mounted in `app/(protected)/layout.tsx:50`, so every open protected tab has its own module instance, its own `syncing`, its own `online` listener and its own drain-on-mount effect. A repo-wide grep for `navigator.locks` and `BroadcastChannel` across `app/`, `components/` and `lib/` returns NOTHING, so there is no cross-tab coordination of any kind. The create is guarded only by reading `draft.remoteId` at :3081 and writing it back after the POST returns — a check-then-act across a network round trip, which IndexedDB transactions cannot serialise because the decision is made outside one. `createDesignWorkshop` carries no client key and the create route (routes/design_workshops.py:836-880) performs no de-duplication, exactly as the store's own comment at :3025-3027 admits. The media loop's skip test has the same shape and a much wider window: it is `remoteMediaId || !blob`, and `remoteMediaId` is only written after the bytes land, so two passes uploading a fortnight of photographs over slow wifi overlap for minutes.

**How it fails.** A designer has the workshop list open in one tab and a stage in another — an ordinary pairing. They created two workshops in a courtyard with no signal. The laptop joins the office wifi; the `online` event fires in BOTH tabs in the same task. Both call `syncDesignWorkshopDrafts`, both get a fresh pass, both `pendingWork()` reads `remoteId === null`, both POST `/design-workshops`. Two records appear under one title; whichever `mutate` lands second owns `remoteId`, so every stage and photograph goes to one and the other is left an empty orphan. The wider window needs no simultaneity at all: tab A is ten minutes into uploading a fortnight of photographs when the designer opens a second protected tab, whose mount effect (DraftSyncBanner.tsx:143-148) drains as soon as `refreshDrafts` answers — both tabs then read the same unconfirmed media rows and upload the same bytes, producing two `DwMedia` rows for one loom.

**What it costs.** Duplicate government records in the list that is the ministry's index, with the duplicate empty and indistinguishable by title; duplicated photographs in the generated .docx; and double the bytes on a metered rural connection. `_clientKey` cannot help — the workshop header has no client key, which the module says itself at :3025-3027.

**Remedy.** Take a cross-tab lease around the whole pass, not just the create: wrap the body of `syncDesignWorkshopDrafts` in `navigator.locks.request("dw-draft-sync", { ifAvailable: true }, …)` and return a no-op result (`stoppedOffline: false`, `pending` from a plain `pendingWork()`) when the lock is held elsewhere. Where Web Locks are unavailable, a holder-id + expiry record written inside a readwrite transaction on the `drafts` store gives the same atomicity `mutate` already relies on. The media loop must sit inside the lease too — it is the longer window of the two, and it is the one that duplicates photographs.

**What the verifier checked.** Confirmed the banner's mount point by reading app/(protected)/layout.tsx:50, and that `syncDesignWorkshopDrafts` has exactly one caller (grep across app/components/lib: only DraftSyncBanner.tsx:96). Searched the whole frontend for `navigator.locks` and `BroadcastChannel` — zero hits, so no guard exists elsewhere. Checked whether IndexedDB serialisation saves it: `mutate` is atomic per transaction, but the create decision is read at :3081 and written at :3098 with an `await createDesignWorkshop` in between, so two realms can both pass the test. Checked the server for idempotency on create — none; `_clientKey` protects only stage rows (`save_stage`'s `by_client_key`). Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md. Kept at MAJOR rather than downgrading because the media half needs no coincidence of timing, only two tabs and a slow upload.

---

### [MAJOR] (frontend) A photograph the server permanently refuses is re-uploaded on every pass forever and strands its stage behind a message promising it will send itself — `DwDraftMedia.lastError`/`.attempts` are written and read by nothing

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** frontend/lib/designWorkshopStore.ts:3163 (the entire retry policy for media), :3181-3197 (the triage that records the refusal), :3614-3623 (`noteMediaFailure`), :3255-3273 (the stage hold-back and its sentence), :2550 (`pendingWork`'s media count), :3637-3645 (`retryDraft`); frontend/components/designworkshop/DraftSyncBanner.tsx:53-55 and :231-235 (`held`)

```ts
// designWorkshopStore.ts:3163
if (media.remoteMediaId || !media.blob) continue;

// designWorkshopStore.ts:3621
await req(stores[STORE_MEDIA].put({ ...media, attempts: media.attempts + 1, lastError: message }));

// designWorkshopStore.ts:3265-3266
`${outstanding.length} attached file${…} still on this device, so this ` +
  "stage has not been sent yet. It sends itself as soon as they upload — nothing has been thrown away.",
```

**What it does.** Verified. The stage arm (:3406-3456) and the workshop arm (:3558-3587) both triage a refusal into a `DwDraftFailure` with `permanent` and `skewRun`, and both are gated by `blocksRetry` on the next pass. The media arm has none of it: its only skip test is `media.remoteMediaId || !media.blob`, neither of which a refusal sets, so the same bytes are re-sent on every pass forever. `noteMediaFailure` writes `attempts` and `lastError`, and a grep across `app/`, `components/` and `lib/` finds no reader for either (the only `lastError` consumers are `ApiKeysPanel` and local variables in `lib/media.ts`). The triage path itself is reachable exactly as claimed: `MediaBatchError` sets `cause` from the first per-file failure (lib/media.ts:1409-1419), so `isUnreachable` (offline.ts:397-405) correctly answers false for a 415/413 and the code takes the `noteMediaFailure` branch rather than stopping the pass. The stage referencing the file is then held back by `unresolvedMediaRefs` with a NON-permanent failure whose sentence promises the opposite of what will happen, and `retryDraft` clears draft and stage failures only — it never touches the media store.

**How it fails.** A designer attaches a short video of a loom to a field on stage 13 through the photo-intake page. The server refuses it (415 on the container, or 413 over the size limit). On the next connection `uploadMediaBatch` escalates the one-file batch, `isUnreachable` answers false, and `noteMediaFailure` records the reason on a row nobody reads; `blocked.add(media.stageKey)` fires and the stage is held back with "It sends itself as soon as they upload — nothing has been thrown away", which the banner prints under the workshop. On a marginal signal the `online` event fires repeatedly, and every pass re-uploads the whole video and receives the same refusal. Stage 13 never reaches the repository, the banner reads "1 stage(s) waiting" indefinitely, no screen ever names the file or the reason, and the "Try again" button is not offered because the held failure is not `permanent` (DraftSyncBanner.tsx:243).

**What it costs.** A whole stage of fieldwork stranded behind a message stating it will send itself, with no name, no reason and no action available; plus an unbounded re-upload of the same rejected bytes on every connectivity flap, on the metered connection this feature exists for. The outbox next door already solves it — `blocksRetry(entry.failure)` stops replaying a refused entry and its banner offers a Discard — and `DwDraftFailure`/`blocksRetry` are already imported into this file at :123.

**Remedy.** Give `DwDraftMedia` the triage the other two arms have: replace `lastError: string | null` with `failure: DwDraftFailure | null` (or add `permanent`/`skewRun` beside the existing fields), have `noteMediaFailure` decide `permanent` from the same "did the server answer" split the stage arm makes, and gate the loop with `if (blocksRetry(media.failure)) continue;` at :3163. Surface it: `pendingWork` should push `{ scope: media.name, message }` into `failures` for a permanent media refusal so the banner draws it with a "Try again", and `retryDraft` should clear media failures for the draft. Finally, the stage's held-back sentence must distinguish "waiting in the queue" from "refused" — "it sends itself as soon as they upload" must never be printed over a file that never will.

**What the verifier checked.** Read the whole media loop (:3160-3210) and confirmed :3163 is the only skip test. Grepped `lastError` and `.attempts` across app/, components/ and lib/ — the only design-workshop hits are the type declaration (:437), two initialisers (:1366, :1454) and the writer (:3621); no reader. Tried to refute the reachability of the refusal branch by checking whether a refusal is misclassified as unreachable: `MediaBatchError` passes `cause: failures[0]?.cause` (lib/media.ts:1415) and `isUnreachable` follows `cause`, so an ApiError 415/413 returns false and the branch IS taken — the finder is right. Confirmed `retryDraft` (:3637-3645) touches only draft and stage failures, and that the banner's "Try again" is gated on `state.refusals.length`, which is built from `permanent` failures only. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

### [MAJOR] (frontend) `foldNote` — the only warning that a fold is about to let the next save delete rows this browser has never shown the designer — is written to IndexedDB and rendered on no screen

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED · found by the verifier, not the original audit

**Where.** frontend/lib/designWorkshopStore.ts:287 (`foldNote?: string | null;`), :1887-1927 (foldNotice), :2343-2354 (the only writer); against android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/StageScreen.kt:601, :666, :674

**What it does.** `grep -rn "foldNote" frontend/ --include=*.ts --include=*.tsx` (excluding `.next`) returns exactly two hits: the field declaration on `DwDraftStage` at designWorkshopStore.ts:287, and the single write at :2354 inside `adoptServerStage`'s dirty branch. There is no reader anywhere — not in the stage page, not in `DraftSyncBanner`, not in any component. The comment above the write says it is "kept on the record rather than raised as a toast, because the read that produced it may have happened while the designer was on another screen", which states an intent to display it later; nothing ever does.

What is being discarded is not decoration. `foldNotice` (:1887) builds two things: an "N answers / N rows were already there and not in this browser, and have been added below" sentence, and — when `fold.sweptRows` is non-empty — the paragraph "You had deleted everything in {entities} in this browser, so N rows the server still holds there have NOT been added back, and the next save will delete them on the server — including anything added there by somebody else since you deleted… If you did not mean to delete them, say so before this stage is submitted." That paragraph is the ONLY place on the web where a designer is told that the fold has just armed a destructive save over rows they have never seen. Android renders its identical `DwStageFold.notice` (StageScreen.kt:601 threads it into the loaded state; :666 and :674 draw the heading and the body), so the two surfaces disagree about whether this warning is shown at all.

**How it fails.** A designer empties (or, per the confirmed finding above, merely appears to empty) a collection on a stage this browser has never read. Later they open the stage with a connection. `adoptServerStage` takes the dirty branch, `foldStageInto` declines to fold the server's rows for that entity back in, records `sweptRows[entity] = N`, stamps `serverLoadedAt`, and `foldNotice` composes the warning quoted above. It is written to the draft record and never drawn. The form shows an empty table. The designer edits something unrelated and saves; `stageSweep` now returns `replaceCollections: true` and the N rows are soft-deleted. The first and only mention of them anywhere is the `removed: N` folded into the save notice's arithmetic — a number the designer has no reason to connect to a collection they were never shown.

**What it costs.** The safety net written specifically for the fold's most dangerous outcome exists, is composed correctly, and is discarded before it reaches a human. It is also the sentence that would have made the phantom-deletion defect above visible: with the note drawn, a designer who pressed Add-then-Delete on a blank row would read "the next save will delete 6 rows on the server" and could stop. Without it, the fold's destructive branch runs with no announcement on the surface that has it, while the handset announces it.

**Remedy.** Read `foldNote` off the stage record in the stage page's load effect — it is already returned by `adoptServerStage` on `merged.stages[stageKey]` at page.tsx:564-566, where only `dirtyAt`/`lastPushedAt`/`failure` are currently taken — and render it in the same notice slot the fold's sibling messages use, matching Android's two-part heading/body at StageScreen.kt:666-674. Clear it (write `foldNote: null`) once it has been shown, so an unchanged re-read does not re-announce, which is the behaviour `foldNotice`'s own null-when-nothing-changed contract already assumes.

---

### [MAJOR] (android) `dwFoldServerStage` honours `emptiedEntities` but ignores `deletedRowKeys`, so the read the status screen tells the designer to make re-adds the deleted row and the next save erases its record

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED · found by the verifier, not the original audit

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/DwStageFold.kt:284-305 (the emptied branch) vs :306-316 (the append loop) — `deletedRowKeys` appears nowhere in the file; android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/StageScreen.kt:2152-2159 (the filter that then drops the key); android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/WorkshopSync.kt:846-878 (the counter and the sentence it prints)

**What it does.** `StageDraft.deletedRowKeys` (WorkshopDraftStore.kt:426) is the record for a deletion that is not the last row of a collection; `statusOf` counts such a stage in `unsentDeletions` and prints "you deleted N rows from … and that deletion has NOT reached the server … Open the stage once with a connection and it goes up on the save straight after" (WorkshopSync.kt:846-877). The fold that read is supposed to reach has no term for it. `dwFoldServerStage` skips an entity named in `emptied` (`if (entityKey in emptied) { … return@forEach }`, :284-305), but its append loop (:306-316) re-adds every server row whose `_clientKey`/`_entryId` is not currently held — and a row the designer deleted is, by definition, no longer held. A grep for `deletedRowKeys` across `android/app/src` returns hits in WorkshopDraftStore, WorkshopSync and StageScreen only; DwStageFold.kt has none. `persistLocally` then drops the key, because its filter removes any key whose row is present in the current state (StageScreen.kt:2152-2159).

**How it fails.** An unread stage (`stageSeen = false`) whose collection holds three rows that are already on the server. Offline, the designer deletes one; `onRowsChange` records it in `deletedRows` and `persistLocally` writes `deletedRowKeys = ["costLine#…"]`. `statusOf` counts `unsentDeletions = 1` and prints the sentence above. The designer does exactly what it says and opens the stage with a connection: `holdsWork` is true (two rows remain), so the fold branch runs, the server's third row is not held and is appended back, `stageSeen` becomes true. The fold notice says N rows appeared, but says nothing about the row the designer deleted. On the next debounced save `persistLocally` drops the key (the row is back in `state`), the payload names all three rows with `replaceCollections = true`, and the server keeps all three. `unsentDeletions` falls to 0 and the workshop reads "Backed up to the server".

**What it costs.** The per-row deletion record — the remedy recorded against the open MAJOR finding, and implemented in this working tree — cannot ever discharge for the case it was written for: following the app's own instruction reverses the deletion and destroys the record in the same breath, and the counter that was watching stops watching. The deleted row prints in the delivered report. This is distinct from the recorded open finding, which is about the record not existing at all.

**Remedy.** Give the fold the same asymmetry it already gives `emptiedEntities`: pass `base.deletedRowKeys` into the append loop and skip a server row whose `dwRowId(entityKey, clientKey ?: entryId)` is in that set, counting it in `sweptRows` so the notice states the collateral exactly as it does for an emptied collection. Then `persistLocally`'s filter is harmless (the row never comes back), the next authoritative save omits the row, `replaceCollections` sweeps it, and `recordStageSent` (WorkshopSync.kt:2299-2326) clears the key against what the acknowledged payload actually swept — which is the discharge rule that field already documents. Pin it with a fold test: local draft holding 2 of 3 rows plus `deletedRowKeys` for the third, server bucket holding 3, assert the folded draft still holds 2 and that `sweptRows` names the entity.

---

### [MAJOR] (android) `dwFoldServerStage` honours `emptiedEntities` but ignores `deletedRowKeys`, so following the app's own instruction — "open the stage once with a connection" — is what resurrects the deleted row and destroys the record of the deletion

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED · found by the verifier, not the original audit

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/DwStageFold.kt:267 (`val emptied = base.emptiedEntities.toSet()`), :278-316 (the append loop, which consults `heldKeys`/`heldEntryIds` and nothing else), :319-353 (the returned draft, which copies `emptiedEntities` forward at :352 and never mentions `deletedRowKeys`); android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/WorkshopSync.kt:873-877 (the instruction); android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/StageScreen.kt:2152-2159 (the filter that then drops the record)

**What it does.** `StageDraft.deletedRowKeys` was added as the written record of a non-last-row deletion (WorkshopDraftStore.kt:396-426) and is honoured by `StageScreen.onRowsChange`, by `statusOf` and by `recordStageSent` — but not by the fold. `dwFoldServerStage` reads `base.emptiedEntities` into `emptied` (:267) and declines to re-add rows of those entities (:284-305), while the append loop's only tests are `dwRowId(entityKey, identity) in heldKeys` and `entryId in heldEntryIds` (:311-312), both computed from `base.rows` — from which the deleted row is, by definition, absent. So the server's copy of a row the designer deleted is appended straight back (:313). `persistLocally` then finishes the job: `deletedRowKeys = (deletedRows + existing?.deletedRowKeys.orEmpty()).filterNot { key -> ... state.collections[entity.key]...any { dwRowId(entity.key, row.rowId) == key } }` (StageScreen.kt:2152-2159) drops the key because the row is back in `state`, and `deletedRows` in composition was re-seeded from the pre-fold draft at :698. The fold's own header argues the emptied case must be honoured because "the designer's action left a record" (DwStageFold.kt:40-56); that record now exists one level down and is not read.

**How it fails.** On a stage this handset has never read, the designer deletes one process step out of three (the row was created on this phone and already synced, or came from the web). `deletedRowKeys` records `processStep#<clientKey>`; the save merges, so the row is still on the server, and the workshop status says exactly this: "you deleted 1 row from processStep on this phone, and that deletion has NOT reached the server ... Open the stage once with a connection and it goes up on the save straight after" (WorkshopSync.kt:873-877). The designer does that. The fold reads the stage, appends the server's copy of the deleted row back into the draft, and sets `stageSeen = true`. The row is on screen again, the fold notice counts it only as one of N rows that "appeared" from the server, and the first keystroke afterwards deletes the key from `deletedRowKeys`. The deletion is now undone with no record anywhere, and because the payload names the row again, `replaceCollections` will never sweep it.

**What it costs.** A deliberate row deletion is silently reversed by the one action the app instructs the designer to take to make it stick, and the evidence of it is erased in the same breath — the outcome the fold's own header calls "not recoverable by anybody, because nobody knows to look" (DwStageFold.kt:58-63). The deleted row stays in the repository and prints in the .docx, while the workshop row stops reporting an unsent deletion and reads as fully backed up.

**Remedy.** Give the fold the same treatment it gives `emptiedEntities`: read `val deleted = base.deletedRowKeys.toSet()` beside :267, and in the append loop skip any server row whose identity resolves into that set — `if (dwRowId(entityKey, identity) in deleted) { record it in a new counter and return@forEachIndexed }` — consulting `_clientKey` and `_entryId` exactly as :308-312 already do. Carry `deletedRowKeys = base.deletedRowKeys` explicitly in the returned copy (:319-353) next to the `emptiedEntities` line, and count what was declined in a `sweptRows`-style field so the notice can say the deletion was kept rather than that rows appeared. `recordStageSent` already clears the keys once a sweep has actually carried them (WorkshopSync.kt:2299-2326), so nothing needs to change there.

---

### [MAJOR] (android) `DwSubmissionReadiness.assess` computes the two report checks the web renders and the handset renders neither, so a stage deliberately excluded from the report is dropped with nothing on any screen saying so

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/DwSubmissionReadiness.kt:442-531, :636-646; ui/designworkshop/StageIndexScreen.kt:245-247 and :286-288; report/ReportTemplates.kt:631-637; report/ReportSettings.kt:568-605; ui/designworkshop/ReportScreen.kt:400 and :594-610

**What it does.** Verified in full. `assess` returns `DwWorkshopReadiness` carrying `blocking`, `checks` (`reportChecks`, :442-531: 'template-unknown' and 'stage-excluded:<key>'), `advisory` and `isSubmittable` (:636-646). `StageIndexScreen` is the only caller in `main/` and it calls `DwSubmissionReadiness.addressBook(DwSubmissionReadiness.assess(...))` at both :245-247 and :286-288 — `addressBook` reads `readiness.blocking` and nothing else, so `checks`, `advisory` and `isSubmittable` are computed and thrown away on every open. A grep across `android/app/src/main` for `readiness.checks`, `.advisory`, `.isSubmittable`, `DwSubmissionReadiness.summary(` and `.fieldName(` returns ZERO hits outside the declarations; every hit is in `test/`.

Meanwhile the exclusion the discarded check warns about IS honoured by the exporter: `applyReportSettings` filters sections by `excludedStages` (ReportTemplates.kt:631-637), and `reportWarnings` (ReportSettings.kt:568-605) covers only template substitution, PDF typefaces and unsupported annexures — it says nothing about exclusions. There is no sentence anywhere on the handset about a stage deliberately left out.

The second half is confirmed too: the template-substitution warning lands in `plan.warnings`, assigned to `exportNotes` at ReportScreen.kt:400 inside `export()`, and `exportNotes` is only rendered inside `result?.let { … }` (:594-610) — after the file has been written.

**How it fails.** Stage 20's `excludedStages` names stage 17 (Costing & market linkage), set on the web while trialling a buyer-facing copy and never cleared. A different designer opens the workshop on the handset: no warning on the stage index (the check was discarded), no warning on the report screen (`reportWarnings` has no exclusion arm), they export and hand the file over. Every cost sheet and the cost-by-head figure are absent, while the Data completeness annexure in the same file — `renderCompletenessAnnexure` walks `schema.stages` (ReportScreen.kt:1407) and knows nothing about the template's section list — scores stage 17 as complete.

**What it costs.** A stage's worth of captured work is silently removed from the submitted document while the document's own annexure certifies it complete, and the check written to catch exactly this runs on every stage-index open and is discarded. The same silence covers 'template-unknown': the substitution is announced only after the file exists.

**Remedy.** Two independent fixes. (1) Keep the whole `DwWorkshopReadiness` in `StageIndexScreen`'s state instead of only `addressBook(...)`, and render `readiness.checks` as a card between the progress line and the report button, each row navigating by `check.stageKey` plus the field named in `at(...)` (the `href` is inert on Android by design). (2) Run `reportChecks` in `ReportScreen`'s `LaunchedEffect` and append its `title` strings to `warnings` — which IS rendered before the buttons (:535-567) — so the sentence reaches the person about to hand the file over, unlike `exportNotes`. If `advisory` is deliberately unrendered, delete it rather than assembling it 22 times per screen open.

**What the verifier checked.** Read DwSubmissionReadiness.kt:420-660 and StageIndexScreen.kt:230-300, :375-400. Tried to refute by grepping all of android/app/src/main (plus test/ and androidTest/) for every field of the result type: only `addressBook` is called from main/. Tried to refute the 'nothing warns' half by reading `reportWarnings`, `UNSUPPORTED_SECTIONS` and `fieldCopyNote` (ReportSettings.kt:540-556) end to end — none mentions excluded stages. Confirmed `applyReportSettings` really drops the sections (ReportTemplates.kt:635). Confirmed the exportNotes timing by reading the composable body: `exportNotes` is drawn only under `result?.let`. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md; it neighbours the non-last-row-deletion entry but is a distinct surface.
