### [MAJOR] (android) The Cards & tags sheet prints QR tags for prototypes deleted on this phone, because `workshopCardRows` lacks the `emptiedEntities` filter `reportSourceFor` carries and none of the 8 collection-only stages can pass its "touched" test

**Verdict:** PARTIAL

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/DwWorkshopCards.kt:153-155 and :375-405; ui/designworkshop/ReportSource.kt:123-124 and :240, :257-258; ui/designworkshop/WorkshopCodesScreen.kt:157-200, :245; ui/designworkshop/StageScreen.kt:1330-1340

**What it does.** Quoted code exists verbatim. `workshopCardRows` (DwWorkshopCards.kt:153-155): `val touchedLocally = stored != null && (stored.values.isNotEmpty() || stored.rows.isNotEmpty())`, then falls back to `remoteStages[source.stage.key]?.collections?.get(source.entity.key).orEmpty()` (:159). `reportSourceFor` makes the same decision at ReportSource.kt:123-124 with a third term — `|| stored.custom.isNotEmpty()` — and `stageDraftFromRemote` filters the server's collections by `entityKey !in emptied` (:240, :257-258). The card path has neither.

The reachability condition checks out against the bundled registry: exactly EIGHT of the 22 stages declare no SINGLETON entity — EXISTING_PRODUCTS_BASELINE, SKETCH_DEVELOPMENT, SKETCH_REVIEW, PROTOTYPE_DEVELOPMENT, PROTOTYPE_ITERATION, PROTOTYPE_VALIDATION, FINAL_PROTOTYPE_DOCUMENTATION, COSTING_MARKET_LINKAGE — so `stored.values` is permanently empty for them, and PROTOTYPE_DEVELOPMENT (the stage `findPrototypeSource` resolves `DwPrototype` to) is one: its entities are `prototype`, `prototypeStageLog`, `materialUsage`, all COLLECTION. Deleting the last prototype row therefore makes `touchedLocally` false and the server's rows are read back. StageScreen.kt:1330-1340 confirms `emptiedEntities` is written on exactly that transition. `findWorkshopCodeInDraft` (:375-405) calls the same `workshopCardRows`, so scanning the stale tag confirms it as 'Prototype Development · row 1'.

WHERE THE FINDER IS WRONG. Their failure scenario defeats itself: they say 'the deletion has not synced because there is no signal', but WorkshopCodesScreen.kt:157-185 populates `remoteStages` only from a SUCCESSFUL `designWorkshopStages` read — with no signal `remoteStages` is empty and nothing stale prints. The defect needs the server read to succeed while the deletion has not reached the server, which is easy to arrive at another way (below) but is not the scenario as written. Separately, the `stored.custom.isNotEmpty()` half of the remedy is a consistency fix, not a defect: with no local rows for the entity, showing the server's rows is the correct card-sheet answer, and I could not construct a case where the missing `custom` term alone prints a wrong tag.

**How it fails.** A designer deletes the one prototype row on stage 13 on a handset that has never READ that stage from the server (the rows were entered here and pushed earlier). `buildStageBody` sets `replaceCollections = false` because the device is not authoritative, so the deletion is withheld and the server still holds the row. The designer walks into signal and opens Cards & tags: the stage read succeeds, `stored.values` is empty (stage 13 has no singleton) and `stored.rows` is now empty, so `touchedLocally` is false, the server's `prototype` rows are returned, and the sheet prints a QR tag for the deleted prototype. The tag is cut out and tied to an object; scanning it later is confirmed as a live row.

**What it costs.** Physical tags are printed and tied to objects for records the designer has deleted, and the scanner corroborates them, so the error leaves the room. It is the same class the report path closed at ReportSource.kt:257-258 and is one function short of closed here.

**Remedy.** Bring `workshopCardRows` in line with `reportSourceFor`, which both cite as the authority: when falling back to the server, drop the collection when `source.entity.key in stored?.emptiedEntities.orEmpty()`, returning an empty list — which the caller already renders as 'this workshop has no prototypes yet'. Pure and testable in `DwWorkshopCardsTest` with no device. Adding `|| stored.custom.isNotEmpty()` to `touchedLocally` is worth doing for consistency but closes nothing on its own. The durable fix is one shared `dwStageAuthority(draft, stageKey)` called by `reportSourceFor`, `workshopCardRows` and `StageIndexScreen`, since the rule is now spelled three times and has already drifted.

**What the verifier checked.** Read DwWorkshopCards.kt:100-260 and :340-406, ReportSource.kt in full, WorkshopCodesScreen.kt:150-200 and :245, StageScreen.kt:1295-1345. Parsed the bundled schema asset with a script: 8 stages with no SINGLETON entity, PROTOTYPE_DEVELOPMENT among them with collections [prototype, prototypeStageLog, materialUsage] — the finder's registry claims check out exactly. Tried to refute via: (1) `remoteStages` being empty on the no-signal path — it is, which kills the finder's stated scenario but not the defect (restated above using a withheld deletion under signal); (2) `codeRow()`/`workshopCardSpecs` filtering deleted rows — neither has any notion of deletion; (3) `emptiedEntities` never being written — StageScreen.kt:1330-1340 writes it on the last-row transition. Distinct from the OPEN_FINDINGS entry on non-last-row deletions: that is about a deletion never RECORDED; this is a recorded deletion one reader ignores.

### [MAJOR] (android) `WorkshopDraft.ownerUserId` is written once and read by nothing, so a shared handset shows one designer another's fieldwork and syncs it into the wrong account

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/WorkshopDraftStore.kt:453-454 (declaration + KDoc); android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/WorkshopListScreen.kt:770 (the only writer), :226-239 (unfiltered local rows); android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/WorkshopSync.kt:957 (syncAll over every draft); android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/WorkshopRepository.kt:1154-1156 (logout)

**What it does.** A grep over `android/app/src` returns exactly two hits for `ownerUserId`: the declaration at WorkshopDraftStore.kt:454 ("The account that owns this draft, so a shared field handset never shows one designer another's work") and the assignment at WorkshopListScreen.kt:770. There is no reader. `WorkshopDraftStore.list` (:895-931) enumerates every directory under `filesDir/workshops` unconditionally; the list screen renders every local-only draft it gets back (:226-239, filtered only by the search string); `WorkshopSyncEngine.syncAll` iterates the same list and pushes each workshop with whatever token the repository currently holds (:957 → `syncOneWorkshop` → `createDesignWorkshop` at :1116). `repository.logout()` clears only the token store (WorkshopRepository.kt:1154-1156) — drafts and media stay on disk — and it is reached from the sign-out button (MainActivity.kt:895) and from a 401/403 on `refreshUser` (:770, :776).

**How it fails.** Two designers share one field handset, the case the field's own KDoc is written for. A captures a fortnight of workshops with no signal: `local-…` drafts, `remoteId` null, `ownerUserId = A`. A signs out (or their token expires). B signs in. The workshop list draws A's drafts as B's own rows, with the titles, crafts, clusters and every artisan answer behind them. MainActivity's `LaunchedEffect(user)` (:807-814) immediately calls `syncOutbox`, which tails into `syncAll`, which creates each of A's workshops on the server under B's credentials and writes the returned `remoteId` back into A's drafts.

**What it costs.** Research data about named artisans is displayed to an account that was never granted it, and A's fortnight is filed on the server as B's: `createdById` is B, the record appears in B's list, and A must be granted access to their own work. It is the one place in the app where a permission boundary is written to disk and then never enforced.

**Remedy.** Read the field. (1) Filter in `WorkshopDraftStore.list`, or at each call site, to drafts whose `ownerUserId` is null (pre-dating the field) or equals `repository.cachedUser()?.id`. (2) In `syncAll`, skip a draft whose non-null `ownerUserId` does not match the signed-in account and say so on the status line, so "cannot sync, its owner is not signed in" is not confused with "up to date". (3) Stamp `ownerUserId` on drafts created by every other path — a workshop opened from the server first writes its draft from the stage screen, which never sets it — or the filter will hide those from everybody.

**What the verifier checked.** Grepped the whole module for `ownerUserId` (two hits, both above), then went looking specifically for the guard that would kill this: (a) does logout wipe drafts? No — `logout()` is one line, `tokenStore.clear()`. (b) Does sign-in reset local state? Read MainActivity.kt:838-899; nothing touches `filesDir/workshops`. (c) Does the list screen filter by account anywhere else? Read :200-250; the only filter on `localOnly` is the search string. (d) Would B's push actually land? For local-only drafts yes — `syncOneWorkshop` creates them under the current token (:1111-1126). Only two things narrow it: the handset must be shared between accounts, and drafts already bound to A's server records will merely fail with a refusal under B. Severity kept at MAJOR.

---

### [MAJOR] (android) A queued record the server refused for good is never retried, listed on no screen, and counted forever in the "uploading when you're online" banner

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/WorkshopRepository.kt:2950 (the skip), :2956-2972 (markFailure + one-shot toast), :3261 (`outboxFailures`, no caller), :2847 (`pendingUploads`); android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/WorkshopSync.kt:634-638 (`blocksRetry`); android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/Offline.kt:227-228 (`count`), :285-286 (`failed`); android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/MainActivity.kt:901-919 (the banner)

**What it does.** `blocksRetry(permanent, skewRun)` returns true whenever `permanent` and `skewRun` is null or blank (WorkshopSync.kt:634-638), so a 4xx that is not a schema skew blocks the entry on every future pass at WorkshopRepository.kt:2950. The only telling is the toast at :2970-2972, fired once, on the pass that first records the reason. `outboxFailures` (:3261) — which the KDoc at :3252 calls the durable half "readable through [outboxFailures] by whatever screen shows it next" — has no caller anywhere in `android/`; a grep finds the declaration and one comment. Meanwhile `pendingUploads` is `OfflineOutbox.count(context)` (:2847) and `count` is `read(context).size` (Offline.kt:227-228), i.e. it includes failed entries, so MainActivity.kt:901-919 keeps drawing a CLOUD-OFF icon and "$n entries saved on this device — uploading when you're online" for as long as the entry exists.

**How it fails.** A designer queues an artisan offline whose Aadhaar number the server already holds. On the first pass with signal the create returns 409; `replayEntry` yields `Rejected`, `markFailure` writes the reason, and a toast shows for a few seconds — typically with the phone in a pocket. From then on: `blocksRetry` skips the entry on every pass produced by the 12/45-second timer (MainActivity.kt:807-814) and the connectivity callback (:815-828); no screen lists it; and the banner reads "1 entry saved on this device — uploading when you're online" for the rest of the study. The record and its staged media sit in `filesDir/outbox` and are never mentioned again.

**What it costs.** A record the designer believes is queued will never upload, and the one persistent surface about it says the opposite every time they look. Recoverable only by an operator with adb. The design-workshop half of the app has `WorkshopSyncStatus.problems` and a retry button for exactly this class; the records outbox one banner along has neither.

**Remedy.** (1) Split the count: `pendingUploads` should exclude entries with a non-null `failure`, and the failures get their own line — "$n could not be uploaded — tap to see why" — leaving the cloud-off icon for entries genuinely waiting on a connection. (2) Give `outboxFailures` its reader: a list of label, `failedAt` and the server's reason with a per-entry "Try again" that clears `failure`/`failedAt` so the next pass re-attempts, the same shape as `WorkshopSyncEngine.retryWorkshop`.

**What the verifier checked.** Verified each quoted line in place. Refutation attempts: (a) is there any other reader of the failures? Grepped `OfflineOutbox.` across the module — the only consumers are enqueue/all/remove/markFailure/update/takeAlert/count inside WorkshopRepository; `failed()` is reached only by the uncalled `outboxFailures`. (b) Does `takeAlert` cover it? No — Offline.kt raises an alert only for a quarantined queue file (:130, :144-175), not for a refusal. (c) Does the app-run reset free it? `blocksRetry` frees only entries carrying a `skewRun`, i.e. `extra_forbidden`-style API skew; an ordinary 4xx has `skewRun = null` and is blocked permanently. (d) Is there a records-outbox screen elsewhere? SharingBatch.kt:151 only quotes markFailure's rule in a comment. Severity MAJOR confirmed: normal-use trigger, permanent, and the UI states the opposite.

---

### [MAJOR] (android) A truncated reference list is cached without the fact that it was truncated, so every later offline open of the picker presents a 50-row slice as the complete list

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED · found by the verifier, not the original audit

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/WorkshopRepository.kt:528 and :555; android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/DwReferenceStore.kt:108-116 (`DwReferenceList`) and :318-343 (`store`); android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/WorkshopRepositoryApi.kt:847-853; android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/DwReferenceField.kt:218, 283, 462, 649-657

**What it does.** `DwReferenceList` — the class that IS the file on disk — carries `model`, `filteredBy`, `items` and `fetchedAt` and no truncation flag; `store` stamps only `fetchedAt` (`val stamped = list.copy(fetchedAt = Instant.now().toString())`, DwReferenceStore.kt:326). Truncation is known only transiently: the repository passes `fetched.truncated` on the network answer (line 555) but hard-codes `onList(it, false)` for the cache answer (line 528), and `DwReferenceField` holds it in a `var truncated by remember(field.key) { mutableStateOf(false) }` (line 218/501). The Retrofit method also sends no `limit`, so every fetch takes the server default of 50 (`REFERENCE_LIMIT_DEFAULT = 50`, backend/app/services/design_workshops.py:202) even though the endpoint accepts up to 200 (`REFERENCE_LIMIT_MAX = 200`) — so truncation is the normal case for the artisan and product registers, not an edge.

**How it fails.** A designer opens stage 6 with signal. The server sends 50 of the cluster's artisans with `truncated: true`; the red notice at DwReferenceField.kt:649-656 appears and the 50 rows are written to disk. Two days later, in a village with no signal, they open the same field. `designWorkshopReferences` serves the cached list through `onList(it, false)` at line 528, the network branch returns early at line 542/553, and `truncated` stays false. The provenance line reads "50 records on this device, last refreshed 2 days ago." with no red notice — a statement whose only reasonable reading is that the device holds the whole list.

**What it costs.** This is precisely the conclusion the code says the flag exists to prevent: `DwReferenceResponseDto.truncated`'s own KDoc (DwReferenceStore.kt:160-165) says a truncated list "looks exactly like a complete one to the person scrolling it, and the conclusion they draw — 'this artisan is not in the system, I will type the name in' — is the conclusion that destroys the join key." Offline, that warning is never shown, so the designer types the name by hand or creates a duplicate artisan record, and the row loses the foreign key that research and the report's before/after linkage depend on.

**Remedy.** Add `val truncated: Boolean = false` to `DwReferenceList`, set it in `WorkshopRepository` from `fetched.truncated` when storing, and pass `cached.truncated` instead of the literal `false` at WorkshopRepository.kt:528. Independently, send `limit = REFERENCE_LIMIT_MAX` from the handset — the picker's stated design (DwReferenceStore.kt:31-37, "the picker fetches the WHOLE list for a (model, filter) pair so it can be cached and searched with no signal at all") is not achievable while every request silently takes the 50-row default.

### [MAJOR] (android) Every media id the on-device report cannot resolve to a local file vanishes with no counter, no screen notice and no line in the document — and `ReportSource`'s own KDoc claims the opposite

**Verdict:** PARTIAL

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/ReportScreen.kt:832, :844-861, :1728, :1888, :1369, :611-619; ReportSource.kt:213-222, :233-266; report/DocxWriter.kt:462,516,525,1352; report/PdfWriter.kt:108,567,682

**What it does.** Verified line by line. `buildWorkshopDocument` builds exactly one index — `val mediaById = draft?.media.orEmpty().associateBy { it.id }` (ReportScreen.kt:832) — and `imageFor` (:844-861) returns null for any token that is not a LOCAL `DraftMedia.id` with bytes on disk. Both consumers discard a null without recording it: `ids.mapNotNull(imageFor)` at :1888 (renderEntity) and the identical line at :1728 (`imagesOf`, used by GALLERY presentation and by the media annexure). `renderMediaAnnexure` then does `if (gathered.isEmpty()) return` (:1369), so the whole 'Photographic record' section — heading and all — disappears from the file and from the contents.

The silence is real; I could not find a counter anywhere. `ReportExport.Result.droppedImages` is appended to ONLY by the writers, and only for an already-constructed `ImageRef` whose bytes fail to load (DocxWriter.kt:516, :525, :1352; PdfWriter.kt:567, :682, :1165). An id that never became an `ImageRef` never reaches a writer, so the screen's notice at ReportScreen.kt:611-619 cannot fire for it.

Reachability is confirmed and ordinary: `stageDraftFromRemote` (ReportSource.kt:233-266) copies `bucket.singleton` and `bucket.collections` verbatim into the draft the document is built from, and those objects carry the SERVER's `MediaFile` ids. `mediaIds = existing?.mediaIds.orEmpty()` — nothing is invented and nothing is downloaded. `WorkshopSync.kt:2006-2013` independently confirms the id space: 'A token that is not one of this device's descriptors is passed through UNCHANGED … it is a server id already — a stage this handset seeded from the server'.

WHERE THE FINDER IS WRONG. (a) This is not data loss. The bytes are on the server and the office's copy prints them; the phone does not hold them and could not embed them offline. The defect is the SILENCE, not the drop — 'CRITICAL / data-loss' overstates it and MAJOR is the honest grade. (b) Remedy (1), a `remoteMediaId` reverse index, is close to inert: a photograph this device uploaded is still referenced in this device's own draft by its LOCAL id (`swapMediaRefs` rewrites the wire payload, not the stored draft), so the fallback would fire only on the narrow case of media captured here and attached to a stage elsewhere. The load-bearing half is remedy (2). (c) The `releaseUploadedMedia` case is a documented, user-initiated trade — WorkshopDraftStore.kt:1197-1214 names this exact outcome in as many words — though it too is uncounted.

The finder is right about the strongest evidence: ReportSource.kt:216-222 states 'a photograph whose bytes are missing costs the report one picture and one warning, never the export', and there is no warning on any surface.

**How it fails.** A designer opens a workshop a colleague captured, on a handset that has never opened its stages. `reportSourceFor` fills them from `designWorkshopStages`, `builtFromLine` reads 'Built from the 19 stage(s) downloaded from the server just now', `serverCopyUnread` is FALSE (the read succeeded) so `fieldCopyNote` says nothing, and the .docx comes out with every table and every paragraph and not one photograph. The 'Photographic record' annexure is absent entirely, `droppedImages` is empty so the screen prints no note, and the `warnings` posted to `recordDesignWorkshopExport` say nothing about it.

**What it costs.** The submitted document is short in a way no surface admits: the export screen's dropped-photo count, the cover provenance line and the office's export log all report the file as complete. It is the same 'valid-looking short report' class `ReportSource` exists to end, reached through the media id space instead of the stage list.

**Remedy.** Make the residue visible — that is the part that closes the defect. Wrap `imageFor` at the ReportScreen.kt:844 call site in a `(String) -> ImageRef?` that appends unresolved tokens to a `MutableList`, and (i) surface `unresolvedImages.size` beside `droppedImages` on the export screen, (ii) add the count to `fieldCopyNote` in the same slot as the `serverCopyUnread` sentence, since the screen notice is gone by the time the file is read, and (iii) in `renderMediaAnnexure`, when `gathered.isEmpty()` but the walk saw media ids, emit the heading and a NOTE rather than deleting the section from the contents. Correct the ReportSource KDoc at :216-222 in the same commit — it promises a warning that does not exist. A `remoteMediaId` fallback index is worth adding but recovers far less than the finder claims.

**What the verifier checked.** Read ReportScreen.kt:780-980, 1180-1470, 1710-1748, 1850-1931; ReportSource.kt in full; WorkshopSync.kt:1930-2020; WorkshopDraftStore.kt:1179-1247. Grepped `droppedImages` across all of android/ — every append is in DocxWriter/PdfWriter and keyed on an `ImageRef.source` that failed to load, confirming nothing counts an id that never resolved. Tried to refute via: (1) a media download on the report screen — ReportScreen.kt:229-364 fetches schema, stages, questionnaires and the custom definition, no media; (2) `stageDraftFromRemote` synthesising local descriptors — it explicitly does not, and its KDoc says fabricating them would be wrong; (3) `serverCopyUnread` covering it — it is false whenever the server ANSWERED, which is exactly the case that drops the photographs. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md. Downgraded from CRITICAL because nothing is destroyed and the office's copy is whole.

---

### [MAJOR] (android) Every RICH_TEXT narrative and bulleted answer prints with no field label on the handset, where the server prints each one under its own heading — 98 of 98 such fields in the bundled registry

**Verdict:** PARTIAL

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/ReportScreen.kt:1910-1920 (and its callers at :1055, :1103, :1984, :1993, :2017) against backend/app/services/report_builder.py:956-1011

**What it does.** Quoted code exists verbatim. ReportScreen.kt:1910-1913 is `if (type == DwFieldType.RICH_TEXT) { toReportBlocks(stored, ParaStyle.BODY, imageFor).forEach(builder::add); return@forEach }` — the blocks are emitted and the field's label is never written. The server's `_render_narrative` emits `self.doc.heading(spec.label, min(4, level + 1), numbered=…)` when `len(text) > 160 or len(blocks) > 1` and `self.doc.para(f"{spec.label}:")` otherwise (report_builder.py:963-971), and for BULLETS emits the heading unconditionally before the list (:986-987).

`renderEntity` is confirmed to be the single writer on the handset for every presentation that reaches a field — the stage singleton (:1055), the custom-section synthetic singleton (:1103), CARDS (:1984), NARRATIVE/KEY_VALUE (:1993) and the table's per-row leftovers (:2017) — mirroring `_render_narrative`'s position on the server.

Driving the bundled registry (android/app/src/main/assets/design-workshop-schema.json, 22 stages) reproduces the finder's count exactly: 81 live fields carry `reportRole=NARRATIVE`, 17 carry `BULLETS`, and ALL 98 are `type=RICH_TEXT`. The `"NARRATIVE"` and `"BULLETS"` arms at :1916-1917 are therefore dead for the current registry, and every one of the 98 takes the unlabelled pre-dispatch branch.

WHERE THE FINDER IS WRONG. The stage-2 example is misstated: `INTRODUCTORY_ADMIN_DOCUMENTATION.introduction` declares SIX NARRATIVE fields (acknowledgement, briefIntroduction, purpose, projectBackground, agencyDetails, institutionalText) and TWO BULLETS (programmeObjectives, expectedDeliverables) — eight fields, not eight NARRATIVE. And CRITICAL overstates it: no answer is lost, every paragraph prints, the order of the prose is preserved. What differs is the heading structure, and with it the contents page and the section numbering.

**How it fails.** A designer fills in all eight of stage 2's rich-text answers and exports on the handset. The .docx prints one level-1 heading, 'Introductory & Administrative Documentation', then eight consecutive unlabelled runs of prose with nothing separating one answer from the next. The office's copy prints eight numbered sub-headings, one per question, which also appear in its contents page. The same happens on every prototype card, where processSummary, problemsAndModifications and audioTranscript merge into one anonymous block per record.

**What it costs.** An officer reading the handset's copy cannot tell which pro-forma question any paragraph answers, and the two copies of one workshop carry different heading trees, different contents pages and different section numbering — the state the file's own comments call 'reads as a designer having altered the document'. Nothing in the file or on the screen says the labels were dropped.

**Remedy.** Port the label emission into the RICH_TEXT branch and the two role arms, mirroring `_render_narrative`: compute `blocks` first, then emit `builder.heading(field.label, level = minOf(4, level + 1), numbered = options.numbered)` when `toPlain(stored).length > 160 || blocks.size > 1` and `builder.para("${field.label}:")` otherwise, then the blocks; for BULLETS emit the heading unconditionally. `renderEntity` needs the caller's heading level threaded in (1 for a stage singleton, `rowLevel` inside a card) — add it to `RenderOptions` with a default of 1 so no existing call site moves. Pin with a test asserting the .docx contains the eight stage-2 labels; the internal `buildWorkshopDocument` overload already exists to make that assertable off-device.

**What the verifier checked.** Read ReportScreen.kt:977-1214 (renderStageSection), :1850-1931 (renderEntity), :1946-2027 (renderCollection) and report_builder.py:840-1115. Tried to refute by looking for a label emitted by a CALLER — renderStageSection emits only section/entity/custom-section headings (:1045, :1094, :1137, :1148) and renderCollection only `rowHeading` per record (:1983, :2016); no per-field heading exists anywhere on the handset. Also checked whether `toReportBlocks` might carry the label into its first block: it converts the stored rich-text document only. Verified the registry counts with a script over the bundled schema asset (81 NARRATIVE + 17 BULLETS, 98/98 RICH_TEXT, zero non-rich in either role). Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md. Downgraded CRITICAL→MAJOR: presentation diverges, no data is lost or wrong.

---

### [MAJOR] (android) A required field that was never filled in prints "Not recorded." in the office's copy and prints nothing at all in the handset's

**Verdict:** PARTIAL

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/ReportScreen.kt:1875-1877 and :2063-2071 (`cellRuns`) against backend/app/services/report_builder.py:848-855 and :1014-1030

**What it does.** Both quotes are accurate. Android: `printable.forEach { field -> val stored = values[field.key]; if (!DwValues.isFilled(stored)) return@forEach` (ReportScreen.kt:1875-1877) drops required and optional fields alike. Server `_printable` (:848-855) appends `(spec, "Not recorded.")` when `spec.required and self.template.show_empty_note`, and both `_render_narrative` and `_cell_runs` deliberately carry that substitute through — `_cell_runs`'s docstring says routing an empty rich value through `plain_runs` 'would replace the note with a blank cell — turning a visible gap into an invisible one'. Android's `cellRuns` (:2063-2071) has no equivalent, so the divergence is in the TABLE path too.

Android's only empty note is per-SECTION: `if (!wrote && template.showEmptyNote) builder.para("Not recorded.", …)` at :1213, which fires only when an entire stage produced nothing — the analogue of report_builder.py:1487, not of :851.

WHERE THE FINDER IS WRONG. 'true for five of the six templates' is false. `show_empty_note: bool = True` (report_templates.py:176) and `showEmptyNote: Boolean = true` (ReportTemplates.kt:161) are the only occurrences on either side — grepping all of backend/app and android/app/src/main finds no template overriding it. It is true for ALL six templates on both surfaces, which makes the divergence wider than claimed, not narrower.

**How it fails.** A workshop is exported on the handset with stage 13 partly captured — prototype rows present, a required field left blank on several of them. The office's copy prints 'Materials: Not recorded.' once per affected row so the reviewing officer can see which; the handset's copy prints nothing for them, and then the same file's Data completeness annexure (`renderCompletenessAnnexure`, ReportScreen.kt:1397, which walks `schema.stages` and scores the same draft) prints the shortfall in its Outstanding column.

**What it costs.** The handset's report understates incompleteness in its body while its own annexure states it — the 'one document, two arithmetics' failure `maskUnresolvableRefs` (ReportScreen.kt:1452-1471) was written to prevent, reached through the other door. An officer matching the phone's copy against the office's sees lines present in one and absent from the other with no explanation. It fires whenever a required field is unfilled, which is the normal mid-workshop state.

**Remedy.** Port the substitution into `renderEntity`: replace `if (!DwValues.isFilled(stored)) return@forEach` with a computed `text` that falls through carrying "Not recorded." when `field.required && template.showEmptyNote`, keeping media-typed fields exempt (the server's `_images` never substitutes, and `_printable` is only asked for non-media roles). `renderEntity` needs `showEmptyNote` threaded through `RenderOptions` beside `maxTier`, defaulted true. Do `cellRuns` (:2063) in the same change or a required blank stays invisible in TABLE presentation, exactly as `_cell_runs` warns.

**What the verifier checked.** Read ReportScreen.kt:1858-1931 and :2053-2071; report_builder.py:840-856, 921-1030. Tried to refute by finding a template that turns the note off — `grep -rn show_empty_note backend/app` and `grep -rn showEmptyNote android/app/src/main` return only the two declarations and the two use sites, so no template on either side sets it false; the finder's 'five of six' is wrong in the direction that makes this worse. Also checked whether Android's section-level note at :1213 could substitute — it fires on `!wrote` for the whole stage, so a stage with one filled field and six blank required ones prints nothing for the six. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

### [MAJOR] (android) After the server acknowledges a collection sweep, the stage screen writes the acknowledged `emptiedEntities` key straight back to disk, so every later save of that stage re-asserts the sweep

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/StageScreen.kt:328 (declaration), :696 (the only seed), :774 (persistLocally call), :2135-2137 (the union); android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/WorkshopSync.kt:2282-2326 (recordStageSent's scoped clear), :1833-1842 (what buildStageBody sends); backend/app/services/design_workshops.py:1184 (the sweep)

**What it does.** `var emptied by remember(stageKey) { mutableStateOf<Set<String>>(emptySet()) }` (StageScreen.kt:328) is seeded from disk exactly once, at load — `emptied = local?.emptiedEntities.orEmpty().toSet()` (:696) — and thereafter changed only by `onRowsChange` (:1339-1342). `persistLocally` rebuilds the whole `StageDraft` and writes `emptiedEntities = (emptied + existing?.emptiedEntities.orEmpty()).filter { key -> state.collections[key].orEmpty().isEmpty() }.distinct()` (:2135-2137). `recordStageSent` deliberately removes exactly the keys the acknowledged body carried — `val remaining = stage.emptiedEntities.filterNot { it in acknowledgedDeletions }` (WorkshopSync.kt:2308) — but nothing tells the composition, and the screen's copy is still authoritative for the next write. The order inside `saveAndSync` makes this永 repeatable: persistLocally writes the key, pushStage sends it, recordStageSent clears disk, the next edit's persistLocally puts it back from RAM. `buildStageBody` then sends it again on the next differing payload (`emptiedEntities = if (authoritative) emptied else emptyList()`, WorkshopSync.kt:1842), and the server sweeps `(touched_entities | set(payload.emptiedEntities)) & collection_keys` (design_workshops.py:1184). Because the key is written back to disk it also outlives the composition: the next open re-seeds `emptied` from it at :696.

**How it fails.** Stage 17 has been read on this handset, so `stageSeen` is true and every save claims `replaceCollections`. The designer deletes the last `costMaterialLine` row; `emptied = {"costMaterialLine"}`. The save lands, the server sweeps, `recordStageSent` clears `costMaterialLine` from the draft. The stage stays open (or is reopened) and the designer types one character into any other field. `persistLocally` writes `emptiedEntities = ["costMaterialLine"]` back to disk and the push carries the sweep a second time. Meanwhile the office re-enters two material lines on the web. The phone never re-reads a stage it has already seen (StageScreen.kt:540 takes the `holdsWork && local.stageSeen` branch and makes no request), so without the stale key those two rows would survive indefinitely — with it, the very next debounced auto-save names no `costMaterialLine` rows, asserts the sweep, and the server soft-deletes both, under an HTTP 200 the screen reports as synced.

**What it costs.** Rows entered on another surface, in a collection this handset once emptied, are silently soft-deleted by an auto-save the designer never asked for, and repeatedly for as long as the key survives in RAM or on disk. It undoes in memory exactly what `recordStageSent`'s scoped clear achieves on disk ("Only the keys this body named are cleared, and anything the designer emptied since is kept for the next save", WorkshopSync.kt:2276-2278). The sibling list is not affected in the same way: `deletedRowKeys` resurrects identically but is never sent on the wire and `statusOf` only counts it behind `!isAuthoritative` (WorkshopSync.kt:829), so no harm follows there.

**Remedy.** Refresh the screen's copy from the authoritative source after a push instead of unioning a stale snapshot. In `saveAndSync`, in the `is StagePush.Sent` arm (StageScreen.kt:802), re-read it: `emptied = WorkshopDraftStore.load(appContext, workshopId)?.stages?.get(spec.key)?.emptiedEntities.orEmpty().toSet()` (and the same for `deletedRows`, so the two stay in step). `pending.record` captures `emptied` too (:987), so the refresh must happen before the next `edit`. The alternative — tracking only what this session emptied since the last acknowledged push and letting the draft's own list be the base — is the same fix with more state to keep honest.

**What the verifier checked.** I read all four cited sites in the working tree; the finder's line numbers are 10-15 lines stale and their `recordStageSent` quote predates the `deletedRowKeys`/`sweptEntities` half now at WorkshopSync.kt:2299-2326, but the quoted logic exists verbatim. I tried four refutations. (1) Does the `.filter { state.collections[key].orEmpty().isEmpty() }` guard drop the key? No — the collection is still empty in the form, so it is kept. (2) Is the second send suppressed by the payload signature? No — `pushStages`/`pushStage` skip only an identical payload, and any edit changes it. (3) Would the phone sweep those rows anyway? No — with no local rows the entity is absent from `touched_entities`, so without the stale key `sweep_entities` excludes it (design_workshops.py:1181-1184) and the office's rows survive. (4) Does an intervening read fix it? No — StageScreen.kt:540 takes the no-request branch for a stage already `stageSeen`, so the fold never runs again and never clears the key. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md: the recorded android item there is the *non-last-row* deletion having no record (now implemented as `deletedRowKeys`), which is a different defect.

### [MAJOR] (cross-surface) The handset cannot unlink a record from a workshop at all: the deliberate null the picker produces is dropped by the JSON encoder before it reaches the wire

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED · found by the verifier, not the original audit

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/ApiClient.kt:54 (`explicitNulls = false` in the Json handed to `asConverterFactory` at :119); the nullable fields it silences at android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/ApiModels.kt:602 (`ArtisanCreateRequest.workshopId`), :713 (`ToolCreateRequest.workshopId`), :1220-1222 (`ProcessCreateRequest.workshopId`) and the craft/product equivalents; the PATCH bodies at android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/WorkshopRepositoryApi.kt:157,169,185,199 and :393 (every update reuses the Create request); the picker at android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/MainActivity.kt:4330 (`fun value(): String? = selectedId.ifBlank { null }`, documented "null when the record is deliberately unlinked") and the blank option at android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/SearchableSelect.kt:240-245 (`onClick = { onSelect(""); … }`), reached because `WorkshopDropdown` (MainActivity.kt:4583-4599) leaves `includeNone` at its default of true; the server contract this defeats at backend/app/services/records.py:244-268 (`CLEARABLE_KEYS`).

**What it does.** Retrofit's converter is built from `Json { … explicitNulls = false … }`. With `explicitNulls = false`, a property whose value is null is OMITTED from the serialised body rather than written as `"workshopId": null`. Every Android record update reuses the Create request as its PATCH body — `updateArtisan`, `updateCraft`, `updateProduct`, `updateTool` and `updateProcess` all take the `*CreateRequest` type — and on each of them `workshopId` is `String? = null`. The server reads those PATCHes with `payload.model_dump(exclude_unset=True)`, so an omitted key means "leave the stored value alone", while an explicit null is the ONE thing `CLEARABLE_KEYS` was added to honour: records.py:244-249 states in a comment that `{"workshopId": None}` means unlink, that stripping it "made unlinking a silent no-op — the save returned 200, the form showed 'Unlinked', and the old link survived in the database". The handset cannot express that null. This is the same shape as the `replaceCollections` blocker already closed in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md: a Kotlin value that is correct on the object and absent from the bytes. Note the model's authors were aware of the encoder — `preProcessAvailable` and `status` carry `@EncodeDefault` at ApiModels.kt:1208 and :1218 with a comment about exactly this class of drop — but `@EncodeDefault` does not help a null under `explicitNulls = false`, and the nullable relation fields carry no annotation at all.

**How it fails.** A designer on the phone files an artisan (or craft, product, tool, process) against the wrong workshop, re-opens the record, opens the Workshop dropdown and taps the blank "Unlinked" row. `SearchableSelectField` calls `onSelect("")`, `WorkshopPickerState.selectedId` becomes `""`, and `value()` returns null. The PATCH body is serialised with the `workshopId` key absent. FastAPI's `exclude_unset=True` never sees the field, `clean_data` never gets a chance to keep it, and the column is not touched. HTTP 200; the form closes; the record is still in the workshop. The same request also leaves the `WorkshopArtisan`/`WorkshopCraft` mirror row, so nothing about the link changes anywhere.

**What it costs.** Unlinking a record from a workshop is a total silent no-op on the handset — the surface where most field records are filed and most misfilings happen — and it reports success. The record keeps appearing in that workshop's lists, its ZIP export and its report annexure, and the only remedy is to re-do it in a browser. The same omission applies to the other nullable relation ids on those bodies (`craftId`, `artisanId`, `productId`), so no relation can be cleared from the phone.

**Remedy.** Do not turn `explicitNulls` on globally — it would start writing nulls for every unset optional on every body and change the meaning of dozens of PATCH fields from "leave alone" to "clear". Instead give the clearable relation ids a three-state representation that survives the encoder: either annotate them so a deliberate clear is encoded (a `JsonElement`/`JsonNull` field, or a dedicated `*Update` request type built with a Json that has `explicitNulls = true` for exactly those keys), or add an explicit `clearWorkshop: Boolean` to the update bodies and honour it server-side. Whichever is chosen, pin it with a test that asserts on the SERIALISED JSON — the way `StageSweepReachesTheWireTest` was written for the `replaceCollections` blocker — because the Kotlin object has been right all along and only the bytes are wrong.

---

### [MAJOR] (cross-surface) Deleting a process step, or a whole process, orphans its photographs: the link is an untyped string tag with no foreign key and no cleanup

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** backend/app/api/routes/processes.py:124-126 (`_sync_steps`'s `delete_many`), :256-260 (`delete_process`) and :63-66 (the only join, `where={"linkedRecordId": {"in": lookup_ids}}`); backend/app/services/records.py:875-889 (`media_relation_data`'s `field_map`, which has no "process" or "processstep" key); backend/prisma/schema.prisma:784-827 (MediaFile: FKs for artisan/craft/workshop/product/tool/questionnaireInterview, none for process or step) and :1222-1235 (ProcessStep, no media relation); the upload side at frontend/components/forms/ProcessForm.tsx:567-620 (`linkedRecordType: "process"` / `"processstep"`) and the step removal at ProcessForm.tsx:808.

**What it does.** Process and step media are attached ONLY through the generic `linkedRecordType`/`linkedRecordId` string pair. I read `media_relation_data`'s `field_map` in full: it maps artisan, craft, workshop, product, tool, questionnaire and questionnaireinterview, and returns `{}` for anything else — so a process or step upload writes no typed foreign key, and Postgres has no relation to cascade or SetNull. `_sync_steps` deletes removed steps at :124-126 and `db.process.delete` at :260 cascades to `ProcessStep` (schema.prisma:1232, `onDelete: Cascade`); neither deletes, re-tags, or even counts the `MediaFile` rows whose `linkedRecordId` names them. `_hydrate` then cannot find them, because their id is no longer in `lookup_ids`. On the client, removing a step is a bare array filter (`setSteps((current) => current.filter((s) => s.key !== step.key))`) with no media call, so the PATCH simply omits that step's id and the server deletes the row underneath the photographs. Note `_sync_steps`'s own docstring says it preserves step ids "so their linked media survives edits" — the code knows the relationship exists and handles only the surviving half of it.

**How it fails.** A designer photographs six steps of a pit-loom process, then removes a duplicated step from the form and saves. The PATCH omits that step's id; `_sync_steps` deletes the `ProcessStep`; the two photographs keep `linkedRecordType='processstep'` and a `linkedRecordId` that now names nothing. They vanish from the process detail, from the process edit form and from the export tree, while remaining in `GET /media`, in the search media bucket and in object storage, with nothing recording what they were of. The same happens wholesale on `DELETE /processes/{id}`, where a fifteen-step process leaves every step photograph dangling — and on `DELETE /products/{id}`, since `Process.productId` is `onDelete: Cascade` (schema.prisma:1210) and takes the steps with it.

**What it costs.** Field photographs become permanently unreachable from the record they document and unattributable to anything, from an ordinary edit, with no warning and no undo. They still occupy storage and still appear in the media list as untitled orphans, so the corpus accumulates files nobody can place. Combined with the unguarded step rewrite above, one unauthorized request can orphan a whole process's photography.

**Remedy.** Give the deletion a defined outcome. Minimum, in `_sync_steps` before the `delete_many`: `await db.mediafile.update_many(where={"linkedRecordType": "processstep", "linkedRecordId": {"in": removed}}, data={"linkedRecordType": "process", "linkedRecordId": process_id})`, so the photographs survive under the parent process; and the same in `delete_process`, re-tagging to the parent product. Properly: add a `processId`/`processStepId` FK to `MediaFile` and extend `media_relation_data`'s `field_map`, which is what gives Postgres a cascade rule and collapses the two half-mechanisms into one.

**What the verifier checked.** Looked for the cleanup that would kill this. Grepped the schema for any MediaFile relation to Process/ProcessStep — there is none; the six FKs present all carry SetNull. Read `media_relation_data` in full and confirmed the two keys are absent, so `{}` is returned and no typed column is written for these uploads. Read `delete_process` in full: `assert_can_delete` (admin-only), `require_record`, `db.process.delete` — three lines, no media handling. Read `_sync_steps` end to end: no MediaFile statement anywhere. Checked the client: `ProcessForm.tsx:808` is a plain filter with no `/media` call, and the step's `SavedMediaList` delete button is a separate deliberate action the user need not take. Checked that step ids ARE round-tripped so this only fires on a genuine removal (ProcessForm.tsx:490 sends `id: step.serverId`; Android's `ProcessStepRequest.id` at ApiModels.kt:1189 likewise) — so the defect is a real removal, not an every-save churn. Kept MAJOR rather than higher: the bytes survive and an operator with database access can still find the rows; what is destroyed is the association, irreversibly, through the UI. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

---

### [MAJOR] (cross-surface) The Artisans list drops the funnel's workshopId and intersects one server page of 20 client-side, on a comment claiming the endpoint has no workshopId — it has one, and a broader one than the client's

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** frontend/app/(protected)/artisans/page.tsx:51-57, :122-127, :265; frontend/components/FunnelFilters.tsx:61-85; backend/app/api/routes/artisans.py:198 and :238-244

**What it does.** The quoted code is exactly as reported. `artisans/page.tsx:51` carries the comment "/artisans supports craftId but not workshopId — the workshop filter narrows rows client-side below", and the request that follows sends only `search`, `craftId`, `page` and `pageSize`. The claim is false: `list_artisans` declares `workshopId: str | None = None` at artisans.py:198 and applies it at :238-244 as an AND-composed `OR` over both `Artisan.workshopId` and the `WorkshopArtisan` join. The server filter is strictly BROADER than the client's — the client intersection at page.tsx:124-125 reads only `funnelWorkshop.artisans`, i.e. the join rows, so an artisan linked only by the `Artisan.workshopId` column is invisible to it.

The premise holds too: `FunnelFilters` (FunnelFilters.tsx:61-85) loads workshops once, sorts by occurrence date, and unconditionally fires `onChange({workshopId: latest?.id ?? "", …}, latest)`, so this page always opens with a workshop selected, and artisans/page.tsx:68-72 deliberately waits for that call before the first fetch. The server returns the newest 20 artisans of the WHOLE table (`order={"createdAt": "desc"}`) with `total`/`pages` for the unfiltered set, and `Pagination` at :265 prints those unfiltered numbers next to the intersected rows.

One correction to the finder: there is a second, opposite failure mode they did not name. `workshopArtisanIds` is `null` when `funnelWorkshop?.artisans?.length` is falsy, and then `rows = data.items` — NO narrowing at all. A workshop whose people are linked only through the `Artisan.workshopId` column therefore shows every artisan in the repository under a workshop filter, while a workshop with join rows shows almost none. The same control gives two different wrong answers depending on which link path was used.

**How it fails.** A designer opens /artisans. The funnel auto-selects the most recently held workshop (FunnelFilters.tsx:81-82). The request omits `workshopId`; the server returns the 20 newest-created artisans out of the measured 431 (docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md:967; 731 in the wildcard table at :51). If that workshop's artisans were entered earlier, none is in those 20, `rows` is empty, the page renders the "No artisans found" EmptyState, and immediately below it `Pagination` reads "Page 1 of 22 · 431 records". Paging forward intersects each new page of 20 against the same handful of ids, so most pages are also empty.

**What it costs.** The central record list shows nothing — or, in the no-join-rows case, everything — for the workshop the designer is standing in, while its own pager insists hundreds of records exist. That is precisely the "an empty list is indistinguishable from having no data" failure FunnelFilters' own header comment (lines 52-60) records as having already bitten this application once. Every flow that begins by selecting an artisan on this page is blocked, and /products, /tools and /processes (which send `workshopId` server-side) disagree with /artisans about who was at the same workshop.

**Remedy.** Send `workshopId: funnel.workshopId || undefined` in the `listResource("/artisans", …)` params at artisans/page.tsx:52-57; delete the `workshopArtisanIds` intersection (:122-127) and the `funnelWorkshop` state that exists only to feed it (:33, :89); rewrite the comment at :51 to match products/tools/processes. The server clause honours both link paths, so this also fixes the no-narrowing case, and `total`/`pages` then describe the filtered set the table is showing.

**What the verifier checked.** I looked specifically for what would make this unreachable. Whether the funnel really defaults to a workshop: FunnelFilters.tsx:81-82 calls `onChange` with `latest?.id` unconditionally and artisans/page.tsx:69 gates the first fetch on it, so the default is guaranteed. Whether the server parameter is real and survives composition: artisans.py:198 declares it, :238-244 appends it to `and_filters`, and `where["AND"] = and_filters` at :253-254 means the search `OR` cannot clobber it. Whether the workshop row even carries `artisans`: `RELATIONS` in workshops.py:82-87 includes `Relation("artisans", "workshopartisan", …, include={"artisan": True})` — join rows only, which is how I found the second failure mode. Confirmed the sibling pages do send it (products/page.tsx:46, tools/page.tsx:56, processes/page.tsx:73). Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

---

### [MAJOR] (cross-surface) The report screen seeds its template dropdown from the workshop header column and sends it on every preview and download, so stage 20's required 'Report template' answer is skipped on the web

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/[id]/report/page.tsx:299-303, :335, :500, :381-389, :874; backend/app/services/design_workshops.py:2201-2205 and :163; backend/app/services/stage_schema.py:495-508 (PROMOTED_COLUMNS); backend/app/services/stage_definitions.py:1552

**What it does.** Quoted code is accurate. `setTemplateId(nextDetail.templateId)` (:303) takes the `DesignWorkshop` ROW's own column — the serializer at design_workshops.py:163 is literally `"templateId": record.templateId` — which is the THIRD and last rung of `resolve_template_id` (2201-2205: `for candidate in (requested, (settings or {}).get("templateId")): … return str(getattr(record, "templateId", "") or "")`). Because the page then sends that value as `requested` on every preview (:335, effect at :364-367 fires whenever `templateId` is non-empty) and every download (:500 `templateId: templateId || null`), the loop returns on its first candidate and the stage-20 answer — a REQUIRED BASIC ENUM field, stage_definitions.py:1552 — is never consulted from this browser. I checked whether stage 20's save promotes into the header column: `PROMOTED_COLUMNS` (stage_schema.py:495-508) maps `workshopSetup.*` only, so it does not. Every other stage-20 setting on this page IS read off the stage entry (`pageSize` :460, header/footer :161-162, `themeAccent`/`themePreset` :470-475), so `templateId` is the single exception. The second half of the finding is also true and is sharper: `templateId` is written exactly once, in the mount effect — `refreshWorkshop` (:381-389) re-seeds only `detail` and `settings` — so changing 'Report template' inside the Report settings panel and pressing Save fires `onSaved` → `loadPreview(templateId)` (:874) with the OLD value, and the panel prints 'Saved. The preview below has been rebuilt from them.' (ReportSettingsPanel.tsx:252) over a preview built from the template that was just replaced, with the dropdown above still naming it.

**How it fails.** The create form defaults the header to `DCH_STANDARD` (design-workshops/page.tsx:139). A designer later answers stage 20's required 'Report template' with `PHOTO_CATALOGUE` and saves it; the completeness gate and readiness both count it satisfied. They open the Report screen: the dropdown reads 'DCH standard workshop report', the preview is DCH, and both downloads send `templateId: "DCH_STANDARD"`, which `resolve_template_id` obeys because it is a valid token. The same workshop generated from the handset or a bare `GET /report/preview` (no `templateId`) resolves stage 20 and produces the photo-catalogue document. If instead they change the template inside Report settings and save, the write lands but the dropdown and the rebuilt preview both keep the old template.

**What it costs.** A required field the form insisted on is inert on the web surface, and two surfaces produce two different documents from one record. The settings-panel path is worse: it asserts the preview was rebuilt from the settings just saved when it was rebuilt from the template they replaced.

**Remedy.** Seed from stage 20 first, mirroring the server: `setTemplateId(settingText(nextDetail.stages?.[REPORT_STAGE_KEY]?.singleton, "templateId") || nextDetail.templateId)`, and re-seed the same way inside `refreshWorkshop` so a template saved through the panel reaches the dropdown and the preview. Better still, track whether the dropdown was touched and send `templateId: touched ? templateId : null`, which lets the server own the precedence — the identical argument the page already applies to `themeAccent` at :510-514.

**What the verifier checked.** I looked specifically for something that would make the header column and the stage answer agree. `PROMOTED_COLUMNS` (stage_schema.py:495-508) promotes only stage 1's `workshopSetup.*`, so saving stage 20 never writes `DesignWorkshop.templateId`; and `PATCH` is the only other writer. I confirmed both server entry points funnel through `resolve_template_id` with the request first (routes/design_workshops.py:3172-3174 in `_report_inputs`, reached by preview at :2733 and by generate), so a non-empty `requested` really does short-circuit the stage answer. I checked that `templateId` is a live, non-deprecated field of the `reportSettings` entity (stage_definitions.py:1552) so the settings panel does render it and can write it. I also checked the preview effect cannot be skipped: `if (!templateId) return` (:365) only fires when the column is empty, which it never is for a row created through this form (:139 defaults it). Not in OPEN_FINDINGS. Severity held at MAJOR: the produced file matches what the dropdown says, so the designer is misled about their saved answer rather than about this file.

---

### [MAJOR] (cross-surface) The questionnaire screen renders the workshop dropdown but never sends workshopId to /questionnaire/interviews, which supports it — the list is a superset the filter claims to have narrowed

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** frontend/app/(protected)/questionnaire/page.tsx:253-266, :275-281, :886; backend/app/api/routes/questionnaire.py:488 and :527-528

**What it does.** Verified verbatim. `loadInterviews` sends only `page`, `pageSize`, `artisanId` and `search`; the refetch effect's dependency array is `[page, funnel.artisanId, searchQuery]`; and the comment above it asserts artisan is "the only list param the interviews endpoint supports". `list_interviews` declares `workshopId: str | None = None` (questionnaire.py:488) and applies `where["workshopId"] = workshopId` (:527-528). The `FunnelFilters` at :886 is rendered inside the same block as the interviews search box, directly above the results section, with `showArtisan`.

The "no bytes leave the browser" mechanism is right but worth stating precisely: `selectWorkshop` (FunnelFilters.tsx:115-118) emits `{workshopId, craftId: "", artisanId: ""}`. If an artisan was selected, `funnel.artisanId` changes to "" and the effect DOES re-run — but with no workshop narrowing, so the list widens to the whole repository. If no artisan was selected, `funnel.artisanId` is unchanged, `setPage(1)` is a no-op on page 1, and the effect does not re-run at all. Either way the workshop pick never narrows the list.

**How it fails.** A designer picks a workshop from the dropdown above the interviews table. The dropdown displays it as the active filter. The table beneath is unchanged — every interview in the repository, newest first, including other designers' work at other workshops — and the pager's `total` counts them all.

**What it costs.** A filter control that states a narrowing the list does not perform. Unlike the artisans defect, nothing is hidden — the list is a superset — so no record looks deleted; the harm is that the reader takes the rows below the control as "this workshop's interviews" and acts on rows that are not. It also breaks the parity the shared funnel exists to enforce: the same dropdown genuinely narrows /products, /tools and /processes server-side. The workshop pick does narrow the artisan dropdown (FunnelFilters.tsx:112-113), which is what makes the list's silence read as a correct answer rather than a dead control.

**Remedy.** Add `workshopId: funnel.workshopId || undefined` to the params at questionnaire/page.tsx:254-260 and add `funnel.workshopId` to the effect's dependency array at :281 (the eslint-disable is already in place). Rewrite the comment at :276-277, which is what will otherwise send the next reader away. The craft dropdown genuinely has no server parameter on this endpoint — it legitimately only cascades into the artisan picker — so say that instead of the current blanket claim.

**What the verifier checked.** Read the route end to end to check the parameter is not dead: `workshopId` is applied to `where` at :527-528 before `where["AND"] = and_filters` at :532-533, so it survives composition and is not overwritten by the search `OR`. Confirmed `QuestionnaireInterview.workshopId` is a real column by finding it in `_MERGEABLE_FILL_FIELDS` further down the same file. Checked the render site at :886 to confirm the funnel sits with the list rather than with the capture form above it — it is in the same block as the interviews SearchInput, immediately preceding the results `<section>`. Checked whether any other effect refetches on `funnel` wholesale — the only interview-list effect is at :278-281. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.
