### [MAJOR] (cross-surface) Every record picker in the web forms loads one capped page and never reads `total`, and the craft-change handler reads absence from that page as "wrong craft" and clears the artisan link

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** frontend/components/forms/ProductForm.tsx:146 (the fetch), :141-143 (`artisansForCraft`), :380-382 (the craft-change handler) and :226 (`artisanId: artisanId || null`); the same capped fetch at ProcessForm.tsx:282 and :337, ToolForm.tsx:142, ToolAssignmentSection.tsx:36-38, ArtisanForm.tsx:399, WorkshopSelect.tsx:129, FunnelFilters.tsx:69-70 and :91; against backend/app/services/pagination.py:4 (`MAX_PAGE_SIZE = 100`) and :14-21 (`page_payload` returns `total` and `pages`).

**What it does.** `normalize_pagination` clamps `page_size` to 100, so `pageSize: 100` is the largest page the API will serve — a picker holds at most one page and there is no second fetch at any of those eleven call sites. `page_payload` carries `total` and `pages`; every call site takes `.items` only, so no picker can tell a truncated list from a complete one and none renders a truncation notice. The list routes accept a `search` parameter (artisans.py:195, crafts.py, tools.py:91) and no picker uses it. The guard at ProductForm.tsx:141-143 (`artisans.filter(a => a.craftId === craftId || a.id === artisanId)`) is written to keep an out-of-craft selection visible, but it filters the loaded array, so it cannot surface an artisan that was never fetched. Line 381 then reads `!artisans.some(a => a.id === artisanId && a.craftId === next)` as "this artisan does not practise the chosen craft" and calls `setArtisanId("")`, which line 226 sends as an explicit `null` that `CLEARABLE_KEYS` makes the server honour.

**How it fails.** docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md records 731 artisans measured on this database. `list_artisans` orders `createdAt desc` (artisans.py:261), so the picker holds the 100 most recent. A designer opens an older product to correct its craft; the product's artisan is not among those 100 and so was never in the dropdown. Changing the craft fires line 381 — `some(...)` is false because the artisan is ABSENT, not because their craft differs — `setArtisanId("")` runs, the field goes blank, and the save sends `artisanId: null`. The link is destroyed under a 200. Separately and without any edit, 631 of 731 artisans simply cannot be selected in the product, tool and process forms or the funnel filter, with nothing on screen saying anything was hidden.

**What it costs.** "Not in the list" and "does not exist" are indistinguishable across every record picker in the web forms — the class of defect this repository has already paid for once in the viewer picker (docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md, closed 2026-08-13, 353 invisible accounts). And on an edit, an existing artisan-to-product link is silently destroyed by an unrelated craft correction.

**Remedy.** Two changes. (1) Stop reading absence from a truncated list as absence from the repository: at ProductForm.tsx:381, clear only when the artisan IS loaded and their craft differs — `const known = artisans.find(a => a.id === artisanId); if (next && known && known.craftId !== next) setArtisanId("")` — and seed the picker with the record's own artisan (`GET /artisans/{id}` when `initial.artisanId` is not in the page) so the option is always renderable. (2) Read the envelope: `listResource` already returns `total`, so render the truncation notice the eligible-viewer picker now carries when `total > items.length`, and give these pickers the server-side `search` parameter all three list routes already accept instead of filtering 100 client-side rows.

**What the verifier checked.** Verified the cap is a real ceiling (`MAX_PAGE_SIZE = 100`, `min(page_size, MAX_PAGE_SIZE)`) so `pageSize: 100` cannot be widened from the client, and that no call site paginates — each is a single `listResource` in a `useEffect` with no follow-up. Verified `total` is in the envelope and unread. Verified the clear really reaches the server: `artisanId` is in `CLEARABLE_KEYS` (records.py:252-268), so `clean_data` keeps the explicit null and `db.productdocumentation.update` writes it. TWO CORRECTIONS to the finder. (a) Merely being off the page does NOT by itself lose the link: `artisanId` is React state seeded from `initial`, so an untouched save still sends the stored id — the loss needs the craft-change handler at :381, which is the sharp half of the finding and the only path I could confirm. (b) The finder's "the picker holds the 100 most recently created" is right for /artisans, /products and /tools but wrong for /crafts, which is ordered `name asc` (crafts.py:86-88, with a comment explaining why) — so the craft picker holds the first 100 alphabetically. Kept MAJOR: real silent loss of one link on a plausible edit, plus a repository-wide invisibility with no notice. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

---

### [MAJOR] (cross-surface) An offline-created workshop's first sync pushes stage 1/stage 3 without `merge`, wiping the designer prefill the create endpoint just wrote — because `putDraftStage` grants `serverLoadedAt` for free while `remoteId` is null

**Verdict:** PARTIAL

**Where.** frontend/lib/designWorkshopStore.ts:1113 (the grant — NOT :1064 as cited; the file has moved ~340 lines), :2612 + :2642-2664 + :2714 + :2752-2757 (buildStageEntries' one never-read test and its three arms), :3081-3110 (the create and its write-back), :3213 (`draft = (await loadDraft(draft.localId)) ?? draft` — the only reload, and it is local); backend/app/api/routes/design_workshops.py:873-880 (create then seed); backend/app/services/design_workshops.py:750-818 (seed_designer_prefill), :1057-1076 (`if entry.merge and previous`), :1319-1357 (_coerce_promoted); backend/app/services/designers.py:191-196 (PREFILL_MAP); backend/app/services/stage_definitions.py:176-217 and :281-283 (the fields)

```
// designWorkshopStore.ts:1113
serverLoadedAt: previous.serverLoadedAt ?? (draft.remoteId === null ? now : null),

// designWorkshopStore.ts:2612 / 2660-2664
const neverRead = (stage?.serverLoadedAt ?? null) === null;
...
entries.push(neverRead ? { entityKey: entity.key, data: values, merge: true } : { entityKey: entity.key, data: values });

# design_workshops.py:795 (inside POST /design-workshops)
await db.dwstageentry.create(data={"designWorkshopId": record.id, "stageKey": spec.key, "entityKey": entity.key, "ordinal": 0, "data": _json(clean), ...})
```

**What it does.** Every quoted line exists, at different line numbers. `putDraftStage` stamps `serverLoadedAt` on any stage written while `draft.remoteId === null`; `buildStageEntries` uses that single field as the whole never-read test and omits `merge: true` when it is set; `runSync` creates the workshop, writes `remoteId` back, and then reloads the draft **from IndexedDB only** (:3213) before pushing every dirty stage — there is no server read between the create and the stage PUTs, confirmed by reading the whole create→media→stages sequence. Meanwhile `POST /design-workshops` calls `seed_designer_prefill`, which really does create `DwStageEntry` rows: `workshopSetup` in stage 1 (`designerName`, `designerInstitution`) and `workshopPlan` in stage 3 (`designerProfile`, RICH_TEXT and `required=True`, plus `designerExperience`), and updates the promoted columns it filled. So the justification in the comment at :1108-1112 ("there is nothing up there for it to overwrite") is false by the time the payload goes up. TWO CORRECTIONS TO THE FINDER. (a) The claim that `merge` would have saved craftName/clusterName/state/district/startDate/endDate/scheme/implementingAgency/sponsor/workshopCode is WRONG: those columns are written by the create route (routes/design_workshops.py:864-871) and are backed by NO stage entry, so they are absent from `previous` as well as from `clean` and `_coerce_promoted` nulls them on this walk with or without `merge`. That is an independent defect (reported under `missed`). `merge` genuinely does save `designerName`, which IS in the prefill row and IS promoted. (b) The finder missed the larger half of the same false flag: `stageSweep` (:2834-2836) reads the identical `serverLoadedAt`, so an offline-created draft is also granted COLLECTION SWEEP AUTHORITY it never earned — `replaceCollections: true` on stages whose server copy this browser has never read.

**How it fails.** A designer with no signal creates a workshop from the list page (`createLocalOffline` → `createLocalDraft`, page.tsx:362-373). They open stage 1: the page takes the `!holdForServer` branch (stages/[stageKey]/page.tsx:519-538, `canReconcile` is false with no `remoteId`) and seeds a blank form — nothing anywhere folds the header or a profile into a local stage. They type the venue; the autosave writes it and, because `remoteId` is still null, `serverLoadedAt` is stamped. They also write a paragraph into stage 3. Two days later the laptop reaches wifi: `runSync` POSTs the create, the server files the workshop AND seeds `workshopSetup` with the designer's name and institution and `workshopPlan` with their biography, and the pass immediately PUTs both stages with `neverRead === false`, i.e. no `merge` key. `save_stage` writes `clean` over each singleton row's `data` wholesale (design_workshops.py:1102-1105) with no `RecordRevision` — `designerName`, `designerInstitution`, `designerProfile` and `designerExperience` are gone in place — and `_coerce_promoted` additionally nulls the `designerName` column. The screen says "Stage saved". Stage 3's `designerProfile` is a required Basic-tier field, so that stage silently reverts from complete to incomplete. Second, later walk: because `serverLoadedAt` stays stamped, a deletion made on any of those stages arms `replaceCollections: true` against rows a co-designer (viewer grant) has since added in the office, and the server soft-deletes them.

**What it costs.** Silent, unrecoverable overwrite of the four values the server seeded for this workshop, on the ordinary offline-create flow the whole store exists for; `designerName` also nulled on the header column, so the list and the report cover lose it; stage 3 becomes unsubmittable for a biography the designer never saw and cannot know to retype. Nothing is reported — `droppedKeys` is empty (no key was unknown) and `errors` is empty (nothing was refused). Bounded, though, and this is why it is MAJOR and not BLOCKER: at first sync the prefill is the ONLY thing the server holds for a workshop that has just been created, so no fieldwork typed by a person is destroyed. The unearned sweep authority is the part that can later reach another person's work.

**Remedy.** Stop conflating "the workshop does not exist on the server" with "this device has read the server's copy of this stage". Either drop the `draft.remoteId === null ? now : null` arm at :1113 and leave `serverLoadedAt` null until a real fold or a non-merge push sets it, or — cheaper and equally sufficient — reset `serverLoadedAt: null` on every stage inside the same `mutate` that writes `remoteId` at :3098-3109, since the create has just produced server-side rows this browser has never read. With it null, the first post-create save carries `merge: true` on all three arms, `{**previous, **clean}` preserves the prefill, and `stageSweep` correctly withholds a sweep it has not earned (surfaced as `DwStageSweep.withheld`, the trade already made). Pin it with a test asserting the SERIALISED body carries `merge: true` for the first stage save after a create, in the spirit of `frontend/e2e/stage-entry-merge-unit.spec.ts`. Note this does NOT fix the promoted columns — see the separate finding.

**What the verifier checked.** Read :1039-1124 (putDraftStage), :2582-2767 (buildStageEntries), :2820-2837 (stageSweep), :3050-3213 (runSync's create, media and reload) in full: there is no server read between the create and the stage PUTs, and nothing resets `serverLoadedAt` on the create write-back. Checked `emptyStage` (:812-826) — `serverLoadedAt` starts null, so the stamp really does come from the `remoteId === null` arm. Checked the stage page's load effect (:466-629): with no `remoteId`, `canReconcile` is false, the form seeds blank and no fold ever runs. Checked `adoptServerDetail` (:2131-2215) and `headerOf` — no path writes header values into a stage singleton, so the local stage genuinely holds neither the prefill nor the create-form values. Server side: read `seed_designer_prefill` (750-818), `PREFILL_MAP`, the create route (836-880), the merge branch (1057-1076), the wholesale `updates.append` (1102-1105) and `_coerce_promoted` (1319-1357). Tried to kill it via `touched_entities` (built unconditionally at :991, so no escape) and via `test_a_promoted_column_can_be_cleared_and_others_are_untouched` (tests/test_stage_sync.py:454) — that test keeps craftName in every payload, so it does not cover this. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md. The one thing the finder got wrong is the promoted-column half, which I checked by tracing where craftName can enter `previous`: only the create route writes it, and only to the column.

---

### [MAJOR] (cross-surface) `adoptServerStage`/`foldStageInto` discard every stored singleton answer whose key this browser's cached registry does not declare, and the next ordinary save deletes it from the repository with no droppedKeys and no banner

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** frontend/lib/designWorkshopStore.ts:1655-1665 (splitSingletons), :1747 (fold path), :2175 (list-adopt path), :2365 (stage-adopt path); frontend/lib/designWorkshops.ts:793 (`if (!options?.refresh && cachedRegistry) return cachedRegistry;`); backend/app/api/routes/design_workshops.py:3119 (`bucket["singleton"] = data`), :1034-1039; backend/app/services/design_workshops.py:993-1001 (droppedKeys), :1057-1077 (merge), :1101-1106 (wholesale `_json(item.data)` update)

**What it does.** Confirmed at every link, though the finder's line numbers are all stale (the code has moved). `_stages_payload` at routes:3119 assigns the singleton row's stored `data` verbatim — no registry filter — so a key this build does not declare genuinely arrives. `splitSingletons` (store:1655) copies across only `field.key in singleton` for keys declared by `entity.fields` of the browser's own `spec`, and it is the ONLY writer of `stage.singletons` on all three read paths (:1747 fold, :2175 list adopt, :2365 stage adopt). The dropped key is therefore absent from IndexedDB, from the form, and from `buildStageEntries`. Because the stage has been read, `neverRead` is false at store:2611 and the singleton entry is pushed WITHOUT `merge` (store:2662-2666), so `save_stage` skips the `if entry.merge and previous:` branch at services:1057 and writes `{"data": _json(item.data)}` wholesale at services:1103. `droppedKeys` cannot notice, because services:999-1001 computes it as `f"{entity.key}.{k}" for k in entry.data if k not in known` — over the keys the CLIENT sent, and this client removed them first.

The asymmetry the finder points at is real on the adopt path: store:2365-2368 filters the singleton and keeps `collections` (`withClientKeys`, which filters nothing) and `custom` verbatim. It is NOT true of the fold path — `foldStageInto` filters collections by `declared` at store:1793 with an explicit justification — so the "two sibling lines" argument holds only for `adoptServerStage`'s clean branch. The Android claim checks out: `DwStageFold.kt:231-238` folds `bucket.singleton.forEach { if (!values.containsKey(key)) values[key] = value }` with no registry test at all.

**How it fails.** The reachable route is not a deploy race but this app's own offline model. A designer's tab loads the registry while the connection is poor, `loadRegistry` (store:1621) falls into its catch, and `adoptStageRegistry` seeds the module-level `cachedRegistry` from the IndexedDB copy — which may be weeks old. `fetchStageRegistry` (designWorkshops.ts:793) then serves that object for the rest of the tab's life; nothing in the frontend ever calls it with `{refresh: true}` (grepped: the only `refresh: true` in the codebase is for the custom-section definition). The connection returns. The designer opens stage 4: `GET /stages/CLUSTER_CRAFT_BACKGROUND` succeeds and carries a colleague's answer under a field this browser's stale registry does not declare. `splitSingletons` drops it. The form does not draw it — correctly; it does not know it. The designer corrects one word of the narrative and presses Save. The PUT's singleton entry does not contain the key and carries no `merge`; `save_stage` writes `clean` over `data`; the colleague's answer is gone in place, with no `RecordRevision`. `droppedKeys` is empty, so no banner fires, and the notice reads "Stage saved — 0 added, 1 updated".

**What it costs.** Silent, unrecoverable loss of another user's answer whenever this browser's field list is behind the server's — which is the state the offline fallback puts it in by design. It is exactly the drift `droppedKeys` exists to report, made structurally unreportable by the one function that removes the evidence before the payload is built. Android does not have it and would report the same key in `droppedKeys`, so the two surfaces disagree about what a stale client does to a stored answer.

**Remedy.** Do not throw the unknown keys away. Carry them on `DwDraftStage` — `singletons[entity.key]` for the keys the spec declares, plus a sibling `unknownSingleton: DwEntryData` holding `Object.keys(incoming.singleton)` minus every declared key of the stage's singleton entity — and have `buildStageEntries` merge them back into the singleton entry it sends. The server then either accepts them (its registry is the newer one) or names them in `droppedKeys`, which fires the existing banner. `foldStageInto` must preserve them on the fold path too, and `splitSingletons` is the single place to change since all three read paths go through it. As a belt-and-braces measure, send `merge: true` on a singleton entry whose registry version does not match the stage response's `schemaVersion`, so a stale client cannot delete by omission — noting that `merge` is deliberately omitted-when-false for `extra="forbid"` compatibility (store:2650-2660), so this must be added as a true-only flag.

**What the verifier checked.** Tried hard to refute. Checked whether the server filters the singleton before sending — it does not (routes:3119, raw `dict(row.data or {})`). Checked whether more than one SINGLETON entity per stage could make the flat `bucket["singleton"]` lossy instead — no: `stage_schema.py:758-760` validates `len(singletons) > 1` as a spec problem, so the invariant holds and there is no second defect there. Checked whether `merge` saves it — it does not: `neverRead` is false for a stage that has been read, and the singleton entry at store:2662 is pushed bare. Checked whether `validate_entry` or a DB constraint preserves the column — no: services:1103 writes `_json(item.data)` over `data` unconditionally for a matched row. Checked whether `droppedKeys` could still fire — it cannot; services:999 iterates `entry.data`. Checked the Android counterpart at DwStageFold.kt:231-238 and confirmed it does not filter. Checked docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md — not recorded, and distinct from the seven open items. Severity held at MAJOR rather than raised: it needs the browser's registry to be behind the server's, which is a real but not universal state.

---

## MINOR — 39

### [MINOR] (backend) Renaming a craft onto an existing name answers 500 where creating one with the same name answers 409

**Verdict:** PARTIAL

**Where.** backend/app/api/routes/crafts.py:142 (`updated = await db.craft.update(where={"id": craft_id}, data=data, include=INCLUDE)`, no try/except) against crafts.py:102-105 (the create, wrapped in `except UniqueViolationError` → 409 "Craft name already exists"); backend/prisma/schema.prisma:363 (`name String @unique`); the generic handler at backend/app/main.py:286-300.

**What it does.** `Craft.name` is `@unique`. The create path catches `UniqueViolationError` and returns a 409 naming the clash; the update path issues the same write against the same index with no handler, so the driver exception reaches the catch-all ASGI middleware in main.py, which logs the traceback and answers 500 with `{"detail": "Something went wrong on the server. The error has been logged.", "error": "UniqueViolationError"}`. artisans.py shows the repository's own standard for this class — `_violated_identity_field` plus `_identity_conflict` wrap BOTH the create and the update (artisans.py:366-373). Crafts wrap only the create.

**How it fails.** A professor opens a duplicate craft ("Bandhej") to rename it to the canonical "Bandhani", which already exists. `clean_data` title-cases the input to exactly the stored name, `db.craft.update` violates the unique index, and the save answers HTTP 500 with an opaque sentence and writes a stack trace to the log. The client is given nothing that identifies the colliding row, so no pre-flight or "open that craft instead" affordance can be built against the endpoint.

**What it costs.** A legitimate taxonomy correction fails with a message that says the server is broken rather than that the name is taken, and each attempt writes a logged stack trace. The user retries, since nothing tells them retrying cannot help.

**Remedy.** Wrap the update as the create is wrapped: `try: updated = await db.craft.update(...) except UniqueViolationError as exc: raise HTTPException(409, detail="Craft name already exists") from exc`. Better, mirror `artisans._identity_conflict` and return the id and name of the craft already holding the name, since a rename collision is nearly always a merge the user wants.

**What the verifier checked.** Verified the quoted lines exist as quoted and that `CraftUpdate.name` (schemas/records.py:202) really can carry a name, so the collision is reachable. Verified `require_craft_manager` gates the route (professor+), so it is not open to everyone. THREE THINGS THE FINDER GOT WRONG, hence PARTIAL. (1) The claimed consequence "the web renders a 5xx from a record surface as a connectivity failure (lib/offline.ts), so the professor is told they are offline" is FALSE against this tree: the crafts page saves through `saveOrQueue` (frontend/app/(protected)/crafts/page.tsx:186-197), and `saveOrQueue` (frontend/lib/offline.ts:609-613) re-throws when the error IS an `ApiError` — `if (isTransient(error) && !(error instanceof ApiError)) return queue(); throw error;` — so a 500 is never queued and never presented as offline. `isUnreachable` in the same file deliberately excludes 5xx, with a docstring explaining exactly why. Grepping the frontend for `status >= 500` finds only offline.ts and the design-workshop stage page. The user sees "Something went wrong on the server. The error has been logged." (2) "The rename is unperformable, so the duplicate crafts stay and the repository keeps splitting one craft across two rows" overstates it: the rename to an occupied name is impossible under the unique index regardless of the status code; only the diagnosis is wrong, not the outcome. (3) Severity is therefore MINOR, not MAJOR — it is an error-reporting defect on one professor-gated path, with no data loss and no wrong data. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

---

### [MINOR] (backend) A `submit=true` save that fails validation has already been committed, and the 422 that reports it discards every other field of the result

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** backend/app/api/routes/design_workshops.py:1034-1039, against the committed transaction at services/design_workshops.py:1199-1222

**What it does.** Exact as quoted. `save_stage` opens `db.tx()` (:1199), applies every update, every create, the `update_many` that soft-deletes the swept rows (:1208-1211) and the header write including `status: IN_PROGRESS` when the workshop was DRAFT (:1220-1221), commits, then queues transcriptions and returns. Only after that does the route look at `result["errors"]` and raise. So a request answered 422 has mutated the record in every way a successful save would have. The 422 body carries `{message, errors}` only — `removed`, `created`, `updated`, `droppedKeys`, `droppedCustomKeys`, `completeness`, `refusedAnswers`, `customSchemaVersion` are all discarded — and the message is unconditional while `result["errors"]` also holds `coerce_value` refusals, so a submit whose only fault is an unreadable decimal is reported as a missing required field.

**How it fails.** A designer finishes a stage, deletes a duplicated cost line and presses "Save and check required fields". The sweep is armed (this browser has read the stage), the duplicate row is soft-deleted and every field is written; the server then finds a blank required field and answers 422. The page's catch branch (frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/[id]/stages/[stageKey]/page.tsx:1044-1076) never reaches `setErrors`/`markStagePushed`, so the draft stays dirty with `removedFrom` set and the designer is told only "Some required fields are missing". They do not learn that the deletion landed, that the workshop moved from DRAFT to IN_PROGRESS, or which field is at fault.

**What it costs.** A request reported as a failure silently succeeded. Any client that reads a 422 as "nothing was written" — a reasonable reading — is wrong about a soft-deleted row and about the status transition. Nothing is lost (the stage stays dirty and re-syncs, and the re-send is idempotent), which keeps this MINOR; the sharper cost is that the only information the strict pass exists to produce is thrown away by the transport — see the separate entry in `missed`.

**Remedy.** Return the full result under the 422: `detail={**result, "message": ...}`, so `removed`, `droppedKeys` and `completeness` survive the refusal and a client can say "the stage was written and 1 row removed, but it cannot be submitted yet". Choose the message from the errors rather than assuming: if every message ends in "is required" say so, otherwise "Some answers could not be read". Moving the gate inside `save_stage` before `db.tx()` is the larger change and would cost the deliberate behaviour that a stage with one bad number still saves its other twenty fields (:1011-1012).

**What the verifier checked.** Read the route (:1013-1040) and the transaction (:1199-1222). Looked specifically for a rollback, a pre-flight validation pass, or a submit gate inside save_stage before the tx: there is none — `enforce_required=payload.submit` is passed to `validate_entry` (:1004-1006) but its errors only populate the map and the loop explicitly keeps going. Confirmed `errors` mixes coercion refusals with required-field refusals (stage_schema.py:1128-1147). Confirmed the web is the only client that sends `submit=true` (page.tsx:1469, "Save and check required fields"; Android's `StageSaveBody.submit` at StageSchema.kt:1301 has no true call site). Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md. Severity left at MINOR: the write that lands is the write the designer asked for, and nothing is lost.

---

### [MINOR] (backend) `/export/dataset` silently drops every media row whose `url` column is null, and neither `truncated` nor `totalMedia` says a file was left out

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** backend/app/api/routes/export.py:243-247 (add_media) and :372-378 (the response), backend/app/services/s3.py:160-172 (public_url_for_key), backend/app/api/routes/data_browser.py:2999-3008 (the sibling fallback), backend/app/api/routes/media.py:510-525

**What it does.** Exact as quoted. `add_media` emits a file only `if m.url`; `media_count` at :372 counts only what was emitted; `truncated` at :171-174 and :226 is computed solely from the six table caps and the media cap and never from a skipped row. Every sibling read path has a fallback the manifest lacks: `download_media` streams from `objectKey` when `url` is falsy (data_browser.py:3000-3008), `datasets._presign_media_row` signs a fresh URL from `objectKey`, and `MediaIndex.prefetch` fetches by `objectKey`. `public_url_for_key` (s3.py:160-168) returns `None` when neither `aws_s3_public_base_url` nor `aws_s3_endpoint` is set (both optional, config.py:171-172), and `complete_media_upload` stores that `None` verbatim (media.py:525) — its own comment at :523-524 says 'It is None when the deployment has no public base URL configured, which the download path already handles by streaming the bytes', a claim that is true of `download_media` and false of the manifest.

**How it fails.** The finder's whole-deployment scenario is unlikely — every `.env` and `.env.example` in the tree sets both `AWS_S3_ENDPOINT` and `AWS_S3_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` (backend/.env:70,73; backend/.env.example:70,73; .env.example:61-62), so a deployment where every row has a null url would be a misconfiguration nobody has shipped. The reachable case is the residual one: `MediaFile.url` is nullable (schema.prisma:792), and any row written while the public base URL was momentarily unset, or by a migration/backfill, or before the column was populated, carries `url = NULL`. Those rows are streamed correctly one at a time by `/data/media/{id}/download`, so storage is demonstrably fine, and they are omitted from `/export/dataset` with `totalMedia` counting only the survivors and `truncated: false`. A researcher's zip is short by exactly those files and both the payload and the screen say the download was complete.

**What it costs.** A repository export presented as complete is short by every row with a null url, with nothing in the response or on screen naming them — and the two states a researcher must be able to tell apart, 'the zip is complete' and 'N files could not be addressed', render as the same sentence.

**Remedy.** Give the manifest the fallback its three siblings have: when `m.url` is falsy, emit `{"path": ..., "url": presign_get_url(m.objectKey, filename=..., mime_type=...)}`, which also removes the manifest's standing dependence on the bucket being anonymously readable. If a row has neither a url nor a resolvable key, count it: add a `skippedMedia` integer to the response at export.py:373-378 and OR it into `truncated`, since `truncated`'s documented job (export.py:24-25) is exactly 'so the client can say so instead of quietly handing over a partial dataset'.

**What the verifier checked.** Read export.py end to end and data_browser.py:2999-3013. Tried to kill it three ways. (1) Is `url` ever guaranteed non-null? No — nullable in schema.prisma:792, and `public_url_for_key` has an explicit `return None` branch (s3.py:167-168). (2) Does the client compensate? `totalMedia` is derived server-side from the emitted list, so the client has no other number to compare against; `truncated` is provably independent of skipping (computed at :171-174 and :226 only). (3) Is the skip actually silent, or does something log it? Nothing logs in `add_media`. Checked docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md — not recorded. Kept CONFIRMED at the finder's MINOR, but rewrote the failure scenario: the finder's 'not one photograph in the zip' deployment is not one that exists in this tree's configuration, and stating it that way overclaims a real but narrow defect.

---

### [MINOR] (backend) Enum-column list filters compare a raw string against a Postgres enum, so a wrong value is a logged 500 instead of the 422 the shared helper exists to produce

**Verdict:** PARTIAL

**Where.** backend/app/api/routes/products.py:116-119 (`where["marketDemand"] = marketDemand`, `where["productType"] = productType`) two lines above the correctly-guarded `statusFilter` at :120-121; backend/app/api/routes/tools.py:110-113 (`where["maker"] = maker`, `where["traditionType"] = traditionType`) two lines above its own guarded `statusFilter` at :114-115 — a pair the finder missed; backend/app/services/record_filters.py:292-293 (`media_where["mediaType"] = media_type`), reached by GET /search (search.py:66) and GET /map/points (map_points.py:782,969); the helper at backend/app/services/records.py:451-479; the route that got it right at backend/app/api/routes/media.py:607.

**What it does.** All four columns are Postgres enums in prisma/schema.prisma: `ProductDocumentation.productType` is `ProductType` (:661), `.marketDemand` is `MarketDemand` (:672), `ToolDocumentation.maker` is `MakerType` (:730), `.traditionType` is `TraditionType` (:731), and `MediaFile.mediaType` is `MediaType` (:787). Each of the five filters assigns the caller's raw query string straight into the `where`. `enum_filter_or_422` was written for exactly this — its docstring says in capitals "A VALUE THE ENUM DOES NOT HAVE IS A 500, NOT AN EMPTY LIST" and names the FieldNotFoundError — and products.py and tools.py both import it and use it for `statusFilter` only, immediately below the lines that need it. `record_filters` does not import it at all, even though `records.MEDIA_TYPES` exists for this and media.py:607 uses it, so `GET /media?mediaType=image` is a clean 422 while `GET /search?mediaType=image` is a 500 for the same parameter name.

**How it fails.** A stale bookmarked URL, a lowercase value, or a client whose dropdown labels its empty option `ALL` sends `?mediaType=ALL` to /search, or `?productType=finished_good` to /products. The query engine refuses to build the query, the catch-all in main.py logs the traceback and answers 500 with `{"detail": "Something went wrong on the server. The error has been logged.", "error": "FieldNotFoundError"}`.

**What it costs.** A malformed but harmless filter value is reported to the user as a server fault with no indication which parameter is wrong, and every such request writes a stack trace, so any authenticated caller can fill the error log at will. The 422 that would name the allowed values is one call away on the same line of the same function.

**Remedy.** `where["productType"] = enum_filter_or_422(productType, PRODUCT_TYPES, field="productType")` and the same for `marketDemand`, `maker` and `traditionType`, adding `PRODUCT_TYPES`, `MARKET_DEMANDS`, `MAKER_TYPES` and `TRADITION_TYPES` frozensets beside `RECORD_STATUSES` and `MEDIA_TYPES` in records.py:451-456 (mirrored from schema.prisma, per the note already there). In `build_record_wheres`, `enum_filter_or_422(media_type, MEDIA_TYPES, field="mediaType")`, which makes /search and /map/points agree with /media.

**What the verifier checked.** Verified every cited line exists as quoted and that each column is genuinely an enum in schema.prisma (a plain String column would make the whole finding vanish, so I checked all five). Verified `enum_filter_or_422` is imported in both products.py and tools.py, so the fix is a one-token change at each site, and that record_filters.py imports neither it nor MEDIA_TYPES. PARTIAL rather than CONFIRMED for two reasons. (1) The finder MISSED two more instances of the same defect, in tools.py:110-113 — the write-up above adds them. (2) The stated consequence "the web renders any 5xx from a list surface as a connectivity failure, so the designer is shown 'you are offline'" is FALSE against this tree, and the finder inherited it from the helper's own now-stale docstring: `frontend/lib/offline.ts` defines `isUnreachable` specifically to EXCLUDE 5xx, with a docstring saying that telling a designer their signal is at fault when the server answered is the wrong answer, and `saveOrQueue:612` re-throws any `ApiError`. Grepping the frontend for `status >= 500` finds only offline.ts and the design-workshop stage page. Severity MINOR held: real, trivially reachable, but no data is wrong and nothing is lost. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

### [MINOR] (backend) GET /secrets/{key}/reveal returns the environment plaintext labelled source "database" when the stored override cannot be decrypted, contradicting its own DTO

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** backend/app/api/routes/secrets.py:64-70 (`reveal_secret`) against backend/app/schemas/secrets.py:42-51 (`ManagedSecretRevealDto`, whose comment at 46-49 reads "Null when the key is unset, or when a stored value cannot be decrypted any more"), with the two divergent resolvers at backend/app/services/managed_secrets.py:486-489 and :598-636

**What it does.** The route resolves the value and its provenance with two different calls one line apart: `value = await managed_secrets.get_secret(key)` — which falls through to the environment when the stored ciphertext cannot be opened — and `described = await managed_secrets.describe_secret(key)`, whose `source` is `SOURCE_DATABASE` whenever a row exists (`stored = row is not None`, managed_secrets.py:606-608). For an undecryptable override with an environment fallback the response therefore pairs an environment plaintext with a database provenance, and `value` is never null in the case the DTO comment says it is null for.

**How it fails.** After a JWT_SECRET rotation with no explicit `SECRETS_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, the master admin presses the eye button on the OpenAI row to check what is stored. A full, plausible key appears, labelled as coming from the database. They conclude the stored override survived. It did not — the row is unopenable and what they were shown is the deployment's environment variable.

**What it costs.** The one screen that exists to let an operator verify which key is stored where misreports provenance in precisely the situation the module warns is unrecoverable, so a broken override is left in place. The declared wire contract is also wrong, meaning a client written against the DTO cannot detect the case from `value` being null either. MINOR: master-admin-only, read-only, no data is changed by the read, and the same operational precondition as the sibling finding is required.

**Remedy.** Resolve value and provenance from a single read. Either honour the DTO and return `value: None` when the row exists but did not decrypt, or return `source: "environment"` alongside a new `overrideUnreadable: true` and correct the DTO comment to describe what is actually returned. What must not stand is a value from one source carrying another source's name. The cheapest form is to expose a predicate on `managed_secrets` (`override_is_readable(key)`, i.e. `key in _overrides` after `refresh_if_stale`) and let both this route and `test_secret` branch on it.

**What the verifier checked.** Read secrets.py end to end (118 lines) and schemas/secrets.py end to end; both quotes are exact and the DTO comment says what the finder claims. I checked for a guard I might have missed: `_require_managed` only validates the key name against the registry, and there is no post-processing of `value` before it is returned. I confirmed the divergence is genuinely between the two calls and not inside one of them by reading `get_secret`/`peek_secret` (486-489, 473-483) and `_describe` (598-636). I also noted the `hint` field has the same split and reported it separately under `missed`, since it is a different field on a different response. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

---

### [MINOR] (backend) `DELETE /tasks/{task_id}` lets any admin delete another admin's assignment, one row at a time, defeating the creator-only rule the batch delete states

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** backend/app/api/routes/tasks.py:1151-1166 (`delete_task_batch`) against :1168-1176 (`delete_task`); same shape at :1084 (`update_task`'s `is_manager`)

**What it does.** Verified verbatim. The batch route is `Depends(require_admin)` and then refuses unless `is_master_admin(current_user)` or every task in the batch has `createdById == current_user.id`, with the docstring stating the reason: "otherwise one admin could quietly delete another's assignments". The single-task route beside it refuses only when `task.createdById != current_user.id and not is_admin(current_user)` — which every admin passes. A batch is nothing but `AssignedTask` rows sharing a `batchId`, and both list routes that hand over those ids (`GET /tasks/batches`, `GET /tasks?view=all`) are admin-readable. `update_task`:1084 has the identical shape (`is_manager = task.createdById == current_user.id or is_admin(current_user)`), so any admin can also reassign or CANCEL another admin's task row by row.

**How it fails.** Admin B lists admin A's batch and its twelve task ids, then issues twelve `DELETE /api/tasks/{id}` calls, each 204. `DELETE /api/tasks/batch/{batchId}` on the same twelve rows would have returned 403 with a sentence explaining why. `AssignedTask` rows are hard-deleted (`db.assignedtask.delete`) and nothing records the deletion, so the twelve assignees' task lists empty with no trace.

**What it costs.** The stated protection against one admin quietly deleting another's assignments is bypassed by iterating. An admin who reads the batch route's 403 reasonably concludes the rule holds everywhere. Admin-versus-admin only, both parties already trusted, hence MINOR.

**Remedy.** Apply the batch route's predicate in `delete_task`: `if not is_master_admin(current_user) and task.createdById != current_user.id: 403`, keeping its wording. Make the same decision explicitly for `update_task`'s `is_manager` — reassignment and CANCEL are the two arms that matter. If any admin genuinely should be able to unsend anybody's task, relax the batch route to `is_admin` and delete its docstring's claim; the two must agree either way.

**What the verifier checked.** Read tasks.py:1151-1176 verbatim — both routes are exactly as quoted, and `delete_task`'s guard is `not is_admin(current_user)`, which is false for ADMIN and MASTER_ADMIN. Read `update_task` at 1056-1146 and confirmed :1084's `is_manager` grants any admin the full manager arm including `assigneeId` reassignment and `status: CANCELLED`. Looked for a hidden guard in `require_task` — it is a plain existence check. Checked that the ids are obtainable: `/tasks/batches` and the `view=all` list are `require_admin`, which B already is. No soft delete: `db.assignedtask.delete(where={"id": task_id})`. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md. MINOR upheld — the finder's own severity is honest.

---

### [MINOR] (backend) Any admin can dismantle another admin's task batch one task at a time, defeating the rule DELETE /tasks/batch/{batch_id} enforces on the same rows

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** backend/app/api/routes/tasks.py:1151-1165 (`delete_task_batch`) against backend/app/api/routes/tasks.py:1168-1176 (`delete_task`), with the enumeration path at backend/app/api/routes/tasks.py:767-820 (`list_tasks`, `view=all` + `batchId`)

**What it does.** `delete_task_batch` is `Depends(require_admin)` and refuses when `not is_master_admin(current_user) and any(task.createdById != current_user.id for task in tasks)`, with a docstring saying why: "Only the admin who sent it (or the master admin) may unsend it — otherwise one admin could quietly delete another's assignments." `delete_task` guards with `if task.createdById != current_user.id and not is_admin(current_user)` — for an admin the creator comparison is short-circuited entirely, so any admin may delete any single task. `list_tasks` accepts `view=all` for any admin (the 403 at 796-797 fires only for non-admins) and accepts `batchId` (807-808), returning serialised items that carry each member task's `id`. So the batch guard is bypassable in two calls: list the batch, delete each member. Both deletions are hard (`db.assignedtask.delete` / `delete_many`), so nothing records that the rows existed.

**How it fails.** Admin A sends a five-person batch. Admin B calls `DELETE /tasks/batch/{id}` and is refused with "Only the admin who created this batch (or the master admin) can delete it". Admin B then calls `GET /tasks?view=all&batchId=<id>`, reads the five member ids, and calls `DELETE /tasks/{id}` five times — all five return 204. Admin A's batch disappears from `GET /tasks/batches`, five designers lose their to-do items, and no tombstone or audit row survives.

**What it costs.** A rule one route deliberately enforces over a table is unenforced on another route over the same table, so the protection is decorative rather than real. Kept at MINOR: every actor on both paths is already an ADMIN or above — this is not a privilege escalation or a hole an ordinary user can reach, it is two admin-level routes stating opposite rules about the same rows, with a hard delete and no audit trail on the permissive one.

**Remedy.** Make `delete_task` agree with `delete_task_batch`: refuse when `task.createdById != current_user.id` unless `is_master_admin(current_user)`, keeping the master admin's override and the creator's own right to withdraw. If per-task deletion by any admin is genuinely intended, relax the batch guard to match instead — but the two must not state opposite rules, and the batch route's docstring argues the strict reading is the intended one. Either way, an audit log line on the single-task delete would be worth having, since the row is hard-deleted.

**What the verifier checked.** Read both routes verbatim; the quoted code is exact. I looked for the guard that would kill this — a creator check inside `require_task`, a role gate on `delete_task`, or a soft delete — and found none: `delete_task` takes `Depends(get_current_user)` and its only test is the quoted one-liner, and the write is `db.assignedtask.delete(where={"id": task_id})`. I verified the enumeration half rather than assuming it: `list_tasks` really does allow `view=all` for any admin and really does accept `batchId`, so an admin who is not the creator can obtain the member ids without guessing. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md. Downgraded in emphasis but not in verdict: the finder's severity of MINOR was already honest and I am keeping it.

---

### [MINOR] (backend) Re-requesting access to a workshop you were denied erases who denied it, when, and why — and leaves the row sorting in the admin queue at its original date

**Verdict:** PARTIAL

**Where.** backend/app/api/routes/workshops.py:463-470 and :486-499; queue at :526-542; against backend/prisma/schema.prisma:1331-1333, backend/app/services/workshop_access.py:9-11 and docs/PERMISSIONS.md:390-391

**What it does.** Quoted code verified verbatim. A DENIED or REVOKED row belonging to the caller is put on `to_rerequest` and then `update_many`'d to `status: PENDING` with `decidedById`, `decidedAt` and `decisionNote` all set to `None` in the same statement. Those three columns are the only record of a workshop-access decision in the schema; there is no history table beside `WorkshopAssignment`. The finder's mechanism is right but missed that this clearing is DELIBERATE and documented in the route's own docstring at :424-428 ("that is a NEW request: the previous decision is cleared so a stale 'denied by X' cannot be mistaken for this one's outcome"). The defect is therefore a three-way contradiction rather than an oversight — schema.prisma:1331-1333 ("kept rather than deleted so the decision stays auditable"), workshop_access.py:9-11 and docs/PERMISSIONS.md:390-391 all promise auditability the route destroys — plus a second inconsistency the finder did not name: `createdAt` is NOT refreshed, so a row the docstring calls "a NEW request" keeps its original timestamp and sorts by it in `GET /workshops/access-requests`, which is `order={"createdAt": "asc"}` (:540) and described as "oldest first". The finder's claim that `assignedById` clearing "reopens a curated workshop's roster test" is not a defect: :426-428 explains that clearing it is what prevents one approval silently re-closing a reopened workshop, and the finder's own remedy concedes this.

**How it fails.** An admin denies a CONTRIBUTE request with the note "embargoed until the ministry review closes". The requester opens the Workshop access page (frontend/components/settings/WorkshopAccessRequestPanel.tsx:118) and re-selects the same workshop; `POST /workshops/access-requests` returns RE_REQUESTED. A second admin opens the queue (`statusFilter=PENDING`, and `statusFilter=ALL`, described at :534 as "the full history for auditing") and sees a plain pending row with no decider, no decision note and the original date, sorted to the top as the oldest unanswered request.

**What it costs.** An admin-authored decision and its reason are permanently destroyed by the person they were made about, and the audit view that claims to hold the full history no longer does. No access boundary is crossed — a PENDING row confers nothing and an admin must still approve — which is why this is MINOR rather than MAJOR.

**Remedy.** Keep `decidedById`/`decidedAt`/`decisionNote` on the row and add `reRequestedAt`, so the queue can render "denied by X on the 3rd — asked again on the 9th"; `status == PENDING` already answers the docstring's worry that a stale decision be mistaken for this request's outcome. If the live row must be cleared, write a history row first (the pattern `dictation_consent.apply_decision` already uses). Whichever way it goes, refresh `createdAt`, or the "oldest first" queue mis-ranks a re-request as older than it is. `assignedById` clearing is correct and should stay. Correct schema.prisma:1331-1333, workshop_access.py:9-11 and docs/PERMISSIONS.md:390-391 in the same change.

**What the verifier checked.** Read workshops.py:408-505 in full — the loop, the `to_rerequest` update and every nulled column are exactly as quoted. Read the queue route at :526-542 and confirmed `order={"createdAt": "asc"}` with `createdAt` untouched by the update, which makes the finder's "sorts to the top as the oldest" claim true. Searched the schema for any decision-history model (`grep '^model'`) — none exists, so the finder's "only record" claim survives. Read schema.prisma:1329-1333, workshop_access.py:9-11 and PERMISSIONS.md:390-391 and confirmed all three assert auditability. Checked the client can actually reach it: WorkshopAccessRequestPanel.tsx:118 posts arbitrary selected workshop ids with no client-side block on a DENIED row. What kills the finder's severity: the route documents the clearing as intentional at :424-428, and the schema's other claim ("a user cannot silently re-request their way around a refusal") is NOT violated — re-requesting grants nothing. Downgraded MAJOR→MINOR and added the un-refreshed `createdAt` the finder missed.

---

### [MINOR] (backend) Renaming an answered custom section rewrites the heading its stored answers were given under, and the definition digest does not move — so a browser holding the definition never sees the new title at all

**Verdict:** PARTIAL

**Where.** backend/app/services/custom_sections.py:1797-1818 (the EDIT branch, which writes `title`, `description`, `sortOrder`), :1236-1280 (`plan_definition`, whose only outright refusal is the stage move at :1261-1269), :449-489 (`custom_schema_version`, whose parts string carries stage key, section key, field key/type/tier/required/label/unit/options/retired and section retired — and neither title, description nor sortOrder)

**What it does.** Both halves of the mechanism are as quoted. A `SectionPlan(action="EDIT")` is emitted for any stored section the payload names, with no comparison of `previous.title` to `section.title` anywhere in `plan_definition`, and `apply_definition_plan` writes the new title straight over the old; `bumps_revision` comes only from a field SUPERSEDE/RETIRE so `revision` does not move. `custom_schema_version` iterates fields only, so a section with a changed title (or a changed sort order, or no fields at all) digests byte-identically. The finder's stated CONSEQUENCE for the handset is REFUTED: `dwCustomDefinition` (android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/DwCustomSectionStore.kt:246-259) refetches the definition unconditionally on every screen open with a connection and falls back to the cache only on failure, and the digest comparison at StageIndexScreen.kt:336-337 decides only whether the SERVER'S COMPLETENESS SCORE may be adopted — a decision the title has no part in. The real consequence is on the web, and it is sharper than the finder said: `fetchCustomDefinition` (frontend/lib/customSections.ts:602-607) and `adoptCustomDefinition` (:637-641) explicitly DISCARD a freshly fetched definition and return the previously cached object when the digest is equal, for identity reasons.

**How it fails.** A designer adds a section keyed `shed` titled "Loom shed" at stage 5, and the answers are recorded. A colleague retitles it "Weaver shed" (or drags it above another section, changing only `sortOrder`) on the web. The PUT is accepted 200 with `superseded: 0`, `retired: 0`, and the stored answers untouched. Any other browser tab that had already loaded that workshop's definition refetches it, compares digests, finds them equal and keeps rendering "Loom shed" over the same answers for the life of the tab, while the server-generated .docx prints "Weaver shed" (report_custom_sections.py:377, `doc.heading(heading or item.title, ...)`) and orders the annexure by the new `sort_order` (report_templates.py:616-619).

**What it costs.** Two surfaces print different headings, and different running orders, over one set of answers, with no signal on either that they disagree — the exact staleness the digest exists to detect, reached through the fields the digest does not cover. The re-attribution half (an answer given under "Loom shed → How many?" reading as "Weaver shed → How many?") is a real inconsistency with the module's own supersede rule for field labels, but no answer is lost and the edit is the designer's own deliberate act, which is why this is MINOR rather than the MAJOR the finder assigned.

**Remedy.** The load-bearing half is one line: put `s.title` and `s.sort_order` into `custom_schema_version`'s parts string (and emit a part for a section with no fields, or a titled empty section still digests to nothing). That makes every edit a client RENDERS invalidate a cached copy, which is the rule the docstring at :455-462 already argues for the field label. The refusal half is a product decision rather than a defect: if section titles are to be evidence like field labels, add the `previous.title.strip() != section.title.strip() and answered_here & {f.key for f in previous.fields}` check to `plan_definition`'s EDIT branch, raising `CustomSectionEditError` (409) in the same shape as the stage-move refusal at :1264-1269. Both are testable in tests/test_custom_sections.py with no Postgres.

**What the verifier checked.** Read plan_definition (:1163-1300), apply_definition_plan (:1760-1850) and custom_schema_version (:449-489) in full; the quoted code is exact and there is no title comparison anywhere in the planner. Tried to kill the staleness claim on the phone and succeeded: DwCustomSectionStore.dwCustomDefinition refetches unconditionally, so the handset picks the new title up on the next open with signal; StageIndexScreen's `serverScoreUsable` gate is about completeness arithmetic, not about copy. Tried to kill it on the web and failed — fetchCustomDefinition returns the CACHED object on an equal digest by design, so the fresh title is thrown away. Verified the title is what the renderer prints (report_custom_sections.py:377) and that sort_order decides the running order (report_templates.py:616-619). Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md (the open custom-sections entry is last-write-wins over the whole PUT, a different defect). Severity lowered to MINOR: no answer is lost and the phone is not affected.

### [MINOR] (backend) `save_stage` builds the `stored` echo block and never returns it, so three docstrings and the hydration comment describe a response field that does not exist on the wire

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** backend/app/services/design_workshops.py:914 (`stored: dict[str, Any] = {}`), :1085-1086 (`bucket = stored.setdefault(entity.key, []); bucket.append(clean)`), :1088-1092 (the comment claiming it is "echoed back to the client"), :866 ("returning what was stored"), :1261-1305 (the return, which has no `stored` key); backend/app/api/routes/design_workshops.py:1020 ("Returns the cleaned data as stored")

**What it does.** Every quotation is exact. `stored` is populated for every non-`_custom` entry of every save and then dropped on the floor: the returned dict carries `stageKey, saved, created, updated, removed, errors, refusedAnswers, droppedKeys, droppedCustomKeys, completeness, transcriptionsQueued, transcriptionConsentRefusal, schemaVersion, customSchemaVersion` and nothing else. So the variable is dead code that was meant to run, and three comments assert a wire behaviour the endpoint does not have. Android has already measured the absence and written it down as fact (android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/DwStageRefusal.kt:27-31, "The save response does not carry it — measured", listing the same keys) and built the three-state `DwHeld.UNRECORDED` around the gap.

**How it fails.** A designer picks a reference on a stage row and saves. The client hydrated locally from the published mapping; the server hydrates again through `coerce_value` (design_workshops.py:739), which can normalise a MONEY value, drop an ENUM token the target field does not admit, and attach a photograph resolved by `_reference_photos` — so the two copies can legitimately differ. The 200 says how many rows were written and nothing about what is in them, so the screen goes on showing the client's guess until a `GET /{id}/stages/{key}` is made. On the phone the same gap forces `DwStageRefusal` to report UNRECORDED for what the repository now holds under a refused key.

**What it costs.** A designer cannot see what was actually written without a second round trip they often will not make on one bar of signal; the handset's refusal surface is permanently one state weaker than it needs to be; and the module's comments send the next maintainer looking for a bug in the client. Nothing is corrupted, which is why MINOR is honest.

**Remedy.** Either return it — `"stored": stored` in the response dict, declared optional on `DwSaveResult` (frontend/lib/designWorkshops.ts) and `StageSaveResultDto` (android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/StageSchema.kt); it is additive and both clients ignore unknown keys — or delete the variable and correct routes/design_workshops.py:1020, services/design_workshops.py:866 and the comment at :1088-1092. Returning it is the better half: it is already computed, and it is exactly what `DwHeld.UNRECORDED` was invented to work around. Note if it is returned that `stored` is keyed by entity with rows in payload order and carries no row identity, so it wants `_clientKey`/`_entryId` alongside each entry or a client cannot address it to a row.

**What the verifier checked.** Read save_stage end to end. Confirmed `stored` has exactly two write sites and no read site, and that the returned dict (:1261-1305) has no `stored` key. Confirmed the route (:1013-1040) returns `result` unchanged on the success path, so nothing enriches the body between the service and the client. Confirmed the Android note at DwStageRefusal.kt:27-31 quotes the same key list (it omits `refusedAnswers` and `transcriptionConsentRefusal`, added later — immaterial to the claim). Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

### [MINOR] (backend) `attach_report_custom_sections` warns that an unanswered section is "not in this file" for exactly the sections the renderer does print

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** backend/app/services/design_workshops.py:2123-2137, against report_custom_sections.py:140-152 (`has_content`) and :373 (`if item is None or not item.has_content: return 0`)

**What it does.** Exact as quoted, and the two tests genuinely differ. Everything in `items` is attached (:2121) and spliced into the template unconditionally (report_templates.py:615-654, one `CUSTOM_SECTION` block per definition section); whether it PRINTS is decided solely by `append_custom_section`'s `has_content`, which is true when any field holds an answer OR any live field is required — deliberately, so an unanswered required question prints "Not recorded." and its absence is visible. The warning uses `not item.answered_count and any(not f.retired for f in item.fields)`, which is true for exactly that class of section. The two disagree on precisely one set: a section with zero answers and at least one live required field.

**How it fails.** A designer adds "Dye bath log" at stage 5 with one required BASIC field "Dye source" and two optional ones, and does not reach it during the workshop. They generate the report: the .docx contains the heading "Dye bath log" with "Dye source — Not recorded." under it, while the warnings returned beside the download (routes/design_workshops.py:3248-3251, folded into the response `warnings` at :3258-3262) say "1 of this workshop's own section(s) have no answers recorded and are not in this file: Dye bath log".

**What it costs.** The designer is told a block is missing from a document that contains it, so they either hunt for a bug that is not there or — having been told the section did not print — submit the file believing the empty heading is absent from what the ministry reads. The warning's own comment says its purpose is the difference between "the feature is broken" and "we did not get to those questions"; as written it manufactures the first. MINOR: no data is wrong, only the sentence about it.

**Remedy.** Ask the renderer's question: `unanswered = [item for item in items if not item.has_content]`. `has_content` is a property of `CustomSectionItem`, already imported here, so it is a one-expression change that makes the warning exactly the set the builder will skip. The sentence stays correct for the genuinely absent case (all-optional sections nobody answered) and stops firing for sections that print "Not recorded.".

**What the verifier checked.** Read the comprehension, `has_content`, `printed_fields` and `append_custom_section`. Checked whether an attached section could fail to print for some other reason, which would make the warning right by accident: the only gates are `has_content` and `custom_section_of` returning None for an unattached key, and `apply_report_settings` splices a block for every section it is handed regardless of content. Checked the retired arms both ways: a retired-and-unanswered section is dropped before `items` (:2107-2118), and a section whose only answers sit under retired fields has `answered_count > 0` so neither test fires. The mismatch is exactly the required-and-unanswered case. Confirmed the warnings reach the caller. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

### [MINOR] (backend) The server honours a collection sweep on an entity whose own entries say `merge: true` — two contradictory claims in one payload, and the sweep is the destructive half

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED · found by the verifier, not the original audit

**Where.** backend/app/services/design_workshops.py:991 (`touched_entities.add(entity.key)`, added regardless of `entry.merge`) and :1181-1197 (the sweep over `(touched_entities | emptiedEntities) & collection_keys`), against schemas/design_workshops.py:158-179 (`StageEntryIn.merge`) and :198-207 (`StageSaveIn.replaceCollections`, `default=True`)

**What it does.** `merge: true` is documented in the schema's own words as "I am sending every key I HAVE, not every key there IS", to be set "when, and only when, the client knows it has not seen the server's copy". `replaceCollections: true` — the value an ABSENT key carries — means the entities named are replaced wholesale and rows the payload does not name are soft-deleted. A payload can assert both at once and the server obeys the second: `entry.merge` is read at exactly one place (:1057, the key-level merge) and has no effect on `touched_entities` or on the sweep.

**How it fails.** Precisely the shipped Android defect closed on 2026-08-13 (docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md, "the handset's whole sweep gate was spelled as silence"): the phone sent `merge: true` with `replaceCollections` omitted, the server read the absent key as true, and three rows the phone had never downloaded were soft-deleted under a 200 reporting `removed: 3`. That client bug was fixed by removing a kotlinx default; the server-side contradiction that turned it into row deletion is untouched, and the same shape is reachable from any other client, an older build, or a direct API caller that sets merge and leaves `replaceCollections` at its default.

**What it costs.** The server's most destructive operation is armed by the ABSENCE of a key, in a payload whose entries are simultaneously telling it the client has not read the collection. Nothing is wrong today because both shipped clients now spell the flag explicitly, which is why this is MINOR — but it is the one guard that would have made that BLOCKER impossible from the server's side, and the register's own lesson was that the never-read question had been asked of a payload's CONTENTS three times and never of the mechanism that decides which ROWS survive.

**Remedy.** Exclude from the sweep any entity whose entries in this payload carried `merge: true`: track `merged_entities: set[str]` beside `touched_entities` in the entry loop, then `sweep_entities = ((touched_entities - merged_entities) | set(payload.emptiedEntities)) & collection_keys`. An `emptiedEntities` name stays honoured, because it is an explicit statement about a whole collection rather than an inference from silence. Pin it with a test that sends one merged entry with `replaceCollections` omitted and asserts `removed == 0` with every unnamed row still live.
