### [MINOR] (backend) Pressing Test on a managed secret whose stored override cannot be decrypted probes the ENVIRONMENT value and stamps that verdict on the database row, erasing the "re-enter this key" flag

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** backend/app/services/managed_secrets.py:712-747 (`test_secret`, especially the `if described["source"] == SOURCE_DATABASE` branch at 734-743), against :415-457 (`refresh_if_stale`, which skips undecryptable rows at 435-438 and writes the FAILED/undecryptable flag at 445-457), :486-489 (`get_secret` → `peek_secret` → environment fallback) and :598-636 (`_describe`, `stored = row is not None` at 606). Reachable from backend/app/api/routes/secrets.py:105-117 and backend/app/api/routes/settings.py:294-334

**What it does.** The three readings really do disagree exactly as described. `refresh_if_stale` puts a row it cannot decrypt into `undecryptable` and does NOT add it to `_overrides` (435-438), so `peek_secret` falls through to `env_value` and `get_secret` returns the ENVIRONMENT key. `_describe` sets `source = SOURCE_DATABASE` purely from `stored = row is not None` (606-608). `test_secret` probes `value` (the environment key) and then, because `described["source"]` says database, writes `lastStatus`/`lastCheckedAt`/`lastError` onto the ManagedSecret row — overwriting the `_STATUS_FAILED` + `_UNDECRYPTABLE_ERROR` that `refresh_if_stale` wrote at 450-457. The flag is not restored until the override cache goes stale and `refresh_if_stale` runs again (it early-returns while the cache is fresh, 421-422), so the row genuinely alternates between OK and FAILED depending on how recently Test was pressed. `list_managed_secrets`' docstring (644-655) names this exact end state as the failure it exists to prevent: a row "indistinguishable from a healthy override, while the environment value was quietly being used instead".

**How it fails.** `SECRETS_ENCRYPTION_KEY` is unset and `JWT_SECRET` is rotated on deploy, so every stored override becomes undecryptable while the environment still carries working keys. The Settings hub flags the provider FAILED with the re-enter message. The master admin presses Test; the probe runs against the environment key, passes, and the row is stamped `lastStatus = OK`, `lastError = None`. The screen now says the stored key is good, so nobody re-enters it. Minutes later the cache expires, `refresh_if_stale` re-flags it FAILED, and the screen contradicts itself.

**What it costs.** The one durable signal telling an operator which overrides must be re-entered after a key rotation is erased by the button meant to check them, and replaced by a verdict about a different value. The secondary consequence checks out too: `_provider_verification` (backend/app/api/routes/settings.py:174-200) maps `lastStatus == "OK"` to `keyState = PASSING` and `rankable = state == STT_KEY_PASSING`, so the stamped OK also thaws the STT provider freeze on the strength of a key the stored row does not contain. MINOR rather than higher because it needs a specific operational precondition (an undecryptable override with a working environment fallback behind it), nothing is destroyed that a re-entry cannot restore, and the flag returns on the next cache expiry.

**Remedy.** Decide where the verdict belongs from where the value actually came, not from whether a row exists. In `test_secret`, check the override cache directly (`key in _overrides`) — or teach `_describe` to distinguish `database` from `database-unreadable` — and when the stored override did not decrypt, leave the row's `lastStatus`/`lastError` alone and record the outcome through `record_environment_verdict` instead, since the environment value is what was probed. Return the true provenance in the response so the panel can say "tested the environment key; the stored one still needs re-entering". The same guard fixes the `value is None` path, which currently writes `lastError = "Not configured"` over the undecryptable message when there is no environment fallback either.

**What the verifier checked.** Read managed_secrets.py:396-489 and 587-757 in full; every quoted line is exact. I attacked the two joints that could have killed it. First, whether `get_secret` really returns the environment value for an undecryptable row: it does — `decrypt` returning None sends the row to `undecryptable` and `fresh` never gains the key, and `peek_secret` returns `env_value(key)` when `_overrides.get(key)` is falsy. Second, whether some caller re-asserts the flag before the admin sees the OK: `refresh_if_stale` early-returns while `_cache_is_fresh()`, and its re-flag loop additionally `continue`s only when the row ALREADY holds FAILED + the undecryptable error — after `test_secret` has overwritten it that guard no longer matches, so the flag is rewritten on the next stale refresh, which is what produces the alternation rather than a permanent erasure. I verified the STT claim by reading settings.py:144-205 rather than trusting it: `rankable` is `state == STT_KEY_PASSING` and `state` becomes PASSING solely from `entry.get("lastStatus") == "OK"`. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

---

### [MINOR] (backend) A designer's custom questions ignore the template's capture tier, so a Standard- or Advanced-tier custom field prints in the template whose description promises "Basic-tier fields only"

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** backend/app/services/report_custom_sections.py:83-91 (`CustomReportField` carries no tier) and :126-138 / :149-152 (`printed_fields`, `has_content` — no tier test); the loader that drops it is backend/app/services/design_workshops.py:2077-2104; the rule it escapes is backend/app/services/report_builder.py:725-727; the stored value is backend/app/services/custom_sections.py:279

```python
# custom_sections.py:279 — the designer's choice IS stored
    tier: Tier = Tier.STANDARD

# design_workshops.py:2078-2101 — six attributes copied, tier not among them
                CustomReportField(
                    key=f.key, label=f.label, type=f.type.value, unit=f.unit,
                    options=tuple((o.value, o.display) for o in f.options),
                    required=f.required, retired=f.retired or section.retired,
                )

# report_builder.py:725-727 — the gate every registry field passes
        return not spec.deprecated and spec.tier.rank <= self.template.max_tier.rank
```

**What it does.** Verified end to end. `CustomFieldSpec.tier` defaults to `Tier.STANDARD` (custom_sections.py:279), is a real designer choice in the editor (CustomSectionsEditor.tsx:773-785, "Which capture tier this question belongs to"), is validated (custom_sections.py:654-657) and is serialised to both clients (:1525, :1747). `attach_report_custom_sections` copies six attributes and not that one, so `CustomReportField` has no tier to test, and `custom_section_blocks` prints every `printed_fields` entry. COMPACT_SUMMARY is the only template with `max_tier=Tier.BASIC` (report_templates.py:412); every other template defaults to ADVANCED (:171), so the blast radius is that one template.

**How it fails.** A designer adds a block of their own questions to a stage — the editor's default tier is Standard — answers them, and generates the COMPACT_SUMMARY report for a review meeting. That template correctly suppresses every Standard and Advanced registry field via `_visible`, and prints the designer's Standard-tier answers in full, spliced in after the anchor stage's section (report_templates.py:615-654). Note the required ones would print under any rule: `custom_sections.py:654-657` refuses a required field above Basic, so only optional Standard/Advanced custom fields are affected.

**What it costs.** The template's own description — "Basic-tier fields only, one photograph per prototype" (report_templates.py:408-411) — is false for the part of the report the designer authored, and a stored, designer-chosen attribute is read by nothing at render time. One document applies two rules to one declared attribute. Small: it makes a short report longer, and prints answers the designer did record; nothing is wrong or missing.

**Remedy.** Add `tier: str = "STANDARD"` to `CustomReportField`, populate it at design_workshops.py:2078-2101 from `f.tier.value`, and filter in `CustomSectionItem.printed_fields` and `has_content` against the template's `max_tier` — which means `append_custom_section` must be passed the tier, as `numbered` already is. The finder's note that the scorer needs no change is correct and I verified it: `stage_completeness` (stage_schema.py:1249-1340) has no tier test at all and reads only `key`, `label`, `required` and `retired` off a custom field (:1271-1275), so filtering the renderer cannot desynchronise the completeness annexure any more than it already is for registry fields. Alternatively, decide that a custom field's tier governs CAPTURE only and say so on the field — but then `_visible` and this renderer must be documented as answering different questions.

**What the verifier checked.** Read all four cited sites; the quotes are accurate. Tried to refute three ways. (1) Looked for a tier filter elsewhere in the custom-section path: grepped `tier` across report_custom_sections.py — zero hits, so the value is not merely unused, it is unrepresentable in that module. (2) Looked for a written decision making custom fields deliberately tier-blind in the report: nothing in report_custom_sections.py, nothing in the `attach_report_custom_sections` docstring (design_workshops.py:2035-2058), and `grep -i tier docs/PLAN-AI-TIERS-AND-CUSTOM-SECTIONS.md` returns nothing about capture tiers or custom fields — unlike findings 2 and 3, this behaviour is written down nowhere. (3) Checked the blast radius honestly: only COMPACT_SUMMARY has a non-ADVANCED `max_tier`, and required custom fields are forced to BASIC by validation, so the finder's MINOR is the right severity, not higher. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

---

### [MINOR] (backend) The questionnaire annexure prints a "Not recorded." row for a RETIRED required question that a sitting never answered, contradicting the rule the loader's own comment states

**Verdict:** PARTIAL

**Where.** backend/app/services/report_questionnaires.py:127-134 (`QuestionnaireAnswer.prints`), :250-255 (`_question_runs`), :266-267 (`_answer_runs`); rows are built for every (section, question) pair at backend/app/services/questionnaire_forms.py:369-382; the intended rule is written at questionnaire_forms.py:392-394 and implemented in the sibling module at backend/app/services/report_custom_sections.py:135-138

```python
# report_questionnaires.py:134
        return self.has_answer or self.is_required

# questionnaire_forms.py:378-379 — every pair becomes an answer row, retired ones included
                            is_required=bool(question.isRequired),
                            is_retired=not question.isActive,

# questionnaire_forms.py:392-394 — the rule the code above breaks, in the same file
                # The questions the form ASKS today. Retired ones are printed where they carry an
                # answer but are not counted here, because this number is what a reader compares
                # against the instrument, and the instrument no longer contains them.

# report_custom_sections.py:135-138 — the same rule, correctly implemented
        return tuple(
            f for f in self.fields
            if not f.retired or _has_answer(self.values.get(f.key))
        )
```

**What it does.** `prints` is `has_answer or is_required` and never consults `is_retired`. `report_items` deliberately materialises an answer row for every (section, question) pair including retired questions (questionnaire_forms.py:381), `supersede_question` copies `isRequired` onto the replacement and leaves it set on the retired original (questionnaire_forms.py:968, :972-975), the plain retire path leaves it set too (:888-891), and `save_answers` refuses new answers to a retired question (:1042-1048). So a required question that was reworded or retired is permanently `is_required=True, has_answer=False, is_retired=True` for every sitting that did not answer the OLD wording, and `_answer_runs` emits the italic "Not recorded." for it. The row IS marked, though — `_question_runs` (:250-255) appends the italic " (no longer asked)" to the question cell — which is the part the finder's consequence omits.

**How it fails.** A designer builds a questionnaire with a required question "How many looms do you own?" and records eight sittings. One respondent answers it, so `guard_question_edit` (questionnaire_forms.py:944-958) returns "supersede" when the designer rewords it, and the API correctly retires the original and creates a replacement carrying the same `isRequired`. Twelve more sittings are then recorded; the app will not accept an answer to the old wording (`save_answers` raises). On generating any report, each of the twenty sittings' tables carries an extra row reading `How many looms do you own? (no longer asked) | Not recorded.` — for nineteen of them the respondent was never asked the question at all. The same happens to a required question that is simply retired after being answered once.

**What it costs.** Noise rather than a false claim: the row says in the same cell that the question is no longer asked, so a reader is not told a live required question was skipped. What is wrong is (a) a respondent who was never shown the question is recorded as having left it blank, in a document whose stated editorial purpose (report_questionnaires.py:88-92) is that a blank means "a gap in the fieldwork"; (b) `_questionnaire_provenance` prints `question_count`, which excludes retired questions (questionnaire_forms.py:395), so the provenance line under a table can say "8 question(s)" over eleven rows; (c) the rows consume the `MAX_ROWS_PER_SITTING` = 400 budget. No sitting is invented — `printed_sittings` (report_questionnaires.py:197-204) still requires a real answer — and nothing is lost.

**Remedy.** Make `prints` agree with the rule its own loader states and with `report_custom_sections.printed_fields`: `return self.has_answer or (self.is_required and not self.is_retired)`. Then add the missing intersection test to backend/tests/test_report_questionnaire_annexure.py — it has the retired case (:220-235) and the required-blank case (:238-258) and nothing that combines them — asserting that a retired, required, unanswered question produces no row at all.

**What the verifier checked.** Read every cited line on disk; all quotes are verbatim. Tried to kill it four ways. (1) Looked for a guard that stops `is_required` and `is_retired` co-existing: `supersede_question` explicitly propagates `isRequired` (:968) and never clears it on the original (:972-975); the retire path (:888-891) writes only isActive/retiredAt. None. (2) Looked for a caller-side filter between `report_items` and `prints`: `printed_answers` (:172-173) is the only consumer and applies `prints` alone. None. (3) Checked reachability of retirement: `guard_question_edit` (:944-958) makes supersede the mandatory path for rewording an answered question, so it is normal use, not an exotic state. (4) Checked docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md — nothing about the report or the questionnaire annexure is recorded there. What I did find that the finder missed, and that cuts both ways: `_question_runs` marks the row "(no longer asked)", so the annexure does not silently assert a skipped required question — which is why I downgrade MAJOR to MINOR; and questionnaire_forms.py:392-394 states the intended rule ("Retired ones are printed where they carry an answer") in the same file, which is stronger evidence of a defect than anything the finder cited.

---

### [MINOR] (backend) The table-of-contents, photographic-annexure and completeness-annexure toggles can only remove a section, so turning one on is inert on the templates that do not already carry it

**Verdict:** PARTIAL

**Where.** backend/app/services/report_templates.py:502-506 (`_SECTION_TOGGLES`) and :580-582 (the only consultation); the sections are declared only in DETAILED_TECHNICAL at :446-451 and TOC in `_standard_sections` at :268 and IMPLEMENTING_AGENCY at :380; the fields are at backend/app/services/stage_definitions.py:1579, :1582, :1591

```python
# report_templates.py:580-582 — removal is the only operation
    for key, special in _SECTION_TOGGLES.items():
        if _asked(settings, key) is False:
            sections = [s for s in sections if s.special is not special]
```

**What it does.** Confirmed: `grep 'SpecialSection.TOC|ANNEXURE_MEDIA|COMPLETENESS'` over backend/app returns only the builder's three dispatch arms (report_builder.py:1929, :1935, :2033), the four template declarations, the toggle map and this removal loop — plus the two `next((i … COMPLETENESS), len(sections))` anchors used to splice OTHER sections in. Nothing inserts any of the three. The Android port is identical (ReportTemplates.kt:623-626), so the two surfaces agree. Where the finder is wrong is in calling it an oversight: the semantics are written down as removal-only in the designer-facing ledger — ReportSettings.kt:327-328, :333-336 and :343-347 each end "An explicit false removes the section; absent leaves the template alone." So this is a knowingly half-implemented switch, not a forgotten branch, and it is consistent across both clients.

**How it fails.** A designer on the default DCH_STANDARD is asked for the completeness table, finds "Include the completeness annexure" in stage 20, switches it on and generates. The .docx has no completeness annexure. I checked `render_report` (design_workshops.py:2324-2373) for a warning path and there is none for this case: `warnings` carries load warnings, dropped-image warnings and font warnings only, so nothing in `X-Report-Warnings` names the toggle or the template. Same for "Include the photographic annexure" on all five non-DETAILED_TECHNICAL templates, and "Include a table of contents" on COMPACT_SUMMARY and PHOTO_CATALOGUE (verified those two lack `SpecialSection.TOC`, :413-435 and :462-484).

**What it costs.** A stage-20 switch that does nothing on five of six templates, with no signal anywhere — which is the failure `apply_report_settings`'s own docstring (:535-541) says it exists to end. But nothing is lost, nothing wrong is printed, and the designer can reach every one of the three sections by choosing DETAILED_TECHNICAL, so this is MINOR rather than the finder's MAJOR: it is a missing capability with a workaround, not wrong behaviour or wrong data.

**Remedy.** Two acceptable fixes; the finder named only the larger one. (a) Give each toggle an insertion branch beside the removal one, using the existing `next((i for i, s in enumerate(sections) if s.special is SpecialSection.COMPLETENESS), len(sections))` idiom already used at :650-654 and :682-686 — TOC after COVER, ANNEXURE_MEDIA before COMPLETENESS, COMPLETENESS last — and port it to ReportTemplates.kt:623-626 in the same edit, since the 485 KB `report_templates_pin.json` compares the two by value. (b) Cheaper and honest: append a generation warning naming the toggle and the template when `_asked(settings, key) is True` and no section of that kind survives, and add help text to the three fields at stage_definitions.py:1579/1582/1591 saying which templates carry them. Either is fine; the current silence is not.

**What the verifier checked.** Grepped the three enum members across backend/app and confirmed there is no insertion site anywhere — the only occurrences are the enum, the three builder dispatch arms, the four template declarations, the toggle map and the removal loop. Confirmed by reading which templates declare them: TOC in `_standard_sections` (:268) and IMPLEMENTING_AGENCY (:380), so COMPACT_SUMMARY and PHOTO_CATALOGUE lack it; ANNEXURE_MEDIA and COMPLETENESS only in DETAILED_TECHNICAL (:446-451). Checked `_asked` (:509-522) — the tri-state argument justifies absent-≠-false but says nothing about true-≠-insert, so it is not the guard. Checked the whole warning path in `render_report` (design_workshops.py:2324-2373) for a signal and found none. Checked docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md — not recorded. What I found that changes the verdict from CONFIRMED to PARTIAL: the removal-only semantics ARE written down, three times, in ReportSettings.kt:327-328/:333-336/:343-347, and the Android port implements the same rule (ReportTemplates.kt:623-626), so this is a stated limitation with a workaround rather than a broken feature — MAJOR is not honest for it.

### [MINOR] (backend) analyse_cost_ratios drops any cluster whose cost sheets are all uncostable, and the count it takes of them is written and never read

**Verdict:** PARTIAL

**Where.** backend/app/services/workshop_analytics.py:556-558 (the skip), :568-569 (the group loop), :951-969 (the notes block) and :971-997 (ArchiveFindings); reached from backend/app/api/routes/analytics.py:177-179

**What it does.** The mechanism is exactly as described. `uncostable: Counter[str]` is incremented at line 558 (`if margin.verdict == "NOT_COMPUTABLE" or not margin.total_cost: uncostable[cluster] += 1; continue`) but the group list is built by `for cluster, entries in by_cluster.items():` at 569 — and `by_cluster` only gains a key at 564, inside the branch a skipped sheet never reaches. `uncostable[cluster]` is read only at 584-590, inside that same loop, so for a cluster with no costable sheet at all the counter is written and never read. I confirmed the trigger in `cost_integrity.compute_margin` (backend/app/services/cost_integrity.py:376-388): a missing `expectedPrice` alone returns NOT_COMPUTABLE with the reason "no expected price is recorded". I also confirmed the exclusion is announced nowhere else: `notes` (951-969) covers superseded rows, the three missing dimensions and `sheets_without_cluster` only, `cautions` (909-949) covers coverage, truncation, concentration and `rows_without_status` only, and `ArchiveFindings` (971-997) has no uncostable total. `cost_sheets_without_cluster` is a different quantity — it counts sheets whose workshop has a blank `clusterName` (line 553-555).

**How it fails.** Every workshop in one cluster records a full cost sheet with material and labour heads but leaves "Expected price" blank, because the price had not been agreed. `compute_margin` returns NOT_COMPUTABLE for all of them, so the cluster never enters `by_cluster`, produces no `CostRatioGroup`, and appears nowhere in `costRatios`. `coverage.workshopsWithCostSheets` still counts those workshops (line 901), `sheetsWithoutCluster` stays 0, and `notes` says nothing.

**What it costs.** On the admin analytics page a cluster that did the costing but not the pricing reads identically to a cluster that recorded no cost sheets at all. It is narrower than the finder made it sound: a cluster with even ONE costable sheet does get a group and its message already ends with "N further sheet(s) could not be costed at all" (line 586-587), so only the all-uncostable cluster disappears. Nothing is corrupted or lost from the database, no report renders a wrong figure, and the totals strip still counts the workshops — it is a silent omission from one comparison section on one admin page, which is MINOR rather than MAJOR, and it is not data loss (the finder's category).

**Remedy.** Iterate `sorted(set(by_cluster) | set(uncostable))` at line 569 and give the all-uncostable case its own branch: `sheets=0`, `ratio=None`, `uncostable=uncostable[cluster]`, message "<cluster>: N cost sheet(s) recorded, none of which can be costed — no expected price is entered on any of them". `CostRatioGroup` already carries `uncostable`, and the sort key at 594 already puts ratio-less groups last, so no shape change is needed. Optionally also add a `cost_sheets_uncostable` total to `ArchiveFindings` and emit a `notes` line for it beside the `sheets_without_cluster` one at 965-969.

**What the verifier checked.** Read 537-595, 880-997 and the payload serialiser's fields at 1039-1091. I tried to kill it by finding a second reader of the counter — grepped `uncostable` across the module: five hits, all inside `analyse_cost_ratios` plus the dataclass field and the payload key at 1091, which serialises `group.uncostable` and therefore only exists for clusters that made it into `groups`. I checked `cautions` and `notes` line by line for any archive-level uncostable message: there is none. I verified via cost_integrity.py:374-388 that a blank expectedPrice really is enough to trigger NOT_COMPUTABLE, so the finder's premise about common data holds. I did NOT run the module, so I am not repeating the finder's claimed reproduction output as my own; the code path is unambiguous on reading. Downgraded to MINOR and re-categorised (silent under-reporting on an analytics surface, not data loss) — that correction is why this is PARTIAL rather than CONFIRMED.

### [MINOR] (frontend) Every 404 from the stage read is reported as "That stage does not exist in this build's field registry", which is the one cause it cannot be by the time that line runs

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/[id]/stages/[stageKey]/page.tsx:518, :546, :548, :616-622; backend/app/api/routes/design_workshops.py:994-1001; frontend/lib/api.ts:183-198 (`ApiError` carries the server's own `detail`)

**What it does.** `spec` is resolved from `loaded.registry.stages` at :518 and the effect returns at :546 (`if (!canReconcile || !spec || !draft.remoteId) return;`) when it is missing, so the request at :548 is only ever made when the stage IS in this build's registry. The message at :618 therefore describes a state that has already been excluded. The server has exactly two 404s on this route, with two readable and different `detail` strings: `load_workshop_or_404` (routes :997) answers "Record not found", and the unknown-stage branch (routes :999-1001) answers "Unknown stage". `apiFetch` puts whichever arrived into `ApiError.message` (api.ts:186), and the ternary at :617-621 discards it for 404 alone and substitutes a fixed sentence. For the "Record not found" case the substitute names the wrong subsystem entirely; for the "Unknown stage" case it is exactly backwards — the stage exists in this build's registry and is missing from the SERVER's.

**How it fails.** A designer opens a stage of a workshop she is not a viewer of — the forwarded-link case above, or after an admin removes her viewer row mid-season. `load_workshop_or_404` answers 404 "Record not found". The stage page tells her the stage does not exist in this build's field registry, printed at :1228 alongside the amber "This device has never downloaded this stage" notice at :1243 and a fully editable, empty form below.

**What it costs.** The designer is told she has an out-of-date app and sent to look for an update or to ask why the field list changed, when the real answer is that she may not open this workshop at all — an access problem an admin could fix in one click on /workshop-access/manage. On a metered rural connection the misdirection costs a reinstall.

**Remedy.** Drop the 404 special case and let `err.message` through — the server's own sentence is the honest one — or branch on the detail: keep the registry wording only when the message is "Unknown stage" (and reword it, since even then the stage is missing from the SERVER's registry, not this build's), and otherwise say "That design workshop could not be opened: it does not exist, or it is not one this account may see." The `err instanceof ApiError && err.status === 404` test at :617 already has the object in hand.

**What the verifier checked.** Read :495-621 of the stage page as one block, not just the cited lines, to check the finder had not misread the control flow. The `return` at :546 does guard the request, and `spec` at :518 is `loaded.registry.stages.find(...)` — this build's registry, exactly as claimed. Read the backend route at design_workshops.py:994-1001 and confirmed both 404 sites and both detail strings. Read `apiFetch` (api.ts:183-186) and confirmed `describeApiDetail(detail, ...)` puts the server's detail into `ApiError.message`, so the honest sentence really is available and really is thrown away. I checked whether the 404 branch might be unreachable behind the earlier `isUnreachable` and `>= 500` branches (:610, :613) — it is not; 404 falls through to :616. Severity: I agree with MINOR — it is a misleading message, not lost or wrong data, and the surrounding banners give a designer other clues. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

---

### [MINOR] (frontend) List pages with no fetch-generation guard let a stale response land last and win, leaving the Next button dead — but it is four pages, not three

**Verdict:** PARTIAL

**Where.** frontend/app/(protected)/products/page.tsx:40-57, :59-63, :221; frontend/app/(protected)/tools/page.tsx:52-68, :70-75, :231; frontend/app/(protected)/processes/page.tsx:68-90, :332; frontend/app/(protected)/media/page.tsx:200-216, :566; contrast artisans/page.tsx:39-65, questionnaires/page.tsx:68-83, design-workshops/page.tsx:159-184, questionnaire/page.tsx:170-176; frontend/components/Pagination.tsx

**What it does.** The mechanism is real and the quoted code is accurate. Products, tools and media each call `setData(await listResource(...))` in resolution order with no generation ref anywhere in the file, while four other list pages carry the guard with a comment explaining it (`const currentLoad = useRef(0)` / `const generation = (currentLoad.current += 1)` / `if (generation !== currentLoad.current) return;`).

**The finder undercounted: it is four pages, not three.** `frontend/app/(protected)/processes/page.tsx` has the identical shape — `async function load(pageToLoad = page)` at :68 with a bare `setData(await listResource<ProcessRecord>("/processes", …))` at :70-79, the same `useEffect(…, [funnelReady, page, applied, funnel])` at :86-90, and `<Pagination page={data.page} …>` at :332. A grep for `currentLoad|generation` across that file returns nothing.

The stuck-Next mechanism holds on all four: `<Pagination>` is driven by the RESPONSE (`page={data.page}`) while the refetch effect is driven by STATE (`page`), and `Pagination` computes `onPage(page + 1)` from the page it was rendered with. With `data.page === 1` and `page === 2`, Next calls `setPage(2)`, React bails on the identical value, no dependency changes, and no request is issued.

**How it fails.** On a slow link a designer types into the Products search; the 350 ms debounce fires request A for page 1. Before it lands they click Next, issuing request B for page 2, which returns first and renders. A then lands and overwrites it with page 1. `data.page` is now 1 while `page` state is 2; pressing Next calls `setPage(2)` — the value already held — so nothing re-renders, nothing refetches, and the button does nothing until Previous is pressed first.

**What it costs.** The table shows results for a query or a page already moved past, and the pager reaches a state where forward navigation silently stops responding, indistinguishable from a broken button. Invisible on an office connection; the deployment it bites is the field one. MINOR rather than higher: it needs two overlapping in-flight requests, nothing is written or lost, and pressing Previous recovers it.

**Remedy.** Copy the four-line pattern the other four pages already carry into ALL FOUR files (products, tools, processes, media): `const currentLoad = useRef(0)` at the top, `const generation = (currentLoad.current += 1)` as the first statement of `load()`, and `if (generation !== currentLoad.current) return;` in front of every `setData` and `setError`. No API change. Separately, `Pagination`'s `onPage(page + 1)` deriving from the ANSWERED page rather than the REQUESTED one is what turns a stale render into a stuck control — passing the requested page would reduce it to a flicker.

**What the verifier checked.** Tried to refute the race by looking for de-duplication upstream: media/page.tsx:212-216 debounces with `setTimeout`/`clearTimeout`, which serialises SCHEDULED loads but not IN-FLIGHT ones, so two requests still overlap once the first has fired; products/tools/processes debounce only the `setApplied` state, not the fetch, so their effect fires immediately on page and funnel changes. Tried to refute the dead-button step by reading components/Pagination.tsx: `onPage(page + 1)` uses the `page` prop, which all four pages bind to `data.page`, so the arithmetic really is done on the answered page. Confirmed React bails on a `setState` to an identical scalar and that the effect deps (`[funnelReady, page, applied, funnel]`) would not change. Then grepped every list page for the guard, which is how I found the fourth omission (processes) the finder missed. Severity kept at MINOR. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

---

### [MINOR] (frontend) "Save this section" is a completely silent no-op whenever nothing is dirty, so a designer cannot tell a successful save from a broken one

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED · found by the verifier, not the original audit

**Where.** frontend/app/(protected)/questionnaires/[id]/answer/page.tsx:209-236, specifically the early return at :219 sitting above the toast at :229-235

**What it does.** `saveSection` builds its payload by filtering the section's answerable questions on `dirty.current` (:213), then `if (!answers.length) return true;` at :219 — which is ABOVE `setSaving`, above the request, and above the `if (!options?.silent) toast({ tone: "success", ... })` block at :229-235. The explicit "Save this section" button at :473 calls `saveSection(section)` with no options, i.e. the non-silent path, so it is the one caller that was written to always produce feedback. When `dirty.current` is empty it produces none at all: no toast, no error, no spinner, no state change.

**How it fails.** A designer reopens a sitting to re-read section 2, changes nothing, and presses "Save this section" to be sure. Nothing happens on screen. More consequentially, this is what hides the sitting-switch data loss verified above: after the Sitting dropdown has emptied `dirty.current` (:165), the eleven answers she typed are gone from `draft` AND unrecorded as dirty, so her instinctive press of "Save this section" is this same silent return — she gets exactly the same non-response she would get from a successful no-change save, and concludes the work is filed.

**What it costs.** The one control on the screen a designer presses when she is unsure gives an identical, empty response for "there was nothing to send" and for "what you typed has already been discarded". It removes the last chance to notice the loss while the respondent is still in the room.

**Remedy.** Move the empty-payload check below the feedback decision, or give it its own answer: when `!answers.length && !options?.silent`, emit an info toast ("Nothing new to save in this section — everything here is already recorded") before returning true. Leave the silent path (`goToSection`, "Finish this sitting") exactly as it is; the defect is only that the explicit button shares the wordless return.
