### [MINOR] (frontend) "Previously uploaded media" lists at most 100 attachments and prints items.length as the number attached, so a record carrying a bulk import reports its page size as a fact

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** frontend/components/media/ExistingMedia.tsx:58-65 (`refresh`), :60, :95; mounted from ArtisanForm.tsx:807, ProductForm.tsx:499, ToolForm.tsx:556, workshops/page.tsx:563, crafts/page.tsx:334

**What it does.** Verified verbatim:

```tsx
      const result = await listResource<MediaFile>("/media", { linkedRecordType, linkedRecordId, pageSize: 100 });
      setItems(result.items);                                                        // :60-61
…
          {items.length} file{items.length === 1 ? "" : "s"} already attached. …      // :95
```

100 is the endpoint's ceiling (`pageSize: int = Query(20, ge=1, le=100)` on `GET /media`), `PageResult.total` is discarded, and there is no pager and no notice in the component. `/media` orders `createdAt desc`, so above 100 attachments the OLDEST are the ones dropped, and the sentence always reads "100 files already attached" however many there are.

One correction to the finder's wording: this panel offers preview (`MediaPreviewTile` → lightbox) and removal (`removeMedia`, :35-56) but no caption editing, so "cannot be captioned" is not among the consequences. The accurate loss is that the older files cannot be opened or removed from the screen that manages them, and are not counted.

**How it fails.** A designer uploads a large batch against one record — the /media form takes a multi-file selection against a single linked record, and lib/media.ts:1575-1580 documents the intake path as designed for "two hundred files" — then opens that record's edit page. The panel says "100 files already attached" and draws 100 tiles. The older attachments are not drawn, cannot be opened and cannot be removed here, and nothing suggests they exist.

**What it costs.** A count that presents itself as the record's truth is really the request's page size, and the older half of a bulk import is invisible on the only per-record screen that manages it. Narrow, because it needs a single record carrying more than 100 files — but multi-file upload against one linked record is a first-class path here, so the condition is reachable in ordinary use rather than exotic.

**Remedy.** Keep the whole payload rather than only `items`: hold `total` alongside, print it in the sentence at :95, and when `total > items.length` say so and offer either a page step or a link to /media pre-filtered by this record. `listResource` already returns `PageResult<T>` with `total`, `page` and `pages`, so nothing extra has to be fetched to make the sentence honest.

**What the verifier checked.** Checked for a pager or a notice anywhere in the component — read ExistingMedia.tsx end to end; there is none, and `items` is only ever `result.items`. Checked the endpoint ceiling so "100 is the maximum askable" is not an assumption — `GET /media` declares `pageSize: int = Query(20, ge=1, le=100)`. Enumerated the five mount sites (all record edit surfaces) so the reachability claim is about real pages. Checked the finder's "cannot be captioned" claim against the render and refuted that part — the tiles expose open and remove only. Checked the "two hundred files" citation at lib/media.ts:1575-1580: real, though it describes the photo-intake EXIF reader; the multi-file path on /media is what produces this state. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

---

### [MINOR] (frontend) A stale token in localStorage yanks a visitor off the public landing page to /login, which the code that does it states it does not do

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** frontend/lib/api.ts:187-194; frontend/components/AuthProvider.tsx:23-41; frontend/app/layout.tsx:53 (AuthProvider is in the ROOT layout, not the protected one); frontend/app/page.tsx (the public landing page renders under it)

**What it does.** `apiFetch`'s 401 handler fires whenever a token was sent: `if (response.status === 401 && token && typeof window !== "undefined") { setToken(null); if (window.location.pathname !== "/login") window.location.assign("/login"); }`. The comment beside it reasons that "anonymous requests — e.g. the landing page's /me probe — sent no token, so they fail without navigating the visitor away from public pages." That holds only for a visitor with NO stored token. `AuthProvider` is mounted in `app/layout.tsx:53`, above both the public `/` and `/login`, and its mount effect (:39-41) calls `refreshMe()` → `apiFetch("/me")` on every page. A visitor with a stale token sends it, gets 401, and is hard-navigated off whatever public page they were reading. `window.location.assign` rather than `replace` also pushes a history entry, so Back returns to the page they were pushed off.

**How it fails.** A designer who last used the portal six weeks ago opens the marketing home page on her phone to show a colleague. Her token expired but is still in localStorage. `/me` 401s, the token is cleared, and the browser hard-loads /login mid-scroll. She presses Back; the landing page reappears and this time stays (the token is gone now), but she was thrown out once with no explanation and has paid for two full page loads.

**What it costs.** The public landing page — the one route the app most needs to be reachable — bounces every returning visitor with an expired session to a sign-in form they did not ask for, and Back appears to malfunction. The invariant the comment at api.ts:189-191 asserts is not enforced by anything.

**Remedy.** Confine the redirect to routes that actually require a session rather than to "a token was sent". Cleanest: give `apiFetch` an options argument (`apiFetch(path, init, { redirectOn401: false })`) and pass it from `AuthProvider.refreshMe`, which already handles 401 correctly on its own (AuthProvider.tsx:30-33 clears the token and sets `user` null; AppShell.tsx:40-42 then does the soft `router.replace("/login")` for protected routes only). Cheapest: skip the redirect when `window.location.pathname === "/"`. Where the hard navigation is kept, use `window.location.replace` so the expired page does not stay in history.

**What the verifier checked.** Read api.ts:177-198 in full and confirmed the condition is `response.status === 401 && token`, not a route test. Confirmed `AuthProvider` sits in the ROOT layout (app/layout.tsx:48-64) rather than in `app/(protected)/layout.tsx`, and that `app/page.tsx` is the public landing page rendered under it. Confirmed the probe is unconditional: `useEffect(() => { refreshMe(); }, [refreshMe])` at AuthProvider.tsx:39-41 with a `useCallback([])` identity, so it runs once per page load with no route check. Checked whether the landing page might be static-only and never reach the client provider — `HeroLanding` is a client island under the same root layout, so the provider mounts. Checked the `/login` self-exclusion: `window.location.pathname !== "/login"` does prevent a loop there. Severity: MINOR is honest — no data is lost and the state self-heals on the second visit, since the token is cleared before the navigation. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

---

### [MINOR] (frontend) The definition editor's pre-flight validator omits both stage-scoped collision checks the server enforces, so Save is offered for a definition the server refuses outright

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** frontend/lib/customSections.ts:927 (`customDefinitionProblems(sections: readonly DwCustomSection[])`), :1000 (`function fieldProblems(section, field, seenFieldKeys, seenLabels)`), :180-185 (the "complete mirror" claim); frontend/components/designworkshop/CustomSectionsEditor.tsx:138 (`const [registry, setRegistry] = useState<DwRegistry | null>(null)`), :229 (`setRegistry(spec)`), :286 (`customDefinitionProblems(sections)`), :1239 (the Save gate); backend/app/services/custom_sections.py:711-753 (`_field_problems`, both checks)

**What it does.** Confirmed, with corrected frontend line numbers (the finder cited :976-981, :273 and :1223; the actual lines are :1000, :286 and :1239 — the backend citation 711-753 is exact). `customDefinitionProblems` takes only `sections`; `fieldProblems` takes only `(section, field, seenFieldKeys, seenLabels)`. Neither is given the registry, so neither can mirror the two stage-scoped rules the server enforces at custom_sections.py:716-722 (a custom key equal to a live, non-`deprecated` field key of any entity of that stage) and :740-753 (a custom label case-folding equal to a live label of that stage's SINGLETON entity). The editor already holds the registry in state (CustomSectionsEditor.tsx:138, set at :229) and uses it only to build the stage picker at :274. `problems` is therefore empty for a colliding definition, and the Save gate at :1239 (`disabled={busy || problems.length > 0 || diff.movedSections.length > 0 || !dirty}`) is open.

The server's own comment confirms why the label rule is the load-bearing one: `StageCompleteness.missing` files a custom field's label bare, exactly as a singleton field does, and `dict.fromkeys` collapses a duplicate while `required_total` still counts two — the "144/144 beside Not recorded printed thirty-six times" failure this repository has already shipped. The narrowing to the singleton is deliberate and correct (a collection field files `"{entity.title}: {label}"` and cannot collapse into a bare label), so a client mirror must narrow the same way.

**How it fails.** A designer adds a section to stage 1 (`WORKSHOP_IDENTITY`) with a required question keyed `startDate`, labelled "Start date", for their sub-activity's own dates. The editor reports no problem, the diff preview says "1 question added", and Save is enabled. The PUT is refused by `validate_definition` on both rules at once. Because `PUT /custom-sections` carries the WHOLE definition, the three unrelated corrections the designer typed into other sections in the same sitting are refused with it, and they must work out which of twelve sections the message names.

**What it costs.** A whole-set definition save is refused after the fact for a rule the editor exists to catch before the fact, taking every unrelated edit in the same body with it. The load-bearing comment at customSections.ts:180-185 asserts this file is "otherwise a complete mirror" of `validate_definition`, so the next person to add a server rule will trust the mirror rather than check it — which is how a second gap gets added.

**Remedy.** Give `customDefinitionProblems` the registry: `customDefinitionProblems(sections: readonly DwCustomSection[], stages: readonly DwStage[] = [])`, resolve `stages.find(s => s.key === section.stageKey)` in `fieldProblems`, and add the two checks — a live (non-`deprecated`) field of ANY entity of that stage whose `key` equals `field.key`, and a live field of that stage's SINGLETON entity whose `label` case-folds equal to `field.label`. Narrow to the singleton exactly as the server does. Pass `registry?.stages ?? []` at CustomSectionsEditor.tsx:286 — defaulting the parameter keeps the function honest when no registry is loaded (it then mirrors what it can, and cannot invent a refusal). Correct the "complete mirror" comment to enumerate what is and is not mirrored.

**What the verifier checked.** Read customSections.ts:927-1010 and the surrounding validator, CustomSectionsEditor.tsx:130-290 and :1100-1245, and custom_sections.py:690-760. Tried to refute three ways: (a) is the registry threaded in by some other route — a second validator, a `useMemo` that pre-filters, or a check inside `definitionBody`? Grepped `problems` across the editor; the only producer is :286 and the only consumers are the banner at :1106 and the Save gate at :1239. (b) Does the editor block colliding keys some other way, e.g. by refusing keys that appear in the stage picker's field list? The registry is used only at :274 to build `{value, label}` pairs for stages. (c) Is the server check perhaps unreachable because `stage` is None for a custom section? No — `_field_problems` returns early only when the stage was already reported unknown (custom_sections.py:706-709), and a section always carries a `stageKey` chosen from the registry. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md (the open custom-sections item is last-write-wins on the whole PUT, a different defect on the same endpoint — this one neighbours it). MINOR held: nothing is lost or corrupted, the refusal is honest and the definition is unchanged; the cost is a whole-body save refused after the fact.

---

### [MINOR] (frontend) An IndexedDB read failure is published as "this browser holds no drafts", so the banner that warns the designer not to clear the browser disappears

**Verdict:** PARTIAL

**Where.** frontend/lib/designWorkshopStore.ts:722-739 (`refreshDrafts`), :757-762 (`loadDraft`'s `.catch(() => [])`), :2538-2562 (`pendingWork`); frontend/components/designworkshop/DraftSyncBanner.tsx:166-172 (`if (!rows.length) return null`), :105-115 (the toast); frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/page.tsx:128 (the list renders the same snapshot)

```ts
// designWorkshopStore.ts:730-736
    cache = rows.map(migrateDraft).filter(draftBelongsToSession).sort(...);
  } catch {
    cache = [];
  }
```

**What it does.** The mechanism is verified: any failure of the `drafts` read collapses to `cache = []`, which is published to every `useSyncExternalStore` consumer — the banner (which then renders `null`, removing the "do not clear its data or hand the laptop on" sentence) and the workshop list at page.tsx:128, which is how local-only workshops appear at all. `loadDraft` has the same `.catch(() => [])`. BUT THE FINDER'S SCENARIO IS PART-WRONG: the "Sync now" button lives INSIDE the banner (DraftSyncBanner.tsx:197-202) and `syncDesignWorkshopDrafts` has exactly one caller, so a designer whose store was unreadable at load time has no button to press and cannot be told "Nothing to send". The toast IS reachable, by a different sequence the finder did not state: the store reads fine at first, the banner renders, the designer presses "Sync now", and `runSync` → `pendingWork` → `refreshDrafts` fails during the pass — `result.pending` is 0, `stoppedOffline` is false, the banner vanishes and the toast then says "Nothing to send" in an informational tone. Severity is also lower than claimed: for the common causes of an unreadable store (eviction, a private-mode window whose storage the browser reclaims) the drafts really are gone, so "empty" is not a false statement about the data, only about the app's confidence.

**How it fails.** A field laptop's `drafts` store becomes unreadable mid-session — an aborted upgrade, a disk error, an origin whose storage the browser has reclaimed. The designer opens the app the next morning holding a fortnight of unsent stages. The amber banner that has been on every screen for two weeks is simply gone and the workshop list shows only what the server returns; nothing distinguishes "there is nothing waiting here" from "this device cannot tell you what is waiting here". If they had the banner up when the read failed and pressed "Sync now", the toast answers "Nothing to send".

**What it costs.** A false all-clear on the only surface that tells a designer whether they may pack the laptop up, at the moment the data is most at risk — the failure the banner's own header names ("A store nobody can see is worse than no store"), produced by the store rather than by its absence. It also, through `loadDraft`'s identical catch, lets `ensureDraft` mint a second local draft for a workshop this browser already holds (see the `ensureDraft` finding).

**Remedy.** Distinguish "there is nothing here" from "this device cannot tell you what is here". Add a module-level `readFailedAt: number | null`, set it in the catch at :731 and clear it on a successful read, expose it beside `getDraftsSnapshot`, and have `DraftSyncBanner` render a distinct red panel instead of returning `null` when it is set: "this browser's local store could not be read, so the app cannot say what is still waiting here — do not clear the browser's data." `runSync` should carry the same flag so the toast can never say "Nothing to send" about a store it could not open. Keep the non-throwing behaviour; only the reporting is wrong.

**What the verifier checked.** Confirmed the catch at :731-736 and `loadDraft`'s at :760. Traced the consumers: `getDraftsSnapshot` feeds DraftSyncBanner.tsx:66 and design-workshops/page.tsx:128, so both surfaces go quiet. Tried to reach the "Nothing to send" toast the finder describes and found the button is inside the hidden banner and is the only caller of `syncDesignWorkshopDrafts` (grep: one call site) — so that half of the scenario as written is unreachable, and I re-derived the sequence in which it IS reachable (failure during a manual pass). Also weighed how often an unreadable store coincides with data that still exists — `openDb` (:448-479) fails wholesale in a private-mode tab where nothing was ever persisted — which is why I downgraded to MINOR. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

---

### [MINOR] (frontend) "Sync now" reports "Nothing to send" for a pass in which everything was refused, because `DwSyncResult.failed` has no reader

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** frontend/lib/designWorkshopStore.ts:3007-3017 (`DwSyncResult.failed`), and the five sites that increment it: :3195 and :3209 (a refused photograph), :3318 (a stage whose registry entry is missing), :3455 (a stage the server refused), :3530 (a partly-refused stage), :3587 (a whole-workshop refusal); frontend/components/designworkshop/DraftSyncBanner.tsx:96-115

```ts
// DraftSyncBanner.tsx:96-110 — the only consumer of DwSyncResult
const result = await syncDesignWorkshopDrafts();
const sent = result.workshopsCreated + result.stagesSent + result.mediaUploaded;
if (sent) { … } else if (trigger === "manual") {
  toast({ id: "dw-draft-sync", tone: result.stoppedOffline ? "error" : "info",
          title: result.stoppedOffline ? "Still no connection" : "Nothing to send", …
```

**What it does.** Verified: `failed` is maintained at six increment sites (one more than the finder counted — the media arm increments it in two places, :3195 for the escalated one-file batch and :3209 for the partial-batch fall-through) and documented as "Things the server refused permanently". The only caller of `syncDesignWorkshopDrafts` reads `workshopsCreated`, `stagesSent`, `mediaUploaded`, `pending` and `stoppedOffline`; `failed` is never read anywhere in `app/`, `components/` or `lib/`. The toast therefore has exactly two outcomes.

**How it fails.** A designer presses "Sync now" on a workshop whose dirty stages the server refuses (a rejected decimal in one, a 500 on a lone surrogate in a name in another) and whose one photograph is a 415. `sent` is 0 because nothing landed and `stoppedOffline` is false because the server answered every time, so the toast — the direct answer to the click — reads "Nothing to send" in the informational tone. The refusals do appear as red lines lower down the banner, but the toast is what renders on top and what answers the button.

**What it costs.** The most explicit "is my work filed?" question the UI offers is answered in the same words for a pass that refused six things as for a pass that had nothing to do. Mild, because the refusals are visible immediately below, which is why this stays MINOR.

**Remedy.** Read the field: in `drain`, add a branch before the "Nothing to send" one for `result.failed > 0` — `tone: "error"`, a title naming the count, and a description pointing at the list below. While there, the `stoppedOffline` early return at :3065-3068 (taken when the session is not yet known) also produces "Still no connection" for a state that is not a connection problem; it wants a third outcome or its own flag.

**What the verifier checked.** Grepped every use of `syncDesignWorkshopDrafts` (one caller) and read the whole of `drain` (DraftSyncBanner.tsx:92-121) — `failed` is genuinely unread. Re-counted the increment sites in `runSync` and found six, not five. Checked whether the refusals are nevertheless surfaced: yes, via `pendingWork`'s `failures` and the banner's `state.refusals`, which is why the severity is honest at MINOR. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

---

### [MINOR] (frontend) `ensureDraft` is a check-then-act across two separate transactions, so two concurrent entries into one workshop fork the local draft in half

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** frontend/lib/designWorkshopStore.ts:973-1007 (`ensureDraft`), :757-762 (`loadDraft`, readonly + `.catch(() => [])`), :885-921 (`mutate`'s single-transaction rule, and its docstring at :868-884), :1349 (`stageLocalMedia`'s fallback use); callers at app/(protected)/design-workshops/[id]/page.tsx:204, .../stages/[stageKey]/page.tsx:478, .../codes/page.tsx:149, .../readiness/page.tsx:137, and `adoptServerDetail` (:2132)

```ts
// designWorkshopStore.ts:977-1004
const existing = await loadDraft(remoteId);      // transaction 1, readonly
if (existing) return existing;
const now = Date.now();
const draft: DwDraft = { schemaVersion: …, localId: localId(), remoteId, … };
await transact([STORE_DRAFTS], "readwrite", async (stores) => req(stores[STORE_DRAFTS].put(draft)));
```

**What it does.** Verified. Every other read-modify-write in this store goes through `mutate`, whose `getAll` and `put` share one readwrite transaction precisely so two writers cannot interleave — the file argues the point at :868-884. `ensureDraft` reads in one readonly transaction, decides, and writes in a second. The store's keyPath is `localId` (:456), so nothing constrains two records carrying the same `remoteId`, and both `loadDraft` and `mutate` resolve by `matchesId` over `getAll().find()` — first match in key order, which is a random UUID. Concurrency within one tab is limited: every caller is a page-load effect, and `stageLocalMedia`'s uses are sequential (photos/page.tsx:347-356 awaits each file), so the realistic route is two tabs opening the same server-owned workshop for the first time — or React StrictMode's double-invoked effects in development.

**How it fails.** Two tabs open the same server-owned workshop for the first time within the same few milliseconds. Both `loadDraft(remoteId)` return null, both mint a `localId`, both write. The store now holds two draft records for one `remoteId`. Each tab's `draftIdRef.current` points at its own record, so each tab's autosaves land in a different draft, and `adoptServerDetail`/`adoptServerStage` — which call `mutate(detail.id)` by REMOTE id — write into whichever record `find()` reaches first, which need not be the one the tab is editing. `runSync` walks both. Once both have been folded (`serverLoadedAt` set, `merge` omitted), the second PUT replaces the singleton row wholesale with the second draft's bucket and the first tab's answers are gone from the server, with both local copies marked pushed.

**What it costs.** A forked local record for one workshop, one half of the designer's answers overwritten on the server under a 200, and both halves reported as sent. Recovery means clearing browser storage, which is the one thing the banner tells them never to do. MINOR rather than higher because the window is a few milliseconds on the FIRST open only, and the more common single-tab paths are sequential.

**Remedy.** Do the check and the create in one readwrite transaction, the way `mutate` does: open `[STORE_DRAFTS]` readwrite, `getAll`, look for `matchesId(draft, remoteId) && draftBelongsToSession(draft)`, and `put` the new record only if none was found — all before the transaction commits. Two lines, and atomic against every other writer in the same scope, in this tab and across tabs. While there, `loadDraft`'s `.catch(() => [])` at :760 means a transient read failure is indistinguishable from "no such draft" and forks the draft even single-tab; it should propagate.

**What the verifier checked.** Read `ensureDraft` and confirmed the two transactions. Checked the object store's keyPath in `openDb` (:456) — `localId`, so no unique index on `remoteId` rescues it. Checked `matchesId`/`find()` resolution in `loadDraft` (:761) and `mutate` (:902) to confirm which record a later write reaches. Enumerated the callers to see whether any of them can run concurrently in one tab: all are page-level load effects, and `stageLocalMedia`'s photo loop is a sequential `for … await` (photos/page.tsx:347), so the single-tab route is essentially StrictMode only — which is why I did not raise the severity. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md; distinct from the recorded `patchDraftHeader` entry, which concerns the same function's seeded header rather than its atomicity.

---

### [MINOR] (frontend) A create whose `remoteId` write-back fails is counted as created and silently skipped, and the next pass creates the workshop again

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED · found by the verifier, not the original audit

**Where.** frontend/lib/designWorkshopStore.ts:3098-3110 (`draft = (await mutate(...)) ?? draft` then `result.workshopsCreated += 1`), :3157-3158 (`const remoteId = draft.remoteId; if (!remoteId) continue;`), :885-921 (`mutate`'s `.catch(() => null)`)

**What it does.** `runSync` POSTs the create and writes `remoteId` back with `mutate`, whose failure mode is `null` (a rejected/aborted readwrite transaction — quota is the realistic one on this device — or a draft that vanished mid-pass). The `?? draft` fallback keeps the OLD draft object, whose `remoteId` is still null, and the pass then increments `workshopsCreated`, falls straight through the media loop's `linkedRecordId` requirement at `if (!remoteId) continue` and moves to the next draft without recording anything. The draft on disk still has `remoteId: null`, so `pendingWork` reports `needsCreate` again and the next pass POSTs a second workshop — the exact non-resumability the comment at :3094-3097 says this write-back exists to prevent, defeated by not checking whether it happened.

**How it fails.** A field laptop's origin storage is full (a fortnight of photograph blobs, persistence never granted). The designer reaches wifi. `createDesignWorkshop` succeeds; the `mutate` that would record `remoteId` is rejected with `QuotaExceededError` and returns null; the toast says "1 saved change sent" and nothing else moves. Every subsequent pass — one per `online` event, and on every tab mount — creates another empty workshop under the same title, and none of the stages or photographs is ever sent.

**What it costs.** Unbounded duplicate government records under one title, each empty, with a success toast and no failure recorded anywhere — while the designer's actual stages and photographs stay unsent. Same precondition as the stage-form banking defect (a failing IndexedDB write), which is why it is MINOR rather than higher, but the blast radius is larger than that one's.

**Remedy.** Treat a null write-back as a hard stop for this draft: if `mutate` returns null after a successful create, record a `DwDraftFailure` on the draft (or, better, distinguish "not found" from "the transaction failed" in `mutate` as the stage-form finding also requires) and `continue` WITHOUT incrementing `workshopsCreated`, so the pass reports the truth and a second create cannot follow. The server id must never be knowable only in memory.

---

### [MINOR] (android) `anyForModel` merges every cached list for a model across ALL workshops on the device, so a WORKSHOP-scoped picker can offer another workshop's records offline

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED · found by the verifier, not the original audit

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/DwReferenceStore.kt:274-278 (`cacheKey`) and :369-392 (`anyForModel`); android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/WorkshopRepository.kt:524-528

**What it does.** `cacheKey` encodes the owner — `val owner = if (scope.equals("WORKSHOP", ignoreCase = true)) workshopId else "ALL"` — and the store's header says WORKSHOP-scoped lists "are meaningless in another workshop and are keyed by workshop id" (DwReferenceStore.kt:39-44). `anyForModel` then defeats that keying: after the exact-key miss it builds `val prefix = safeName(model) + "__"` and merges EVERY file whose name starts with the model, i.e. `Artisan__ALL__…` and `Artisan__<any other workshop id>__…` alike, flattening them into one list (`items = merged.flatMap { it.items }.distinctBy { it.id }`). The workshop id is passed in but is used only for the exact-key attempt at line 370, never as a filter on the merge. `WorkshopRepository` reaches this whenever the exact key is missing (line 525) and hands the result to the picker.

**How it fails.** A designer runs two clusters from one handset. Workshop A's stage 5 picker has been fetched, so `ProductDocumentation__<A>__ _.json` is on disk. In workshop B, offline, they open the same field: there is no `ProductDocumentation__<B>__ _` file, `anyForModel` falls through to the prefix merge, and workshop A's documented products are offered — narrowed only by `narrowedTo(filterValue)`, which filters on the artisan id, not on the workshop. Picking one writes A's product id and hydrated product name/material/price onto B's row.

**What it costs.** A cross-workshop join key written from an offline picker that gives no sign the options came from another cluster's cache. It is independent of the missing-`scope` defect and would survive fixing it: even once the server is asked for the narrow list, the very first offline open in a new workshop still falls through to this merge. MINOR rather than higher because it needs two workshops on one device, a stage not yet fetched in the second, and the same model already cached for the first.

**Remedy.** Filter the merge by owner: build the prefix as `safeName(model) + "__" + safeName(if (scope WORKSHOP) workshopId else "ALL") + "__"`, so a WORKSHOP-scoped fallback can only merge lists cached for THIS workshop, and an ALL-scoped one only merges ALL-owned files. The cascade behaviour the docstring actually wants — "the device holds the whole model from an earlier unfiltered fetch" — is preserved, because those files share the same owner segment.

---

### [MINOR] (android) MONEY coercion on the handset rounds with `String.format`, which rounds half AWAY from zero on the shortest decimal, while the server and this file's own derived path round half to EVEN — so ordinary typed amounts store one paisa apart on the two clients

**Verdict:** PARTIAL

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/StageSchema.kt:533 against :776-792; android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/FieldRenderer.kt:737-746 (`commit`, the path every typed amount takes); backend/app/services/stage_schema.py:1052-1054; frontend/components/designworkshop/FieldInput.tsx:488-514

**What it does.** `DwValues.coerce`'s MONEY arm stores `JsonPrimitive(String.format(Locale.ROOT, "%.2f", parsed))` (StageSchema.kt:533). 250 lines later `DwDerived.formatted` deliberately refuses that function for this exact reason — "Java's `Formatter` rounds half AWAY from zero and Python's `f\"{x:.2f}\"` rounds half to EVEN" (:776-780) — and uses `exact.setScale(2, RoundingMode.HALF_EVEN)` (:788). The server stores `f"{checked:.2f}"` (stage_schema.py:1053-1054), and the web's MONEY input sends the raw typed string (FieldInput.tsx:504-511, `onChange(event.target.value ...)`), so the server does the rounding for the browser and accepts the phone's already-rounded string verbatim. The divergence is therefore permanent, not corrected on save.

**How it fails.** Wider than the finder claimed, and measured. Java's `Formatter` rounds the SHORTEST decimal representation half-up, not the exact binary value, so the disagreement is not confined to exactly-representable halves. On JDK 17 vs this repo's Python: `2.675` → Java `2.68`, Python `2.67`; `5.005` → `5.01` vs `5.00`; `1.005` → `1.01` vs `1.00`; `0.045` → `0.05` vs `0.04`; `12.125` → `12.13` vs `12.12`. A designer typing a per-unit rate of `₹2.675` on the phone stores `2.68`; a colleague typing the same three characters on the web stores `2.67`. Both are then multiplied through `costMaterialLine.amount` (a PRODUCT derivation), so the gap scales with quantity.

**What it costs.** Two clients produce different stored amounts from the same keystrokes in the cost sheet an officer signs, in the one field type this repository has already pinned elsewhere for precisely this reason (`DwDerived.formatted` here, `pyRound` in frontend/lib/marketAnalysis.ts:227-255). Any money value typed with three decimals ending in 5 — a rate, a per-metre price — is a candidate, which is why this is MINOR rather than the LOW the finder assigned.

**Remedy.** Replace StageSchema.kt:533 with the expression `DwDerived.formatted` already uses: `JsonPrimitive(BigDecimal(parsed).setScale(2, RoundingMode.HALF_EVEN).toPlainString())`. `BigDecimal` and `RoundingMode` are already imported in the file. Note the input to `BigDecimal` must be the double (not `BigDecimal(text)`), so the value rounded is the same binary double the server will parse.

**What the verifier checked.** Verified line 533 and the contrasting :776-792, and verified via FieldRenderer.kt:737-746 that every hand-typed amount really does go through `DwValues.coerce` and is pushed to the store already formatted. Checked the other side of the wire: the web sends the raw string (FieldInput.tsx:488-514) and the server rounds it, so the two clients genuinely disagree rather than both pre-rounding. I compiled and ran a JDK 17 probe against the repo's own Python interpreter rather than trusting the finder's numbers — which confirmed their examples AND showed the mechanism is broader than "an exactly-representable half" (2.675, 5.005, 1.005, 0.045 all diverge because Java formats the shortest repr). Marked PARTIAL for that mechanism correction and raised LOW→MINOR.

---

### [MINOR] (android) `removeMedia` sweeps the whole media directory against a stale snapshot, so a detach can delete a photograph another import is copying

**Verdict:** PARTIAL

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/WorkshopDraftStore.kt:1158-1177 (removeMedia), :1015-1090 (importMedia: copy at 1039-1061, registration at 1080-1088); android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/StageScreen.kt:1015-1054 (attach) and :1056-1061 (detach)

**What it does.** The quoted sweep exists exactly as reported: after `update` returns the post-removal draft as `doomed`, the function lists `media/` and deletes every file no descriptor in that SNAPSHOT names. `importMedia` copies the bytes into `media/<uuid>.<ext>` and fsyncs them with the store lock NOT held (:1039-1061, its KDoc step 1 of 3) and only then takes the lock to register the descriptor (:1080-1088). So two windows exist: a file mid-copy, and a file whose descriptor was registered after `removeMedia`'s own `update` returned but before its `listFiles()`. Both are deleted. The unlink succeeds while the writer's fd is open, so the copy completes, `fd.sync()` succeeds and a descriptor is written for bytes that no longer have a directory entry.

**How it fails.** Inside one stage screen. `attach` multi-selects five photographs and imports them in ONE coroutine on the composition scope (StageScreen.kt:1015-1054); each `importMedia` suspends on `Dispatchers.IO` for the seconds a multi-megabyte copy takes, which frees the same scope to run `detach` (:1056-1061) when the designer taps the X on a photograph attached earlier. `removeMedia` takes its snapshot, lists `media/`, and deletes the file the import is currently writing (and any whose descriptor landed in the gap). The import reports success; the draft holds a descriptor with a caption and a stage assignment for bytes that are gone. Later `uploadPending` tells the designer the file "is no longer in this workshop's media folder on this device … Nothing has been deleted by the app" (WorkshopSync.kt:1222-1227).

**What it costs.** Silent loss of a photograph the app itself deleted, mis-reported afterwards as an external cause. Narrow in trigger — it needs a detach inside the seconds an import is copying — but total and unrecoverable when it fires.

**Remedy.** Capture the removed descriptor's `relativePath` inside the same `update` transform and delete `mediaFile(context, workshopId, thatPath)` and nothing else. If reclaiming genuinely orphaned bytes is still wanted, make it a separate start-up sweep (like `sweepStagedObjects`) that re-reads the draft under the store lock at the moment it decides. Cheap belt-and-braces: have `importMedia` copy to `<uuid>.part` and rename to the final name inside the same critical section that registers the descriptor, so bytes-without-a-descriptor cannot exist in `media/`.

**What the verifier checked.** Read `removeMedia`, `importMedia`, `update` (:826-844, which returns the POST-transform draft, so descriptors registered earlier are safe) and both call sites. Two of the finder's claims do NOT hold and I corrected them: (1) the bulk intake is NOT a self-collision — `dwConfirmIntake` (PhotoIntakeScreen.kt:813-884) imports strictly sequentially in one coroutine, so its in-loop `removeMedia` at :876 sees every earlier descriptor registered and only deletes the duplicate's own file; and it runs on its own screen, so a concurrent stage detach requires leaving that screen. (2) The directory-wide sweep is deliberate and documented, not an accident — StageScreen.kt:1070-1074 keeps camera captures in a sibling `captures/` directory precisely because of it, and DwIdentityOcr.kt:131 says the same. The real, reachable collision is the single-screen attach/detach interleaving, which survives refutation because `importMedia` explicitly refuses to hold the lock across the copy. Severity downgraded from MAJOR to MINOR: real and silent, but it needs a detach inside an in-flight import.

---

### [MINOR] (android) "Place of recording" is asked on all 22 stages and read by nothing — it never syncs, never prints, and its presence alone makes a stage count as holding work in the report merge

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/DwLocationField.kt:195-197 (the claim), :202 (the key), :204-268 (the card); android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/StageScreen.kt:1260-1269 (the only writer), :519-520 (`holdsWork`); android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/ReportScreen.kt:1867-1869 (`renderEntity`); android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/ReportSource.kt:123-124 (`touchedLocally`)

**What it does.** `DwRecordingPlaceCard` writes a full location object into `state.singleton[DW_RECORDING_PLACE_KEY]` (StageScreen.kt:1263-1267), which `persistLocally` stores in `StageDraft.values`. `wireData` strips every `_`-prefixed key from the payload, correctly. But the on-device report writer's `renderEntity` walks `entity.liveFields` only — `val visible = entity.liveFields.filter { ... }` / `val printable = visible.filter { ... }` (ReportScreen.kt:1867-1869) — and `_recordingPlace` is not a registry field, so it can never be printed; `ReportPlan` and `ReportFigures` never mention it. A repo-wide grep for `recordingPlace` finds the constant, the card, the single StageScreen write, and one passing mention in WorkshopDraftStore.kt:240 — no reader anywhere. The KDoc at DwLocationField.kt:195-197 asserts the opposite: "It survives in the local draft, which is what the report is generated from, so the provenance prints even though it never leaves the phone."

**How it fails.** Primary: a designer answers "where were you when this stage was filled in?" on 22 stages over a fortnight; none of it appears in the .docx, none of it syncs, and a reinstall loses it. Secondary and sharper: the designer opens stage 14 in a courtyard where the server read fails (StageScreen.kt:624 seeds a blank state), sets only the recording place, and the draft for stage 14 now holds `values = {_recordingPlace: ...}` and nothing else. When the report is built, `reportSourceFor` asks `stored.values.isNotEmpty()` (ReportSource.kt:123-124), judges the stage "the device's", and returns before merging the server's copy (:125-128) — so the office's full stage 14 is excluded from the document and the "built from" line counts it as kept from the device.

**What it costs.** Capture effort that no surface consumes, behind a comment that tells the next reader it does print. The merge consequence is the one with teeth: a stage whose only local content is provenance that can never travel keeps the server's copy out of the on-device report. StageScreen itself recovers (its `holdsWork` branch at :554-603 folds the server's values back in on the next online open); `reportSourceFor` has no fold and is all-or-nothing per stage.

**Remedy.** Either make it print — a per-stage provenance line in `ReportPlan`/`renderStage` reading `values[DW_RECORDING_PLACE_KEY]` by name, since no field-list walk will ever reach an underscore key — or drop the card until the registry has a field for it. Whichever is chosen, exclude `_`-prefixed keys from the two work tests, `ReportSource.kt:123-124` and `StageScreen.kt:519-520`, so a stage whose only content is untransmittable provenance is not judged to hold work. Fix the KDoc at DwLocationField.kt:195-197 either way.

**What the verifier checked.** Grepped `recordingPlace`/`RECORDING_PLACE` across android/app/src: writer and constant only, no reader. Read `renderEntity` (ReportScreen.kt:1858-1890) to confirm it iterates `entity.liveFields` and that both filters are registry-driven, so an underscore key is unreachable by construction. I tried to refute the secondary claim by checking whether the stage screen would repair the draft first: it does (the fold branch at :554-603 adds the server's values), which is why the consequence lands on `reportSourceFor` and not on the form — I have corrected the finder's wording, which blamed both. Kept at MINOR: the primary half is wasted effort with a false comment rather than data loss, and the merge half needs a failed read plus a report built before the next online open of that stage.

---

### [MINOR] (android) The handset draws tables with every TABLE_COLUMN field, ignoring the six-column cap the server applies, and its leftover block cannot carry the overflow either

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/ReportScreen.kt:1966 and :2007-2009 against backend/app/services/report_builder.py:1032-1041 and :1083-1103

**What it does.** Confirmed. Android: `val columns = visible.filter { it.reportRole == "TABLE_COLUMN" && !DwFieldType.of(it.type).isMedia }` (:1966) — no cap. Server: `return columns[:6]` (report_builder.py:1041), with `_render_table` printing the overflow under each row via `_render_narrative(entity, row, level + 1, skip=column_keys)` where `column_keys` holds only the six that were drawn (:1087, :1103). Android's leftover set is `visible.filter { it.reportRole != "TABLE_COLUMN" && it.reportRole != "HIDDEN" && it.captionFor.isBlank() }` (:2007-2009), keyed on the ROLE rather than on membership of the drawn columns, so the overflow could not reach the per-row block even if the cap were added.

Driving the bundled registry confirms the three live collections and their counts exactly: `existingProduct` (stage 6, 7 columns), `prototypeValidation` (stage 15, 7), `followUp` (stage 22, 7). I also checked the tiers — every one of the 21 columns is BASIC or STANDARD, so no template's `maxTier` trims them below seven. All three reach the table path: `asCards` is false whenever `columns` is non-empty under AUTO or TABLE (ReportScreen.kt:1977-1980), matching `_render_stage`'s `Presentation.TABLE if self._table_columns(entity) else CARDS` (:1462-1464).

ONE CORRECTION AND ONE ADDITION. The finder writes `TABULAR` for the presentation of stages 15 and 22; no such value exists — both `Presentation` enums are {KEY_VALUE, NARRATIVE, TABLE, CARDS, GALLERY, AUTO}, and these entities reach TABLE through AUTO. And the media-TABLE_COLUMN divergence is worse than 'the server includes them': on Android such a field is excluded from `columns` by `!isMedia` AND from `leftovers` by `reportRole != "TABLE_COLUMN"`, so it would be dropped from the document entirely. There are zero such fields in the bundled registry, so it is latent — but it is a silent drop, not a layout difference.

**How it fails.** A designer exports the DCH standard report for a workshop with twelve follow-up visits. Stage 22's table comes out on the phone as seven columns — Product, Interval, Follow-up date, Adoption status, Units produced, Units sold, Revenue — each about 14% of the text column on A4. The office's copy prints the first six as a table and the seventh as a key-value line under each row. Stages 6 and 15 diverge the same way.

**What it costs.** The two copies of one workshop have visibly different tables for stages 6, 15 and 22 — different column counts, row heights and pagination — with nothing in either file admitting it. Nothing is lost today, which is why MINOR is honest; the latent media case would lose a field.

**Remedy.** `val columns = visible.filter { it.reportRole == "TABLE_COLUMN" }.take(6)`, and change `leftovers` (:2007) from a role test to a membership test: `visible.filter { it !in columns && it.reportRole != "HIDDEN" && it.captionFor.isBlank() }`, which is exactly what `_render_table`'s `skip=column_keys` makes. Dropping `!isMedia` at the same time closes the latent drop and matches `_table_columns`; if the media exclusion is wanted, add it to the server instead — one of the two, not one each.

**CLOSED — all three clauses are in the tree, and the surface each landed on is the point. Verified 2026-08-20 by opening both files.** The cap and the membership test landed on the HANDSET. `renderCollection` now reads `visible.filter { it.reportRole == "TABLE_COLUMN" && !DwFieldType.of(it.type).isMedia }.take(6)`, and `leftovers` now tests `it.key !in columnKeys` instead of the role — so the seventh column of stages 6, 15 and 22 reaches the per-row block, which is what `_render_table`'s `skip=column_keys` makes of it, and the latent media-typed TABLE_COLUMN can no longer be dropped from the document by both filters at once. The media clause landed on the SERVER, which was the surface missing it: `_table_columns` now filters `not f.type.is_media`, so neither surface acted unilaterally. Stage 22 no longer prints as seven columns on the phone and six at the office.

**AN EARLIER VERSION OF THIS PARAGRAPH SAID "the cap was not" TAKEN. That was false**, and it was written without re-opening `ReportScreen.kt` — the fourth wrong claim about this finding's own code in as many waves. `.take(6)` is at `renderCollection`, under a comment beginning "SIX COLUMNS, AND NO MORE"; `tableColumns`' KDoc states the agreement from the other end ("What both surfaces DO agree on is the six-column cap ([tableColumns]'s callers `.take(6)`, `_table_columns`' `columns[:6]`)"). Both were in the tree when the false sentence was written. Open the two functions before amending this row again.

**WHAT IS STILL OPEN IS A DIFFERENT DIVERGENCE AND IS ROUTINELY CONFUSED WITH THIS ONE: the column WIDTHS.** The server discards the declared `column_width_pct` and re-weights (2.0 free text against 1.0) whenever their sum is not 100; `ReportScreen.tableColumns` keeps them and normalises. The two coincide only where the declared sum is already 100, and for five entities it is not — `sketch`, `prototype`, `prototypeValidation`, `finalProduct` and `followUp`, recorded with their declared sums and their handset vectors in `_WIDTHS_THAT_DO_NOT_GOVERN` and pinned by `backend/tests/test_reference_carry.py::test_the_two_surfaces_lay_out_the_same_table_the_same_way`, which fails if a recorded vector moves, if a sixth entity joins, or if a listed one silently converges. **That test pins WIDTHS; it says nothing about the cap**, and its own docstring calls the cap "the part the two surfaces DO agree on and must keep agreeing on". Rebalancing those five is the owner of the printed page's call — this row being closed is not licence to touch them.

**The phrase "one of the two, not one each" is this Remedy's, not `ReportScreen.kt`'s.** Two backend sites — `report_builder._table_columns`' docstring and `test_a_media_field_is_never_a_table_column_whatever_role_it_declares` in `backend/tests/test_report_attachments.py` — once attributed it to the Kotlin, which never contained it, and both now cite this document instead. **The previous version of this paragraph then got the audit of that correction wrong in its turn**, asserting that the test's copy was line-wrapped and therefore invisible to a single-line grep, and that "three hits is the expected count". Re-measured on 2026-08-20 by grepping `*.py`, `*.kt` and `*.md` for the phrase — spelled out in the Remedy above and deliberately not repeated in this sentence, so that the paragraph does not inflate the number it is reporting: **four lines in three files.** `report_builder.py` once; `test_report_attachments.py` once, on a single line, where it matches perfectly well; and this file twice, the Remedy and this paragraph's own opening. **Do not write a hit count down here again as a property to check.** It moves the moment any of those four paragraphs is rewrapped or a further correction quotes the phrase, which is how the last two versions of this caveat went wrong. The property worth checking is the one that was wrong in the first place, and it is stable: **no file under `android/` is among the hits.**

**What the verifier checked.** Read ReportScreen.kt:1946-2027 and report_builder.py:1032-1104. Verified with a script over the bundled schema that exactly three live entities carry more than six TABLE_COLUMN fields, that all their tiers are BASIC/STANDARD (so no template trims them under six), and that zero fields in the whole registry are both TABLE_COLUMN and media-typed. Compared the two `Presentation` enums directly to check the finder's `TABULAR` claim — it does not exist on either side. Tried to refute via the AUTO fallback sending these to CARDS: `asCards` requires `columns.isEmpty()`, which is false here. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

### [MINOR] (android) Within one record the handset prints prose, then metrics, then the key-value grid, then photographs; the server prints the key-value grid, then prose, then bullets, then metrics, then photographs — and on a card the server puts the photographs first

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/ReportScreen.kt:1875-1929 and :1981-1987 against backend/app/services/report_builder.py:947-1011, :1109-1114, :1435-1447

**What it does.** Confirmed on both sides. Android's `renderEntity` walks the field list once, emitting RICH_TEXT blocks INLINE as it meets them (:1911) while buffering metrics, key-values and images, then flushes `MetricRowBlock` → `keyValues` → `placeImages` at :1923-1929. The server's `_render_narrative` emits the `KeyValueBlock` FIRST (:947-955), then narratives (:956-984), then bullets (:985-1011); `_render_stage` adds the metric row and the photographs after that for a singleton (:1436-1447); and `_render_cards` places the photographs immediately under the row heading, BEFORE any field (:1109-1114). Android's CARDS path is `builder.heading(...)` then `renderEntity(...)` (:1981-1987), which puts the photographs last.

So for a stage singleton: server = grid, prose, bullets, metrics, photos; handset = prose/bullets interleaved in field order, metrics, grid, photos. For a card: server = heading, photos, grid, prose; handset = heading, prose, metrics, grid, photos.

The finder's description is accurate. I found two further order-related divergences in the same code that are inert on today's registry and do not change the grade: the server caps a metric row at four (`metrics[:4]`, :1440) and Android does not; and the server's `_render_narrative` handles no METRIC role at all, so a METRIC field on a COLLECTION entity would print on the handset and not on the server. The registry declares only three METRIC fields, all on singletons, at most two per entity.

**How it fails.** A designer exports stage 13 with the DCH standard template, which renders `prototype` as CARDS. The office's copy reads: heading, the prototype photographs, the key-value grid (code, name, sketch, artisan, materials, making time), then processSummary and problemsAndModifications. The handset's copy reads: heading, that prose, the grid, then the photographs.

**What it costs.** Every record is laid out in a different order in the two copies, so a reader comparing them — the case the whole port exists to serve — sees a document that has been rearranged, and the page boundaries and therefore the contents page's page numbers diverge with it.

**Remedy.** Restructure `renderEntity` to `_render_narrative`'s order: collect `pairs` (KEY_VALUE + COVER_FIELD + TABLE_COLUMN), `narrative`, `bullets` and `metrics` in separate passes; emit the KeyValueBlock first, then the narrative blocks (carrying the labels from the label finding), then the bullets, then the MetricRowBlock. Lift the image placement out to the caller so `renderCollection`'s CARDS path can place it before the fields as `_render_cards` does while the singleton path in `renderStageSection` keeps it after. Do this in the same edit as the label fix — they touch the same loop.

**What the verifier checked.** Read ReportScreen.kt:1858-1931 and :1946-2027 against report_builder.py:921-1030, :1106-1115 and :1420-1489. Tried to refute by checking whether a caller re-orders after the fact — neither `renderStageSection` nor `renderCollection` reorders blocks; `DocumentBuilder` appends. Verified the CARDS asymmetry is genuinely in the server (`_render_cards` calls `_place_images` before `_render_narrative`) and not something `_render_rows` undoes. Checked the registry for METRIC fields to see whether the extra divergences I spotted matter: three fields, all singleton, max two per entity, so both are latent. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

### [MINOR] (android) The handset never prints the photograph of a record a REF field points at, where the server deliberately does — the second of `_images`' two passes has no Android counterpart

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED · found by the verifier, not the original audit

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/ReportScreen.kt:1710-1731 (`imagesOf`) and :1880-1889 (the media branch of `renderEntity`), against backend/app/services/report_builder.py:860-917 (`_images`)

**What it does.** The server's `_images` gathers a row's pictures from TWO sources in a fixed order and says so in its docstring: 'the row's own media fields, then the photograph of any record a REF field on the row points at… the whole of "a photograph appears beside the thing it is a photograph of" for the half of the registry where the picture was never copied onto the row' (report_builder.py:862-873). Pass two is report_builder.py:897-907: for each visible REF field it resolves `self.data.reference(row.get(spec.key))` and, where that record has a photo, adds it captioned with the REFERENCE's own label. It also deduplicates by media id (`wanted: dict[str, str]`, :878) so a participant's picture cannot print twice.

Android has only pass one. `imagesOf` (:1717-1729) walks `visible`, skips anything that is not `type.isMedia`, and returns; there is no REF arm and no dedup map. `renderEntity`'s media branch (:1880-1889) repeats the same single pass inline. Nothing in either function notes the omission as deliberate — and this file is meticulous about naming its deliberate divergences (the transcripts arm at :1269-1276 and the AI-layers arm at :1292-1309 each carry a paragraph explaining why they emit nothing). The registry makes it reachable with no network: the referenced rows are in the same draft, `DwRefLabels` (already passed into every one of these call sites) already resolves a REF to the row it points at, eleven REF fields are printed, and five entities name a REF as their `labelField` — e.g. `prototype.sketchRef` points at a sketch row whose own photographs are in stage 11 of the same workshop.

Checked by reading ReportScreen.kt:1710-1748 and :1875-1931 against report_builder.py:858-917, and by grepping the Android report code for any REF-to-media resolution: `DwRefLabels` resolves labels only, and nothing under ui/designworkshop reads a referenced row's media field. Not recorded in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

**How it fails.** A designer exports a workshop where stage 13's prototypes carry `sketchRef` and `artisanRef` but some rows have no photographs of their own. The office's copy prints the referenced sketch's or artisan's picture under each such prototype, captioned with that record's label. The handset's copy prints no picture for those rows, and the 'Photographic record' annexure — which uses the same `imagesOf` (ReportScreen.kt:1361) — has correspondingly fewer plates than the office's contact sheet of the same workshop.

**What it costs.** Two contact sheets of one workshop with different numbers of plates and nothing in either file admitting it — the exact failure `renderMediaAnnexure`'s own KDoc says it walks the registry rather than the template to avoid (ReportScreen.kt:1317-1323). MINOR rather than higher because no photograph is lost from the record: the borrowed picture is context the server adds, and every picture the row itself holds still prints.

**Remedy.** Give `imagesOf` a second pass mirroring `_images`: after the media-field walk, iterate the visible REF fields, resolve each through the `DwRefLabels` the caller already supplies to find the referenced row's first media value, and append it captioned with the reference's label falling back to the field label. Keep the passes separate for the reason the server states — the registry's field order would otherwise let a borrowed catalogue picture lead a prototype's own progress shots — and add the `LinkedHashMap<String, String>` dedup by media id so a picture already copied onto the row cannot print twice. Have `renderEntity`'s inline media branch call `imagesOf` rather than repeating the single pass, so the two cannot drift again.

### [MINOR] (cross-surface) The create form's craft, cluster, state, district and dates are written to promoted columns that no stage entry backs, so the FIRST stage-1 save nulls every one of them

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED · found by the verifier, not the original audit

**Where.** backend/app/api/routes/design_workshops.py:864-871 (the create writes the columns), backend/app/services/design_workshops.py:1319-1357 (`_coerce_promoted` nulls a promoted column of any touched entity), :991 (`touched_entities.add(entity.key)`, unconditional); frontend/lib/designWorkshopStore.ts:2642-2664 (a READ stage always sends its singleton entry, answered or not); frontend/app/(protected)/design-workshops/page.tsx:344-357 (the create form that collects them)

**What it does.** `POST /design-workshops` copies `craftName`, `clusterName`, `state`, `district`, `startDate` and `endDate` from the create form onto the `DesignWorkshop` columns, but writes NO `workshopSetup` stage entry holding them (only `seed_designer_prefill` writes entries, and it writes only the four profile keys). Every one of those columns is declared in `PROMOTED_COLUMNS` under `workshopSetup.*` (stage_schema.py:494-508), and `_coerce_promoted` sets a promoted column to NULL whenever its source entity appears in the payload and the value is blank — with `title` as the only exception. `touched_entities` gains `workshopSetup` for any entry naming it, regardless of contents, and `buildStageEntries` sends the singleton entry for a stage this browser HAS read whether or not it is answered. So the create route is a second writer of columns whose single-writer is supposed to be the stage entry, and the first stage-1 save with those boxes still blank erases them.

**How it fails.** A designer creates a workshop from the list page and fills in craft "Ikat", cluster "Barpali", state, district and the fortnight's dates. The list shows them. They open stage 1 (whose craft/cluster/state/district/date boxes start blank — nothing seeds them from the header, verified in `adoptServerDetail` and the stage page's `seed`), type the venue, and press "Save stage". The payload names `workshopSetup` and carries no craftName, so `_coerce_promoted` sets craftName, clusterName, state, district, startDate, endDate, scheme, implementingAgency, sponsor and workshopCode to NULL under a 200 reported as "Stage saved". The same walk happens on the sync pass for an offline-created workshop.

**What it costs.** Until stage 1 is completed, the workshop is invisible to every list filter and search on craft, state, district and date, and shows "—" in the list's own columns — for data the designer typed and can see nowhere else. It repairs itself when stage 1 is finally filled in, which is why this is MINOR rather than higher, but the interval is the whole fortnight of capture and nothing on any screen explains it.

**Remedy.** Make the create route write what it promotes: after `db.designworkshop.create`, seed the `workshopSetup` singleton entry with the craft/cluster/state/district/date values the payload carried (the same shape `seed_designer_prefill` already writes, through `validate_entry`), so the columns have a backing entry and a later stage save merges rather than contradicts them. Alternatively stop accepting those fields on the create body. Pin it with a test that creates a workshop with craftName and then PUTs `WORKSHOP_SETUP` carrying only `venue`, asserting the header still reads `craftName` — the existing `test_a_promoted_column_can_be_cleared_and_others_are_untouched` keeps craftName in every payload and cannot catch this.

### [MINOR] (cross-surface) GET /review/pending caps at 200 rows per record type, reports the capped length as `total`, and carries nothing that says the queue was cut

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED · independently found by 2 agents

**Where.** backend/app/api/routes/review.py:91 (`PENDING_TAKE = 200`), :133-134 (the route signature — no pagination parameters), :152-158 (the six capped reads) and :172-173 (`return {"items": items, "total": len(items)}`)

**What it does.** `list_pending_reviews` takes only `reviewer: Any = Depends(require_reviewer)` — no `page`, no `pageSize`, no `cursor`. It loops the six `_PENDING_SOURCES` (artisan, workshop, product, tool, process, questionnaire), reading each with `take=PENDING_TAKE`, and returns `total=len(items)`, which is the length of the assembled, already-capped list rather than a count. There is no `count()` anywhere in the function and no `truncated` flag on the response, so a reviewer with 340 pending artisans and a reviewer with exactly 200 get byte-identical shapes. Because the per-source order is `{"createdAt": "desc"}`, the rows hidden by the cap are the OLDEST — the ones most overdue.

**How it fails.** Four researchers file 340 artisan records while a professor is in the field. The professor opens the review queue and sees 200 items. They work through all 200 and refresh; 140 older records surface from behind the cap and the count reads 200 again. There is no page 2 to ask for and nothing on the response ever said the list was cut, so the only way to discover the backlog is that the number refuses to go down.

**What it costs.** The queue silently hides the oldest pending work whenever more than 200 of any one record type are pending for a reviewer, with no signal on the wire. One correction to the finder: on the web the mislabelled `total` is not what the reviewer reads — frontend/app/(protected)/review/page.tsx destructures `{ items, total }` at line 102 but renders `<Pagination … total={items.length} />` at line 497, so the displayed number is the same capped length by a different route. The misnamed field is therefore a wire-contract defect (a client written against `total` gets a number that is not a total) rather than the cause of the on-screen number. MINOR: nothing is lost or corrupted, the hidden rows resurface as the queue drains, and the peer-review role filter already runs in the query so the 200 are rows this reviewer can actually act on.

**Remedy.** Read `take=PENDING_TAKE + 1` per source, trim to `PENDING_TAKE`, and add `truncated: bool` (OR-ed across the six) so the cut is detected exactly rather than inferred from a length. Rename the capped length to `shown` and return a real `total` from six `count()` calls against the same `where` — `map_points.point_records` already draws exactly that `shown` vs `total` distinction for the same stated reason. If a reviewer is expected to work a backlog larger than 200, add `page`/`pageSize` in the shape every sibling list route uses, and have the web read `total` instead of `items.length`.

**What the verifier checked.** Read review.py:56-173 in full; the constant, the six-source loop, the take and the return are exactly as quoted, and I confirmed by reading the signature that no pagination parameter exists. I looked for the thing that would refute it — a second endpoint giving a true pending count, or a client-side pager that requests more — and found neither: grep across frontend for `/review/pending` returns one call site (review/page.tsx:102) with no query string, and its Pagination is purely client-side over the already-returned `items`. I also confirmed the cap is applied AFTER the reviewability filter (`where["createdBy"] = {"is": {"role": {"in": roles}}}`, line 148-149), so the docstring's claim about the cap holding actionable rows is true and does not rescue the truncation. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md; the eligible-viewers cut it resembles is closed and is a different endpoint. Severity MINOR confirmed as honest.

### [MINOR] (cross-surface) A workshop an admin deleted answers 404 to the designer, not 409, so every stage is stamped with a permanent refusal blaming the designer's answers — and the correct sentence the store already contains is unreachable for the only user who can hit the condition

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** frontend/lib/designWorkshopStore.ts:3406-3407 (the status split), :3447-3452 (the sentence a 404 gets), :3569-3573 (the 409 sentence); backend/app/services/design_workshops.py:122-142 (`load_workshop_or_404`); backend/app/api/routes/design_workshops.py:1028-1029 (`_require_designer` then `for_edit=True`)

```
// designWorkshopStore.ts:3406-3407
const answered = error instanceof ApiError;
if (!answered || error.status === 408 || error.status === 429 || error.status === 409) throw error;

# design_workshops.py:126-141 — the 404 fires FIRST for a non-admin
if record.deletedAt is not None and not admin:
    raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_404_NOT_FOUND, detail="Record not found")
...
if for_edit and record.deletedAt is not None:
    raise HTTPException(status_code=status.HTTP_409_CONFLICT, detail="This workshop is deleted. Restore it before editing.")
```

**What it does.** Both halves verified. The stage arm rethrows 408/429/409 and records everything else as a permanent, designer-caused refusal with `skewRun: null` (`isSchemaRefusal` is 422-only, offline.ts:303-308, so a 404 never takes the skew branch). The 409 rethrow exists so the workshop-level catch can print the one correct sentence (:3569-3573). But `load_workshop_or_404` raises 404 for a soft-deleted workshop at :126-127 — before the `for_edit` branch at :137 — for everyone who is not an admin, and `save_stage_data` (routes:1028-1029) admits designers. A designer, who is the only person whose stages sit in this store, therefore always gets 404 and the 409 sentence is dead code for them. The same 404 also covers a revoked `DesignWorkshopViewer` grant (:128-136, deliberately, to avoid confirming the id exists) and — a case the finder missed — an `Unknown stage` 404 from routes:1030-1032, i.e. a genuine client/server registry skew, which likewise gets "correct the answer that caused it" with `skewRun: null` instead of the self-clearing skew treatment the file built for exactly that.

**How it fails.** An admin soft-deletes a duplicate workshop (or an operator revokes a viewer grant) while a designer's laptop holds unsent stages for it. The laptop reconnects; `runSync` walks the draft's dirty stages; every `PUT /design-workshops/{id}/stages/{key}` answers 404 "Record not found". `answered` is true and 404 is not in the rethrow set, so each stage is stamped `permanent: true, skewRun: null` with "…it will keep being refused until the answer that caused it is corrected — this is not a connection problem. Open the stage, then use Try again." `blocksRetry` returns true for all of them thereafter. The banner lists one red line per stage telling the designer to correct answers that nothing objected to.

**What it costs.** The designer is sent to audit answers that are not wrong, and the one actionable instruction the store knows how to give ("Ask an admin to restore it, then sync again") can never be shown to them. Nothing is lost — everything stays on the device and is still listed — and the banner DOES offer "Try again" (the failure is `permanent`, so `state.refusals` is non-empty), which clears the refusals and succeeds once the workshop is restored. That recoverability, plus the narrow precondition, is why this is MINOR rather than the MAJOR claimed: it is a wrong-cause message and a wasted audit, not stranded work.

**Remedy.** Rethrow 404 alongside 409 at :3407 so it reaches the workshop-level catch, and widen the deleted-workshop branch at :3569 to `status === 409 || status === 404` with a sentence honest about both readings — "this workshop is no longer available to you on the server: it may have been deleted, or your access to it withdrawn. Everything you captured is still on this device. Ask an admin to restore it or restore your access, then sync again." A 404 should not be `permanent` with `skewRun: null` either, since restoration is an external event the designer cannot signal: stamp `APP_RUN_ID` so the next app run re-probes by itself. While there, an `Unknown stage` 404 deserves the skew treatment the missing-registry-stage branch at :3302-3320 already gives its sibling. Moving the server's `for_edit` 409 above the non-admin 404 for an owner/grantee would help but is not sufficient, because the 404 also covers a revoked grant.

**What the verifier checked.** Read `load_workshop_or_404` in full and confirmed the ordering: the non-admin deleted check at :126 precedes the `for_edit` 409 at :137, so a designer can never receive the 409. Confirmed `save_stage_data` passes `for_edit=True` and that `_require_designer` admits DESIGNER (routes docstring :34-41), so the caller in question is a non-admin. Confirmed `isSchemaRefusal` is 422+`extra_forbidden` only, so a 404 gets the designer-blaming arm. Checked `blocksRetry` (offline.ts:391-395): `permanent` with a null `skewRun` blocks forever. Looked for a recovery path before downgrading: DraftSyncBanner.tsx:243-258 does offer "Try again" for permanent refusals and `retryDraft` clears them, so restoration is recoverable without clearing storage — hence MINOR. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

### [MINOR] (cross-surface) `docs/PERMISSIONS.md` §5 lists 7 of the 14 rules in `ROUTE_GUARDS`, and the omissions include both design-workshop guards

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** docs/PERMISSIONS.md:588-597 against frontend/lib/permissions.ts:183-322 (`ROUTE_GUARDS`)

**What it does.** Counted both. `ROUTE_GUARDS` declares 14 entries: `/users`, `/admin`, `/admin/analytics`, `/admin/designers`, `/settings/api-keys`, `/settings/tasks`, `/review`, `/data`, `/design-workshops`, `/questionnaires`, `/designers/profile`, `/artisans/new`, `/products/new`, `/tools/new`. §5's table covers seven rows spanning nine of them. Missing: `/admin/analytics` (`require_admin`), `/admin/designers` (`require_designer_roster_manager`), `/design-workshops` (`can_run_design_workshops`), `/questionnaires` (`can_run_design_workshops (_require_designer)`), `/designers/profile` (`require_designer`). The two `/admin/*` omissions are harmless in practice — they nest under the listed `/admin` isAdmin rule and are no looser — but the three design-workshop-family rules are the non-monotonic ones that cannot be re-derived from the rank ladder, and §5's closing line at :597 ("Anything unlisted is open to any signed-in user") is affirmatively false about `/design-workshops`, `/questionnaires` and `/designers/profile`.

**How it fails.** A maintainer adding a page under a new root beside the design-workshop tree reads §5, finds no row for it, reads the closing line and concludes the tree is open to any signed-in user. They ship without a guard entry; the page renders its chrome to a researcher or a professor whom every underlying route refuses — which permissions.ts:275-278 records as the exact bug this repository has already shipped for `/design-workshops`.

**What it costs.** The only table describing the client's route gating is half incomplete, and the rows it drops are the ones whose rule cannot be inferred. It sends a reader to the wrong default for the product's primary surface. Documentation-only, hence MINOR.

**Remedy.** Add the five missing rows with their `gate` strings taken verbatim from each `ROUTE_GUARDS` entry, and note beside `/design-workshops` and `/questionnaires` that the gate is a SET rather than a threshold, so a professor is refused. Since `ROUTE_GUARDS` is a single literal array, extend `docs/tools/check-docs.mjs` to diff its `path` values against the table the way it already diffs `ROLE_RANK`.

**What the verifier checked.** Read PERMISSIONS.md:586-600 — the table has exactly the seven rows the finder lists and the closing sentence at :597 is as quoted. Grepped `path:` in frontend/lib/permissions.ts and read entries 183-322 in full to confirm the 14 rules, their `can` predicates and their `gate` strings. Checked whether the omissions are harmless: `/admin/analytics` and `/admin/designers` sit under the listed `/admin` rule (and permissions.ts:219-225 says so itself), so those two are cosmetic — I have said so rather than passing the finder's flat "five rows" claim through. The three that matter are real. Verified `/questionnaires` really is `can_run_design_workshops` and that `routeMatches` compares whole segments so `/questionnaire` singular is untouched. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md. MINOR upheld.
