### [LOW] (android) The handset does not mirror the server's conditional-requirement rule, and `DwValues.validate` — the declared client-side copy of `validate_entry` — has no caller outside one unit test

**Verdict:** PARTIAL

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/StageSchema.kt:616-636 (`DwValues.validate`); android/app/src/test/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/NonFiniteValueTest.kt:121 (its only caller); backend/app/services/stage_schema.py:1151, :1165-1167, :1170-1193 (`_check_conditional`)

**What it does.** Two verified facts. (a) `stage_schema.py:1151` runs `_check_conditional(entity, cleaned, errors)` on every entry, deliberately not gated on `enforce_required` (:1172-1177), and `_CONDITIONALLY_REQUIRED = {"outcomes": ("countOverrideReason", ("designsCountOverride", "prototypesCountOverride"))}` (:1165-1167). Neither client mirrors it: `grep countOverrideReason` finds nothing in frontend/ and only the two report readers on Android. (b) `DwValues.validate(entity, data, enforceRequired)` (StageSchema.kt:616-636) has no arm for it and, more to the point, `grep -rn '\.validate('` over android/app/src returns exactly one hit, in `android/app/src/test/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/NonFiniteValueTest.kt:121`. The only validation the form runs is `ScalarInput.commit`'s per-keystroke `DwValues.coerce` (FieldRenderer.kt:737-746), which never asks about requiredness and never sees a value written by `onPatch` hydration or by the photo-measure panel.

**How it fails.** At stage 18, offline, a designer fills `designsCountOverride = 24` and leaves "Reason for the override" blank. The registry marks that field `required: false` (verified in android/app/src/main/assets/design-workshop-schema.json), so `computeStageCompleteness` does not count it, the readiness screen never lists it, and the stage bar can read 100%. Every save of stage 18 from then on comes back with `errors["outcomes"]["countOverrideReason"] = "Reason for the override is required once ... is filled in."`, which the designer first sees when the phone next syncs — a fortnight later — as a refusal card.

**What it costs.** Delayed, not silent, and no data is lost: design_workshops.py:1007-1012 records the error and keeps saving every other key, and :1042-1055 preserves whatever was stored under the refused key. The real cost is that the refusal is invisible for as long as the phone is offline, which is the failure `DwValues` says it exists to prevent, and that a whole validator with a load-bearing KDoc is dead, so any rule added to it in future will not run either.

**Remedy.** Port `_CONDITIONALLY_REQUIRED` into `DwValues` as a data-driven map keyed by entity key, apply it inside `validate` unconditionally (not under `enforceRequired`, matching stage_schema.py:1172-1177), and give `validate` a caller — per entity in `EntitySection`, so the box carries the inline mark the other refusals get. The same gap exists in `frontend/lib`, so fixing only Android leaves the web behind. Do NOT fold it into `isSubmittable`: no client sends `submit`, so it is not in fact a submit gate.

**What the verifier checked.** Confirmed `validate` at StageSchema.kt:616-636 and its single test-only caller by grep. Confirmed the server rule and that it is ungated. Three parts of the finder's story do not survive: (1) "nothing on screen says it is needed" is false — the registry asset gives the field the help text "Required if either count above is filled in. It is printed under the headline figure...", which `FieldCaption` renders under the box; (2) "a web submit 422s" is unsupported — I found no caller that sets `submit` true (frontend/lib/designWorkshopStore.ts:3387 hardcodes `submit: false`, StageScreen.kt:797 says the same for Android), so `enforce_required` is never true from either client and the "told it is submittable when the server will refuse" framing does not hold; (3) it is not an Android-vs-web contract divergence — the web has the identical gap. Severity dropped MINOR→LOW accordingly: the answer is refused but reported, no value is destroyed, and the trigger is a field a designer fills only to override the record.

---

### [LOW] (android) `DwValues.isFilled` has no rich-text arm, so the handset scores a textually-empty narrative document as answered where the server and the browser score it blank

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/StageSchema.kt:465-470, consumed at :863, :900, :916; backend/app/services/stage_schema.py:1229-1245; frontend/lib/designWorkshops.ts:1635-1650; android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/report/RichText.kt:722-726

**What it does.** Verified as quoted. `DwValues.isFilled` ends with `is JsonObject -> value.isNotEmpty()` and has no `blocks` case, while `_is_filled` (stage_schema.py:1239-1243) does `if "blocks" in value: … return not is_empty(value)` and the browser's `isFilled` (designWorkshops.ts:1645) does `if ("blocks" in value) return !isStoredRichTextEmpty(value);`. `computeStageCompleteness` uses the single predicate for the singleton (StageSchema.kt:863), the custom container (:900) and every collection row (:916). The correct port `isEmptyDocument` really is dead: a ripgrep over the whole `android/` tree returns exactly one hit, its own declaration at report/RichText.kt:722.

**How it fails.** A stored value shaped `{"blocks":[{"kind":"PARAGRAPH","spans":[]}]}` in a required narrative scores `requiredFilled` one higher on the phone than on the server and the browser, so the phone's stage index and readiness screen call the stage complete while the same workshop on a laptop marks the field outstanding. Reachability is narrow and I checked why: `coerce_value`'s RICH_TEXT arm normalises it away on the way in (`if is_empty(raw): return None, None`, stage_schema.py:962-966), and the handset's own editor emits null for such a document (RichTextEditor.kt:383), so a value in this shape reaches a draft only from an older build or a direct API caller.

**What it costs.** Two arithmetics for one workshop, with the phone optimistic — the wrong direction, since the phone is the surface a designer uses to decide the fieldwork is done. The handset's scorer also disagrees with the handset's own report model about the same value.

**Remedy.** In the `is JsonObject` arm, return `!com.designprototype.workshop.report.isEmptyDocument(value)` when the object has a `blocks` key and keep `value.isNotEmpty()` otherwise (that arm is right for GEO and for the custom container). That also gives the dead port a caller so R8 stops stripping it.

**What the verifier checked.** I tried to refute it as unreachable and as a duplicate. Unreachability: I read the server's RICH_TEXT coercion arm at stage_schema.py:956-966 and it does normalise empty documents to None, so no value written through the API today can be in this shape — that is exactly why LOW, not higher, and the finder already said so honestly. I also checked whether the handset itself could produce one: the editor's only emit path is RichTextEditor.kt:383, which sends null for a text-empty document (note this is the same predicate as finding 2 — the two defects are adjacent but distinct: one is about what is EMITTED, this is about what is SCORED). I verified the dead-code claim myself with a tree-wide grep rather than trusting it. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md. Severity LOW confirmed — real disagreement, no path a current client can take to create the value.

---

### [LOW] (android) The staged-object journal is written non-atomically and reads a damaged file as empty, stranding uploaded multipart parts in the bucket

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/StagedJournal.kt:64-72 (read/write), :133-148 (sweep); contrast android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/Offline.kt:201-211 and :157-175, WorkshopDraftStore.kt:757-772, StageSchema.kt:1480-1489

**What it does.** Quoted verbatim and correct: `read` returns `runCatching { … }.getOrDefault(emptyList())` on any parse failure, and `write` is a bare `file.writeText(...)` (or a delete when the list is empty). Every sibling durable store in the module uses temp file → `out.fd.sync()` → `renameTo` and treats an unreadable file as damage to be quarantined rather than as an empty document (`OfflineOutbox.write`/`read` at Offline.kt:201-211/:157-175, `WorkshopDraftStore.writeLocked` at :757-772 with its three-point KDoc, `StageSchemaStore.writeCacheFile` at :1480-1489). `writeText` truncates before it writes, so a kill inside that window leaves a truncated array; the next `read` yields an empty list with no error and the next `record` overwrites whatever survived. `sweep` (:133-148) then reclaims nothing because it has nothing to read.

**How it fails.** A designer uploads a large video over a village connection; the presign journals the key with its `uploadId` before the parts move (the field the KDoc at :21-31 was added for). Later in the session a second capture calls `record`, and the process is killed mid-`writeText` by the low-memory killer — the exact condition this journal exists for. On the next launch `sweepStagedObjects` reads the truncated file, gets `emptyList()` silently, and the first `record` of the new session overwrites the remains. The uploaded parts can now be removed only by `AbortMultipartUpload`, which needs the `uploadId` that was in that file.

**What it costs.** Storage billed indefinitely that no sweep, then or later, can name — the exact loss the `uploadId` field was added to prevent, arriving through the write path instead of the record shape. Nothing the designer can see is lost, and it needs a kill inside a small write window, hence LOW.

**Remedy.** Adopt the same three lines as its four siblings: write `staged-objects.json.writing` in the same directory, `out.fd.sync()`, `renameTo` the target, delete the temp and rethrow on failure. And do not read damage as emptiness — either rename it aside the way `OfflineOutbox.quarantine` does (Offline.kt:175-190), or at minimum refuse to overwrite it so the keys stay reclaimable by a later launch or an operator.

**What the verifier checked.** Read the whole file (149 lines) and the three siblings it is compared against; the asymmetry is exactly as described, and its own KDoc at :42-43 claims "the same file + Mutex + kotlinx mechanism the offline outbox uses", which is true of the mutex and false of the write. Tried to refute: (a) is the file small enough that a torn write is impossible? No — `writeText` truncates first, so even a one-entry file has a window in which the file exists and is empty/partial. (b) Does anything else re-derive the keys? `sweep` is the only consumer and it reads this file alone. (c) Would `drop`/`checksumFor` notice? They call the same `read` and get the same silent empty list. Severity LOW is honest: cost is money and orphaned bytes, not field data.

---

### [LOW] (android) An older build that opens a newer draft writes it back stamped with its own schemaVersion, dropping fields it did not know

**Verdict:** PARTIAL

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/WorkshopDraftStore.kt:539 (`private val json = Json { ignoreUnknownKeys = true; encodeDefaults = true }`), :665-670 (the "document from the future is left alone" comment), :835-841 (`update` stamping `schemaVersion = WORKSHOP_DRAFT_SCHEMA_VERSION`), :801-810 (`save` doing the same); android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml:38

**What it does.** The code behaviour is exactly as claimed, at different line numbers: `ignoreUnknownKeys` lets an older build decode a newer document, kotlinx retains none of the unknown keys on the decoded object, and `update`/`save`/`updateBookkeeping` re-encode the model wholesale while forcing `schemaVersion` down to this build's constant (currently 2, :89). After the first debounced save the document is a well-formed v2 with the newer build's fields gone, and no future migration rung can tell. `StageDraft.values`/`custom` are `Map<String, JsonElement>` so unknown ANSWERS survive; the model's own fields do not.

**How it fails.** The finder's scenario is largely blocked by the platform, and this is where they went wrong. (a) The app's own updater fetches "the currently-published release (highest versionCode)" (WorkshopRepository.kt:1729-1730) and Android's package manager refuses to install a lower versionCode over an installed app, so a rollback cannot arrive that way; uninstall-then-install wipes `filesDir` and the drafts with it. (b) `AndroidManifest.xml:38` sets `android:allowBackup="true"` but does NOT set `android:restoreAnyVersion`, whose default is false — so an auto-backup taken on the newer build is not restored onto an older one. What survives is an operator-driven downgrade (`adb install -d`, or an MDM push that keeps app data) on a fleet that side-loads its own APKs, after which the first debounced stage save rewrites every edited draft without the newer build's fields and stamps it v2.

**What it costs.** A silent, unrecoverable downgrade of the document the whole store exists to protect, in the one scenario the ladder's comment says it has handled. Confined to an operator-assisted downgrade, so LOW.

**Remedy.** Refuse to WRITE a draft whose on-disk `schemaVersion` exceeds this build's: return the decoded copy for reading, and quarantine-and-alert rather than overwrite when a save is attempted — the same never-destroy rule the rest of the file already follows (`quarantine`, :721-734). That is about a dozen lines. The heavier alternative (keep the parsed `JsonObject` from `readLocked` and merge the encoded model over it in `writeLocked`, preserving the higher `schemaVersion`) is only worth it if forward-compatible round trips become a requirement.

**What the verifier checked.** Confirmed the code facts at the corrected line numbers (the finder's :508/:631-638/:795-813 are all ~30 lines off but the text matches). Then attacked the reachability specifically, because that is the whole weight of the finding: read the manifest (allowBackup true, no `restoreAnyVersion`, so Android's restore path refuses a newer-version backup onto an older build) and the updater (`latestAppRelease` = highest versionCode; there is no downgrade install path in the app). Also checked `migrate` (:671-709) — it genuinely only climbs, so nothing there rejects or preserves a future document. Verdict PARTIAL: the mechanism is real, the failure scenario as written (a rolled-back release, a phone restored from backup) is mostly unreachable, and the severity stays LOW for the operator-downgrade path that remains.

---

### [LOW] (android) A stage held in a draft but absent from the resolved registry is never pushed and never counted

**Verdict:** PARTIAL

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/WorkshopSync.kt:801 (`schema.stages.forEach` in statusOf) and :1374 (`for (spec in schema.stages.sortedBy { it.number })` in pushStages); android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/StageSchema.kt:1407-1417 (load), :1436-1449 (readCacheFile), :1480-1489 (writeCacheFile)

**What it does.** The structural half is confirmed: both passes enumerate the REGISTRY and look each stage up in the draft, and there is no residual term for `draft.stages.keys - schema.stages.keys`. A stage key the draft holds that the resolved registry does not declare contributes nothing to `pendingStages`, nothing to `problems`, is never built and never sent, and `isFullySynced` can therefore be true over it. The finder's TRIGGER, however, does not hold: `writeCacheFile` (:1480-1489) is a temp → `fd.sync()` → `renameTo` write with exactly the discipline `WorkshopDraftStore.writeLocked` documents, so "a power loss during a `store` leaves the cache file undecodable" is the failure that write was built to prevent. The remaining triggers are narrower: a server registry that RENAMES or REMOVES a stage key that drafts on this handset already hold, or a cache this build's serializer genuinely cannot decode (`readCacheFile` deletes it and `load` falls back to the APK asset) on a device that then stays offline.

**How it fails.** The server registry drops or renames a stage key. Handsets that fetched the older registry hold drafts under the old key (`persistLocally` always writes `stage.key` from whatever registry drew the form). On the next `StageSchemaStore.store` the newer registry replaces the cache, both loops now walk a stage list that no longer contains that key, and the stage's answers are invisible to `pushStages` and to `statusOf` — the workshop scores `isFullySynced` and the row reads "Backed up to the server" over answers that will never leave the phone. The same hole opens the other way round if a decode failure forces the fallback to the bundled asset while the device is out of signal.

**What it costs.** Field answers stranded under a positive claim that they are safe. Real but narrow: it needs a registry whose stage set actually changes (or a cache the build cannot decode plus no connection to refetch), which is why LOW is the honest severity. Note the repo already treats registry drift as a live concern one level down — `dwFoldServerStage` skips rows under entity keys the registry has dropped (DwStageFold.kt:279-283).

**Remedy.** After the registry loop in both passes, walk `draft.stages.keys - schema.stages.map { it.key }` and, for any such stage holding values, rows or custom answers, add a `problems` line naming the key and count it (its own counter, or `failedStages`) so `isFullySynced` cannot be true while it exists. The actionable sentence is "this phone's copy of the stage list is older than this workshop — open the app with a connection"; `designWorkshopSchema(context, forceRefresh = true)` is the remedy and can be offered from the same row.

**What the verifier checked.** Confirmed both loops and the absence of any residual term. Then attacked the premise: read `writeCacheFile` in full, which refutes the "power loss during a store" trigger the finding leans on; read `load`/`readCacheFile` to confirm the delete-and-fall-back-to-asset path is real but requires an actual decode failure; checked that the only writer of `draft.stages` keys is `persistLocally` with `stage.key` from a resolved registry, so the key must have existed in SOME registry version — i.e. the defect cannot fire unless the server's stage set shrinks or renames. Verdict PARTIAL: the hole is real and correctly located, the trigger and therefore the reachability were overstated; severity LOW stands.

---

### [LOW] (android) The RICH_TEXT arm of the field renderer accepts an `error` and draws nothing with it, so a narrative field is the one field type that can never carry an inline refusal mark

**Verdict:** PARTIAL

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/FieldRenderer.kt:293-313 (the arm), :315-518 (every other arm); android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/StageScreen.kt:1602 (`error = errors[field.key]` handed in); backend/app/services/stage_schema.py:956-966 and backend/app/services/rich_text.py:292-303

**What it does.** `FieldRenderer` takes `error: String?` and every arm of the `when` either renders `InlineError(error)` itself (LONG_TEXT via ScalarInput, TIME :349, ENUM :369, MULTI_ENUM :407) or hands `error` to a component that renders it (DateField, BoolField, TagsField, DwGeoField, ArtisanPhoneField, DwReferenceSelectField, MediaField). The RICH_TEXT arm (:293-313) passes `value`, `onChange`, `enabled`, `label`, `help`, `listKind`, `media`, `mediaField`, `onMessage`, `onError` — and never `error`, and `RichTextEditor` has no such parameter. `EntitySection` routes the refusal in at StageScreen.kt:1602 and its KDoc argues the mark must be on the box "rather than only in the card above the form", so the contract is stated and this one arm silently drops it.

**How it fails.** Not reachable today, which is the correction. The only source of `errors` is a server refusal decoded from a save response, and no server path can key an error to a RICH_TEXT field from this client: `coerce_value`'s RICH_TEXT branch returns `to_json(from_json(raw)), None` (stage_schema.py:956-966) and `rich_text.from_json` is documented "Build a document from whatever a client stored, never raising" (rich_text.py:292-293) — it has no raise path and no length bound, so the `except (TypeError, ValueError)` at :1092 cannot fire for it. `_check_conditional` covers one TEXT field only, and no client ever sends `submit`, so the "X is required" refusal cannot be produced either. The arm's `error` is therefore always null as the code stands.

**What it costs.** No designer can hit this today; what exists is a silently broken contract in the largest type in the registry, which goes live the moment any bound, conditional rule or required-enforcement reaches a narrative field — and it would go live silently, since nothing fails and the refusal card would still list the field while the box beside it stayed unmarked.

**Remedy.** Wrap the arm in braces and add `InlineError(error)` after `RichTextEditor(...)`, exactly as the TIME/ENUM/MULTI_ENUM arms do — the `when` sits inside the shared `Column`, so no parameter needs adding to `RichTextEditor`.

**What the verifier checked.** Read FieldRenderer.kt:277-518 in full and confirmed RICH_TEXT is the only arm that neither draws nor forwards `error`; confirmed StageScreen.kt:1602 does supply it. I then went to the server to test the finder's stated reachability ("reachable when the server's coerce_value RICH_TEXT branch raises inside from_json") and it does not hold: `from_json`'s own docstring promises it never raises, there is no `max_length` check on the RICH_TEXT path, and both clients hardcode `submit: false`, so the required-error route is closed too. The finder had already rated it LOW; I keep LOW but marked PARTIAL because the failure scenario as written asserts a route that the code rules out. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

### [LOW] (android) The export screen states the completeness percentage "across the 22 stages" from a hard-coded literal, on the one screen whose own warning exists because the registry can gain stages

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/ReportScreen.kt:522-526 and :277-280, against ReportPlan.kt:169-182 and StageIndexScreen.kt:389-393

**What it does.** Confirmed verbatim. ReportScreen.kt:522-526 renders `"$percent% of the required fields across the 22 stages are filled in."`. `percent` is `overallPercent(computeWorkshopCompleteness(registry, stored, customSections))` (:277-280), where `registry` is `repository.designWorkshopSchema(appContext)` (:232) — whatever the server served, walked as `schema.stages`. `unknownStages`/`unknownStageWarning` (ReportPlan.kt:169-182) exist precisely because 'a stage added on the server appears in the phone's registry' while the template catalogue is compiled into the APK, and that warning is appended to the SAME `warnings` list this screen renders eight lines below the sentence. `StageIndexScreen` gets it right one screen away: `"$overall% overall · $done of ${stages.size} stages complete"` (:390).

**How it fails.** The ministry adds a 23rd stage on the server. A designer opens the report screen on a handset that has refreshed the registry: the percentage is computed over 23 stages, the warning card below correctly says 'This workshop has 1 stage(s) that are newer than this app's report templates', and the line above it says the figure is 'across the 22 stages'.

**What it costs.** Two sentences on one screen disagree about how many stages the workshop has, on the screen a designer reads to decide whether the report is fit to hand over. Narrow — it needs a registry change to fire, hence LOW — but it is a one-token fix on a screen that already holds the answer.

**Remedy.** `"$percent% of the required fields across the ${schema?.stages?.size ?: 0} stages are filled in."`, or hold the count in state beside `percent` when the effect runs, matching `StageIndexScreen`'s `stages.size`.

**What the verifier checked.** Read ReportScreen.kt:200-330 and :505-535, ReportPlan.kt:127-182, StageIndexScreen.kt:375-400. Tried to refute by checking whether the handset pins its registry to 22 — it does not; `designWorkshopSchema` is a network refresh and `unknownStageWarning` is written for exactly the case where it returns more. Confirmed the bundled asset holds 22 today, so nothing is wrong on current data. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md. Severity LOW is the finder's own and is honest.

### [LOW] (android) `runCatching` around the local stage write and the push catches the coroutine's own cancellation, contradicting the rule this same file states 800 lines later

**Verdict:** PARTIAL

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/StageScreen.kt:774-779 (the local write), :799-801 (the push), :908-914 (the debounce that cancels), :1608-1617 (the rule the file states); android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/MainActivity.kt:1212-1217 (`showMessage`)

**What it does.** `runCatching { persistLocally(...) }.onFailure { error -> saveState = SaveState.PENDING; onError(error.message ?: "Could not write this stage to the device."); return }` (:774-779) and `runCatching { WorkshopSyncEngine.pushStage(...) }.getOrElse { StagePush.NotSent }` (:799-801) both catch `Throwable`, which includes `CancellationException`. `saveAndSync` runs inside `LaunchedEffect(revision)` (:908-914), which Compose cancels on the next keystroke, and `persistLocally` suspends on `Dispatchers.IO` and on `WorkshopDraftStore`'s store-wide `Mutex` (WorkshopDraftStore.kt:830-831), so cancellation can and does land inside it. The file states the opposite rule at :1608-1617 — `catch (e: CancellationException) { ... throw e }` with the comment "a `runCatching` here would eat the cancellation".

**How it fails.** A designer types, pauses 800 ms so the debounce fires, then resumes typing while the draft write is waiting on the store mutex behind a neighbouring stage's own auto-save or a media import. The effect is cancelled, `runCatching` catches it, and the cancelled coroutine runs on: it sets `saveState = SaveState.PENDING` and calls `onError`, or — in the push arm — falls into the `NotSent` branch and assigns `saveState = SaveState.ON_DEVICE` / `syncNote = null` after the newly-launched effect has already set `PENDING`, so the status line can read "Saved on this device" over an edit that is not yet written. The next debounce corrects it 800 ms later.

**What it costs.** A transient wrong status line, and a stated invariant of this file broken in the one place it matters most. NOT the red "Could not write this stage to the device." alarm the finder describes: that string is only used when `error.message` is null, and a cancellation always carries a message.

**Remedy.** Still worth doing, as hygiene rather than as a bug fix: rethrow before treating a throwable as failure at both sites — `.onFailure { error -> if (error is CancellationException) throw error; saveState = SaveState.PENDING; onError(...); return }` and `.getOrElse { if (it is CancellationException) throw it; StagePush.NotSent }`. `kotlinx.coroutines.CancellationException` is already imported at StageScreen.kt:96. Do not rely on the string filter below as the guard — it is a backstop keyed on exception prose, not a contract.

**What the verifier checked.** I confirmed the two `runCatching` sites, the cancelling `LaunchedEffect(revision)`, and the contrasting rethrow at :1608-1617, and confirmed `WorkshopDraftStore.update` really does suspend (`withContext(Dispatchers.IO) { mutex.withLock { ... } }`, WorkshopDraftStore.kt:830-831) so cancellation is deliverable inside it. Then I chased the caller, which is what kills the headline: `StageScreen` is wired `onError = { showMessage(it) }` (MainActivity.kt:2154), and `showMessage` returns early for any message containing "left the composition", "was cancelled" or "job was cancelled" (MainActivity.kt:1212-1217, with a comment saying it exists for exactly this). A cancellation from `LaunchedEffect`/`withContext`/`Mutex` propagates a `JobCancellationException` whose message is always of the form "<Coroutine> was cancelled", so `error.message` is non-null, matches the filter, and nothing is shown — the fallback literal is unreachable on this path. Severity dropped MINOR→LOW and the failure scenario rewritten to the stale-status race, which does survive.

### [LOW] (android) A saved stage silently deletes any draft row whose entity key the loaded registry no longer declares, while unknown singleton and custom KEYS are deliberately carried through

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/StageScreen.kt:2032-2039 (`fromDraft`), :2108-2112 (`persistLocally`'s `rows`), contrast :2093 and :2101 (`values`/`custom` written wholesale); android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/DwStageFold.kt:270, :278-283; android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/data/StageSchema.kt:1436-1449 (the registry fallback)

**What it does.** `fromDraft` builds the form's collections from the SPEC — `collections = stage.collections.associate { entity -> entity.key to draft.rowsFor(entity.key)... }` (:2032-2039) — so a row filed under an entity the loaded registry does not declare is never read into `state`. `persistLocally` then rebuilds the whole `rows` list from that same spec walk, `rows = stage.collections.flatMap { entity -> state.collections[entity.key].orEmpty().map { ... } }` (:2108-2112), so the undeclared rows are not written back and are gone from disk after one debounced save. `values` and `custom` are written wholesale (:2093, :2101) precisely so unknown keys survive; `rows` is the exception. `dwFoldServerStage` preserves them (`val rows = ArrayList(base.rows)`, DwStageFold.kt:270) and guards the same drift condition explicitly at :278-283, so the condition is treated as reachable elsewhere in the same feature.

**How it fails.** The handset fetches a registry declaring a new collection on stage 9 and the designer records four rows in it. The registry cache is then lost or damaged — `readCacheFile` deletes a file it cannot decode and falls through to the bundled asset (StageSchema.kt:1439-1448), and a server rollback re-storing an older registry does the same via `store` (:1427-1434). The designer opens stage 9, sees no sign that anything is missing, types one character into an unrelated field, and the four rows are removed from the draft — never drawn, never counted, never warned about, and unrecoverable on the device.

**What it costs.** Captured rows destroyed by the one code path in this file that does not honour the survival rule the file's own header states for values. Latent: it needs the phone's registry to lose an entity it previously had, which takes a corrupt cache or a server-side registry rollback.

**Remedy.** Carry undeclared rows through untouched, as `values` and `custom` are. In `persistLocally`, append `existing?.rows.orEmpty().filterNot { row -> stage.collections.any { it.key == row.entityKey() } }` to the rebuilt list. `DraftRow.entityKey()` already exists (StageSchema.kt:1534) and `buildStageBody` walks `spec.collections` (WorkshopSync.kt:1696), so nothing undeclared can reach the wire.

**What the verifier checked.** Read both cited sites; the quoted code is present, at :2032-2039 and :2108-2112 rather than the finder's :1990/:2064. I looked for a guard that would make it unreachable and found the opposite: `dwFoldServerStage` starts from `ArrayList(base.rows)` so the fold preserves undeclared rows, which means only this write destroys them — an asymmetry inside one feature, not a consistent policy. I also checked whether a registry can actually regress on a device, and it can by two routes (cache decode failure → bundled asset, StageSchema.kt:1439-1448; `store` writing any fetched registry including an older one, :1427-1434, whose return value exists specifically to warn that "drafts written against the previous registry may hold keys this one no longer knows"). LOW is honest. Not in docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md.

### [LOW] (cross-surface) `GET /designers/directory` derives its role set from the rank ladder and so includes PROFESSOR, whom the viewer write refuses — but no picker consumes it, so the failure scenario as written cannot happen

**Verdict:** PARTIAL

**Where.** backend/app/api/routes/designers.py:57-62 and :235; against backend/app/core/deps.py:37-48 and :99; docstring at designers.py:231-232; frontend/lib/designers.ts:251-260 and :274-280

**What it does.** The code fact holds. `WORKSHOP_CAPABLE_ROLES = [role for role, rank in ROLE_RANK.items() if rank >= ROLE_RANK["DESIGNER"]]` evaluates to ['DESIGNER','PROFESSOR','ADMIN','MASTER_ADMIN'] because PROFESSOR is 40 and DESIGNER is 35 (deps.py:43-46), while the actual capability is the non-monotonic set `DESIGN_WORKSHOP_ROLES = frozenset({"DESIGNER","ADMIN","MASTER_ADMIN"})` (deps.py:99). A professor therefore comes back from the directory with `canSignIn: true` (designers.py:262-263 computes `gated = role_value(user) == "DESIGNER"`, so a professor is ungated and unmarked). What does NOT hold is the finder's failure scenario. The finder calls this route "the assignment picker"; it is not. The viewer picker on both clients is `GET /design-workshops/eligible-viewers` (frontend/lib/designWorkshopViewers.ts:185, frontend/components/settings/DesignWorkshopViewersPanel.tsx, android/…/WorkshopViewersScreen.kt), which builds its own eligible set. Every live consumer of `/designers/directory` uses it only as an email→account-id join so a roster row can offer "Open designer profile": frontend/app/(protected)/admin/designers/page.tsx:143-147 and android/…/DesignerRosterScreen.kt:178 (via WorkshopRepository.kt:826-827), both documented as exactly that. No screen offers a directory row as a viewer grant, so no 422 and no discarded roster change is reachable.

**How it fails.** Reachable today only as documentation drift: `designers.py:231-232` ("Accounts above DESIGNER are listed unconditionally — they can run a workshop") and `frontend/lib/designers.ts:255-259` ("for a professor or an admin — who can run a workshop and whom the roster does not gate at all") both state something the server refuses at design_workshop_viewers.py:456-460. A maintainer who builds the assignment picker this endpoint's own name promises — or who copies its role set into a new surface — ships a picker offering professors, whose grant is refused all-or-nothing ("Nothing was changed.") for the whole PUT body.

**What it costs.** No live user-visible failure. The concrete cost is that the one non-monotonic rule in the permission model has a second, wrong copy on the server, in an endpoint whose module docstring names it "who an admin may hand a workshop to" — a copy that will be trusted the first time somebody builds that picker.

**Remedy.** `from app.core.deps import DESIGN_WORKSHOP_ROLES` and set `WORKSHOP_CAPABLE_ROLES = sorted(DESIGN_WORKSHOP_ROLES)`. The comment at designers.py:57-59 argues for the opposite property — a tier inserted above DESIGNER must NOT enter this set — and should be replaced. Correct the docstring at :231-232 and the type doc at frontend/lib/designers.ts:255-259 in the same change. Either rename the endpoint to what it actually serves (the roster's email→account join) or make it delegate to `eligible_viewers`, so there is one server answer to "who may be handed a workshop".

**What the verifier checked.** Verified WORKSHOP_CAPABLE_ROLES at designers.py:57-62 and ROLE_RANK at deps.py:37-48 (PROFESSOR 40, DESIGNER 35) — the set does include PROFESSOR. Verified DESIGN_WORKSHOP_ROLES at deps.py:99 and the refusal at design_workshop_viewers.py:452-465 — all-or-nothing 422 confirmed. Then hunted for the picker the finder describes: grepped all of frontend/ and android/ for `listDesignerDirectory` / `designers/directory` / `designerDirectory`. Only three consumers exist — frontend/lib/designers.ts (the wrapper), admin/designers/page.tsx:143 (builds `Map(email→id)` for profile links, and `DIRECTORY_CAP`), and DesignerRosterScreen.kt:178 (identical purpose, documented at :89-101 as "the only join between the two … fetched here for that single purpose"). Grepped for the real viewer picker and found it on eligible-viewers on both clients. So there is no screen on which an admin can select a directory row and have it become a viewer grant; the 422/discarded-roster failure is not reachable. Downgraded MAJOR→LOW accordingly and rewrote the scenario.

### [LOW] (cross-surface) The web mirror refuses a master admin managing a master-admin peer; the backend allows it, and the only peer protection keys on the configured email rather than the role

**Verdict:** CONFIRMED

**Where.** frontend/lib/permissions.ts:60-63 (`canManageUser`) against backend/app/api/routes/users.py:51-60 (`assert_can_manage_target`) and :63-74 (`is_master_email`, `assert_not_demoting_master`)

**What it does.** Both quotes verified. `canManageUser` returns `target.role !== "MASTER_ADMIN" || target.id === user?.id` for a master admin — so a peer master-admin row is unmanageable in the browser. `assert_can_manage_target` returns unconditionally for a master admin (`if is_master_admin(current_user): return`) with no peer test, so `PATCH /users/{id}` and `DELETE /users/{id}` accept a second MASTER_ADMIN as target. The only peer protection is `assert_not_demoting_master`, whose first line is `if not is_master_email(target_user.email): return` — keyed on `MASTER_ADMIN_EMAIL` from settings, not on the role. So any MASTER_ADMIN account other than the configured address (one a master admin may mint, since `assert_role`:42-43 permits granting MASTER_ADMIN when the caller is one) is fully demotable and deletable over the API while both browsers render the row as unmanageable.

**How it fails.** A master admin promotes a deputy to MASTER_ADMIN for a handover, then wants to reverse it. The Users screen shows no controls on that row, so they conclude the account is protected the way theirs is. `PATCH /api/users/{deputy}` with `{"role":"ADMIN"}` succeeds, and so does `DELETE`. Symmetrically, the deputy can demote or delete any master admin except the configured-email one, from the API, with both browsers showing the rows as unmanageable.

**What it costs.** Not an escalation — the UI is the stricter side — but the two mirrors disagree about the highest-privilege account class, and docs/PERMISSIONS.md §2 states the frontend's rule as the system's. An operator's model of who can remove whom is wrong in whichever direction they formed it. LOW: it needs a second MASTER_ADMIN account to exist at all.

**Remedy.** Decide once and write it once. If a master admin may act on a peer (the backend's answer, consistent with `can_review_record`'s master-admin short-circuit), relax `canManageUser` to `if (isMasterAdmin(user)) return true;` and amend §2. If peers are protected (the frontend's answer), key `assert_not_demoting_master` — or `assert_can_manage_target` — on `role == "MASTER_ADMIN"` rather than on the configured email, so protection follows the privilege rather than one address in the environment.

**What the verifier checked.** Read users.py:36-74 verbatim: `assert_can_manage_target` has no peer branch for a master admin, and `assert_not_demoting_master` short-circuits on `is_master_email` before any role test. Read permissions.ts:56-63 and confirmed `canManageUser`'s peer refusal plus its self-exception. Checked whether a second MASTER_ADMIN can exist at all: `assert_role`:42-43 permits `role == "MASTER_ADMIN"` when `is_master_admin(current_user)`, so it can. Looked for another backend guard on the PATCH/DELETE routes that would catch it — the two asserts above are the only ones. Confirmed the direction: the UI is stricter, so there is no privilege escalation, only a mirror disagreement, which keeps this at LOW exactly as the finder rated it.

---

## Refuted

Reported by an audit agent and destroyed by its verifier. They are kept because the next person
to read this code will have the same thought, and the reason it does not hold is worth more than
the finding would have been.

### The photographic annexure ignores `section.include_photos` and `section.photo_columns`

**Where.** backend/app/services/report_builder.py:1850-1868 (`_render_media_annexure`); the deliberate specification of this behaviour is at android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/ReportScreen.kt:1325-1331 and the per-section control is `includeMediaAnnexure` at backend/app/services/report_templates.py:502-506, :580-582

**Why it does not hold.** Read report_builder.py:1850-1868 — the quoted code is verbatim and `section.include_photos`/`photo_columns`/`max_photos` are genuinely unread. Confirmed apply_report_settings:702-710 does stamp both onto every section including this one, and confirmed every other renderer gates on them (1096-1097, 1112-1113, 1236, 1443-1444, 1749). So the finder's facts are right. What kills the finding is the file the finder cited for its consequence but did not read: ReportScreen.kt:1325-1331 names both behaviours, calls them the server's, and says they are reproduced deliberately because a divergence between the phone's copy and the office's is the defect that area exists to end — and names `includeMediaAnnexure` as the control that removes the section. I checked that control works: it is in `_SECTION_TOGGLES` (report_templates.py:504) and DETAILED_TECHNICAL is the only template carrying the section (:446), so an explicit false removes it. Also checked ReportSettings.kt:333-336, which tells the designer-facing ledger the same thing. A behaviour specified in two places, implemented identically on both surfaces, with a dedicated per-section control, is a design decision — rule 5 puts it out of scope.

### A stage excluded from the report still contributes its photographs to the photographic annexure

**Where.** backend/app/services/report_builder.py:1853 (`for spec in stages():`); the exclusion is at backend/app/services/report_templates.py:587-591; the decision is recorded at android/app/src/main/java/com/designprototype/workshop/ui/designworkshop/ReportScreen.kt:1317-1323

**Why it does not hold.** Verified the code: report_builder.py:1852-1861 is verbatim, `stages()` is the global registry, and `excludedStages` (report_templates.py:587-591) touches only `sections`, so an excluded stage's photographs really are re-gathered with `caption or spec.title`. I then looked for the guard the finder did not look for, in the port they cited as having 'the identical scope' — ReportScreen.kt:1317-1323 states this scope as deliberate and names the excluded-stage case explicitly. I also confirmed the escape hatch works (`includeMediaAnnexure` in `_SECTION_TOGGLES`, report_templates.py:504, and DETAILED_TECHNICAL is the only carrier). One further check: `_images` still applies `_visible`, so tier-suppressed media fields do not leak in. A documented, cross-port-consistent scope decision with a working per-section off switch is taste, not a defect.

## What this audit did not do

Stated so the coverage is not read as wider than it is.

* **Nothing was executed on a handset.** Every Android finding is from source. The JVM suite
  (1156 tests) covers the pure decisions; it cannot cover the platform.
* **No browser was driven.** Frontend findings are from source and from the pure specs, not from
  a rendered page.
* **The AI surfaces were not audited at all** — see the scope table. A defect reachable only
  through an AI file was reported, and says so.
* **This is a correctness audit.** Performance, bundle size, accessibility and visual design were
  out of brief except where a defect happened to sit in one of them.
* **No fix here has been verified**, because no fix here has been written. That is the difference
  between this file and `docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md`.

## How this document is kept true

Generated on 2026-08-15 from the audit run's structured output. **Every finding names a
`file:line`; the way to keep this document true is to re-read those lines.** As items are fixed,
move them into `docs/OPEN_FINDINGS.md` with the test that pins them, and strike them here — the
rule that file already states: *if you close something, mark it closed in the same commit that
closes it.* A finding that disagrees with the code is worse than no finding, because it sends the
next reader hunting for a bug that is gone.
